# 07. AI and weapons: entities, the generic AI, weapons, damage, radar

Scope: the other aircraft and objects of a flight and everything that fights. Part A (sections 1 to 6) is the entities: the 16 records and the 16 secondary objects, the spawner and the template copy, the generic AI and its steering, the dying path of non-player records, the tracking cameras and the ejection. Part B (sections 7 to 13) is the weapons, guidance, hits, countermeasures, damage, effects, the radar list, target designation, the trail and the jolt, and the sound trigger points. The two parts are written by different authors; each edits only its own sections and its own sub-headings under Constants, Open questions and Verified by.

Sections 1 to 13 together are this note's "Format or Behaviour" part (`docs/re/README.md`); "Location" is section 1.

Tags used for rules: **V** = verified on the reference emulator (a capture) or in the Musashi rig, with the count stated; **R** = read from the code and not run; **C** = a port choice or convention, labelled as such. Game text is never quoted.

## 1. Location and the entity records

This section settles what the other aircraft, ships and objects are in memory: the 16 entity records, the 16 secondary objects, who steps which record, and every record field that the AI, the weapons and the mission scripts read or write. Sections 2 to 6 build on it. Addresses are flat image addresses (`00-binary-layout.md`); `V:xxxxx` is a variable in the zero-filled hunks 71 to 73. The step counter `V:4A992` is the pass counter of the world update (one increment per pass, note 08 section 5.1); the port's step is one pass, so every rule stated "every Nth frame" below is "every Nth step" with the same phase [C, with the conversion argued in section 3.4].

### 1.1 Where things live

| what | address | notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the 16 entity records | `0x4B23C`, stride `0x200` | record i at `0x4B23C + 0x200 * i`; `V:4AA6C` is the index of the record being stepped and `V:4AA6E` its byte offset (`0x200 * i`) |
| the 16 secondary objects | `0x4D23C`, stride `0x20` | created by the explosion routine `0x2AA8E` (1.5) |
| run-time model descriptors | `0x2600C`, stride 20 | slot s holds the five-long template record the entity uses (section 2.3); slot 0 is the player's |
| template table | `0x25ECC`, 134 records of 20 bytes | note 02 section 1; copied into the descriptors by the spawners |
| the world update | `0x26AF8` | per pass; the order of records and the routines each calls are in 1.2 |
| class dispatch of a record's step | `0x278F6` | class nibble 0: the weapon routine `0x27DC2`; `0x30`: the seat and parachute routine `0x27BFA`; `0x20`: no step of its own; anything else: the AI of sections 3 and 4 |
| AI state routine | `0x30A7E` | section 3 |
| the per-step flight routine | `0x2A2A2` | note 05; calls the AI state routine for every record but the player |
| placement at mission start | `0x2CE36`, entry placer `0x2D248`, destination and aim `0x2CF14` | section 2 |
| region triggers and exits | `0x2D0AA` (every 16th step), `0x2D22A`, `0x2D116`, `0x2D53C` (per record, every 32nd step) | sections 2 and 3 |
| position blocks and waypoint lists | `0x2DCE8` | note 02 section 9; a word table of offsets, each naming a run of blocks of five words |
| spawn rows, lists and regions | `0x2DEDA`, `0x2DE28` | note 08 section 6.1 |

### 1.2 Who is stepped: the roles of the 16 records

The world update visits the records in index order, once per pass. Each visit has a gate before the flight step `0x2A2A2`; the player (record 0) is handled first by the pre-steps of note 05 section 1. Roles are fixed by the update, not by the data:

| record | role | gate before its flight step | evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | the player | the player's pre-steps (note 05) | R |
| 1, 2, 3 | the player's weapon and seat records (missiles, ejection seat, parachute) | a valid record (`W0` bit `0x0040`) is stepped. An invalid one: if the launch request `V:4A872` is clear the seat routine `0x26F60` is asked (section 6) and the record is stepped only if it filled it; if the request is set the player launch `0x275C4` runs (it clears the request and fills the record when the player has a missile to fire) and the record is stepped | R; the launch is note 07 part B |
| 4, 6, 8, 10, 12 | the aircraft slots (hostile, friendly, the training lead in slot 4) | stepped only when valid (`W0` bit `0x0040`) | R, placement data: V (section 2.4) |
| 5, 9, 11, 13 | the missile records of the aircraft in 4, 8, 10 and 12 (owner = the aircraft in the record one below) | when not valid: record 5 tries `0x26F8E` (the seat routine again, without its parachute branch) unless the request `V:4A873` is set (then `0x275F6` launches); records 9, 11 and 13 ask the launch decision `0x2701A` (section 4.6) and launch with `0x275F6`; a valid record is stepped by the class dispatch (class nibble 0) | R |
| 6, 7 | slot 7 is never visited (the update loads its address and does nothing with it); record 6 is an aircraft slot without a missile record | R |
| 14, 15 | carrier and second ship (class `0x20`) | stepped only when valid; the bit `0x04` of the flag byte (not in any queue) is set every pass | R; carrier record 14 (note 05 17.14) |

**The seat can take record 5.** When the player's ejection cycle reaches the nibble 3 with records 1 to 3 all in use, the visit of record 5 (its second seat entry `0x26F8E`) spawns the seat there, and the latch `V:4AA7A` is then `0x0A00` [V: `seat_check.py` part 4, records 1-3 holding weapons: the seat in record 5 at the same pass]. **Slot 7** is never named by a shipped placement entry (the slots used are 4, 6, 8, 10, 12 for aircraft, 14 for the carrier, 11 for the pod, 15 for the submarine; the training lead is in 4) [R: all entries of the table `0x2DEDA` and the regions read].

The world update clears the launch requests `V:4A872`, `V:4A873`, `V:4A874` at its end (`0x2852E`), so a request that finds no free record in its pass is dropped, not kept [R]. Placed pods, the cruise missile and the airliners sit in the slots the placement entry names (for example the pod in slot 11, the missile record of aircraft 10), which is why class decides the step and not the slot.

### 1.3 The class byte `+0x62`

| class | what | stepped by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `0x10` | F-16 (the scenario's friendly, the training lead) | the AI, the flight step |
| `0x11` | the player's F/A-18 | the player step |
| `0x12`, `0x13` | MiG-29, MiG-25 | the AI |
| `0x14` | Air Force One | the AI (own tables: the throttle rule 4.2, the rate table `0x31358` and `0x3136E`) |
| `0x15` | cruise missile | the AI; reaching the end of its waypoint list sets the crashed bit (4.3); its height is forced to `0xB000` after every position update (4.12) |
| `0x16`, `0x17` | 707 and 767 airliners | the AI |
| `0x20`, `0x21` | carrier, submarine | the flight step only (no AI) |
| `0x30` | the ejection seat (record built by `0x27522`) | `0x27BFA` (section 6) |
| `0x31` | the parachute (built by `0x2758E`) | `0x27BFA` |
| `0x32` | rescue pod | `0x27BFA` (the class nibble is 3: the dispatch sends all of `0x30` to `0x32` there; section 6) |
| `0x00`, `0x01` | a missile of the player or of an AI aircraft: the launch routine `0x275F6` writes class 1 when the owner's range class `+0x63` has the high nibble 3 (the long-range missile) and class 0 otherwise | `0x27DC2` (note 07 part B) |

Every class test in the AI compares the high nibble except the exact tests `0x14` and `0x15` (and `0x10` in the table choices of 3.6) [R].

### 1.4 The field table

Offsets in bytes from the start of the record. W0 is the word at `+0x00`, W2 the word at `+0x02`; their low bytes are `+0x01` and `+0x03`. Fields that only the flight step uses are listed in note 05 (state block); this table gives them one line and adds every field that the AI, the weapons, the placement and the scripts touch. "Writers" lists the routines that store the field; "R" in the last column marks rows read from code only.

| offset | size | name | meaning | writers | tag |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `+0x00` | word | W0 | flags. `0x8000` hit this step; `0x4000` radar bookkeeping; `0x2000` set by the wing-levelling of the input conversion and cleared by the dying path; `0x1000` **active in the simulation** (the step moves the record; the AI gate requires it); `0x0800` brake; `0x0400` **dying** (countdown running, section 5); `0x0200` crashed; `0x0100` keeps moving in the external view; `0x0080` **AI valid** (the state routine `0x30A7E` runs only with it; the placement sets it for every class but `0x20`); `0x0040` **valid / in play** (the "active" bit of the display note: the record is a member of the world and may be queued); `0x0010` **pending** (note 04a: set on all records by every accepted list rebuild, cleared when the record is inserted into a queue); `0x0008` **not counted** at placement (bit 15 of the entry's flags, 2.2) and, for a weapon record, collidable; `0x0004` **not in any queue** (set for records 14 and 15 every pass); `0x0002` spawn flag (set by the spawn tail `0x2781A`, section 2.8, read by the weapon and AI routines `0x27E54` and `0x27BEA`; the world update clears it **for the player only**, at `0x26BEE`, before the player's step: other records keep it until they are freed; note 05's table says "every entity" and is corrected); `0x0001` **engaged**: set when the AI finds its target record valid (`0x27EE6`), by the placement flag bit 13, cleared when the record is sent to a destination block (`0x2D116`, `0x2D53C`, `0x281D6`) | placement `0x2D248`; AI `0x27EE6`; step `0x2A2A2`; dying path | V (placement), R |
| `+0x02` | word | W2 | `0x0100` **scripted** (the AI steering is replaced by the mission script or the training sequencer; the AI state routine is not called in state 0); `0x0080` on the ground; `0x0020` ejection release; `0x0010` recentre request (set by the AI states, consumed by the flight step); `0x0008` afterburner; `0x2000` military power; `0x0001` **proximity**: another record's collision scan found an overlap with this record this pass; the world update clears it for records 0 to 14 at the start of every pass; the AI uses it to start an avoidance (4.4) | flight step, scripts, scans | V, R |
| `+0x04` | byte | B4 | `0x80`, `0x40` deck points; `0x20` **threatened**: the AI's target is pointing at this record (set by `0x281E2`, 4.5); `0x01` the range word `+0x4A` has been computed | AI `0x281E2`, `0x28472` | V |
| `+0x05` | byte | state | the AI state (3.2): 0 idle, 1 re-evaluate, 6 wait, 8 waypoint path, `0x0A` training wait, 2, 5, 9 evasion, 7 break-turn pull, `0x0B` to `0x28` the training manoeuvres (note 08 8.5); also the wreck drift for `0x0A` (note 05) and 1 after an entity collision | AI, placement (flag bit 14 sets 8), collisions | V |
| `+0x06`, `+0x08` | word | cell x, z | position >> 22 (table C cell) | placement, flight step | V |
| `+0x0A`, `+0x0B` | byte | sub-cell, neighbour | note 05 | flight step | V |
| `+0x0C`, `+0x0E` | word | fine x, z | `(position & 0x3FFFFF) >> 8` | placement, flight step | V |
| `+0x10` | long | y >> 8 | altitude in 256-unit steps | placement, flight step | V |
| `+0x14`, `+0x18`, `+0x1C` | long | position | x, y, z in position units | placement, flight step | V |
| `+0x16` | word | low half of x | read by the avoidance routine as a pseudo-random bit; the result is a dead store (4.4) | | R |
| `+0x20` | byte | B20 | `0x02` **out of control** (wreck: the AI is replaced by the wreck rule 4.1, the steering of 3.5 uses the stored height), `0x08`, `0x10` phase flags of the training manoeuvres, stall and skid bits of note 05 | flight step, AI | V |
| `+0x26` | word | ground term / missile life | note 05; for class 0 the AI steering counts it down (3.5) | | R |
| `+0x28`, `+0x29`, `+0x2A` | signed byte | commands | pitch, rudder, roll command | input conversion | V |
| `+0x2B` | signed byte | throttle | `-32..120`; **placed at 0**; cleared by the Air Force One rule 4.2; the AI changes the *speed* through the key byte, not this byte | scripts, `0x27B0A` | V |
| `+0x2C`, `+0x2E` | word | destination cell x, z | the point the steering aims at, as a table C cell; **negative x means no destination**: the AI returns at once (3.5) | placement 2.2, AI 4.3, 4.5 and exits, scripts | V |
| `+0x30`, `+0x32` | word | destination fine x, z | fine position inside the cell (256-unit steps, 14 bits) | as above | V |
| `+0x34` | long | destination height | in the units of `+0x10` | as above | V |
| `+0x38` | byte | target / leader | `0xFF` none (the AI then treats the player as its target when the player is near, 4.4); bit 7 set with bits 0 to 6 = the **record index** of the target or leader; values with bit 7 clear are read by the steering as a shift count of the destination words (3.5); placement writes `0xFF` or `0x80 + index` | placement, AI, scripts | V |
| `+0x39` | byte | range timer | the high nibble counts the calls of the range routine (4.2) and is the gate of the threatened test; the low nibble is the throttle-key ramp of note 05 | AI `0x283E0` | V |
| `+0x3A` | byte | list index | bits 8 to 14 of the entry's word 2: the index of the waypoint run in the table `0x2DCE8` that state 8 follows | placement | V |
| `+0x3C` | byte | hit counts | the low nibble counts the gun hits taken (the third sets W0 `0x0200`, section 5.1) and the high nibble a second count raised by the missile hits (note 07 part B); counted from the placement's zero | gun and missile hit code `0x2B25A`, `0x2B2D2` | R |
| `+0x3D` | byte | trail length | note 05 | camera trail | R |
| `+0x3E`, `+0x42`, `+0x46` | long | velocity | the displacement added per step; `+0x42` negative means descending in the AI's altitude rules | flight step | V |
| `+0x4A` | word | range | distance from the record to its destination (3.5, 4.2), 0 to `0x36C0`, `0x7FFF` = beyond range or unknown (set by the waypoint advance too). Unit: 256 position units (the fine step of the cell words; a cell is 16384). **For the player** the same routine tail computes it toward the designated target `V:4AA78` (`0x2835A`, called from the world update's player pre-steps, with the same `+0x39` gating), and the HUD range reads it | AI `0x283E0`, `0x27BAE`; player `0x2835A` | V |
| `+0x4C` | word | counter | decremented once per step by `0x278F6`; the AI state timer; the dying countdown (15 at crash, 20 after expiry) | | V |
| `+0x50`, `+0x52`, `+0x54` | word | roll / yaw / pitch rate targets | written by the steering for non-aircraft classes (3.5); read by the flight step | AI | V |
| `+0x56`, `+0x58`, `+0x5A` | word | angular rates | pitch, heading, roll | flight step | V |
| `+0x5D` | byte | placement tag | `0xFF` for a mission-start placement; for a region placement the number of the region plus 1 when the entry is the first of its pass, else 1 after a placed entry and 0 after a refused one (2.5); `+0x5D = 0` makes the region check post a notice (3.4) | placement | V |
| `+0x5E` | byte | group | the slot number; records with equal group do not collide (note 05) | placement | V |
| `+0x5F` | byte | stock | missiles left: the high nibble short-range, the low nibble long-range (which one a launch uses depends on the range class `+0x63`, note 07 part B); **placement writes `0x44`** | placement, launch | V |
| `+0x60` | word | rounds | gun rounds; **placement writes 500 (`0x01F4`)** | placement, weapon code | V |
| `+0x62`, `+0x63` | byte | class, range class | `+0x62` class (1.3); `+0x63`: the high nibble is the **range class** of the record's destination or target (1 within `0x300`, 2 within `0xC00`, 3 beyond up to `0x1800`, `0xF` beyond; set by `0x283E0` for records but the player), the low nibble `0xD` from the placement | placement, AI | V |
| `+0x64` | byte | AI flags | see 3.7: bit 0 lateral sign, bit 1 roll hysteresis, bit 2 pitch hysteresis, bit 3 which body axis is the roll axis, bit 4 epilogue (a state ran), bit 5 ahead/behind, bit 6 pitch sign / second axis, bit 7 roll-up fallback | AI `0x30A7E` | V |
| `+0x65` | byte | key byte | the controls the AI "presses": bits 0 and 1 throttle up / down, `0x04`/`0x08` roll right / left, `0x10`/`0x20` stick forward / back, `0x40`/`0x80` rudder | AI | V |
| `+0x66`, `+0x68`, `+0x6A` | word | pitch, heading, roll | note 05 | | V |
| `+0x6C`, `+0x6E` | word | target speed S, indicated speed W | note 05; placement writes `0x2000` for an airborne record | | V |
| `+0x71` | byte | radar heading sector | `0xFF` from the placement; the radar scope code (`0x30044` and following, note 07 part B section 12) tests it (negative: recompute), computes the sector of the record's heading `+0x68` (steps of `0x708`, 0 to 15, after an offset of `0x384`) and stores it (`0x30164`); read at `0x300D8` and `0x3013A`. The player's own `+0x71` is written by the flight start (`0x1D91C`) | placement, radar code | R |
| `+0x72` | long | fuel | placement writes `0x61A800` (full) | placement | V |
| `+0x7A` | byte | engagement | 3 far, 4 near, 5 released to its destination: the range routine switches 4 to 3 beyond `0x1E00` and 3 to 4 within it, the region exit sets 5, the acquisition sets 5 back to 3; 3 and 4 enable the roll bands of 3.6; placement writes 3 | AI | V |
| `+0x7C` | byte | gear | note 05; an airborne placement writes `0xE0` (gear up) | placement, hook `0x1F0E2` | V |
| `+0x7D` | byte | model unit | the low nibble is the model-unit shift taken from the template's first block (2.3); the high nibble is 0 at placement | placement | V |
| `+0x7E` | word | turn limit | `0x1400` written at the entry of the state routine for every class but `0x30`; the steering overwrites it with the bank limit of 3.5 | AI | V |
| `+0x80` to `+0xA2` | words | matrices | `+0x80`: matrix A; `+0x92` to `+0xA2`: the attitude matrix the AI reads (columns at `+0x92/+0x98/+0x9E`, `+0x94/+0x9A/+0xA0`, `+0x96/+0x9C/+0xA2`: lateral, up, forward) | placement, flight step | V |

The placement clears only the first `0xA4` bytes of a record (41 longs); the words from `+0xA4` on keep what they held (note 05: rotated vertices). Fields not named here are not read by the AI.

### 1.5 The secondary objects

The 16 secondary objects at `0x4D23C` are short-lived pieces: the explosion fragments and the effect cones (note 07 part B section 11). An object is the first `0x20` bytes of a record plus three overwritten words:

| offset | size | meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `+0x00` | word | `0x0040`: valid (the low byte has the same bit meanings as a record: bit 6 valid, bit 4 pending, bit 2 not in queue) |
| `+0x02` | word | model id: the caller's `d1` when it is not 0; with 0 the creator takes `0x12` (18) or `0x13` (19) according to the **parity of the slot it found** (`0x13` when 15 minus the slot index is odd, `0x12` otherwise: the routine tests bit 0 of its own scan counter, not of the caller's flag) |
| `+0x04` | word | life counter: `0x7F` at creation; the world update decrements the 16 words at `0x4D240` (stride `0x20`) once per pass unless the game is paused; the draw handlers read it as the phase |
| `+0x06`, `+0x08` | word | cell x, z (as a record) |
| `+0x0A`, `+0x0B` | byte | sub-cell, neighbour |
| `+0x0C`, `+0x0E` | word | fine x, z |
| `+0x10` | long | y >> 8 |
| `+0x14`, `+0x18`, `+0x1C` | long | position x, y, z |

Allocation [V: `dying_check.py` part 5, 300 calls of the original with random occupancy, 60 of them with all 16 in use, 0 differences]: the creator `0x2AA8E` scans the 16 objects in order and takes the **first whose valid bit is clear**; the 32 bytes at the caller's pointer are copied over it, then `+0x00`, `+0x04` and `+0x02` are overwritten as above and the display request `V:4A910` is set to `0xFF`. **When all 16 are valid the call does nothing**: no object is created, no object is reused and no request is made [R]. Nothing clears the valid bit in the creator; the draw state machine owns the end of life (note 07 part B).

## 2. The spawner and the template copy

This section is the rule set of the three spawners that fill the aircraft slots: the mission-start placement `0x2CE36`, the region trigger `0x2D0AA` and its exits, and the creator of secondary objects (1.5). The weapon and seat spawners (`0x275C4`/`0x275F6`, `0x27522`, `0x2758E`) are in note 07 part B and section 6. The entry format, the tables `0x2DEDA`, `0x2DE28`, `0x2DCE8` and the training placement `0x2CFDA` are described in note 08 section 6.1 and section 8; this section corrects and completes them with the rig results of `spawn_check.py`.

### 2.1 The entry (five words) and what each word does

An entry is five words in a sub-list or a region (note 08 6.1). The placer `0x2D248` reads them in this order and applies these rules, **in this order** [V: `spawn_check.py`, 7,040 mission-start placements and 672 region runs, 13,291,520 record bytes compared, 0 differences]:

| step | rule |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | the **mode filter**: bits 8 to 11 of word 4; when non-zero and not equal to the mode byte `V:4A95E`, the entry is skipped |
| 2 | the **slot** is bits 0 to 6 of word 2. When the record `0x4B23C + 0x200 * slot` is **valid** (`W0` bit `0x0040`), the entry is skipped: **a placement never reuses or replaces a live record** (the allocation rule, 2.6) |
| 3 | the **template copy** (2.3): the five longs at `0x25ECC + word 0` are copied to the descriptor slot `0x2600C + 20 * slot`. This happens before the refusals of steps 4 and 5, so a refused entry still rewrites the descriptor of its slot [V: the 7,040 runs compare all descriptors] |
| 4 | the **hostile cap**: when `V:4A962` (the cap from the row, 2.4) is not greater than `V:4A961` (the count of counted aircraft placed so far), the entry is skipped. The test is made for **every** entry, whatever its class: once the cap is reached the rest of the sub-list (ships, friendlies, pods) is refused too. Rows list their hostiles last, so this refuses the late hostiles only in the shipped data (mode 3, sub-lists 2 and 3, progress 2 or more: the third MiG-29 is refused) [V] |
| 5 | an existing record of class `0x15` whose cell x word (`+0x06`) is non-zero is not overwritten (the cruise missile that was placed keeps its slot even if the valid bit has been cleared) [V, milestone 7: `combat7-placer15.txt` (generator `gen-combat7.py placer15`): the F1 flight snapshot with the mode byte `V:4A95E` poked to 8, the cap raised, the player moved into region 3 (cells 75 to 77) and record 4 staged; the region's one entry (slot 4, class `0x15`, mode filter 8) is tried at the tick (`V:4A992` low nibble 3): with the record staged as class `0x15`, cell x 1 and W0 clear it is **kept** (class, cell x and W0 unchanged, the hostile count `V:4A961` not raised) while the template copy of step 3 was done (the descriptor of slot 4 rewritten); staged as class 0 and cell x 0 it is **placed** (W0 `0x11C1`, cell 76,76, the count raised by one). The rule is reachable in the mode 8 mission (F6) when its cruise missile has been freed while the player stays in region 3; `tests/test_entities.c` pins both cases] |
| 6 | the first `0xA4` bytes of the record are cleared |
| 7 | the model-unit nibble (2.3), the class (`+0x62` = low byte of word 1), the group (`+0x5E` = slot) and `+0x3A` (bits 8 to 14 of word 2) are written |
| 8 | the **position block** is the block at `0x2DCE8` + the table entry at byte offset word 3 (note 02 section 9): cell x, cell z, fine x, fine z, height (signed words) |
| 9 | word 4 **bit 15 set**: W0 bit `0x0008` is set (the entry is *not counted*); clear: the bit is cleared and, when the class has the high nibble 1, the hostile count `V:4A961` is incremented (so friendlies placed without the bit, such as a class `0x10` F-16 or an airliner, **count against the cap**) |
| 10 | bit 14: `+0x05` (the AI state) = 8; bit 13: W0 bit `0x0001` (engaged) set |
| 11 | bit 12 (**random offset**): the cell x and cell z are changed by a small amount, 2.4; bit 7 halves it (arithmetic shift, so -1 stays -1) |
| 12 | `+0x5D` = the register d7 plus 1: `0xFF` for a mission-start placement (the caller loads -2), see 2.5 for a region |
| 13 | `+0x7A` = 3; unless the class has the high nibble 2 (ships), W0 gets `0x1080` (active in the simulation, AI valid); then in every case `0x0140` is set and `0x8000` cleared. An aircraft is therefore `0x11C0` (plus `0x0008` when not counted, plus `0x0001` when engaged), a ship `0x0140` |
| 14 | the **destination or leader** (word 2, bit 15 and bits 8 to 14): see below |
| 15 | cell words `+0x06`, `+0x08` = block cell x, z (with the offset), fine words `+0x0C`, `+0x0E`, and `+0x10` (long) = the height word, sign-extended |
| 16 | **a non-zero height** makes the record airborne: `+0x7C` = `0xE0` (gear up, retract progress 6) and the speed words `+0x6C` and `+0x6E` = `0x2000` (8192) |
| 17 | the constants: stock `+0x5F` = `0x44`, rounds `+0x60` = 500, fuel `+0x72` = `0x61A800`, `+0x63` = `0x3D`, `+0x71` = `0xFF`, `+0x64` = `0x10` |
| 18 | the position longs: x = cell x * 2^22 + fine x * 256, z likewise, y = height * 256 (+0x14, +0x18, +0x1C); the attitude and matrices are written by the matrix routine `0x3202C` with all three angles 0 (the identity, 0x4000 words) |
| 19 | the placer returns to its caller, which (mission start only) aims the record, 2.4 |

**Word 2 (destination or leader)** [V, same runs]:

| word 2 | what the placement writes into `+0x2C`..`+0x38` |
| --- | --- |
| bit 15 set, bits 8 to 14 = 0 | nothing: the destination words stay 0 (cell 0, 0; **not** the "none" value -1) and `+0x38` = `0xFF` |
| bit 15 set, bits 8 to 14 = n > 0 | the block number n of the table `0x2DCE8` (the n-th word entry) into `+0x2C`..`+0x34` and `+0x38` = `0xFF` |
| bit 15 clear, bits 8 to 14 = r | `+0x2C`..`+0x34` = the record's **own** start block (including the random offset) and `+0x38` = `0x80 + r`: the **leader** is record r (r = 0 is the player, 14 the carrier) |

Bits 8 to 14 are also stored in `+0x3A` in both cases (the waypoint run index of state 8, section 4.3).

### 2.2 Aiming at the mission start

Only the mission-start path (`0x2D212`) calls the aim routine `0x2CF14` after a placement; the region path does not [V]. For a destination (bit 15 set, n > 0) the target is the block's position; for a leader the target is the leader record's position **provided that record is valid**, and then `+0x2C`..`+0x34` are overwritten with the leader's cell, fine position and `+0x10` and `+0x38` = `0x80 + r`; if the leader is not valid nothing more happens (the record keeps the heading 0 and its own block as destination). The aim is the external-camera aim routine of note 03 with the vector from the record to the target (x and z only, no height) and the "snap" limit: the record's heading `+0x68` becomes the heading that routine returns (rig: 10,879 aims compared with the original's routine, 0 differences), the pitch and roll stay 0, and the matrices are rebuilt from the three angles.

### 2.3 The template copy and what the placement writes itself

The five longs of the template (note 02 section 1: draw handler, model base, detail list, alternate list for the player, auxiliary pointer) are copied **unchanged** to the descriptor of the slot. Nothing about the template enters the record except one value: the **model-unit shift** `+0x7D` low nibble, which is the low nibble of the byte at offset 6 from the model base inside the first block named by the first word of the detail list: when that word is negative the block offset is the word itself, otherwise the word at +2 (when bit 14 is set) or at +4, masked to 12 bits [V: 7,040 runs compare the nibble for every class and model]. The record has no model id field the draw code reads: the drawer takes the model from the descriptor of the slot, so **a slot's model is whatever the last placement into that slot copied**.

Copied or computed, per word of the record (rows not listed are left 0):

| record word | source |
| --- | --- |
| model (descriptor) | copied: template of word 0 |
| class `+0x62` | copied: low byte of word 1 |
| group `+0x5E`, slot | computed: bits 0 to 6 of word 2 |
| W0 | computed: `0x11C0` (aircraft) or `0x0140` (ship), `|` bit 3 from word 4 bit 15 (inverted rule 9), `|` bit 0 from bit 13 |
| `+0x05` state | word 4 bit 14 (8) else 0 |
| stock, rounds, fuel, `+0x63`, `+0x64`, `+0x71` | constants (rule 17) |
| throttle `+0x2B` | not written (0) |
| speed `+0x6C`, `+0x6E`, gear `+0x7C` | `0x2000` and `0xE0` when the block's height is non-zero, else 0 |
| destination `+0x2C`..`+0x34`, target `+0x38`, list `+0x3A` | word 2 (the table above) |
| position, cells, fine words, `+0x10` | the block of word 3 plus the offsets |
| heading | the aim (mission start) or 0 |

### 2.4 The mission-start order and the random choices

`0x2CE36` runs once at the dispatch of every mode (note 08 6.1): it clears `V:4A961`, `V:4A963` and `V:4A83A`; mode `0x7D` takes the training placement `0x2CFDA` (note 08 section 8); modes 1 to 9 take the row mode - 1 of the table `0x2DEDA` and modes `0x7E` and `0x7F` the tenth row; other modes place nothing. The rest follows from one **selector word** `V:4ABB0` (the low word of the system timer, read at mission start) [V for the use, R for its distribution]:

| use | rule |
| --- | --- |
| the variant (the column of the row) | on a fresh start the low two bits k of the selector: 0 gives variant 0 (the stored variant `V:4ABD4` is **not** updated and keeps its old value), 1 and 2 give variants 0 and 1, 3 gives variant 2, and for k = 1 to 3 the variant is stored in `V:4ABD4`. On an **ESC restart** (the byte `V:4A8E3` set by the ESC request, note 08 5.11) the stored `V:4ABD4` is used as it stands. A **crash restart does not run the placement at all**: the callers of `0x2CE36` are the mission-start states (`0x11C2C`, `0x120CC`, `0x1223A`, `0x12268`) and the training sequencer (`0x27286`, `0x27378`); the crash restart `0x1D538` only restores the player, so the other records, `V:4A961`/`V:4A963` and the variant stay as they were [R: all call sites scanned]. (That a start with k = 0 leaves `V:4ABD4` unchanged is read from the code; `spawn_check.py` sets `V:4ABD4` itself for the restart runs and does not check its value after a fresh start [R].) So a restart after a fresh start with k = 0 uses the variant of an **earlier** start (0 when there was none) |
| the hostile cap | the count word of the row's pair becomes `V:4A962` |
| the sub-list | the byte at (**the long stored at `0x1D024`**, a pointer to the flight-log record in memory) + `0x12` + the mode byte `V:4A95E` (the mission-list unlock byte, 0 for the demonstration rows), doubled, selects one of the four header offsets of the list. A rig that pokes a byte at `0x1D024 + 0x12 + mode` changes nothing, because `0x1D024` holds a pointer: poke the long at `0x1D024` with the address of a scratch area and the selector byte at that address + `0x12` + mode (`spawn_check.py` does this and compares all four sub-lists) [V] |
| the first entry | placed without the threshold test; the others only when their threshold (bits 0 to 6 of word 4) is not above the progress counter `V:4A95F` |
| the random offset (entries with bit 12) | on a fresh start: dx = (selector >> 1) & 3, negated when bit 4 of the selector is set; dz = (selector >> 2) & 3, negated when bit 5 is set; both are stored in `V:4ABD0` and `V:4ABD2`; bit 7 of the entry halves each by an arithmetic shift right; the cell x and cell z are increased by dx and dz. **All entries of the call use the same pair** (the selector does not change within a call). On a restart the stored `V:4ABD0`, `V:4ABD2` are reused (the stored values are the unhalved ones) |

**The distribution of the selector (measured; the selector is not uniform).** The selector is the low word of the microsecond field of the system time: `0x18E54` asks the timer device for the system time and stores the seconds at `V:4ABAA` and the microseconds at `V:4ABAE`, and `V:4ABB0` is the low half of that long. The system time advances by 20,000 microseconds per vertical blank and the game asks at a point of its pass that lies a short and almost fixed time after the blank, so the microseconds are **20,000 times the number m of the blank within the second (0 to 49) plus a constant delta that belongs to the start path**, and not an arbitrary 20-bit number. [V: `followup-combat-selector.txt` (`selector.log`): 30 restores of the menu snapshot and a key 1 each, of which 13 reached the demonstration's start (the others were read before the start): m = 4, 6, 7, 10, 13, 14, 21, 23, 33, 43, 43, 45 and 47 (all over the second), **delta = 1,172 microseconds in 8 starts**, 1,166, 1,168, 1,170 and 1,173 in the others; `followup-combat-selector-missions.txt`: four real mission starts (the countercode answered from the briefing snapshots, to the first flight pass): **delta = 1,612 (F1, mode 3, m = 20), 1,620 (F2, mode 4, m = 34), 1,594 (F4, mode 6, m = 6), 1,635 (F6, mode 8, m = 3)**, one start each.] Only the bits 0 to 5 of the selector are used (the table above), and 20,000 is a multiple of 32: **the bits 0 to 4 of the selector are the bits 0 to 4 of delta** (the clock's own jitter of a few microseconds moves them: the lowest two bits, the variant, are the least stable), **and bit 5 is the parity of m plus bit 5 of delta**, uniform over a long session. The consequence is that the variant and the offsets of a start are **almost determined by the start path**, not drawn from all 64 values:

| path (delta) | variant k (two lowest bits) of delta alone | dx (bits 1-2, sign bit 4) | dz (bits 2-3, sign bit 5 = parity of m) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| demonstration (1,172) | 0 (variant 0; 1,173 gives k = 1, also variant 0; 1,166 gives k = 2, variant 1) | -2 | magnitude 1, sign by m |
| F1, mode 3 (1,612) | 0, variant 0 (not stored) | +2 | magnitude 3, sign by m |
| F2, mode 4 (1,620) | 0, variant 0 | -2 | magnitude 1, sign by m |
| F4, mode 6 (1,594) | 2, variant 1 | -1 | magnitude 2, sign by m |
| F6, mode 8 (1,635) | 3, variant 2 | +1 | 0 |

**The other paths, measured** [V: `followup-combat-batch-h.txt` (modes 5 and 7 from the briefing prompts of F3 and F5, the flight school's part 1) and `followup-combat-batch-i.txt` (part 2, the qualification, the free-flight start), one start each]: delta = **1,620 (free flight, mode 1), 1,888 (flight school part 1, mode 2), 1,597 (mode 5, F3), 1,619 (mode 7, F5), 583 (qualification, mode 9), 335 (flight school part 2, mode 0x7D)**; the table above with these: mode 1 variant 0, dx -2, dz +-1; mode 2 variant 0, no offset (0, 0); mode 5 variant 0, dx -2, dz +-3; mode 7 variant 2, dx -1, dz 0; mode 9 variant 2, dx +3, dz +-1; mode 0x7D variant 2, dx +3, dz +-3. (Mode 0x7E, the demo's other row, was not measured: it uses the demonstration's delta 1,172.) A delta that is not near 1.2 to 1.9 ms (583 and 335: the qualification and the practice part read the clock earlier in their start) shows the constant belongs to the path, as stated. **The jitter is not uniform**: the 13 demonstration starts gave delta - 1,172 of 0 (8 starts), +1 (2), -2, -4 and -6 (one each); the port draws the jitter from that list (0 with probability 8/13, +1 2/13, -2, -4, -6 1/13 each) for every path (the other paths have one start each, so their spread is unmeasured).
(Offsets before the halving of an entry's bit 7. A jitter of one or two microseconds changes k and the dx magnitude, so the cells of the table are the values at the measured delta, not certainties; modes 5 and 7 were not measured.) With a uniform 16-bit selector the variant would be 0 with probability 1/2 and the offsets spread over -3 to +3; the real starts of a given mode instead repeat nearly the same variant and offset pair, the dz sign alternating with the second's frame parity. [Rule for the port (C, the closest model of the measurement): **draw the frame number m uniformly from 0 to 49 from the seeded core generator, take the delta of the start path from the table above (1,172 demonstration, mode 0x7F and 0x7E; 1,620 free flight, mode 1; 1,888 flight school part 1, mode 2; 1,612 F1, mode 3; 1,620 F2, mode 4; 1,597 F3, mode 5; 1,594 F4, mode 6; 1,619 F5, mode 7; 1,635 F6, mode 8; 583 qualification, mode 9; 335 flight school part 2, mode 0x7D), add a jitter drawn from the measured list of 13 (0 with probability 8/13, +1 with 2/13, and -2, -4, -6 with 1/13 each), and form selector = (m x 20,000 + delta + jitter) mod 65,536**; the tables above then apply verbatim. The real machine's delta may differ by a few microseconds from the emulator's (cycle exact, this machine's timing), which the jitter list stands for; the jitter list is the demonstration's, applied to every path because the others have one start each.]

### 2.5 Region triggers, the tags and the exits

The tick `0x2D0AA` runs when the low nibble of `V:4A992` is 3 (every 16th step; the demonstration flag `V:4A848` switches it off, and `0x2D22A` places nothing in modes 1, 2 and `0x7D`). It walks the regions of `0x2DE28` (up to eight; four exist) with a pointer that advances by one region per iteration and a **bit number d7 that starts at 0**. For each region the player's cell (`V:4B242`, `V:4B244`) is tested against the box of four words: 

* region bit `d7` of `V:4A855` clear and the player inside: the bit is set and the entries are placed (`0x2D22A`: the count word says how many; zero places nothing);
* bit set and the player inside: the placer is called again every tick (entries whose slots are still valid are refused, entries whose slot has been freed are placed again unless the hostile cap stops them: the count `V:4A961` never goes down);
* bit set and the player outside: the exit `0x2D116` re-addresses every entry's record (below) and the bit is cleared.

**The quirk**: the entry placer leaves its working register d7 at 0 after a placed entry and -1 after a refused one, and d7 is also the bit number of the loop. After any trigger the bit number of the next region is therefore 1 (after a placed last entry) or 0 (after a refused or empty one), instead of the region's number; bit numbers are taken modulo 8. Effects: after a trigger the following regions of the same tick use bit 1 or 0 for their inside flag, and the exit of a region clears whichever bit the number names. The port rule: **reproduce the loop exactly**: the bit number is one variable that starts at 0 at each tick, is taken modulo 8, is set to 0 by the entry placer after a placed entry and to -1 after a refused or empty one, and is increased by 1 at the end of every region iteration, while the region pointer advances independently. The next tick starts from 0 again [V: `spawn_check.py` models this, 672 region runs over modes 3 to 9, eight player cells, 3 progress values and 4 selectors: 0 differences, 168 of them with the shifted bits].

**Observable effects.** (i) The region number stored in `+0x5D` (and so the region box and entry list that the per-record check of 3.4 uses to release the record) is the bit number at the moment of the placement, which differs from the region's number after a trigger; (ii) an entry placed after a refused one gets `+0x5D` = 0, which the per-record check treats as "no region" (it stores the debug word `0x1E`, 4.8, and never releases the record); (iii) a region whose bit was set in an earlier pass can be judged "inside again" or "left" with the wrong bit. In the shipped data only regions 2 and 3 have entries (regions 0 and 1 have a count of 0, which sets the bit number to -1 for the next region), and the boxes of regions 1 and 2 overlap (a small block of cells), so a player there makes region 2 use bit 0 in the pass in which region 1 triggered, and the regions 2 and 3 boxes overlap in a strip two cells wide [R for which positions reach it; V for the mechanics]. Coverage of `spawn_check.py`: the exit `0x2D116` ran only where a shifted bit number met a region the player had left (a part of the 168 shifted runs); the re-placement of an already inside region ran with every slot valid (a no-op) and the case of a slot freed in between was not tested [honest limits].

`+0x5D` of a region placement is set from d7 as the placer finds it: the region number plus 1 for the first entry of a region (d7 = the loop's bit number then), 1 after a placed entry, 0 after a refused one. Mission-start placements get `0xFF`. The per-record check `0x2D53C` (section 3.4) reads it.

**The early returns of the entry placer `0x2D22A`**: for the modes 1, 2 and `0x7D` it returns before its first entry and leaves d7 as the loop had it (no clobbering; those modes place nothing here); for a region with a count word of 0 it leaves d7 = -1. The clobbering therefore happens only for the modes 3 to 9 and the other modes that place regions [V: `spawn_check.py`; R for the modes 1, 2, 0x7D].

**The exit** (`0x2D116`) walks the entries named by the region's **count word** (a region whose count word is 0 has entries in the data but none are ever re-addressed: regions 0 and 1) [V: `spawn_check.py`]. Per entry: the record named by the entry's slot is retargeted unless it already follows someone (`+0x38` with bit 7 set and a non-zero index): with word 2 bits 8 to 14 = 0 the record goes to its entry's own block, `+0x7A` becomes 5 when it was 3 or 4, `+0x38` = `0x80`, W0 bit 0 is cleared; with bit 15 set and an index it goes to that block with `+0x38` = `0xFF`, `+0x7A` = 3 and W0 bit 0 cleared; with a leader index it copies the leader's destination words (if the leader is valid, else the first case), `+0x38` = `0x80 + r` and `+0x7A` = 3 [V, same runs]. The record is left alone when its `+0x38` has bit 7 set with a non-zero index, which includes `0xFF` (destination mode: a record that already goes to a block is not redirected): a target byte of `0x80` is redirected, `0x81` and above are skipped [V: `spawn_check.py`, 160 runs: leader numbers 0 to 15, valid and not, target bytes 0, 0x80, 0x81, 0x8E, 0xFF]. A **valid leader's position words** are copied (cell x, cell z, fine x, fine z and `+0x10`, i.e. the altitude in `+0x10` units), not its destination words. When the leader is **not valid** the record takes its own entry block, but the register that held the block's fine z has been overwritten by the leader field, so **the destination's fine z (`+0x32`) becomes the leader number shifted left by 8 (`0x0E00` for leader 14)**, whatever the block says (cell x and z, fine x and the height come from the block) [V, same runs]. The case **bit 15 clear and bits 8 to 14 = 0** (leader 0, the player) takes the first case: the record goes to its own entry block and `+0x38` becomes `0x80`, i.e. it keeps the player as its target; no shipped entry has it [R, modelled in `spawn_check.py`].

### 2.6 When every record is in use (Review Focus 2)

| creator | rule when no free slot | tag |
| --- | --- | --- |
| mission-start and region placement | the slot is **named by the entry**; a valid record in it makes the entry **skipped** (no search for another slot, nothing replaced). There is no array overrun: the slot field is 7 bits, the shipped data use 4 to 15 | V (2,703 refused placements in the runs) |
| secondary objects `0x2AA8E` | the first free of 16; **none free: nothing happens** (no object, no request) | V: `dying_check.py` part 5, 60 full-table calls |
| the player's launch `0x275C4` | the first free of the records 1 to 3 in the order the update visits them; with none free the request is dropped at the end of the pass (1.2) | R (note 07 part B for the launch itself) |
| an AI launch | fixed record (5, 9, 11, 13); valid means no launch | R |
| the seat and the parachute | the first free of records 1 to 3 in the order visited, and **record 5 when 1 to 3 are all in use** (1.2), under the latch (section 6) | V: `seat_check.py` part 4 |

### 2.8 The spawn tail `0x2781A` (weapons, seat, parachute, pod)

The routine that the plan names "the spawner" is the common tail of the three launch-type spawners; it fills a record that the caller has already copied from the owner (`0x275F6`, `0x27522`, `0x2758E`; the attitude and the template copy are done before it, 2.3 and 6.4). Its entry points are `0x277F4` (sets the lifetime counter `+0x4C` to 150 for class 0, else 200), `0x27808` and `0x2781A`. It then:

1. sets `+0x26` = `0x14`; W0 gets `0x10C0`, `0x0100` and `0x0002` (valid, active, AI-valid, keeps moving, spawn flag); `+0x76` = 0;
2. if the owner's ground bit (W2 `0x0080`) was copied, the vertical velocity `+0x42` is cleared; the ground bit is then dropped from W2;
3. `+0x2C` = `0xFFFF` (no destination); `V:4A910` = `0x8C` (the display request for a spawned record); `+0x38` = the owner's target byte;
4. for the classes with the high nibble 3 only: the thrust of length `0x3C0` along the owner's up axis (down for classes `0x31` and `0x32`) is added to the velocity, and `+0x4C` = -20.

The class 0 and 1 launch details (the weapon records, stock, the offset index) are note 07 part B; the seat's use is verified in section 6.4 [V: `seat_check.py` part 3].

## 3. The generic AI

The generic AI is the state routine `0x30A7E` plus the gate `0x278F6` (section 4). It does not fly the aircraft: it writes **the key byte `+0x65`** (the controls the record "presses"), the rate targets `+0x50`..`+0x54` of non-aircraft classes, the bank limit `+0x7E` and its own flags, and the unchanged flight step of note 05 turns those into motion (the non-player branches of note 08 section 8.3 apply). The training instructor uses the same routine with its manoeuvre states (note 08 section 8.5); this section covers the states the generic AI itself enters. Verified by `ai_check.py` (rig, random records, 24,000 compared in all, 0 differences) and `ai_tactics_check.py` (section 4).

### 3.1 When the routine runs

The flight step `0x2A2A2` calls the state routine for every record except the player, when the flight simulation switch `V:4A984` bit `0x40` is set and the step is not skipped by the pause `V:4A866` (or, for a non-player, by `V:4A840`). It is called **before** the input conversion of the same step, so the key byte written here is the one converted. A record with W2 bit `0x0100` (scripted) and state 0 does not call it: only the recentre bit is set (note 08 8.5). The routine returns at once, writing only `+0x64` = 0 (the flags cleared), when the class nibble is `0x30` (before `+0x7E` is written) or when W0 bit `0x0080` is clear (after `+0x7E` has been set to `0x1400`); it returns with nothing written when `V:4A866` or `V:4A867` is set [V].

### 3.2 The states

The state byte `+0x05` selects the code. The states that the generic AI enters are 0, 1, 6 and 8; 2, 5 and 9 are entered by the evasion choice (4.5); `0x0A` and `0x0B`-`0x28` are the training states of note 08. States 3 and 4 run the code of state 1 and nothing sets them; 7 follows 2. The floor test below runs first for the states it applies to.

| state | meaning | per step | leaves when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | idle, no plan | the steering (3.5) runs | an unscripted record with the counter `+0x4C` negative: the end routine (3.3); a scripted one steers without looking at the counter |
| 1 | re-evaluate / pull up | the steering runs; the control commands are applied (3.6) | as state 0; also entered whenever the **floor test** fails (below). States 3 and 4 behave as 1 |
| 6 | wait | nothing is commanded: the previous key byte stands | unscripted: the counter is negative: state 1 with counter 30; or the floor test fails: state 1 with counter 30 (the debug word `V:4AA56` = `0x3C` is stored first when the failure is a descent below the floor; it has no effect, 4.8). Scripted: with `V:4A852` not negative and the counter negative: state 8 with counter 30 and the state 8 code runs in the same step; with `V:4A852` negative nothing. Out of control: nothing at all |
| 8 | follow the waypoint run | the steering runs with the destination of the current waypoint (4.3); applied at half the bank limit | the roll is inverted (roll between 7200 and 21600 words) with the pitch within 45 degrees of level (not between 3600 and 25200 words): state 6 with counter `0x25`. Scripted: with `V:4A852` not negative the step does nothing; with the counter negative: state 6 with counter 11 (`0x0B`) |
| `0x0A` | training wait | nothing | counter negative: state 0 (note 08) |
| 2 | evasion: roll to 90 degrees | roll command toward 90 or 270 degrees | within 15 degrees: state 7, counter 32 |
| 7 | evasion: pull | pitch command (pull) | counter negative: the end routine |
| 5, 9 | evasion: hold a shallow bank | roll command toward a bank of 14 degrees (state 5, tolerance 6 degrees) or 8 degrees (state 9, tolerance 4 degrees) on the side the aircraft is already on (roll word below 14400: that bank; otherwise 28800 minus it); no roll key inside the tolerance | counter negative: state 0 |

**The floor test** (every state except 0, 1 and 8; not for a scripted record or one that is out of control): the speed word S (`+0x6C`) times 64 is the floor in position units of height; with the record descending (`+0x42` negative) a height at or below the floor (the height not above the floor, i.e. floor at or above the height), or with it climbing a height strictly above `0x800000` minus the floor, sets state 1 with counter 30 and continues as state 1 [V].

**Out of control** (`+0x20` bit 1) in the states 0, 1, 3, 4 and 8 (not 6, where an out-of-control record does nothing): the steering runs when the pitch word is above 14400 or below 2800; with the pitch from 2800 to 14400 the state becomes 6 (the counter is not changed) and nothing is commanded this step [V: `ai_check.py`].

**Mode 5, not-counted aircraft** (the two F-16s of the mode 5 rows): a record in mode 5 with W0 bit 3 set and a counter at or below 0 and a state other than 8 takes state 8 and its first waypoint at once: the waypoint list is the run of position blocks that the table at `0x2DCE8` names with `+0x3A`; the state byte is written 8 **before** the run is tested, so with an empty run the byte stays 8 while this step still runs the handler of the old state [V].

### 3.3 The end routine and the response delays

The end routine is entered by an **unscripted** record in state 0, 1, 3 or 4 whose counter is negative, and by state 7 (scripted or not) when its counter is negative; a scripted record in state 0 or 1 steers without testing the counter. It picks a wait: a **scripted** record goes to the training wait (state `0x0A`, counter 36 in the part 1 flight, 60 otherwise) while `V:4A852` is not negative, else state 6 with counter 10. An unscripted record goes to state 6 with counter 10 unless it **has a target** (`+0x38` with bit 7 set, not `0xFF`) and (the progress `V:4A95F` is 2 or less, or the flags `+0x64` have both bits 5 and 6 set); then the counter is set by the **progress**:

| progress `V:4A95F` | counter (steps of waiting in state 6 before state 1) |
| --- | --- |
| 0 or less | `0x4B` (75) |
| 1 | `0x25` (37) |
| 2 | `0x12` (18) |
| 3 or more | 4 |

so the AI re-plans more often the further the player has got in the campaign [V]. State 6 (unscripted) counts down and falls into state 1 with counter 30; state 1 runs the steering and, when its counter reaches negative, comes back here.

### 3.4 The per-record check `0x2D53C` and the tick `0x2D0AA`

`0x2D0AA` (regions) runs every 16th step (2.5). The step `0x2A2A2` calls the **per-record check** `0x2D53C` for the record of index i when the low five bits of `V:4A992` equal i, so each AI record is checked once every 32 steps at its own phase (records 1 to 15, phases 1 to 15) [R]. The check does nothing for a class whose high nibble is not 1, for state 8, or for a mission-start record (`+0x5D` negative). For `+0x5D` = 0 (a region placement that followed a refused entry, 2.5) it stores the debug word `0x1E` in `V:4AA56` and calls the empty post routine (4.8): nothing happens. Otherwise it takes the region number `+0x5D - 1`, tests the record's cell against the region box, and when the record is **outside its region** (and an entry of the region names this slot) it releases it as the exit of 2.5 does, except that it also sets `+0x38` = `0xFF` and uses the entry's block: `+0x7A` becomes 5 when it was 3 or 4, W0 bit 0 is cleared. The conversion: the original's frame counter `V:4A992` counts passes of the world update, and the port's step is one pass, so the tick runs at the steps whose counter has the low nibble 3 and the check at the steps whose counter's low five bits equal the record index [C: identity; the counter starts at 0 at the mission start and the pause stops it].

### 3.5 The steering (`0x30BE8` onwards)

Every step in which state 0, 1, 3, 4 or 8 reaches it, the routine computes where the destination lies in the record's body frame and which controls bring the aircraft nearer.

1. **No destination**: `+0x2C` negative: the routine returns with the flags `+0x64` cleared.
2. **The vector** (X, Y, Z), in 32-bit arithmetic: X = (destination cell x - own cell x, taken as a 16-bit difference) * 2^14 + destination fine x - own fine x; Z likewise from `+0x2E`, `+0x32`; Y = the target height - the record's `+0x10`. If `+0x38` is non-negative the destination words are first shifted left by that count and the fine words masked to 14 bits [R; no shipped record has such a `+0x38`].
3. **The target height** is `+0x34`, except for records of class nibble 1 other than classes `0x14` and `0x15` that are not wrecks. For those let h be the speed word times 128 (times 64 in state 8). Descending (`+0x42` negative): the height is h when h is not below the record's `+0x18` and not below `+0x34`, else `+0x34`. Climbing: let g = `0x800000` - h; the height is g when g is not above the record's `+0x18` and is below `+0x34`, else `+0x34`. So a fast aircraft aims at a speed-dependent floor when it is below it and at a ceiling when it is above it [V: `ai_check.py`].
4. **Normalising**: while the largest component is `0x4800` or more, all three are shifted right by 2. The normaliser `0x29C0E` is then called with the length L = `0xC0` (the other callers use `0x100`, `0x200`, `0x3C0`): let A be the approximate length of the vector (the hypot approximation of note 04a 5a; a zero vector or L = 0 gives the zero vector). The routine finds the scale d0 = L * 4^j (with L shifted right by 2 per step, truncating, when L itself is above A) such that d0 is above A and at most 4 A, with the shift d2 = 8 + 2 j (never below 2 when shrinking); q = floor(d0 * 256 / A) (an unsigned 16-bit divide: q is at most 1024 and above 256); each result component is floor(component * q / 2^d2), a signed word product shifted right arithmetically, negated when L is negative, and kept as a word (the callers sign-extend it). Equivalently the largest power of four whose quotient stays within 1024 is used, and when L is below 1024 the length is truncated by (10 - s) bits [the implementer's description of a rig comparison of the port's normaliser with the original over 100,000 random lengths, 0 differences; the loop above read from the code and matching it]. The scratch word triple `V:4AB04` receives the result.
5. **The limits** (all in the scale of the dot products of the normalised vector with Q14 matrix words, so a body-axis component of 1.0 is `0xC0 * 0x4000` = `0x300000`):

| limit | state 8 | class nibble other than 1 | aircraft (nibble 1) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| dead zone, vertical axis (A) | `0xC000` | `0x10000` | `0x30000` |
| dead zone, lateral axis (B) | `0x10000` | `0x10000` | `0x40000` |
| hysteresis width (C): **never used** (below) | `0x18000` | `0x20000` | `0x50000` |
| "forward" limit (F) | `0x30000` | `0x20000` | `0x80000` |
| "close" limit (K) | `0x80000` | `0x20000` | `0x80000` |

   The negative dead-zone limits are the negatives of A and B.
6. **The axes**: the vector is projected on three body axes with the attitude matrix words at `+0x92`: lateral (`+0x92`, `+0x98`, `+0x9E`), vertical (`+0x94`, `+0x9A`, `+0xA0`) and forward (`+0x96`, `+0x9C`, `+0xA2`). The routine picks the **control axis**: the lateral component when its magnitude is greater than the vertical one (flag bit 3 clear), else the vertical (bit 3 set: the target is above or below the wings and the aircraft pitches onto it). The signed component c on that axis decides everything below; flag bit 0 is set when c is not negative, clear when it is.
7. **Aligned**: c within the dead zone (A on the vertical axis, B on the lateral, either sign). The rate targets `+0x50`..`+0x54` of non-aircraft classes are cleared. On the lateral axis flag bits 1 and 5 are set, bit 0 is toggled and the epilogue bit 4 is set; nothing is commanded. On the vertical axis, if the forward component is not negative, flag bits 2 and 6 are set, bit 0 is toggled, bit 4 set, nothing commanded; if the forward component is negative the pitch command below is issued with c as it stands (and with the table index taken from the signed value, 3.6).
8. **Not aligned, lateral axis**: the code tests flag bit 1 and the width C here to end the routine (hysteresis), **but the mask `0x79` has cleared bit 1 at the start of the same pass, so the test never succeeds and C is never used** [V: the model has no hysteresis and agrees in 24,000 records]. |c| below K sets flag bit 7. |c| at or above F clears flag bit 5; otherwise flag bit 5 is set when the forward component is not negative and cleared when it is. Flag bit 1 is cleared. The table index is the high word of |c|, at most 10. Aircraft go to the bank command, other classes to the rudder command (3.6).
9. **Not aligned, vertical axis**: the same dead test on flag bit 2 and C. |c| at or above F clears flag bit 6; otherwise bit 6 follows the forward component as bit 5 did. Flag bit 2 is cleared. The pitch command follows (3.6).

### 3.6 The commands

The key byte `+0x65` keeps its throttle bits (the speed control of 4.2 owns them) in every command below.

| command | when | what is written |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **bank command** | lateral axis, aircraft class, bank under 75 degrees (roll word below 6000 or above 22800) and pitch within 20 degrees of level (word 1600 or less, or above 27200) | the roll-rate target t is the table `0x31342` entry (`0x3136E` for class `0x14` and in state 8), halved when S is `0x1800` or more, negated when c is negative. The **bank limit** written into `+0x7E`: the magnitude of c, replaced by `0x600000` minus itself when the target is behind (the forward component is negative), shifted right by 8, then limited: more than `0x3000` gives `0x3000`; 32 or less gives `0x1000`; below `0x1800` gives `0x1800`; halved in state 8. With the engagement byte `+0x7A` 3 or 4 the target t is replaced in bands of the roll word r (see below); The roll command is issued only in states 0, 1 and 8, and in state 8 only when the on-ground bit of W2 is clear |
| **roll command** | from the bank command | if flag bits 5 and 6 are both set: t above 0 presses the rudder `0x80` only if t exceeds the heading rate `+0x58`; t below 0 presses `0x40` only if t is below it; when a rudder bit is pressed the roll bits come from the roll word: `0x04` for 81 to 14400, `0x08` for 14401 to 28720, none otherwise. Else if flag bit 7 is set: the roll bits drive the bank back to level, `0x04` for a roll word of 400 to 14400, `0x08` for 14401 to 28400, otherwise by the sign of t (positive `0x08`, negative `0x04`). Else by the sign of t only. The rate target of non-aircraft classes is also stored at `+0x54` |
| **rudder command** | lateral axis, a steep bank (roll word 6000 to 22800) or steep pitch (1601 to 27200), or any non-aircraft class | the table entry (`0x31316` for non-aircraft, else the bank table), signed against c (negated when c is not negative for non-aircraft); a positive target above the heading rate `+0x58` presses `0x80`, a negative one below it `0x40`; non-aircraft classes also store it at `+0x52`. Aircraft end here without the epilogue |
| **pitch command** | vertical axis | the table entry (`0x31316` for non-aircraft, `0x31358` for class `0x14`, else `0x3132C`) negated when c is not negative; a positive target above the pitch rate `+0x56` presses stick forward `0x10`, a negative one below it stick back `0x20`; applied only in states 0, 1 and 8 (and in 8 not on the ground); non-aircraft classes also store the target at `+0x50`. No epilogue |

Tables (words, read from the image):

| table | address | shape (index 0 to 10) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| non-aircraft rate | `0x31316` | 11 words, strictly increasing, from the low hundreds to a little above a thousand, in steps of one to two hundred and fifty |
| aircraft pitch rate | `0x3132C` | 11 words, non-decreasing (two plateaus of equal neighbours), from under a hundred to the mid hundreds |
| aircraft roll rate | `0x31342` | 11 words, non-decreasing (one plateau), from under a hundred to under a thousand |
| class `0x14` pitch rate, and roll rate for state 8 and class `0x14` | `0x31358`, `0x3136E` | 11 words each, the two tables equal, an arithmetic ramp (index + 1) times a constant under ten |

The words are the first five sub-tables of `tables/ai.bin` (the 130 bytes from offset 0, headers included; FNV-1a-32 `627c7507`); the extractor exports them from the image and the port reads them at run time, so the repository prints none of them. The values that the rules use are named where a rule uses them (an index and a result), as in the bands below.

**The bands for `+0x7A` = 3 or 4** (a level-off before a reversal; r the roll word, "both" = flag bits 5 and 6 both set):

| t | rule |
| --- | --- |
| negative | with both and 14400 <= r < 28000: t = 240; otherwise r <= 14400 or r > 26800: unchanged; 26400 < r <= 26800: 0; 14400 < r <= 26400: 240 |
| not negative | with both and 800 < r <= 14400: t = -240; otherwise r >= 14400 or r < 2000: unchanged; 2000 <= r < 2400: 0; 2400 <= r < 14400: -240 |

**The aligned pitch path reads before the table (span for the extractor).** In the aligned case with the target behind, the table index is taken from the *signed* component, not its magnitude, so a negative c gives a negative index and the table is read from the words before it (the previous table's tail). The index reaches -3 at most (the vertical dead zone is at most `0x30000` wide: the index is the high word of c, so -1 for a non-aircraft class or state 8, -1 to -3 for aircraft): for the aircraft tables the words before the base are the last entries of the previous table, which are contiguous (`0x3132C`: entries 10, 9, 8 of `0x31316` = 1280, 1120, 1040; `0x31358`: entries 10, 9, 8 of `0x31342` = 960, 880, 800), so exporting `0x31316` to `0x31383` as one span of 55 words (the five tables of 11 words are contiguous) covers them. For a non-aircraft class the single word before `0x31316` is `0x31314` = 20085 (the code word `0x4E75`); no shipped record has a non-aircraft class with AI. The model reproduces this [V: it showed in 1 of 4,000 records before it was modelled].

### 3.7 The flags `+0x64`

| bit | meaning |
| --- | --- |
| 0 | the sign of the control-axis component (set: c not negative); toggled in the aligned case |
| 1 | roll hysteresis: set when aligned on the lateral axis; always cleared again by the mask `0x79` before it is read (dead) |
| 2 | pitch hysteresis: set when aligned on the vertical axis; dead like bit 1 |
| 3 | the vertical axis is the control axis |
| 4 | the epilogue: an AI state commanded the controls this step; the input conversion of note 08 8.3 reads it (for unscripted records only with it) |
| 5 | roll path: the target is ahead |
| 6 | pitch path: the target is ahead (bits 5 and 6 together allow the rudder-plus-roll command and the missile launch, 4.6) |
| 7 | closer than K: the wing-levelling roll command is allowed |

The steering clears bits 1, 2 and 7 at its start (mask `0x79`) [V: `ai_check.py`, 24,000 random records (8,000 over the states 0 to 9 and `0x0A`, 16,000 over 0, 1, 3, 4, 6, 8 and `0x0A`), classes `0x10` to `0x16` and `0x20`, 0 differences; the model is a transcription of this section].

### 3.8 The AI share of the attitude step (`0x31AE0`, from `0x31B6E`)

The attitude step of a non-seat record first conditions the key byte and the wing-levelling mark before the key byte is turned into rates (the common part is note 05 section 5) [V: `conv_check.py`, 1,800 random records in two seeds, key byte, W0, W2, state and counter compared, 0 differences]:

1. an **unscripted** record whose flag bit 4 (the epilogue, 3.7) is clear: the key byte is ANDed with `0xC3` (the roll and stick bits are dropped, rudder and throttle stay) and nothing else happens;
2. otherwise, in state 6 or `0x0A`: the **levelling**: W0 gets `0x2000`; the roll bits of the key byte are set from the roll word, `0x04` for 281 to 14400, `0x08` for 14401 to 28519, none outside (a dead band of 280 units around 0 and a band of 280 around a full circle), the throttle bits kept; and, when the flag bits 1 and 2 are both set (never, the mask of 3.5 clears them), the recentre request W2 `0x0010`;
3. a scripted record in any other state is left alone;
4. an unscripted record with the epilogue flag in another state needs the flag bits 5 and 6 (with 1 or 2 supplying the missing bit as 3.7 describes); W0 `0x2000` is **cleared** on the way when the hysteresis bits 1 or 2 are clear (they always are); then for an aircraft class: state 0 or 1 become **state 6 with counter 30** and the levelling of item 2 follows; state 8 gets the levelling; other states nothing. For a non-aircraft class the roll word picks a rate target instead: -960 for a roll word from 281 to 14400, +960 from 14401 to 28519, 0 otherwise.

So an aircraft that points at its target (both "ahead" flags set) holds its controls for 30 steps in state 6 with the wings levelled by the conversion.

## 4. The AI gate: speed, waypoints, targets, evasion

The AI has two layers. The **gate** `0x278F6` runs before the flight step of every AI record and decides the speed (the throttle bits of the key byte), the waypoint, the target, the destination point and the evasive state; the **state routine** of section 3 then turns the destination into roll, pitch and rudder keys. This section is the gate and its helpers. The model in `ai_tactics_check.py` is a transcription of the rules below, run over random scenes (an AI record, its target, the player, the lists and the variables) against the original: 787 scenes compared in two seeds, 0 differences [V].

### 4.1 The gate

For a record whose class nibble is not 0, `0x20` or `0x30` (those go to the weapon routine, the flight step alone, and the seat routine) the gate does, in this order, and returns 1 (the flight step then runs) in every case:

1. paused (`V:4A866`): nothing;
2. the counter `+0x4C` is decreased by 1;
3. W0 bit `0x1000` (active in the simulation) clear, or `V:4A846` (set to 1 at the start of every flight) clear: nothing more;
4. **a wreck** (`+0x20` bit 1 out of control): the destination is moved onto the record itself (cell, fine words) with height 0 (`+0x34` = 0); the speed word S moves by `0x90` toward `0x1200` and stops there (both `+0x6C` and `+0x6E`); W2 bits `0x2000` and `0x0008` are cleared. Nothing else;
5. the **speed control** (4.2) runs only for an unscripted record whose destination exists (`+0x2C` not negative); a scripted record skips it;
6. then, for every record: the waypoint rule (4.3), target handling (4.4), the threatened test and the evasion choice (4.5).

### 4.2 The range word and the speed control

The range routine `0x283E0` computes `+0x4A`, the distance from the record to its destination point. Its counter is the high nibble of `+0x39`, increased by 1 every call. The distance is recomputed on every call when it was under `0x480`, otherwise only when the counter exceeds 2 (distance under `0x900`) or 5 (further): then the nibble is cleared and the low nibble kept. With a destination:

* the horizontal components are (destination cell - own cell, a 16-bit difference) * 2^14 + (destination fine - own fine), taken as magnitudes; the height component is `|+0x34 - +0x10|`; each component's magnitude above `0x7F00` makes the range `0x7FFF`; otherwise the range is the hypot approximation of note 04a (5a) of the three magnitudes, at most `0x7FFF`;
* `+0x04` bit 0 is set (only when the hypot was computed: the branch that stores `0x7FFF` for a component above `0x7F00` leaves it alone);
* above `0x36C0` the high nibble of `+0x63` is set to `0xF` and the range stays; between `0x1E00` and `0x36C0` an engagement byte `+0x7A` of 4 becomes 3 (it is set the other way, 3 to 4, below `0x1E00`); the range class is then stored in the high nibble of `+0x63` for records but the player: above `0x1800` the range becomes `0x7FFF` and the nibble `0xF`; from `0x300` (exclusive) to `0x1800` the nibble is 2 when the range is up to `0xC00` and 3 beyond; up to `0x300` it is 1 [V].

The **speed control** (before the waypoint rule, destination required) writes the two low bits of the key byte (throttle: 1 up, 2 down; the flight step turns them into the throttle byte `+0x2B`). The desired speed is derived as follows; every number is a speed word (`0x1200` is the cruise speed of the wreck rule and the carrier's `0x3C0` scale is separate):

1. no target (`+0x38` = `0xFF`): class `0x14` flies a pattern: on the ground, below speed `0x100` it zeroes the throttle byte `+0x2B` and the throttle keys, otherwise it takes half its speed as the target (skipping the lower limit of step 6); in the air it aims at `0x1680` above a height of `0x6C0` (in `+0x10` units) and otherwise clears the gear-up command and aims at `0x600`. Other classes take the **cruise target** of step 4.
2. a target (the record `+0x38` names; the player when it is not valid, and then `+0x38` becomes `0x80`): its speed word v. (The code first tests the AI record's own valid bit and, if it were clear, would take the player and set `+0x38` = `0x80`; the gate runs only for valid records, so this test is dead [R].) With the threatened bit (`+0x04` bit 5): range up to `0x240` goes to step 3, `0x600` or more to step 5, between them to step 6. Without it: for states 0, 1 and scripted records, with range under `0x180` step 5; with the flags `+0x64` both bits 5 and 6 not set: range up to `0x300` step 5, else step 6; both set: step 3. Other states: range up to `0x360` step 6, state 6 step 3, else step 5;
3. if the range is `0x300` or more go to 4; else the target is twice v and, if that is `0x1BC0` or less, to step 6;
4. cruise target: `0x1EC0` for class `0x15`, `0x15C0` for a record that is not counted (W0 bit 3), `0x1D40` otherwise; go to step 6;
5. slow down: v minus the smaller of `v / 8` and `0x420`;
6. at least `0x900`.

Let R be the desired speed after the clamps and S the record's `+0x6C`. The average A = (R + S) shifted right by 1 (16-bit, arithmetic) is formed; if |A - S| is `0x90` or more (exactly `0x90` counts), A is replaced by S plus or minus `0x90` (toward A). The throttle bits are then set by comparing A with the **unmoved** S: 1 (up) when A is above S, 2 (down) when below, 0 when equal; so with |A - S| below `0x90` the key is pressed for any difference of 1 or more, and the aircraft's speed word changes only through the flight step's throttle response (note 05) [V: `ai_tactics_check.py`; the two bits of the key byte are the only effect].

The comparisons of this routine are signed: the schedule compares the high nibble of `+0x39` times 16 with 0x20 or 0x50 as **signed bytes** (the nibble reaches 6 at most, so the result equals the unsigned reading), and `+0x4A` with `0x480` and `0x900` as a signed word [R].

### 4.3 The waypoint rule (state 8)

Runs for an unscripted record in state 8 whose range is `0x480` or less. The list is the **run** of position blocks that the table at `0x2DCE8` names with the index `+0x3A`: the table is 60 words (`0x2DCE8`-`0x2DD5F`), each an offset from `0x2DCE8` to the first block of the run; a run is the consecutive blocks of five words (10 bytes) from there **up to, not including, the first block whose first word has bit 15 set** (the data end the runs with `0xFFFF` or `0xFF00`; in two cases the stop is a word of the following tables). The lengths (blocks before the stop) of entries 0 to 59: 0:33 (the table itself, unused), 1-9:2, 10-17: 9, 7, 5, 3, 2, 4, 8, 6, 18-19:2, 20:2 (the same run as 1), 21-22:3, 23:11, 24-26: 12, 11, 10, 27-28:3, 29:3, 30:5, 31:4, 32-37: 15, 14, 13, 12, 11, 10, 38-39:3, 40-59: 21 down to 2 (the 20 consecutive blocks from `0x2DD60`, each entry starting one block later and all running into the region table that follows at `0x2DE28`, whose first words are positive: the "blocks" past the 20th are the region pointers read as blocks) [R: the table read from the image; the walk of the rule is V in the 787 scenes of `ai_tactics_check.py`, which place the destination on random blocks of these runs]. The cruise missile (class `0x15`, placement entries with `+0x3A` 5 to 8) therefore flies from its first block through the **second** block of a two-block run and crashes on arriving there; an extractor must export every run to its stop, not the first block only. The record's destination (cell x and z, fine x and z) is searched in the list; the following block becomes the destination (height from its fifth word) and the range is set to `0x7FFF` so that the range routine recomputes it. If the destination is not in the list the debug word `0x35` is stored (4.8). At the end of the list W0 bit 7 (`0x0080`, AI valid) is cleared, and a class `0x15` record also gets W0 bit 9 (`0x0200`, crashed): the cruise missile **ends its flight** by crashing when its last waypoint is reached [V on the rule; the crash itself is section 5].

### 4.4 Target handling, acquisition and avoidance

Only for records that are *counted* (W0 bit 3 clear) and not in state 8: records that are not counted (friends, airliners) skip everything below and return after the speed and waypoint rules (a W0 bit 1 on a record that is not counted is read by the weapon routines only [R]).

1. The **target record** is the one `+0x38` names. A target that is not valid sends the record to the **lost-target rule** (below); otherwise the engagement byte 5 becomes 3 and W0 bit 0 (engaged) is set. With `+0x38` = `0xFF` (no target) none of that happens: the code jumps to step 2 with the player as the record, so W0 bit 0 is **not** set and the byte `+0x7A` is not changed, and a counted record without a target steers to the point of step 3 behind the player **at any distance**.
2. If the target is not the player and the state is not 8: when the player is within a box of half-size `0x180000` (progress 0 and 1), `0x240000` (2) or `0x300000` (3 or more) position units on every axis, the target becomes the player (`+0x38` = `0x80`) [V].
3. **Avoidance or pursuit point**: with the proximity bit (W2 bit 0) set, the record steers to a point beside, above or below its target (in the target's frame), 48 model units behind it, the placement of the point taken from the flags `+0x64`: bit 0 set, 96 units up (bit 1 set) or down; bit 0 clear, 168 units to one side (bit 1 set) or 96 to the other; without it, to the point **36 model units behind the target** (a model unit is 1,024 position units). The destination words are rebuilt from that world point (cells from the high word divided by 64, fine words from the position shifted right by 8 and masked to 14 bits, the height long shifted right by 8) [V]. Every 256th step the routine also stores a bit of the target's x position into `V:4A842` when the target is maneuvering hard; nothing reads it.
4. **The lost-target rule** (`0x28156`; the target is not valid): the record looks for the mission-start entry whose slot is its own index, first in the list of the current sub-list (`V:4A7F2`), then in the region its `+0x5D` names; with a match its destination becomes that entry's block, `+0x38` = `0xFF`, W0 bit 0 cleared. With no match and `+0x5D` not positive the debug word `0x34` is stored (4.8); with no match and a region number the original loops without end (no shipped state reaches it; the port ends the search) [V on the rule; **V on the loop, milestone 7**: `combat7-losttarget.txt` (generator `gen-combat7.py losttarget`, `airborne.vasnap`, record 8 staged as a counted AI hostile with W0 `0x11C0`, its target byte `+0x38` poked to 1 (a record that is not valid) and `+0x5D` poked): with `+0x5D` = 2 the region named holds an entry for slot 8, the rule finds it (`+0x38` becomes `0xFF`, W0 bit 0 stays clear, the pass counter runs on); with `+0x5D` = 0 the debug word is stored and the record goes on; **with `+0x5D` = 3** (the region whose only entry is for slot 12) **the pass counter `V:4A992` stops at the pass of the poke and stays there for the 58 video frames logged: the machine is in the loop** (the frame routine never completes the pass)]. The port ends the search with no match (`ai.c`); a state that hangs the original is not a state a player can sit in, so the port's ending is a safe choice and not a rule of the game (UNOBSERVABLE as a behaviour: there is nothing to compare after the hang).

### 4.5 The threatened test and the evasive state

The test `0x281E2` runs when the high nibble of `+0x39` is 1 (the first call after the range routine cleared it). If the target is further than `0x3000` the threatened bit (`+0x04` bit 5) is cleared. Otherwise the vector from the record to the target is normalised to `0xC0`; the bit is set when the target's nose points back at the record (the dot product of the target's forward axis with that vector is at most -`0x2C0000`, within about 24 degrees) and the two forward axes are nearly parallel (their dot product at least `0xD000000`, within about 36 degrees): the record is being chased from behind. Otherwise it is cleared [V].

**The test for a state 8 record** reads the register that points at the target record without loading it (the code jumps over the target lookup); the register then still holds what the world update left in it: in the rig it is the address of the previous AI aircraft record in the loop when that record directly precedes (4 to 6, 6 to 8), and the player's address after a launch-decision visit (records 9, 11, 13) and for record 4 [V, one scene of six AI records traced in the rig, `a3trace`: record 4: player; 6: record 4; 8: record 6; 10, 12, 14: player]. It matters only for state 8 records that are not counted (the mode 5 F-16s and the cruise missile), whose threatened bit then follows the previous record; a port that wants the original's result must use the same rule. The range word in the test (`0x3000` of the target's `+0x4A`) is in units of 256 position units, i.e. 12,288 fine steps = three quarters of a cell.

The choice runs when the bit is set, for states other than 6, and for state 8 only in mode 5 with the player's launch flag `V:4A86F` set. It selects a pair (counter, state) from the table at `0x30284` of 19 groups of four pairs, one pair per progress value 0 to 3 (`V:4A95F`, at most 3): the counter goes to `+0x4C` and the state to `+0x05` (a not-counted record in state 8 becomes counted: W0 bit 3 cleared and both `V:4A961`, `V:4A962` raised by one). The group is `0x10 * g` bytes into the table and is built as follows (range r is the *target's* range word `+0x4A`; dv the absolute difference of the two speed words; "slower" means the record's speed is the lower):

| r | group index g |
| --- | --- |
| up to `0x300` | 0; plus 2 when dv is `0xC0` or more and the record is slower, plus 4 when it is faster |
| `0x300` to `0xC00` | 6; speed rule as below |
| `0xC00` to `0x1E00` | 12; speed rule as below |
| over `0x1E00` | 18 (no speed rule) |
