The speed rule for the two middle ranges: plus 2 when dv is `0xC0` or more and the record is slower, plus 4 when faster; then plus 1 when the player's designated target (`V:4AA78`) is this record and the launch flag `V:4A86F` is set (the flag is cleared by this test and at the end of every AI gate that reaches its tail). The groups (counter, state) for progress 0, 1, 2, 3:

The table has 19 groups (g = 0 to 18) of four (counter, state) pairs. Its shape [R]: within a group the four counters are equal, a short hold (the smaller of two values) or a long one (twice as long), and the long one belongs exactly to the groups that the speed rule's +2 or +4 reaches; the state at progress 0 is the same in every group (state 9, the shallow hold); at progress 1 to 3 the state is 9 or 5 in most groups, and state 2 (a break turn, 3.2) occurs only in a few groups, at progress 1 to 3; the last group (g = 18, the long range) holds state 9 at every progress. The pairs are disk data and are not listed; the port reads `tables/ai.bin` (the evasion sub-table; FNV-1a-32 of its 19 * 16 bytes, little-endian words, counter then state, group by group and progress by progress, `b4a179a5`).

State 9 holds a 8-degree bank for the counter's steps, state 5 a 14-degree bank, state 2 breaks into a turn (3.2). The campaign progress therefore replaces a shallow hold by a hard break turn at the close and middle ranges and a bank hold at the long ones. After any of this the gate clears the launch flag `V:4A86F` and returns [V].

### 4.6 The launch decision (AI missiles)

The records 9, 11 and 13 ask `0x2701A` whether the aircraft in the record below fires; record 5 never asks (the aircraft in slot 4 fires only on the script's request `V:4A873`). The decision is yes when the owner is not hit, dying or crashed (W0 mask `0x8700` clear), not out of control, and either `V:4A874` is set (a request) or all hold: the owner is valid; its target is valid, not out of control and **not engaged** (W0 bit 0 clear); the owner's flags `+0x64` have bits 5 and 6 set; the owner's W0 bit 0 is set; the range class (high nibble of `+0x63`) is 2 or 3. The launch routine then needs a stock (note 07 part B); the request `V:4A874` is cleared on a yes. The decision's tests are, in order: the W0 mask, out of control, the request, the owner valid, **the target tests only when the target byte has bit 7 set** (a byte with bit 7 clear skips them and the decision goes on to the flags test), the flags `0x60`, W0 bit 0, the range class. The order of the tests is the one above: the W0 mask is `0x8700` (the bit `0x0100` is cleared by a record's first step, so it behaves as `0x8600` afterwards), then out of control, then the request [V: `conv_check.py` part 2, 400 random owners and targets against the decision routine, 0 differences]. **With the target byte `0xFF`** (bit 7 set, index 127) the routine reads the record at `0x4B23C + 0x7F * 0x200 = 0x5B03C`, which is **outside the entity array and outside the program image** (the image ends at `0x58FEC`): the original reads whatever memory lies there; the rig reads zeros (not valid, no launch). No shipped state reaches it (a record that fires has a leader or target, and `0xFF` only appears after an exit or a lost target, when W0 bit 0 is clear and the later tests would fail anyway). The port treats a target byte of `0xFF` as "no valid target" for this test. The same holds for any index of 16 or more (a script-set target byte of `0x90` or more would read the secondary objects at `0x4D23C` and then the variable area): not reachable in the shipped data.

**Which request launches which record** [V: `launch_order_check.py`, the whole world update, owners 4, 8, 10, 12 with and without out of control and with the request byte pending]. The record 5 (owner slot 4) is launched by `V:4A873` **before any test of the owner**: it fires with the owner out of control (the implementer's case: B20 = 02, request pending, W0 end `0x20C8`). The records 9, 11, 13 use the decision above with `V:4A874`: an out-of-control owner does not fire. `V:4A874` is **one global request**: the first of the records 9, 11, 13 that asks consumes it whatever its owner is (the routine clears it when it answers yes, before the owner's validity or stock is looked at), so with the owner in slot 10 or 12 the request is taken by record 9 whose owner (slot 8) cannot fire, and nothing launches. Both orders of the earlier two findings are therefore right, for different records. (A first run of the check seemed to show the request ignored for the owners 10 and 12; that was the global request being taken by record 9, not an order effect.) **Both requests in one pass** [V: `launch_order_check.py`, the last part: the owners 4, 8, 10 and 12 all able to fire with a rate gate that is open, `V:4A873` and `V:4A874` both set, one world update]: the launches follow the record order of the update. **Record 5 (the script request) launches first and clears `V:4A873`; record 9 (the decision, owner 8 able) asks next and takes `V:4A874` and launches; the records 11 and 13 find no request and no other reason to fire**: launched records 5 and 9, both bytes 0 afterwards. With the owner in slot 8 out of control the decision of record 9 answers no **before it reads the request** (the order of tests above), so the request stays and the next asker, record 11 (owner 10), takes it: launched records 5 and 11. So the two findings of the earlier rounds are the one rule, and the "conflict" is closed: record 5's request is read at the head of its own step (no test of the owner) and is the first in the order of the update, and the global request goes to the first of 9, 11 and 13 whose owner passes the W0 mask and the out-of-control test. **No code in the image sets either request** (note 07 7.6, rescanned in this round: `V:4A873` has one read, `0x26D26`, and one clear, `0x28534`; `V:4A874` one read, `0x2702C`, and clears at `0x27096` and `0x2853A`), so in play both are 0 and the order cannot arise; an emulator capture with both bytes poked adds nothing the whole-update rig run does not show (`followup-combat-launch-order.txt`, below).

**The post code `V:4AB98`** is one global word. A post (`0x29BC8`) stores its code there and sets bit 0 of `V:4A984`; a second post in the same pass overwrites the first, so **the last post of the pass is the one the message line sees** (the arbiter of note 06 decides, on its next run, whether it is shown). Posts made from the world update: the player's damage and ejection posts (`0x2A87C`, `0x2A8A0`, `0x2A456`), the proximity warning `0xD00A` of the **viewed** record only (`0x2A48A`), and the target-destroyed post `0x4016` (`0x28512`). Other AI records post only debug words (4.8). A port needs one global word with last-writer-wins, not a copy per record.

**The launches of the records 9, 11 and 13 against the original** [V: `test_weapons_diff` part `launchdec` (Task 6 third round): 2,000 random owners (records 8, 10, 12) and targets, in the record order 9, 11, 13, the original's `0x2701A` (answer and the request byte after, so the consumption of the global request by the first asker) and, after a yes, `0x275F6` (the rate gate against `V:4A992`, the stock), against the port: 1,517 yes decisions, 663 of them taking the global request, 322 launches, 800 refusals by the rate gate, 395 by the stock, 0 differences].

### 4.7 The lift-off hook and the stale-lock drop

The hook `0x1F0E2` is called by the flight step at lift-off (`0x1724E`) for every record but the player: if the wheels are not on the ground (W2 bit `0x0080` clear) it sets the gear-up command (bit 7 of `+0x7C`) of the record being stepped (`V:4AA6E`); the gear then retracts as the flight step of note 05 section 8 does for any record. Its second entry `0x1F0EC`, which would clear the command, has no caller [R: all call sites of the image scanned]. A lead or hostile therefore retracts its gear in the step after it leaves the ground; nothing lowers it again.

The drop `0x26F28` runs in every pass, paused or not: when the player's designated target `V:4AA78` is not negative and the record it names is no longer valid (W0 bit 6 clear) or has gone out of control (`+0x20` bit 1), `V:4AA78` and `V:4A9F2` are set to `0xFFFF` and `V:4A920` to 0 (no lock) [R].

### 4.8 The order inside a pass, the step counter, and the debug words

**Order of the world update `0x26AF8`** (read from the code; each item once per pass) [R]:

1. if not paused: the 16 life words of the secondary objects (`0x4D240`, stride `0x20`) are decremented;
2. the **region tick** `0x2D0AA` when the low nibble of `V:4A992` is 3 (also when paused);
3. W2 bit `0x0001` (proximity) is cleared for records 0 to 14;
4. if not paused: the damage cool-downs `V:4A907`, `V:4A906` count down;
5. the **stale-lock drop** `0x26F28`;
6. the record loop, records 0 to 15 in index order (1.2): for each, the gate `0x278F6` (4.1 to 4.5) and then the flight step `0x2A2A2`, which calls the state routine (3.1), then converts the keys, and runs the per-record region check (3.4) at the head of its dying test;
7. the tail `0x2852E`: the launch requests `V:4A872` to `V:4A874` are cleared, the stale-lock drop runs **again**, and the mission script dispatcher follows (note 08 part C).

A record placed by the tick is stepped in the same pass (the tick runs before the loop; its first pass makes no motion, note 08 8.3); a record freed in the loop is seen by the tick only in a later pass.

**The step counter `V:4A992`** counts passes: it is increased once per frame-routine pass while not paused (`0x114B8`, after the pause test), so a pause freezes the phases. It is cleared at the mission start (`0x12EC0`, the state that opens the gate: an ESC restart passes through it) and by the last state of the game-over path; it is **kept across a crash restart** (note 05 section 17.17, captured: the counter runs through the whole restart), so the 16- and 32-step phases do not restart there. The second clear in the image (`0x138B6`, in the state `0x138AC`) has no reference and is dead, and so is the block at `0x1EDF8` with its rounding of the counter (`0x1EE00`): nothing branches to it (note 06 item 8 is right; an earlier version of this section said otherwise). For the port: clear the core's step counter at the mission start and in the game-over path, hold it in a pause, and leave it alone in a crash restart.

**The debug words.** The code stores a value in `V:4AA56` and calls `0x1F1D6` at six places in this section: `0x1E` (3.4), `0x34` (4.4), `0x35` (4.3), `0x3C` (3.2), `0x3E` (6.2) and `0x3A` (the result handler, for a record that is hit while dying with its counter above 15, which cannot happen). The address `0x1F1D6` holds a bare return; the code that follows it (`0x1F1D8`) is a dump routine gated by the byte `V:4A864`, which a key handler toggles (`0x1434E`) and which is called only from `0x1F51C`. The stores therefore have **no visible effect** [R]; the port needs no message for them.

### 4.9 What Task 5 implements from sections 3 and 4

Per step and per AI record, in this order: the gate (4.1 to 4.5), then in the flight step the state routine (3.1), then the input conversion; the region tick before the record loop (2.5, 4.8); the per-record check at the phase of the record index (3.4); the launch decision (4.6) at the record's turn in the world update. Randomness: the mission-start selector (2.4) is the only source in this section; the avoidance's `V:4A842` bit is a dead store and needs none. Nothing in the AI draws from the timer after the placement [R: the selector `V:4ABB0` is read in three places only, all in the placement].

### 4.10 Verdicts on the corrections found by the port's rig comparison

Each item of the port's implementation round that the notes did not state, checked against the original (V = rig run in this round, R = code):

| item | verdict |
| --- | --- |
| seat landed: vx rises by `0xC` only while below `0xC0`; vz moves one toward 0 and becomes 1 from 0; vy 0 | confirmed [V `seat_check` part 1], stated exactly in 6.4 |
| seat damping shift 3 for every class from counter 3; threshold `0xF0` minus the counter, larger for a negative counter | confirmed [V], 6.4 |
| view-switch groups; the parachute and pod never take the view and use the reversed thrust (negated before the normaliser) | confirmed [V parts 1 and 3], 6.4 |
| a landed record does not move | confirmed [V `seat_check` part 5; code `0x2A540`], 6.3a |
| rudder table = the bank table (`0x3136E` for class `0x14` and state 8, halved at speed `0x1800`) | confirmed [V `ai_check`], 3.6 |
| no bank limit outside states 0, 1, 8 | confirmed [V `ai_check`], 3.6 |
| signed range-schedule compare | stated above (4.2); equal to the unsigned reading in all reachable values |
| B4 bit 0 not set when out of range | confirmed [V `ai_tactics_check`], 4.2 |
| speed control compares with the unmoved S | confirmed [V], rewritten above |
| not-counted state 8: threatened test reads a stale record register | stated in 4.5 |
| scripted state 6 waits | confirmed [V `ai_check`], 3.2 |
| launch mask `0x8600`, request before out-of-control | **both right for different records** (4.6): the decision routine (records 9, 11, 13) has the mask `0x8700` and tests out of control before the request [V `conv_check` part 2]; record 5 (owner slot 4) tests its request `V:4A873` first and never looks at the owner [V `launch_order_check`] |
| state 6 with counter 30 when flags 5 and 6 are set | confirmed [V `conv_check` part 1], 3.8 |
| levelling-mark clearing | confirmed [V `conv_check` part 1], 3.8 |
| the region check only when `+0x7A` is 3 to 5 | **refuted**: the code has no such guard (3.4); for the values that occur it changes nothing |

### 4.11 The throttle of a non-player record

There is **no throttle branch that is specific to non-player records**. The speed step `0x15ED4` turns the key bits 0 and 1 of `+0x65` into the throttle byte `+0x2B` with one rule for every index; the player's pointer lever and the notices are the only index-dependent parts [V: `throttle_check.py`: 2,000 steps of a scripted record under random key sequences and 3,000 steps of an unscripted AI record against the player, the key byte read in front of the speed step by a stub, throttle byte and W2 bits compared, 0 differences]. The rule, per step, with T the signed throttle byte, c the low nibble of `+0x39` and s a step:

| situation | result |
| --- | --- |
| fuel `+0x72` is 0 | T = 0 and the afterburner bit (W2 `0x0008`) cleared; nothing else |
| no throttle key bit | the low nibble of `+0x39` becomes 1 (the high nibble, the range timer of 4.2, is kept) |
| a key bit | c + 1 is the ramp: if it is above 8 the step is 8, else it is stored in the low nibble of `+0x39` and is the step; s = step shifted right by 1 (0 to 4) |
| bit 0 (up) | W2 bit `0x0400` (idle detent) cleared; T = T + s as a signed byte. If the sum is `0x78` (120) or more: with W2 bit `0x2000` (military-power detent) clear the bit is set and the key bits 0 and 1 are cleared; with it set the afterburner bit W2 `0x0008` is set; in both cases T = `0x78` |
| bit 1 (down) | W2 bit `0x2000` cleared; with the afterburner bit set it is cleared and T = T - 1; else with T negative T = T - s limited to -32 (`0xE0`); else T = T - 4, and when that is negative: with B4 bit `0x08` (catapult armed) T = 0, otherwise, with W2 bit `0x0400` clear, the key bits are cleared, T = 0 and the bit `0x0400` is set (a negative T stays when the bit was already set) |
| both bits | nothing |

So an entity that the AI presses up for many steps reaches the afterburner in two stages (military power first, afterburner on the next up-step at 120). The range timer of 4.2 shares the byte `+0x39`: both writers keep the other's nibble. A port trajectory whose throttle differs by 4 (one down-step) from the original after a thousand steps has an error in one of these rows or in the order of the AI's key byte and the speed step within the pass (the AI's key is written first, 3.1 and 4.8).

### 4.12 The ground contact of a non-player record

The floor and the vertical motion are the player's code; the differences are few [R: `0x16E1A`-`0x16F30`, `0x17180`-`0x17262`; V: `ground_check.py`, five class/gear/speed cases]:

| rule | non-player |
| --- | --- |
| floor height (position units) | the same for every record: class nibble 0: -512; gear fully down (`+0x7C` bits 4-6 all 0): `0x708` (1,800), class `0x14`: `0xA8C` (2,700); gear not down: `0x2EE` (750); a record whose B4 bits `0x40` or `0x80` are set (over a deck or pad) adds `+0x4E` * 256 to the floor (the deck height written by the collision phase; so a floor at a height of 176 in `+0x10` units is a deck or pad height `+0x4E` of about 169 plus the 1,800) [V for the three plain floors] |
| contact | when the new height is at or below the floor: B4 bit `0x04` cleared, and for every class but 0 the height is set to the floor [V: heights 1800, 750, 2700 from the second step on] |
| terrain query | `0x2F75A` as for the player, skipped when B4 bits `0xC0` are set, and when the ground bit is already set and `+0x4C & 7` is not 0 |
| the touchdown block (landing statistics, gear collapse, hard-landing tests, `V:4A857`) | **skipped**: the jump at `0x16F30` goes to `0x17180`: W2 bit `0x0080` is set (unconditional) and, when the indicated speed `+0x6E` is below `0x3C0`, **W2 bit `0x1000` (the skid or smoke-trail bit) is cleared**. (An earlier version of this table said W0 bit `0x1000`, the active bit; the pointer in that code is the W2 word. The active bit is never touched: it stays set at every speed [V].) The block runs on **every step in which the height is at or below the floor**, with the ground bit already set or not, over land or over water: in the rig the trail bit was cleared at W = 708 and 738 and kept at W = 6,024 and 6,114, with the ground bit initially clear or set, and over a water cell [V: `g17` probe, 12 cases] |
| the cruise missile (class `0x15`) | its **height is forced** to `0xB000` (45,056 = 176 units of 256) after every position update (`0x2A5B2`-`0x2A5BA`: the standard motion block sets `+0x18` to `0xB000` for class `0x15`), whatever the placement block's height word or the floor says: the missile flies 176 units above the sea from its second step on (the first step makes no motion, 8.3). The height words of the waypoint blocks are not used for it [R; V in the port's rig run: y = 176 << 8 in the second step] |
| lift-off (height above the floor with the ground bit set) | the ground bit is cleared; B4 bit `0x08` (catapult armed) is cleared and the jolt kind 4 requested; **the hook `0x1F0E2`** instead of the player's flight-clock bookkeeping (4.7) |
| the vertical velocity adjustment while rolling on the ground and the drift blocks that read `V:4A857` / `V:4A858` | player only (`0x16BF4`, `0x16C5A`, `0x16CCE`, `0x16D42` test the index) |

**Which vertices the contact tests read** [V: test-ai 6,000 cases, test-ai-traj 6 of 6 and 60 of 60, test-nearground 0 differences, the port fed the same words]: the three-vertex ground test (`0x2C08C`), the landmark phase and the ship test of the scan read the **mover record's own** `+0xA4` slots (0 to 2 for the ground test and the landmark phase; 0, and 3 for a viewed mover, for the ship test, 10.2), for every record, never the player model rotated by the record's matrix.

The first step after a record is placed makes no motion (note 08 8.3), so a trajectory test must start one step later. **The dive comparison's "x speed drift" (settled, `ai_check.py dives` and the trajectory harness).** The same dive was flown in the original's world update by a class `0x12` aircraft (gear up) and by a class `0x15` cruise missile from identical states (a record that is not AI valid, as the trajectory harness stages its dives; position longs, the three velocity longs, the speed words and W2 logged per step, 16 dives of both classes, pitch 300 to 6,000 and 22,800 to 28,500, speeds 500 to 6,000, heights 3,000 to 200,000 position units) [V]. Two things make the classes differ, and there is no third. **(a) The height**: the missile's height is forced to `0xB000` after every position update (the table above), the aircraft's is not: the y words differ from the first step. **(b) The ground rows of the motion step**: while the aircraft is at its floor (W2 bit `0x0080` set), its x and z velocity words are taken from the target velocity at once (no smoothing), whereas the missile, which is never on the ground, keeps the smoothing; in 6 of the 16 dives the aircraft reached its floor (the terrain of the staging cell) in the second step and the horizontal velocity words differed from the third step by the ratio of the two rules (for example 5,424 against 3,240), so x and z drift apart by thousands of position units. Of the 10 dives that stayed in the air, 6 agreed in x and z for all 40 steps and 4 differed by 4 to 20 units from the 11th to the 39th step, **because the indicated speed words differ by 4 from the same step on** (the speed step reads the height, and the two heights differ); the velocity differences follow from that. Neither is a defect of the port: the port's trajectory harness runs class `0x15` dives in one case of five (`AI_TRAJ_DIVE`, every field of the record compared per step) and agrees in 400 of 400 cases (seed 7, 100 steps) and in the 900 of 900 of the earlier round. The earlier remark that the horizontal velocity words differ only in the player-only blocks was incomplete: the ground rows of the motion step are the third place, for a record of any class that stands on the floor.

## 5. The dying path of non-player records

This section is how a record that is not the player leaves the world: how it starts to die, how long it takes, when the kill is counted and what frees the record. The player's own dying path is note 05 section 17.3 and 17.12; the damage rules that decide *when* a hit becomes a kill (hit counts, cool-downs) and the explosion objects are note 07 part B.

### 5.1 The three ways in

| entry | where | what it sets |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **crashed bit** (the usual kill) | the tail of the flight step `0x2A2A2` when no collision result occurred (`0x2AA1E`): W0 bit `0x0200` (crashed) is set, which the gun's third hit (`+0x3C` low nibble reaches 3, `0x2B286`) and the missile hits (`0x2B798`) leave behind together with the out-of-control bit `+0x20` bit 1, the camera-follow record `V:54E72`, W2 bit `0x0010` and the state byte 1 (classes `0x15` and class nibble 0 get only the crashed bit) | `0x2A99A`: W0 bit `0x0400` (dying) is set, `+0x4C` = 15, and the crashed bit is **cleared again** for every record but the player |
| **collision result 0x10 or 0x20** (ground, hull, landmark) | the result handler of the step, `0x2A8E6` for a non-player | W0 bit `0x8000` (hit) set; out of control (`+0x20` bit 1); W0 bit `0x1000` (active) cleared; then the same dying start as above. **An aircraft (class nibble 1) gets no explosion object and no impact back-up**: for results below `0x40` the handler jumps from the class test straight to the dying start (`0x2A94E` to `0x2A99A`); only a record of class nibble 0 (a missile) passes the object code, below. A record that is already dying does not restart (the handler ends) |

**Result 0x10 for a non-player record** [R: the code of `0x2A758`-`0x2A9C0`; V: the handler called in the rig with chosen registers]: the handler does **not** set W2 bit `0x0080` (only the player's branch does, note 05 17.12; the ground bit of a wreck comes from the ground-contact code of the flight step, 4.12) and makes **no speed test** (the `0x3C0` test belongs to the player's branch): every non-player record that gets the result goes through the hit bit, out of control, inactive and the dying start.

**The ground and hull object, class nibble 0 only** (`0x2A970`-`0x2A996`): a missile that gets the result 0x10 or 0x20 passes `0x2A970` (the result `0x40` frees it at once instead, 5.1). For the result 0x20 it creates an object of model `0x11` with the creator of 1.5 at its position. For the result 0x10 it first backs the impact up (`0x2AA5E`): with the step's vertical displacement shifted right by 8 (**when that is zero the back-up is skipped but the object is still made**), the record's longs `+0x14` and `+0x1C` are moved back along the displacement to the plane y = 0 (x gets D.x * 16 / D.y16 * h / 16, z likewise, 16-bit divides and products, h the height `+0x18` shifted right by 8 and negated) and `+0x18` is set to 0 [V: the record's `+0x14`/`+0x18`/`+0x1C` change from (293,621,760; 5,000; 293,629,952) to (293,621,836; 0; 293,629,990) for a displacement of (131,072; -8,388,608; 65,536); the cell and fine words `+0x06`..`+0x10` keep the values of the position update]. The object is a copy of the record's first 32 bytes after the back-up, so **its long position words carry the backed-up point while its cell and fine words are the record's own**. The \"over land\" test is the terrain query `0x2F75A`, called at the record's (backed-up) position for the record named by `V:4AA6E`; it leaves B4 bit `0x02` (over water) set or cleared and the object is made only when the bit is clear: 36 cells of the grid probed, land cells made one object each and water cells none [V]. Model of the object: 12 or 13 by the creator's slot parity (1.5).

### 5.2 The counter

`+0x4C` is set to 15 at the start (after the gate's decrement of that step) and the gate `0x278F6` decreases it by 1 at the beginning of every later step for classes other than the weapon, ship and seat classes. The dying test at the top of the step (`0x2A2E8`) reads it after the decrement: while it is **above 0** the record only has W0 bit `0x2000` cleared and goes on; at **0 or below** the expiry of 5.3 runs. So the expiry happens **15 steps** after the step that started the dying (the start step is 0; the expiry is the step in which the counter reaches 0) [V: emulator capture of the training lead, below, and the rig timeline].

### 5.3 The expiry

At the expiry the dying bit `0x0400` is cleared and then, in this order [V: `dying_check.py`, 2,000 random combinations of class, W0, W2 and counter in two seeds, 0 differences]:

1. class `0x15` (cruise missile) or class nibble 0: the record is **freed** (5.4); no kill is counted;
2. class nibble 1 (aircraft) with W0 bit `0x0008` clear (counted) **and** W2 bit `0x0080` (on the ground) clear: the kill counter **`V:4A963` is incremented**. (The bit `0x0008` is the placement's "not counted" bit; friendlies and airliners placed with it are never counted. Classes with other nibbles are never counted.);
3. W2 bit `0x0080` set (the wreck is on the ground): the record is freed;
4. else W0 bit `0x8000` (the hit bit set by a collision result) set: the record is freed;
5. else (an airborne wreck without a collision yet): the record is **not freed**; W0 bit `0x2000` is cleared and the step goes on with the wreck falling.

So a shot-down aircraft that is still in the air at the expiry is **counted but kept**; when it touches the ground (the touchdown block of the flight step sets W2 bit `0x0080`; a collision result 0x10 or 0x20 does not, 5.1) the impact starts a second dying count of 15 steps (the start routine of 5.1 runs again, now with the active bit cleared), and at its expiry the record is freed **without a second count** because it is on the ground. **But a second impact that is not a touchdown counts again**: a collision result 0x10 or 0x20 for a non-player record (a hull, a landmark, the ground test) sets the hit bit and restarts the count without setting W2 bit `0x0080` (the handler sets that bit only for the player, note 05 17.12), so at the second expiry the record is still "in the air": the kill counter is incremented a **second time** and the record is freed because of the hit bit [V: `dying_check.py` part 3, the handler's end state poked after the first expiry: count 1 at step 20, count 2 and freed at step 39; R for the reachability of the result with a wreck]. A wreck that has already hit the ground at its first expiry (W2 bit `0x0080` set) is freed at once, uncounted [V: the rig timeline: crash flag at step 5, count at 20, touchdown at 37, freed at 52].

### 5.4 What frees a record

Freeing is: W0 bit `0x0040` (valid) cleared, `+0x4C` = 20, W0 bits `0x4000` (radar) and `0x2000` cleared; nothing else in the record changes (class, position, the hit bit and the rest of W0 stay, so a freed record typically reads W0 `0x0085`-like values). The display request `V:4A910` is **not** set (only the immediate removal of 5.1 sets `0x0C`); the record leaves the display queues at the next rebuild because queued entries are matched by the valid bit (note 04a). The slot can be written by the next placement, which clears the first `0xA4` bytes (2.1). The counter after freeing keeps going down by itself and nothing reads it.

### 5.4a Other classes

| class | how the record dies or expires |
| --- | --- |
| `0x20` (ships) | the gate never decrements a ship's counter, so a ship with the dying bit would stay in that state for ever [V: `dying_check.py` part 4, counter 3 after 8 passes: unchanged; **and in the emulator, milestone 7** (`combat7-subkill.txt`, generator `gen-combat7.py subkill`: the staged record 8 put into the dying state with the counter 3 and class `0x21`): over 100 video frames (ten passes) its counter stays 3, it is never freed and `V:4A963` stays 0, while the same record as a counted aircraft (class `0x12`) is freed at the expiry and `V:4A963` rises to 1 (frame 19), and as a not-counted aircraft (W0 bit 3) it is freed and `V:4A963` stays 0]; no shipped path puts a ship there. **Shooting the submarine** (F6, class `0x21`) therefore adds nothing: a tracer on it is stopped with no counter and no mark (the big-hull rule of 8.2), and a ship has no step of its own, so a crashed or dying mark on it is never converted or counted down; the fourth count of F6 (note 08 12.9) is not the submarine |
| class nibble 0 (missiles) | the weapon routine `0x27DC2` decrements the counter itself and frees the record at 0 (clearing the whole W0 word and requesting `V:4A910` = `0x0C`, the same code as the seat's); the dying branch of this section frees a class-0 record at its expiry without a count (rule 1 of 5.3). The weapon rules are note 07 part B |
| `0x30` to `0x32` | the seat routine frees the record at once when the dying bit is set (6.4) and never reaches the expiry test |

### 5.5 What the port does with `V:4A963`

The mission scripts compare `V:4A963` with the hostile cap `V:4A962` (note 08 section 6.1 and the part C scripts), and the placement clears it. It therefore counts **kills of counted aircraft at their first expiry in the air**, not at the moment of the hit: with the 15-step dying delay, a script that tests it sees the kill 15 steps after the destroying hit. A counted aircraft that dies on the ground or is removed by a collision result 0x40 never adds to it; one that is hit twice in the air (5.3) adds to it twice.

### 5.6 Evidence

Rig, `tools/m68k-rig/tests/lists_model/dying_check.py`: part 1 runs the step `0x2A2A2` on 2,000 random records (classes `0x12`, `0x13`, `0x10`, `0x16`, `0x15`, `0x14`, `0x00`, `0x01`, `0x21`; W0 with the dying bit and random active, not-counted, hit and `0x2000` bits; W2 with and without the ground bit; the counter -3 to 5) and compares the dying bit, the valid bit, the counter and `V:4A963` with the rule above (1,335 of them past the counter, 1,039 freed, 234 counted, 0 differences); part 2 runs the whole world update `0x26AF8` on a placed hostile and prints the timeline (counter 15 at step 5, count at step 20, touchdown at step 37, freed at step 52).

Emulator, `tools/vamiga-ref/scripts/combat-dying-lead.txt` (generator `gen-combat-dying-lead.py`): the training lead of part 1 (record 4, class `0x10`, placed by the original; not counted) is given W0 bit `0x0200` and out of control in the real world update. Log `extracted/captures/logs/combat-dying-lead.txt` (git-ignored): in pass 3 the first sample after the poke reads `0x13C8` (the poke, not yet stepped) and a later frame of the same pass reads `0x14C8` with the counter 15 (the step ran); the counter is 14 at pass 4 and falls by one per pass to 1 at pass 17; at pass 18 W0 reads `0x10C8` (dying bit cleared), the counter 0, and `V:4A963` stays 0 (the lead is not counted) and the record stays valid and active (airborne, no hit bit): exactly the rule of 5.3 step 5. A counted hostile was not captured in the emulator: a staged copy of the player record in slot 8 (the earlier attempt, `combat-dying-air.txt`) stops the machine in some runs, so the count itself rests on the rig [R for the emulator, V for the rig].

## 6. Cameras, the fixed camera and the ejection

This section settles the cameras that note 04 calls "tracking cameras" and note 06 leaves open, the fixed camera, and the ejection. The result for the first is a correction: **there are no camera modes for following weapons or crashes**. The camera-mode byte `V:4A966` belongs to the front end; the rest of the "follows" are the viewed-record mechanism, the trail record and the ring tables, all stated below.

### 6.1 The camera-mode byte `V:4A966` and the stage machine `V:4A86E`

`V:4A966` is written by the front-end states only (note 08 6.4 lists the writers; this section adds the mission-approach chain). The values: `0xFF` after a reset, **0 in every flight** (and 1 is never written: a mode 1 would behave as 0), 2 the approach to the mission's first hostile (briefing), 3 arrived, 4 the map overview, 5 the location selection, 6 the fly-in. In a flight it is therefore always 0 and the in-flight external views are the **ring cameras** (note 06: ring offsets by class, direction `V:4A96A`, distance level); no camera mode follows a missile or a wreck. What follows an object is, apart from the trail drawer, the viewed record, which the seat code and the mission scripts switch. What the notes called "weapon and crash follows" are three separate mechanisms:

| mechanism | what it is | writers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the viewed record | `V:4A994` (index) and `V:4A996` (byte offset): the record the views are about, 0 = the player. In the cockpit view the HUD data, the neighbour test of the flight step and the camera cells are the viewed record's | set by the routine `0x1EC14` (record index in d1; viewed record, display request, lock cleared, notice `0xFE`): called by the seat spawn and the seat's step (6.4), by the **mission scripts** at `0x286DE` and `0x289DE` (the "tracking camera on the landing aircraft or on the cruise missile" of note 08 part C) [R]; set to record 4 for the training part 1 lead (`0x121C2`); back to the player by the crash hand-over and the mission start (`0x130AE`) and by `0x14392` when the viewed record is no longer valid (W0 bit 6 clear) |
| the trail record | `V:54E72`: the record whose camera trail (`0x2AC5A`) the drawer shows; part B section 13 | the gun's third hit and the missile kill (`0x2B2AC`, `0x2B7BC`), an aircraft collision (`0x2A958`), the mission start (record 4, `0x1D268`) |
| the ring tables | the camera offset of the external view by the viewed record's class | `0x2D756` (6.2) |

**The camera routine `0x2D756`** (once per pass, after the world update) returns at once unless the view is external (`V:4A83D` non-zero), the camera-update request `V:4A86D` is set and the game is not paused. With the map view (`V:4A865`) and mode 0 it follows the viewed record's x and z at the saved altitude (note 06). With the camera mode 5 or below 2 it takes the **ring path**: the offset triple for the viewed record's class (table `0x2D718` for class nibble 3, `0x2D6A0` for class `0x11`, `0x2D6DC` for `0x14`, `0x2D664` otherwise), direction `V:4A96A` (0 to 9), scaled by the distance level `V:4A83D` (level -1: as it stands; 0 and 1: one and a half times; 2 to 4: shifted left by level - 1), turned into a world point (the directions 0 and 4, ahead and behind, of a non-seat class in the viewed record's own frame, `0x1D466`; every other direction and every direction of the seat in the frame of the camera matrix at `0x4ACC6`, `0x1D440`) and kept at least 7 height units above the deck height `+0x4E` of the viewed record (0 when it is over neither deck nor ground, B4 bits `0xC0`). With the mode 2, 3, 4 or 6 it runs the **mover** (6.2). Because `V:4A86D` is cleared only by the fixed camera (6.3), the ring camera is updated every pass until then.

The ring offsets of class nibble 3 (the seat; model units, x right, y up, z ahead; direction 0 behind-top ... see note 06 for the direction numbering) [R, read from the image]:

Ten directions of three words each (x, y, z). The values are disk data and are not listed: the port reads them from `tables/ai.bin` (the ring sub-table; FNV-1a-32 of its 10 * 6 bytes, little-endian words, direction by direction, `1150c766`, also `tables/camera_ring_30.bin`).

### 6.2 The mover and the mission approach

The mover `0x2D8E8` runs the stage machine `V:4A86E` (nine stages, jump table `0x2D640`). Each pass: the vector from the camera `V:4ACF6` to the target `V:4AD0E` (three longs, position units); D = its largest absolute component; the stage routine compares D with its thresholds, may change the stage and the **target for the next pass**, and chooses the step shift s; then the damped step moves the camera. The thresholds, shifts and targets are the table of note 08 6.4 [now V: `camera_check.py`, 400 random cases in each of two seeds around every threshold, all nine stages, camera and offsets compared bit for bit, 0 differences], with these precisions:

* the step uses the shift chosen **before** a stage-changing target routine runs (the offset-target routines restore it), so stage 3 to 4 steps with 3 and stage 4 to 5 with 2;
* the blend target of stages 0 to 2 is built from the camera and the viewed record: a = (camera + record) / 2 in all three coordinates; b = (a + camera) / 2 in x and z and a / 2 in y; c = (b + a) / 2; e = (c + a) / 2; target = (e + c) / 2 (arithmetic shifts);
* the damped step: the difference is divided by 4 until D is below `0x4800`, normalised to length `0x200` (note 04a P4), each component sign-extended from its low word and shifted left by s, and averaged with the previous step `V:4AD02` (new = old + (new - old) / 2, unless the previous step is zero); the camera moves by it; the view offsets `V:4ACEA` are the negated camera position (x and z masked to 22 bits);
* arrival: with D at most `0x240` and the camera mode below 6 nothing happens; in mode 6 the stage becomes 6, `V:4A95D` = 5 and `V:4A86C` = 0, and each later pass decrements `V:4A95D` and, at 0, sets the cockpit-view request (bit 1 of `V:4A97E`) and the camera mode 0 (the cockpit appears 5 passes after the arrival).

**The mission approach (stages 7 and 8, camera modes 2 and 3).** The briefing state `0x12764` calls the target choice `0x2DA7C`: `V:4A967` = 1, `V:4AA72` = 0, stage 7. It walks the sub-list of the mission (`V:4A7F2`) in order and remembers every entry whose record is valid and has the class nibble 1; it stops at the first one that is *counted* (W0 bit 3 clear). With a record found the target is its position with the camera's height reduced by a quarter, `V:4A967` = `0xFF`; with none the debug word `0x3E` is stored and the target is the constant point (`0x10C00000`, `0x5000000`, `0x11400000`). [V: 300 random sub-lists and record sets, 0 differences.] Stage 7 (after the state `0x12780` set the mode 2): a non-zero `V:4A967` that is not negative sends the camera straight to the arrival; otherwise it steps with shift 14 above `0x800000` and shift 12 down to `0x100000` (or at once when the target's y is `0xFFF00000` or higher), then sets the mode 3, the stage 8 and the map target `0x1D3A4`; stage 8 steps with 14 and 12 on the same thresholds and sets the mode 4. The distance to the first hostile at which the briefing's camera arrives is `0x100000` position units (about 0.25 cell).

**Keys by mode**: note 08 6.4 (the key table is unchanged by this section).

### 6.3 The fixed camera (SHIFT and KP-ENTER, `0x1E3EE`)

The key sets the camera position from the start-location index `V:4A900` (0 to 4 in play) and nothing else: no camera state is created. Index 0 to 2 (the three airfield starts) use the table `0x47B30`, six words each: cell x, cell z, the index k into the table of fine offsets `0x206F2` (pairs of words), height h, then two offsets in 256-unit steps: position x = cell x * 2^22 + (fine x[k] + offset x) * 256, z likewise, y = h * 256. Index 3 and 4 (the carrier starts) use the carrier record named by the table `0x47ADE` (the first word of the entry; **the record index is that word with bit 15 removed**, `0x800E` = record 14, `0x800F` = record 15): the point (-36, 47, -48) model units in that record's frame when its class is `0x20`, else (0, 20, -106) (the frame transform of `0x1D478`). The three entries of `0x47B30` are disk data and are not listed (the cells are within a few cells of each other, the height h is the same in all three, k differs; `tables/ai.bin`, the camera-point sub-table, FNV-1a-32 of its 3 * 12 bytes, little-endian words, `c7edde9b`; the three records lie 16 bytes apart in the image, the index is shifted left by 4, and the two words between records are not read; extractor 27) [R]. The position goes to `V:4ACF6` and the view offsets `V:4ACEA` as in 6.2; `V:4A85E` = `0xFF` (the next aim snaps) and bit 2 of `V:4AA54` (sound request) are set; `V:4A86D` is **cleared**, which stops the camera routine from moving the camera (6.1); in the cockpit view the key then enters the external view as KP-ENTER does (the view toggle code, `V:4A86C` = 1, `V:4A865` = 0, `V:4A97E` bit 1, `V:4A8EB` = `0xFF`), in an external view it clears `V:4A85E` again. The external view's aim (note 03) then turns the camera to the viewed record every frame, so the fixed camera is a tower at the start location looking at the aircraft. The next ring key sets `V:4A86D` again and the camera returns to the ring [V: `camera_check.py`, 200 cases over the five indices, random carrier records and both view kinds, camera position, offsets and flags: 0 differences].

### 6.3a The flight step of the classes `0x30` to `0x32` (attitude and position)

These classes (seat, parachute, pod) take special branches in the flight step `0x2A2A2` (index not 0) [R, read from `0x2A4C2`-`0x2A570` and `0x31AE0`; the seat's velocity law is V in 6.4]:

* **Attitude step** (`0x31AE0`, called for these classes even when the hit bit is set, for other classes only without it): with the counter `+0x4C` above 0 and the game not paused, the roll word is brought to 0 (in an external view at once; in the cockpit view by 2,000 units per step, through 0 and 28800 as a circle), the pitch and heading follow the **direction of the velocity** (`+0x3E`, `+0x42`, `+0x46`) through the aim routine of note 03 with the turn limit 2,000 per step (the first call snaps when `V:4A966` and `V:4A85E` are both 0, which sets `V:4A85E`); the matrices are rebuilt from the three angles. With the counter 0 or below (the first 20 steps after the spawn) or paused, the stored angles are only rebuilt into the matrices.
* **Position**: the displacement is the velocity. Height: `+0x18` + `+0x42`; when the sum is not above 0 the hit bit `0x8000` is set and the height becomes 0. **A record with the hit bit set does not move any further** (the rest of the position update and the cell bookkeeping are skipped for the step) [V: `seat_check.py` part 5, x and z constant from the landing pass]. Otherwise x and z get `+0x3E` and `+0x46` added with the clamp of the other classes (a negative x is not clamped and clears z first; note 05).
* The **cell bookkeeping** (cell words, fine words, `+0x10`, sub-cell, neighbour and the display requests) is the common block that every record runs after its position update (note 05 item h); for these classes it runs from the position above, so the seat's cell words are what the view setup copies into `V:4AA04`/`V:4AA06` while it is viewed (6.4).

### 6.4 The ejection

**The key.** SHIFT and E (not paused) toggles `V:4A863`, sets the lamp counter and **ORs** 10 into the low nibble of the player's `+0x7C`: the new value is the old nibble ORed with `0x0A`. Without SHIFT the key does nothing [R, note 08 11.2]. **Only at the nibble 0**: a press while the low nibble is not 0 is ignored whole (6.4a).

**The nibble cycle** [V: `seat_check.py` part 2, the original's world update step by step, and the emulator capture below]. The player step advances the nibble once per pass: 10, 9, 8, 7, then 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 and **it stays at 6** while W2 bit `0x0020` (the release) is clear. A value above 6 counts down by one but 7 goes to 1, not 6. With the release bit set the step sets the nibble to 0 and clears the release bit in the same pass. A second key press is **ignored** (6.4a); the cases below that OR into a non-zero nibble are the world update on a poked nibble, which no key reaches. The key handler runs in the frame routine **before** the world update, and the player's step then moves the nibble once more before the seat routine `0x26F60` looks at it in the same pass. That routine treats a value of **14 or more** (after the player's step) by clearing the nibble and spawning the **parachute** (`0x2758E`, class `0x31`) instead of the seat. A value of 15 before the step becomes 14 and gives the parachute; a value of 14 becomes 13 and gives nothing. A value of 15 comes from an OR with 10 at the nibble 7 (7 or 10 = 15), 14 at 4 or 6, 10 or 11 at 8 to 10 and 11 or 10 at 1 to 3, **but only as arithmetic of the step on a nibble that was set by something other than the key**: the key handler does not run at a non-zero nibble (6.4a), so the second press of the old reading never happens; after the seat has spawned the latch blocks everything [V: `seat_check.py` part 6: the nibble set to 0x0A, 0x0B, 0x0E, 0x0F, 0x09, 0x07 before one world update: only 0x0F spawns the parachute (class `0x31`, latch `V:4A97A` = 0x200, nibble 0); 0x0E leaves 13 and no record; and `part 2c`, the OR at the nibble 7 in the cycle, called as a body without the key handler's gate]. SHIFT-F in mode 6 is in the same gated chain (6.4a): it ORs `0x0F` only at the nibble 0.

| nibble seen after the player's step | effect (record 1 stands for the first inactive of 1 to 3) |
| --- | --- |
| 9 | cockpit view: the canopy sound request `0x26FFE` |
| 1 | sound request `V:4A922` = `0x3F`, `V:4A921` = `0x78`, `V:4A870` = `0xFF` |
| 3 | **the seat spawns** (`0x27522`) if the latch is clear |
| 14 or more | the parachute spawns (above); not reachable from the cycle itself |

The class `0x31` record is also created by SHIFT and F in mode 6 (the rescue operation): the handler `0x1EE4E` ORs `0x0F` into the nibble when `V:4A97A` is clear and one of the records 1 to 3 is free, which the same routine turns into the parachute. The parachute differs from the seat only in the sign of its thrust (down), its offset (0, -3, 0) model units and its template (model id 11); its step takes the view only through the negative-counter path (below).

**The spawn** [V: `seat_check.py` part 3, 60 seat and parachute spawns called directly against a model, record fields, velocity, position, descriptor and latch, 0 differences; the attitude words are composed by the routines of note 03 and not compared]. The new record is a copy of the player's first `0xA4` bytes with W0 bit `0x8000` cleared; class `0x30`; the descriptor slot gets template 2 (the cone object; the parachute template 11); the angle rates and the AI flags `+0x64` are cleared; W2 bit `0x0004` is cleared; the position is the player's plus the offset (0, 3, 20) model units rotated by the player's matrix (rows at `+0x92`, `+0x98`, `+0x9E`, each product sum shifted right by 6); W0 gets `0x10C0`, `0x0100` and `0x0002`; `+0x26` = `0x14`, `+0x76` = 0, `+0x2C` = `0xFFFF` (no destination), `+0x38` = the player's; if the player is on the ground (W2 bit `0x0080`) the seat's vertical velocity is cleared and the ground bit dropped; the velocity is the player's plus a thrust of length `0x3C0` along the player's up axis (the matrix words `+0x94`, `+0x9A`, `+0xA0`, each shifted right by 2 and normalised); `+0x4C` = -20 (the parachute and the pod the same); the latch `V:4AA7A` (parachute: `V:4A97A`) = the record offset; `V:4A910` = `0x8C`. In the cockpit view the routine also switches the view to the seat (`0x1EC14`: viewed record = the seat, notice `0xFE`, lock cleared, the view requests of note 05 17.10).

**The view switch of the seat's step** leaves the cockpit view selected: with `V:4A83D` = 0 the view byte stays 0 and the viewed record becomes the seat; in an external view the byte is replaced by the saved view byte `V:4A8EB` (the external view stays, or drops to level 1 from level 2 in the run); the ring direction `V:4A96A` is set to 4 (behind) by the second routine `0x1E4D2` and `V:4A8EE` = 3; a class `0x31` or `0x32` record does not switch while its counter is 0 or more (the class test is made on that path), **but it can switch while its counter is negative** (the first 20 steps after the spawn): the negative-counter path goes to the common height test without a class test, and the switch point is reached when the player is crashed or dying (W0 `0x0600`), when the record's height is below `0x2000`, or when the counter is `0x14` or more [R, and V: `seat_check.py` part 1 models it for every class]; a spawned parachute whose height is above `0x2000` and whose player is not crashed does not switch in those 20 steps [V: `seat_check.py` part 7, six cases at counter 25].

**The seat's step** (`0x27BFA`, once per pass for the classes `0x30` to `0x32`; not in a pause) [V: `seat_check.py` part 1, 700 random records, velocities, counters, hit and dying bits, request bytes: the whole variable block against the model, 0 differences]. The step has three branches by the record's state:

| state | rule |
| --- | --- |
| W0 bit `0x0400` (dying) | the player's W2 gets bit `0x0020` (**the release**); the seat's W0 is cleared whole (the record is free); `V:4A910` = `0x0C`; the step returns 0 (no flight step) |
| W0 bit `0x8000` (landed) | the counter `+0x4C` is set to 4 if it is 5 or more, else decremented, until it reaches 0; at 0 or below, for class `0x30` and with no request pending (`V:4A8E2` = 0), **request 2** is made: `V:4A8E2` = 2, `V:4A8E4` = 4; if vx is below `0xC0` it gets `0xC` added (no clamp: it can end up to `0xCB`); vy is set to 0; vz moves one toward 0, and from 0 it becomes +1 (a positive vz loses 1, anything else gains 1) |
| flying | the counter counts up by 1 (from -20); a damping shift d is chosen: 6 while the counter was negative, 2 from 0, 3 from the counter 3 (**every class**: only the view-switch rules below for a non-negative counter are for class `0x30` alone). The damping threshold is `0xF0` minus the counter (a counter of -20 gives `0x104`, a larger threshold). A **view-switch point** is passed in these cases: for a class `0x30` seat at the counter 3 or more, when the player's record is not valid, or is valid and has W0 bit `0x0004` set while the counter is below 5; with the counter negative or the player valid without that bit, when the player is crashed or dying (W0 `0x0600`) or the seat's height is below `0x2000`, or the counter is `0x14` or more. At the point, if the camera-update flag `V:4A86D` is set and the seat is not the viewed record, the view is switched to it (`0x1EC14`, `V:4A891` = 0, `0x1E4D2`). Horizontal velocity: when the larger of |vx| and |vz| is not below `0xF0` minus the counter (0 when negative) both are reduced by their own value shifted right by d; vertical: from counter 3 a positive vy drops by `0x3C0`; otherwise vy drops by `0x3C` down to `-0x300` and then converges on it (halving the distance to it); the speed word `+0x6E` is the largest |component| shifted right by 2 |

The flight step does the rest (motion from the velocity, the attitude step `0x31AE0` for class nibble 3, no collision test against the ground: **the seat lands when its y word reaches 0 or below**, which sets the hit bit `0x8000`; the seat has no other landing test). A class `0x30` record that is hit by weapons follows the AI-style kill of section 5 (crashed bit, dying), which frees it and releases the player.

**What ends the ejection.** Two things, both read from the seat's step: the seat landing (request 2, below) and the seat being destroyed (dying: the release bit). The release bit is the only writer of W2 bit `0x0020` in the whole image (`0x27DAA`), so **the nibble holds at 6 for as long as the seat flies and is not destroyed**.

**Request 2 and the end** [V: emulator capture, below]. The request byte `V:4A8E2` = 2 comes with the countdown `V:4A8E4` = 4, which the flow dispatcher (`0x11748`) decrements once per pass; in the first pass in which it is below 0, **six passes after the request**, the dispatcher acts (note 08 5.11 says "below 0": the same rule): the end code `V:4A850` becomes `0xF0`, `V:4A87D` = 1, the flight stops (`V:4A984` bit `0x40` clears) and the front end returns to the menu with the bail-out end of note 08 5.13; the log counts it once through the same path as any end code. The crash hand-over of note 05 17.17 tests the nibble (at `0x12F18`); **but the crash itself clears the nibble** (the capture below: an aircraft that hits the sea with the nibble at 4 and the seat flying has the nibble 0 in the crash pass), so **the hand-over is not skipped by an ejection that is under way: it runs as for any crash** (end code 4, the 40-pass hold, the allowance down by one, the restart) and the seat is freed at the restart [V: `followup-combat-crash-after-eject.txt`, 6.5].

**The seat view.** While the seat is the viewed record (`V:4A994` = its index): the cockpit view draws the seat's frame (the head triples of the class `0x30` table at `0x323DC`, note 06 view rules), the HUD step 0 with offset 800 and window row 167 (note 06 item 2b), and the camera cell variables `V:4AA04`, `V:4AA06` and the sub-cell byte `V:4A909` come from the **seat record** (`0x1F5E4`), not from the player. The milestone-3 rig divergence class "seat-viewed" (19 of 3,000 envelope sequences and 4 adversarial ones in `tests/rig/test_flight_diff.c`) is exactly this: the flight step of the original tests the new cell of every stepped record against the **viewed** cell `V:4AA04/06` (within 2 cells: the neighbour index `+0x0B` and the display request), and the world update runs before the camera set-up of the frame, so the test uses the viewed record's cell of the **previous** pass. The port's flight step used the player's cell. Rule for Task 5: keep the viewed cell as a core variable, updated after the world update from the viewed record (cockpit view) or from the camera (external view, `0x1F6E8`), and give the flight step that variable. With the player viewed the two readings are the same. [R for the lag; V for the divergence classes (milestone 3 report).]

### 6.4a What gates the ejection key [R from the handler `0x1DAA0`; V: reference emulator, three runs of `airborne.vasnap`, one video frame per sample, 2026-10-15]

**R.** The key E (raw `0x12`) is tested in the first chain of the flight keys (`0x1DB54` to `0x1DC08`), whose entry at `0x1DB2E` reads the low nibble of the player's gear byte `V:4B2B8` and goes to the second chain (`0x1DC0C`: RETURN, the head and view keys, the F-keys, the brackets, ESC) when it is not 0. The other tests in front of the chain are the camera mode `V:4A966` = 0, the game mode not 2, and `V:4A903` not 1 or 2 (note 08 11.1). Behind the chain's compare for E the body (`0x1DE52`) tests the shift flag `V:4A930` (no flag: nothing) and the pause byte `V:4A866` (paused: nothing); then it sets the lamp counter, toggles `V:4A863`, sets bit 5 of `V:4AA52` and ORs `0x0A` into `V:4B2B8`. **Nothing else gates the key: not the gear state, not the latch `V:4A85B`** (the latch only decides whether the press also goes into the typed-character mailbox, which happens after the chain) **and not the gear sound** (the countdown `V:4A93D` is set by the G body and read by the sound routine; the E body does not look at it). The same entry gates every key of the first chain: SPACE, the four cursor keys, `,` `.`, `=` `-`, `\` and BACKSPACE, E, R, G, A, F (and SHIFT-F), C, J, H, S, T, Y and M. The whole ejection (the nibble stays 6 while the seat flies) therefore leaves those keys dead, among them G, the brake, the hook, the flares and the map; the release codes, the F-keys, RETURN, the head and view keys and the brackets act as before.

**V.** (1) A SHIFT-E from nibble 0: the nibble reads 10 three frames after the press and the lamp `V:4A863` is 1. (2) A second SHIFT-E with the nibble at 6 (the seat flying, 60 frames after the first, 12 samples afterwards): the nibble stays 6, the lamp stays 1, record 1 stays the seat (W0 `0x10CA`), no record is added. (3) The gear key and then SHIFT-E in the same short window: the gear byte reads `0x8A` for one frame (bit 7 set by the G body, the nibble 10 by the E body) and `0x90` in the next: **the step that moves the retract progress stores the byte with the low nibble 0** (note 05 17.18 c), so a press of E while the gear is travelling (up to six passes after the gear command) toggles the lamp and starts no cycle. That is what a debug flight showed as "the press did not set the nibble" with the gear sound running: the gear sound runs for the same few passes as the travel. The key was handled; the nibble was wiped by the player step of the same pass, and `V:4A863` had toggled. The port does the same (`flight.c` `gear_counters`, which stores the progress with the low nibble cleared) and ignores the key at a non-zero nibble (`game.c` `chain_open`; tests `tests/test_eject.c`, `tests/test_game_sound.c`).

### 6.5 Captures and what the port does

**Captures** (reference emulator, `airborne.vasnap`, cockpit view, SHIFT and E; scripts `combat-seat-eject-cockpit.txt` (250 samples, 4 video frames apart, which ends before the landing) and `combat-seat-eject-long.txt` (330 samples, 30 video frames apart; a pass takes 4 to 6 frames in this flight, so consecutive samples are 3 to 7 passes apart), generator `gen-combat-seat.py`; logs git-ignored) [V, one run each]:

| event | pass (step counter `V:4A992`) | what the log shows |
| --- | --- | --- |
| key | before 233 | the nibble reads 9 at pass 233 |
| nibble 9, 8, 7 | 233, 234, 235 | player W0 `0x11C8`, W2 `0x2000` unchanged |
| nibble 1, 2 | 236, 237 | |
| nibble 3, **seat spawns** | 238 | record 1: W0 `0x10CA`, class `0x30`, counter -19, y = 627,502 units (the player's height; vertical speed +9,247); notice `V:4A968` = `0xFE`; viewed record `V:4A994` = 1; the player's W0 gains `0x4000` one pass later |
| nibble 4, 5, 6 | 239, 240, 241 | the hold: the nibble stays 6 for the rest of the log |
| flight of the seat | 238 to 1337 | the counter rises by one per pass; y climbs to about 822,000 (pass 269) and then falls at the terminal rate of 769 units per pass (the rule of 6.4: vy converges on -0x300 = -768 plus the damping), reaching 0 at pass 1337; W0 toggles bit `0x0010` (the pending bit of the display rebuild); the player's aircraft stays valid and active (W0 `0x51C8` to `0x51D0`) and flies on |
| seat lands | the hit bit is set in the step of **pass 1335** (y falls from 2,914 at pass 1331 by 769 per pass: 2,145, 1,376, 607, then -162 at pass 1335) | the sample at pass 1337 reads W0 `0x90DA`, y 0, counter 3 (4 at pass 1336); at pass 1344 W0 `0x90CA`, counter 0 |
| **request 2** | pass 1340 (the counter reaches 0 five passes after the landing pass; the sample at 1344 reads `V:4A8E2` = 2 and `V:4A8E4` = 0) | the request byte `V:4A8E4` is set to 4 with the request and counted down once per pass by the dispatcher |
| end code | pass 1346: `V:4A850` = `0xF0`, `V:4A87D` = 1 | **six passes after the request**: the dispatcher decrements `V:4A8E4` at the end of each pass (4 to 0 in four passes, then 0 to -1) and acts when it is below 0; this is the same rule as note 08 5.11 ("below 0"); `V:4A984` bit `0x40` clears and the pass counter restarts a few passes later |
| menu | the next samples | `V:4A83D` = 1 and the notice `0xFE` in the state of the front end |

The rig fixes the pass-exact rule: a seat with the hit bit set steps through the counter 4, 3, 2, 1, 0 in the five passes after the landing pass and makes **request 2 in the fifth of them** (`seat_check.py` part 5: hit bit at pass 2, request at pass 7), and the capture agrees to the pass: landing pass 1335 (from the heights), counter 3 at 1337, request at 1340 (`V:4A8E4` already 0 in the sample at 1344), end code at 1346 = 1340 + 6. From the landing pass to the end code are **11 passes**. A per-frame capture of the landing (`gen-combat-seat-landing.py`, `stepuntil` on the hit bit, then every video frame) was written; it was waiting for the emulator lock at the time of writing and is not part of the evidence.

**The per-frame capture of the first passes** [V: `followup-combat-eject-frames.txt`, generator `gen-followup-combat.py eject-cockpit`: `airborne.vasnap`, cockpit view, SHIFT and E, then one sample and one frame dump per video frame for 150 frames (note 01a)]. The passes are 5 to 6 video frames long up to the spawn; the pass that spawns the seat and the three after it are 9 to 14 frames long. Per sample: the nibble reads **10** in the frame in which the key has been taken (the key handler runs before the world update), **9** in the next frame (the player's step of the same pass), then 8 (the next pass), 7, 1, 2 and **3 in the sixth pass**, in which the seat spawns **one frame after the nibble is seen** (record 1: W0 `0x10CA`, class `0x30`, counter -19, height 617,556 and vertical speed +9,375, the latch `V:4AA7A` = `0x200`, the notice `V:4A968` = `0xFE`, the viewed record `V:4A994` = 1, `V:4A8EE` and `V:4A8EF` = 3); ten frames later the notice returns to 0 and the player's W0 gains `0x4000`; the next pass the nibble is 4, then 5, 6 (the hold), the window row `V:4AA3C` = `0xA7` with `V:4AA3E` = `0x32` from the pass after the spawn. **What the screen shows in the five passes before the seat (the nibbles 9, 8, 7, 1 and 2)**: the cockpit picture does not change except for the HUD digits and one lamp. The picture is renewed at the end of each pass (the frame dumps change only in those frames: the world rows 0 to 144 differ by 8 to 32 pixels, the HUD digits; the panel rows by up to 12 pixels, the readouts); **the EJECT lamp of the panel (the label at the left, rows 156 to 163, columns 18 to 63 of the game area) alternates between dark red (red level 106 of 255) and its normal green on successive pictures**: the frame dumps show it lit at dump 11 (the key sample is 0: the nibble reads 10 at sample 4 and 9 at sample 5, so the first lit dump is 7 frames after the nibble-10 sample, the flip at the end of the key's own pass), unlit at 18, lit at 23, unlit at 28, lit at 33. In pass terms (the key handler runs before the world update and the panel draw, as in note 08's pass order): **the lamp is lit in the pictures drawn by the key's own pass and every second pass after it (offsets 0, 2, 4, 6) and unlit at 1, 3, 5**, which is note 06's rule (the timer is set to 8 by the key and decremented by each draw; lit when the decremented value is odd: the key pass's draw gives 7, lit); the picture of a pass is the one that pass draws and the flip shows it at the pass's end; there is **no canopy picture, no change of the sky or the horizon and no view change** in those passes (the 3D window is identical frame for frame). The seat's view appears with a delay: **the first picture with the seat's view is the one finished 15 frames after the spawn** (the draw of the spawn pass): the horizon and sea seen from above and behind the aircraft with the black shape of the aircraft's fuselage (the grey edge of the nose and wings) in the middle of the window, and **the cockpit panel still drawn at the bottom** (one picture, as the panel counter `V:4A8EE` is 3); the next picture, 13 frames later, shows the seat's view **without the panel** (the world fills the rows down to 166 and the panel rows are black: the HUD step 0 and window row 167 of note 06 item 2b); from there the pictures change every 8 to 9 frames (the aircraft shrinking below the seat). The picture count is therefore: five pictures of the cockpit with the flashing lamp, one picture of the seat with the old panel, then the seat's picture without it [rule for the port: the seat's view is drawn from the pass of the spawn, with the cockpit panel for that one picture and without it from the next; the five passes before show the unchanged cockpit with the flashing lamp].

**The ejection from an external view, and the seat's "level 2 to level 1"** [V: `followup-combat-eject-frames-ext.txt` (150 frames, one sample and one frame dump each) and `followup-combat-eject-frames-ext-long.txt` (330 samples of 4 frames, a frame dump every 60 frames), `gen-followup-combat.py eject-external` and `eject-external-long`: `airborne.vasnap`, KP-ENTER, the level byte `V:4A83D` poked to 2 (the level the keypad's distance keys select; before the poke it read `0xFF`, the KP-ENTER's own), SHIFT and E]. The nibble cycle and the spawn are the same as in the cockpit (the nibble 9 in the first pass, 3 and the spawn in the sixth, 6 for the hold; record 1 W0 `0x10CA`, class `0x30`, counter -19 at pass 243 in the long run), but **the spawn does not switch the view**: `V:4A83D` stays 2 and the viewed record `V:4A994` stays 0 (the player; the camera keeps following the aircraft, the seat is a small object above it in the chase picture) for **39 passes** (pass 243 to 281), while the seat's counter runs from -19 up. **In pass 282, when the seat's counter reaches `0x14` (20), the view switches**: `V:4A83D` becomes **`0xFF`**, the saved view byte `V:4A8EB` (it reads `0xFF` throughout: every KP-ENTER pass writes it, and the keypad's level keys do not touch it), the viewed record `V:4A994` = 1, the notice `V:4A968` = `0xFE` for three samples, `V:4A8EE` = 3 (counting down 2, 1, 0 in the next passes) and `V:4A8EF` becoming `0xFF` three passes later; the window row `V:4AA3C` stays `0xA7` and `V:4A96A` = 4 (the ring direction behind). So **the "level 2 to level 1" of the earlier text is: the external view returns to the level byte `0xFF`, the chase distance of the KP-ENTER view, whatever level it had** (the level 2 here); the view stays external (`V:4A83D` non-zero). The picture after the switch (frame dumps 105 and 120, 36 and 60 frames later) is the seat seen from behind and above in the external chase view: **the seat's model is drawn as an open parachute canopy with its cords** (the cone object's largest list for the counter 5 or more, 11.5) over the sea, with the speed readout at 18 knots, the altitude text line below and no panel; before the switch the picture is the ordinary external view of the aircraft (the seat appears as a small object above it from the first pass after the spawn). The panel counters count down once per pass at each stage (3, 2, 1, 0 over the next three passes; the periodic writer of 12.1 sets them to 3 again at every 32nd pass, seen at passes 241, 273, 305, 337 and 369).

**The parachute in flight** [V: `followup-combat-parachute.txt`, `gen-followup-combat.py parachute`: `airborne.vasnap`, the low nibble of the player's gear byte poked to 15, 300 samples of 12 video frames]: the nibble is cleared and record 1 is the **parachute**: class `0x31`, W0 `0x10CA` (then `0x50CA`, the pending bit `0x0010` toggling as for the seat), counter -18 in the first sample (it starts at -20 and counts up by one per pass: 18 in pass 270, 113 in pass 365, 580 in pass 832), the latch `V:4A97A` = `0x200` (and `V:4AA7A` stays 0, the seat's latch), **no view switch** (`V:4A83D` = 0 and `V:4A994` = 0 for the 600 passes: the cockpit view keeps the player), no request and no end code. Its height rises from 586,882 (pass 234) to about 739,000 (pass 262 to 270: the spawn velocity of the player plus the thrust, damped by the rules of the seat's step) and then **falls by 769 position units per pass** (vertical velocity -774, then -769 for ever: the seat's terminal rate of 6.4, since the classes `0x30` to `0x32` share the step), reaching 307,227 at pass 832 without landing in the capture; the speed word `+0x6E` is `0xC0` (the largest component shifted right by 2). So the step rules of 6.4 hold for the parachute in the emulator (and the rig's 6,000 cases of the parachute list in `seat_check.py`); the parachute is not drawn in the cockpit view.

**The crash with the seat in flight, in the emulator** [V: `followup-combat-crash-after-eject.txt`: `dive-precrash.vasnap` with the height poked to 36,808 position units (about 8 passes above the sea; the descent is 4,376 per pass), SHIFT and E, one sample per 4 frames, frame dumps every 8 samples; the first version of the crash capture, `followup-combat-batch-b.txt` part `crashnibble` from the unmodified snapshot, crashed with the nibble at 8, before the seat]. The nibble runs 9, 8, 7, 1, 2 and **3 in the sixth pass (pass 414): the seat spawns** (record 1: W0 `0x10CA`, class `0x30`, `V:4AA7A` = `0x200`, the viewed record 1); the nibble reads 4 in the next pass; **in pass 416 the aircraft reaches the floor** (height 1,800): W0 `0xD3C8` (the hit bit `0x8000`, the radar bit, `0x1000`, the crashed bit `0x0200`), W2 `0x2080` (the ground bit), B20 `0x41`, **the nibble reads 0**, the end code `V:4A850` = **4**, `V:4A87D` = 1; in the next pass **`V:4A951` = 40** (the crash hold) and it counts down to 0 over the next 22 samples (88 frames; two per 4-frame sample); **the allowance `V:4A94F` falls from 5 to 4**; during the hold the seat keeps flying (W0 `0x10CA`, class `0x30`, the viewed record 1: the picture follows the seat); at the end of the hold the seat record is freed (W0 `0x0010`, class 0) in the pass in which the player's record is restarted on the ground (W0 `0x91D8`, then `0x11C8`, height 1,800, ground bit) and the viewed record returns to the player. There is no request 2 and no end code `0xF0`: **the ejection does not save the aircraft's crash penalty**. The capture from the unmodified dive (the crash in the third pass after the key, with the nibble at 8 in the pass before) shows the same crash flow: nibble 0 in the crash pass, end code 4, the hold from 40 to 0, the allowance 5 to 4 and the restart on the ground.

**A destroyed seat, in the emulator** [V: `followup-combat-batch-c.txt` part `seatshot` (the first version: the crashed bit `0x0200` poked into the seat's W0 in flight, 160 samples of 2 frames) and `followup-combat-batch-e.txt` part `seatshot` (the dying bit `0x0400` poked: W0 `0x14CA`)]. **A seat that only carries the crashed bit is not touched by it**: it flew on for 100 passes (counter 2 to 20, the pending bit toggling, the viewed record still 1): for a class `0x30` record nothing converts the crashed bit, so a seat that a tracer marks crashed stays alive (the hit's other marks, B20 bit 1 and the state byte, were not poked). **A seat with the dying bit is freed at once** (6.4's first row): in the next pass its W0 is `0x0010` (the record is free: the valid bit gone), the viewed record `V:4A994` returns to the player (0) in the same pass, **the player's W2 gets bit `0x0020` (the release) in that pass**, the nibble goes on 5, 6 and is **cleared to 0 two passes later, with the release bit cleared in the same pass** (W2 `0x2020` for 11 frames, then `0x2000`, nibble 0), the display request `V:4A910` = `0xFF` rebuilds the lists, the latch `V:4AA7A` keeps `0x200` (no second ejection) and **no request and no end code** (`V:4A8E2`, `V:4A850` stay 0): the player's aircraft flies on, the hold is released, exactly as 6.4 says. The release bit (W2 `0x0020`) therefore does appear when the seat is destroyed. The captured sequence has no crash hand-over: the player's aircraft was not lost. An earlier attempt to capture the aircraft **crashing during the hold** (pokes of height and pitch inside the hold) failed: the aircraft did not reach the ground (the velocity is recomputed from the attitude every pass). The capture below starts the dive instead from a higher poked height, so that the seat has spawned before the crash; it settles the hand-over with an ejection nibble, which note 05 17.17 rule 8 had from the rig only.

**What the port does (C, for Task 5).**
1. The nibble cycle, the seat and parachute spawns (including the double-press and SHIFT-F in mode 6 cases), the seat step with its three branches and the 4-pass landing counter are implemented from 6.4 and tested against `seat_check.py`'s cases.
2. The ejection **ends the flight** with request 2 (end code `0xF0`, the bail-out end of note 08 5.13) a few passes after the seat lands; the nibble holds at 6 until then; the aircraft keeps being simulated and may crash or be shot meanwhile; **a crash during the ejection clears the nibble and hands over like any crash** (end code 4, the 40-pass hold with the seat still flying, the allowance down by one, the restart that frees the seat; no request 2, no end code `0xF0`) [V, 6.5]. Review Focus 3: the mission script's end code and the ejection's request 2 share the byte `V:4A8E2` / `V:4A850`; the request is made only when `V:4A8E2` is 0, and the log is written by the single end path, so a script end code that arrives while the seat is flying wins by being first and the later request is ignored.
3. The viewed cell for the flight step's neighbour test is the viewed record's cell, one pass late (6.4, seat view).
4. The camera: in a flight the camera mode is 0; the front-end approach uses the mover of 6.2 (or the 188-step substitute of note 08 6.4); the fixed camera is a position set from the table of 6.3 and a cleared update flag.

## 7. Weapons: the gun and the missiles

This section is the firing side: the keys, the stores, the gun and the tracer pool that the gun, the flares and the chaff share, and the launch of a missile. Section 8 is what a missile does after the launch, the hit tests and the log counters. Section 9 takes the countermeasures (their keys, counts and the effect on a missile) and section 10 the damage. Time is the pass of the world update (`0x26AF8`), one per frame, about 8 per second (note 05 section 14); "per step" below is per pass. All the rig counts are from `tools/m68k-rig/tests/lists_model/weapon_check.py` (see Verified by); addresses are in the layout of note 00.

### 7.1 The keys and the state they set

| key | body | what it does | tag |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SPACE press | `0x1DF48` (ignored with the shift flag) then `0x1F07A` | nothing unless the low nibble of the gear byte `E+0x7C` is 0 (`E` = record 0, `0x4B23C`; the gear byte is `V:4B2B8`; nibble 0 = gear up, not moving). Then `V:4AA52` bit 2 is set (a key-click event for the display) and the high nibble of the weapon selector `E+0x63` decides: 0 nothing; 1 (gun) `V:4A97E` bit 3 is set (the trigger is held); 2 or 3 (Sidewinder, AMRAAM) the launch request `V:4A872` = 1. In mode `0x7D` the press does nothing when `V:4A852` is non-negative and, when it is negative (the training sequencer's turn), sets W2 bit `0x0800` of record 4 (the instructor's own fire) instead. | R |
| SPACE release | `0x1DF58` -> `0x1F0D0` | `V:4AA52` bit 3 set, `V:4A97E` bit 3 cleared | R |
| RETURN | `0x1E8A6` (modes other than 2 and `0x7D`) | the weapon select: needs the same gear nibble 0; `V:4AA52` bit 4; the high nibble of `E+0x63` goes down by one step (3 -> 2 -> 1 -> 0) and from 0 back to 3, i.e. AMRAAM, Sidewinder, gun, none, AMRAAM; the low nibble (radar range) is kept; the weapon readout is asked to redraw (`V:4A8FB` = `V:4A8FC` = 3) and the fire prompt state `V:4A96C` is cleared. In mode 2, and in mode `0x7D` with `V:4A852` negative, the key sets W2 bit `0x0800` of record 0 (the sequencer's key); in mode `0x7D` otherwise it posts `0x401F` | R |
| the world update | `0x2852E` (last act of every pass) | clears the three launch requests `V:4A872`, `V:4A873`, `V:4A874`: **a launch request is valid for the pass in which it was made only** | V (rig, 600 gun cases and the staged launches) |

The weapon records are the player's three missile records, record 1 to 3 (`0x4B43C`, `0x4B63C`, `0x4B83C`). The stores live in the player record: the missile stock `E+0x5F` (high nibble Sidewinders, low nibble AMRAAMs; the rearm routine `0x1F11E` writes `0x24`, two Sidewinders and four AMRAAMs; the placement of the AI writes `0x44`), the gun rounds `E+0x60` (word, 500 after a rearm), the burst reservoir `E+0x7D` (high nibble) and the reload timer `E+0x3B` (7.3). The rearm routine is reached only from the landing (`0x170A6`); it also writes the flare and chaff counts (`V:4A905`, `V:4A904` = 16) and zeroes records 1 to 3. The mission start writes the stores of the placed records (section 2) and, for the player, the flight start of note 05 section 15.

### 7.2 The tracer pool

Ten usable slots of a 20-slot pool at `0x4AD2A`, 64 bytes each. **Only the even slots are ever used** (the routine steps its index by two; the draw scan `0x2AD1A` and the decoy lookup `0x282D0` index the whole pool). The slot table:

| offset | size | meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `+0x00`, `+0x04`, `+0x08` | long | position x, y, z in position units; x and z are the part inside the cell (0 to `0x3FFFFF`, wrapped below), y is absolute |
| `+0x0C`, `+0x10`, `+0x14` | long | the step vector: the displacement per pass, eight times the sub-step vector (plus the player's velocity for a flare or chaff). Gravity and drag act on it (7.4) |
| `+0x18`, `+0x1C`, `+0x20` | long | the sub-step vector, one eighth of a step; the hit test advances the tracer by it eight times per pass (8.2) |
| `+0x24` | word | 0 at creation: the first pass after creation does not move the slot (it only sets this word to 1) |
| `+0x26` | word | flags. `0x0001` active; `0x0002` stopped (it hit something or the ground: it stays for two passes, no longer moves); `0x0008` cleared by a hit; `0x0010` set by a hit (together with 2: the value `0x12`); `0x0100` the stopped slot drifts with a quarter of the player's velocity (no code sets it); `0x0200` a drawn latch cleared by the plain-tracer drawer when its pixel is not on screen; `0x0400` set at creation (`0x401`), no reader found; `0x1000` a flare (section 9); `0x2000` chaff (section 9) |
| `+0x28` | word | life: set at creation (20 for a tracer, 30 for a flare, 60 for chaff); **every slot's life word is decreased once per pass unless `V:4A875` or the pause `V:4A866` is set, active or not**; at 0 or below the slot is deactivated (bit 0 cleared) and the references `V:4A944`/`V:4A945` to it are cleared |
| `+0x2E` | word | owner: the record index of the shooter (always 0, the player; the hit test skips the owner) |
| `+0x30`, `+0x32` | word | the cell x and z of the position (0 to 127, wrapped) |

### 7.3 The gun routine `0x172DC`

Called from the three branches of the frame routine (`0x11228`, `0x11242`, `0x1126E`), once per pass. It first resets `V:4A79E` to `0xFFFFF` (the clip state of the pixel routines, note 04), then decreases the reload timer `E+0x3B` (signed byte) by one and walks the slots 0, 2, ..., 18. For every slot in turn:

1. its life word is decreased (unless the pause/`V:4A875` rule above);
2. a **request** exists when the trigger is held (`V:4A97E` bit 3 set and the pass not paused) or the countermeasure request byte `V:4A943` is non-zero;
3. with a request and the slot inactive, **at most one slot per call** is filled, and only if the burst reservoir (high nibble of `E+0x7D`) is non-zero and the rounds `E+0x60` are at least 1 (signed).
