A request that finds the slot active, or has fired already in this call, or has no reservoir or no rounds, does nothing; a call with a request whose free slot could not fire (the reservoir or rounds are short, or a shot was made already) sets the "burst running" mark when the reload timer is above 0 (below);
4. a **shot** (gun): the reservoir loses one (high nibble minus 1), the log counter `+0x3A` (gun rounds fired) gains **3**, `V:4A87D` = 1 (a log-dirty flag), the slot flags become `0x401`, the owner 0; the sound `0x1A0DC(0x1C, 0x30)` plays unless `V:4A865` (the map-view byte, which also mutes the sounds; 7.3 below) is non-zero; **if bit 5 of `V:4AC13` is set the sound is `0x1A2F0(6, 0x1E)` in the external view (`V:4A83D` non-zero) and `0x1A2F0(6, 0x16)` in the cockpit instead** (table in section 13); the rounds go down by **3** (not below 0); the weapon readout is asked to redraw (`V:4A8FB` = 3); the reload timer is set to 20 (`0x14`). The same slot is initialised (7.5) and stepped in the same call;
5. a **countermeasure** (`V:4A943` non-zero): the same reservoir and rounds conditions, the same log and reservoir cost (the countermeasures draw on the gun's rounds and reservoir, and the log counts them as rounds fired), but no change of rounds, readout or timer; sound `0x1A0DC(0x1C, 0x30)` (unless `V:4A865`); `V:4A943` = 1 makes the slot a chaff (`0x2000`, `V:4A945` = slot + 1), any other value (the key sets 2) a flare (`0x1000`, `V:4A944` = slot + 1); `V:4A943` is cleared. The initialisation differs by flag (7.5);
6. a slot that is active after the above is stepped (7.4).

At the end of the walk, if no shot was made in this call **and** the "burst running" mark is not set, the timer is set to 0 and the reservoir is refilled to 5 (high nibble). The mark is set by a shot, and by a request that found a free slot it could not use while the timer is above 0. So: **a held trigger fires one tracer per pass for five passes (the reservoir 5 to 0, 15 rounds), then waits until the timer, set to 20 by the last shot and decreased once per call, reaches 0 (the pass in which it does refills the reservoir and fires nothing), then fires five more**; **releasing the trigger refills the reservoir and clears the timer at the very next call** (a call with no request never sets the mark), so tapping the key gives one shot per press with no penalty. The 500 rounds last 167 shots (the last takes the remaining 2). The log counter reads 15 for a full burst (note 08 section 10.9 `+0x3A`).

The routine runs in every flight mode with the player's stores; the AI aircraft have no gun (their weapon is the missile, 7.6). Which weapon is selected does not matter to the routine: only the trigger bit and the countermeasure request count.

**When the frame routine calls it** [R: the frame routine `0x11124`, the calls at `0x11228`, `0x11242`, `0x1126E`; V for the pause screen by note 08 N4]. The calls are in the **flight path**, the one the gate byte `V:4A84D` selects (non-zero); with the gate 0 the frame routine takes the short path (`0x114C0`: the sound routine and one graphics call) and **neither the world update nor the gun routine runs**. State by state: **the pause screen** (`0x13B76`/`0x13BA0`, gate 0): no call; the passes before it with the pause byte `V:4A866` at 1 to 5 (gate 1): called, but the trigger is ignored and every slot's life is frozen (7.3 conditions on `V:4A866`/`V:4A875`); **the crash hold** (cockpit view; note 05 section 14 and 17.17 are right): the hold is the flip routine's loop of 40 iterations of 2 blanks that runs **inside one call after the frame routine of the hand-over pass**, so during the hold **no frame routine runs at all: no world update, no gun routine, no entity pass, no scope walk, no arbiter, no step counter** (the screen holds the last frame). Before and after it the passes are ordinary flight-path passes with the gate 1: the hand-over pass (`0x12EFE` crash branch: its world update, draw, **gun routine**, walk and arbiter all run, with one simulation step), and the seven restart passes A, B, C1 to C5 after the hold (each a full frame routine with the world update, the display lists, the draw, the **gun routine**, the walk and the arbiter; the flight simulation bit `V:4A984` bit 6 is clear in them, so the player's input conversion, speed step and warnings are skipped but the world update, the other records' steps and the gun's slot steps run); in the **external-view crash** there is no hold: the dying passes are full passes with the gun called. A new shot in these passes needs the SPACE key to be accepted by the key handler (the gear nibble `E+0x7C` low nibble must be 0, 7.1). (An earlier version of this paragraph said the gun ran every pass of the hold; it does not run during the 80-blank loop.)
   Other states: **the outcome screens, the briefing, menu, fades and credits** (gate 0): not called. The call position also varies inside the pass: in the map view (`V:4A865` non-zero, note 06; the sound routines test the same byte, which 7.3 and 9.6 call "sound off") the frame is: queues A and B and the near-ground pass are walked first (`0x1FA24`, `0x1FA36`, `0x2C0EC`, before the test of `V:4A865`), then the gun alone is called and the entity pass `0x1FBCC` is skipped (`0x11228`; the hit test walks the queue C left by the last frame that built one, and the draw handlers do not run). **The altitude gate** `0x111E0`: with the camera height `V:4AB1E` at or below `-0x8000000` (signed compare against `0xF8000000`; reachable with three presses of the `[` key) the frame jumps to `0x11274` and skips **all** of it: the three walks, the gun and the entity pass; only the arbiter, the sound and the HUD routines follow [R]; otherwise the draw scan `0x2AD18` decides: non-zero (the **highest-index active slot** passes its tests: a chaff slot, or any slot in an external view, `V:4A83D` non-zero, and the owner is no farther from the camera than the slot; 12.5) -> the gun **before** the entity pass `0x1FBCC` (`0x11242`), zero (every tracer in the cockpit view) -> the entity pass first and the gun **after** it (`0x1126E`). The consequence for a kill is in 12.5.

**The frame counter at the call** [R, V]. `V:4A992` is incremented once, at `0x114B8`, at the very end of the flight path (and not while `V:4A866` is non-zero); the world update, the damage handler of 10.4, the gun routine (the wobble of 7.5 reads its low two bits) and the arbiter all read the same value n during a pass. [V: the parity captures `combat-hit-player-even.txt` (pinned `0x00E8`: post `0x9014`) and `combat-hit-player-odd.txt` (pinned `0x00E9`: post `0xD00B`), where the pin is poked before each pass; `combat-hit-gun.txt`: the burst's log counter `+0x3A` steps 3, 6, 9, 12, 15 at the consecutive values n = `0xE9` to `0xED`, one shot per pass.]

### 7.4 The step of a slot (`0x1754C` with the hit test `0x2ADDE`)

For an active slot with life above 0 and flag bit 1 clear, in this order:

1. the **hit test** (8.2, called with the slot number). It returns 1 for a hit: the flags become `(flags & ~0x08) | 0x12` and the life 2;
2. no hit and **y below 0** (below the ground): flags `(flags & ~0x18) | 0x02`, life 2; the vertical component of the step vector shifted right by 8 is stored in `V:4AB88`; when it is non-zero the position is backed up along the path to the plane y = 0 (the tracer is moved by the horizontal step components times the height above ground divided by the vertical step component, the 16-bit signed divisions of the original; y becomes 0);
3. otherwise the cell wrap: x below 0 -> cell x - 1 (0 wraps to 127) and x + `0x400000`; x at `0x400000` or more -> cell x + 1 (127 wraps to 0) and x - `0x400000`; the same for z with the cell z;
4. a **stopped** slot (bit 1) only drifts when bit `0x0100` is set (never);
5. the drawer is called (7.7).

Inside the hit test, when no entity or landmark is hit (the fall-through), the **motion** runs: the step vector is changed (y component minus `0x100`: gravity, one 256th of a position unit-per-pass squared), a flare (`0x1000`) loses a 64th of its x and z components per pass, a chaff (`0x2000`) takes no gravity and loses a quarter of all three components per pass (a plain tracer keeps its vector but for the gravity); then, if the word `+0x24` is 0 it is set to 1 and the slot does not move, else the position gains the step vector. The vertical step component therefore decreases by 256 position units each pass over the tracer's 20 passes of life.

### 7.5 The initialiser `0x177D8` (with `0x17AFE` and the normaliser `0x29CB0`)

Called for the new slot with the request kind in the flags. Let M be the player's attitude words at `+0x92` (Q14: 1.0 = `0x4000`): the rows (`+0x92`, `+0x94`, `+0x96`), (`+0x98`, `+0x9A`, `+0x9C`), (`+0x9E`, `+0xA0`, `+0xA2`); a vector (a, b, c) given as (lateral, up, forward) maps to the world as the three row dot products.

| kind | model-frame offset (a, b, c) | life | direction (b2, c2) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| gun, F/A-18 (`E+0x62` = `0x11`) | (0, 0, 56) | 20 | (64 + w, 1024) |
| gun, any other class (the F-16) | (6, 1, 24) | 20 | (64 + w, 1024) |
| flare | (0, -8, 0) | 30 | (-64, 0) |
| chaff | (0, -8, -56) | 60 | (-64, 0) |

`w` is 3, -3, 5, 0 for the frame counter `V:4A992` low two bits 0, 1, 2, 3 (the wobble of the gun's elevation). Steps:

1. the offset vector o = (M . (a, b, c)) >> 6 per component (32-bit sums of 16-bit products, arithmetic shift) is stored in `V:4AB0A`, `V:4AB0E`, `V:4AB12`;
2. the cell words `+0x30`, `+0x32` = the player's cells (`V:4B242`, `V:4B244`); the position is x = (the player's x and `0x3FFFFF`) + o.x, y = the player's y + o.y, z likewise;
3. the direction d = (M . (0, b2, c2)) >> 4 (the lateral term is absent);
4. the length parameter is 13 for a flare, 5 for chaff, and for a tracer `0xC6` + (the player's target speed S `+0x6C` shifted right by 6, arithmetic) + the wobble (0, +16, 0, -16 for the frame counter low bits 0 to 3). The vector from the position to the aim point, (d - o) shifted right by 8 per component as 16-bit words, is scaled by the normaliser `0x29CB0` to that length. `0x29CB0` is the normaliser of note 04a (P4, `0x29C0E`) with the scale exponent starting at 3 instead of 8, so its result is 32 times as long; the result words are written to `V:4AB04..`. The tracer therefore leaves at about 8,250 position units per sub-step for S = `0x1091` (a step of 66,000 position units, 258 draw units) independent of the aircraft's own velocity;
5. step vector (`+0x0C`..) = the result words times 8, sub-step vector (`+0x18`..) = the result words, `+0x24` = 0;
6. for a flare or chaff the player's velocity longs (`+0x3E`, `+0x42`, `+0x46`) are **added** to the step vector and one eighth of them (arithmetic) to the sub-step vector, so the decoy leaves with the aircraft's velocity.

### 7.6 The missile launch (`0x275C4`, `0x275F6`, `0x27630` onwards)

The launch fills the record named by the world update: for the player the first free of records 1 to 3 in the order they are visited, when the request `V:4A872` is set (a request with no free record is dropped by `0x2852E`, 7.1); for the AI the fixed record of each aircraft (records 5, 9, 11, 13 for the aircraft in 4, 8, 10, 12; record 5 only on the request `V:4A873`, the others through the decision `0x2701A`, section 4.6 of the AI part; **no code in the image ever sets `V:4A873` or `V:4A874`**: every absolute reference was scanned (reads at `0x26D26` and `0x2702C`, clears at `0x27096`, `0x28534`, `0x2853A`; the flag block fill `0x1D35A` zeroes them; no table holds their addresses and the register-based stores near them are the one-byte toggles of `0x1EF40`), so the record 5 launch of `0x26D36` and the request branch of the decision are dead in play: the decision of 4.6 always runs its flag tests, and record 5 is only ever used by the seat routine `0x26F8E` [R]). The step, per call (A1 = the new record, A2 = the owner):

1. **player entry `0x275C4`**: `V:4A872` = 0, `V:4A86F` (the launch flag the AI reads) = 1; when `V:4AC08` bit `0x4000` (the target is inside the lock box, section 12) is set it is cleared and `V:4AC0C` bit 3 (the launch cue) set. **No lock is needed to fire.**
   **AI entry `0x275F6`**: nothing happens (the record's valid bit is cleared) when `V:4A841` (AI weapons enabled) is 0; the rate gate: with the progress `V:4A95F` m = 0 or 1 the mask is 7, 2 the mask 5, 3 or more 3; with the ECM switch `V:4A96D` on the mask is halved (3, 2, 1); the launch happens only when `V:4A992` AND the mask is 0 (one pass in 8, 4 or 4, and one in 4, 2, 2 with the ECM on: **the ECM makes the AI fire more often**);
2. the stock: the owner's range class `+0x63` high nibble 3 selects the long-range missile (stock = low nibble of `+0x5F`), anything else the Sidewinder (high nibble). A stock of 0 refuses the launch (the valid bit of the new record is cleared, nothing else);
3. if the owner is the record the camera follows (`V:4A996`): the notice `V:4A968` = `0xFB` and `V:4A84F` = 8;
4. the first `0xA4` bytes (41 longs) of the owner are **copied** to the new record, and its state byte `+0x05` is cleared. The missile therefore starts with the owner's position, velocity, speeds, attitude matrices, group (`+0x5E`), W0 flags (the player's `0x11C8` keeps the bit `0x0008`, which makes the shot a *player weapon* in the hit tests), target byte and range class;
5. class: long-range -> `+0x62` = 1 and template 1 (`0x25ECC + 0x14`); otherwise class 0 and template 0. For the **player** the log counters `+0x3E` (AMRAAM fired) or `+0x42` (Sidewinder fired) gain 1 and `V:4A87D` = 1. The template (five longs) is copied to the record's descriptor `0x2600C + 20 * (record index)`;
6. the rates `+0x56`, `+0x58`, `+0x5A` and the AI flags `+0x64` are cleared and W2 bit 2 cleared;
7. the owner's stock loses one of the chosen kind; `V:4A8FB` = `V:4A8FC` = 3 (readout redraw); the **hard point** index h is the stock left of that kind for a Sidewinder and the stock left + 2 for the AMRAAM;
8. the position is moved by the hard-point offset: the table (six entries of three words, model units) is at `0x278AA` for class `0x10` owners (the F-16) and at `0x278CE` for all others; entry h rotated by the owner's matrix (the same row dot products as 7.5, shifted right by 6) is **added to the position longs**. The cell, fine and `+0x10` words are not updated here (the missile's first step refreshes them). The entries are (x, y, z) in model units and are disk data (not listed): in each owner kind they come in three mirror pairs (x and -x, the same y and z: the wing tips, the near pylons, the inner pylons), the two kinds differ in the first two pairs and agree in the third; the port reads them from `tables/ai.bin` (the offset sub-table, records 2 to 13; FNV-1a-32 of those 12 * 6 bytes, little-endian words, `23776797`). A Sidewinder (h 0 to 3) thus leaves from the wing tips and the near pylons, an AMRAAM (h 2 to 5) from the inner pylons;
9. the life `+0x4C` = 150 (class 0) or 200 (class 1); `+0x26` = 20 (the ramp of the turn rate, 8.3); W0 gets `0x10C0` (active in the simulation, AI valid, valid) and `0x0100` and `0x0002` (the spawn flag); `+0x76` = 0; if the owner was on the ground (W2 `0x0080`) the vertical velocity `+0x42` is zeroed and the bit cleared; the destination x `+0x2C` = `0xFFFF` (no destination: the steering returns at once until the guidance sets one); the display request `V:4A910` = `0x8C`; the target byte `+0x38` = the owner's.

The launch is stepped in the same pass (the gate `0x278F6` and the flight step run on the new record at once). [V: `weapon_check.py launch`, whole state hunk and the descriptor table compared over 3,000 random owners, 1,303 launched and 1,697 refused, 0 differences.] In the launch pass **all steps of the gate run once** (8.1 steps 3 to 6: the life 150 or 200 becomes 149 or 199, the speed gains the boost `+0x1E0` on top of the owner's speed word as the player's step left it, the guidance runs), and the steering counter `+0x26` goes from 20 to 19; the launch pass is pass 1 of the missile [V: `weapon_check.py focus`, speed = the owner's + `0x1E0` in pass 1; the demo AIM-120: owner `0x2263`, missile `0x2443` in the launch pass].

### 7.7 Drawing the tracers

The plain tracer (`0x31384`, pen 9) is **one pixel**: its position relative to the camera (the cell difference times 16384 plus the position shifted right by 8, minus the camera position, rotated into the view by the view matrix) is projected like any point (note 03: x' = `0x13F` - (160 + 160 X / Z), y' = `0xB3` - (90 + 90 Y / Z) + 1, drawn when Z is positive, |X| and |Y| are below Z, the pixel is in the window and y' is not below the lower window limit `V:4AA3C`) with the one-pixel point routine `0x340D4`. The slot step (`0x1754C`) picks the drawer from the flags at `0x17740`: **flags `& 0x1002` non-zero (a flare, or any stopped slot) -> `0x31478`; else chaff (`0x2000`) -> `0x3140C`; else the tracer's one pixel (`0x31384`)** [R, read from the code and the drawers]. They run once per pass for every active slot; the draw scan `0x2AD1A` (note 04 stage 9) decides whether the entity pass runs.

* **`0x31478` (the flare, and the spark of a stopped tracer)**: three short line segments in pen 9 around the slot, through the 3D line routine `0x3385C` ("unclipped" in note 03's sense: a line is drawn only when both end points lie inside the closed view pyramid, z > 0 and |x|, |y| <= z, and then both projected end points are clamped to the window; there is no geometric clipping of the 3D line; the pixel line routine `0x3455E` it calls still clips to the window with the clip state `V:4A79E` = `0xF`, `V:4A7A0` = `0xFFFF`, `V:4A7A2` = 0). The slot's view-space centre is built as for the tracer (cell difference to the camera cells times 16384 plus the position shifted right by 8, plus the camera offsets `V:4AB2A`, `V:4AB2E`, `V:4AB1E`), the endpoints are the centre plus the offsets of the table at `0x31542` (18 words: 3 segments of 2 points of (x, y, z), small values in position units divided by 256, three strokes through the centre in different directions; exported as `tables/star.bin` by the extractor, not quoted here), each rotated by the viewing matrix `V:4AC90` (`0x31566`) and projected. So a flare is a small three-stroke star about 6 du across, and a tracer that has hit the ground or an entity (flag `0x0002`) turns into the same star for its two remaining passes.
* **`0x3140C` (chaff)**: it stores the pen word `V:4AA0C` = **13** and, while the life word is above `0x3A` (the first two of its 60 passes), jumps into the tracer's pixel code **after** the tracer's own pen store (`0x3139E`, the store is at `0x31396`), so the one pixel (`0x34168` reads `V:4AA0C`) is drawn in **pen 13**, not 9 [V: `weapon_check.py chaff`: the pen word seen by the pixel routine is 13 for a chaff of life `0x3B` and 9 for a tracer; code R]. From life `0x3A` down it calls the burst routine `0x31766` at the slot's position with the age min(`0x3A` - life, 12), **kind 0** and no disc size (11.4): a growing figure for 58 passes whose size stops growing at age 12, drawn in **pen 13**.
* The tracer (`0x31384`) is the one pixel above.

**The pixels of the flare's star, measured** [V: `followup-combat-flare-chaff-flare.txt`, `gen-followup-combat.py flare`: `airborne.vasnap`, KP-ENTER (the chase view, `V:4A83D` = `0xFF`), a flare requested by poking the request byte `V:4A943` to 2 at a pass boundary (the same request the F key sets), 60 single-frame steps with a frame dump, the slot bytes, the view matrix `V:4AC90`, the camera variables `V:4AB1A` to `V:4AB32` and the cells `V:4AA04/06` logged per frame; the palette of that view has colour 9 = `0xD92` and colour 13 = `0x777`; and `flare_chaff_replay.py`, the original's flare drawer run in the rig on those logged bytes with the line routine logged]. The slot appears in the pass of the request (pass 271, flags `0x1401`, life 30); **the first picture that shows it is the flip 3 video frames later, and the picture is renewed once per pass** (7 to 9 frames in this view). In that picture the star is **50 game pixels of the pen-9 colour (the frame colour (197, 123, 0), each game pixel 2 frame pixels wide and 1 line high), 23 pixels wide and 17 high at that distance**: three one-pixel lines through a common middle, a long one from (176, 104) to (155, 121), a short one from (163, 107) to (167, 115) and a long one from (177, 119) to (155, 105) (game coordinates, origin at the window's top left), **exactly the three line calls that the original's drawer makes in the rig for the logged slot and camera** (the same 12 numbers; the line routine leaves one end pixel of each line out, so three of the six end points are lit and the others are the exclusive ends). The next pass's picture (the slot has made its first move, 7,758 less in x, 5,556 more in y and 14,443 more in z position units; the camera climbs with the aircraft) shows the same star 32 rows lower, (176, 136) to (155, 154), (163, 143) to (167, 147) and (177, 153) to (155, 137), again the rig's numbers; from the pass after that the slot is behind the view pyramid (z not positive: the drawer draws nothing, as in the rig replay), so **the star is visible in three pictures only, whatever its life of 30 passes**. [Rule for the port: a flare is drawn as the three strokes of the star table in pen 9 through the line routine, as 7.7 states; it is visible only while the point is in front of the camera.]

**The chaff, seen** [V, milestone 7: `combat7-chaffdraw.txt`, `-back.txt`, `-head.txt` (generator `gen-combat7.py chaffdraw`, `chaffdrawback`, `chaffdrawhead`: the chaff drawer `0x3140C` is stopped by `runto` at each call, so that the pool, the camera variables `V:4AB1A..`, the view matrix, the cells and the clip words are logged **at the draw**, with a frame written at each stop), `chaffdraw_check.py` (the original's drawer run in the rig on each stop's state), `combat7-chaffheadorder.txt`, `combat7-chaffpix.txt`, `combat7-chaffptr.txt`]. The earlier captures (batch a, c and e of `followup-combat-*`) logged the camera after the pass and searched the frames for the colour and row of the replayed point; both were wrong. **At the draw-time camera the replay predicts**: in the **chase view** and in the **cockpit look-back view** nothing at all, from the first pass (the drawer's calls are empty: the slot, 20 to 30 du away, is outside the view pyramid in all nine passes), and the frames hold no extra pixel (the colour count of the window is constant); in the **head-on external view** a one-pixel plot in the **creation pass** (life 60, the stop at pass `0x10F`: game point (157, 86), pen 13) and in the next pass (life 59: (157, 83)), and nothing from the third (life 58, `0x3A`: the burst's twelve lines are all outside the view pyramid, in this view as in the other two). **The plot in the creation pass is in the picture**: the plane memory (`chaffpix`) holds colour 13 (planes 0, 2 and 3 of the lowest-address set, plane 1 clear) at plane row 86, x 157, of the page drawn in pass `0x10F` at the next frame routine entry and colour 8 (the surrounding) before the drawer, and the frame written at the next stop shows one pixel of a lighter grey at (157, 85) against the aircraft's body grey around it: **the picture shows the routine's y minus 1**, because the plane pointers of the draw table `V:4A76E` (here `0x16128`, `0x141E8`, `0x122A8`, `0x10368`: the planes from the highest address down, page A, one row above the first row the window clear touches) sit one row above the displayed first row. The colour in the frame is that display's palette for index 13, not the `colors` reading of the stopped machine (the palette below and above a copper split differ: the aircraft's colour 8 shows as `0x333`-grey, the pen 13 pixel as the lighter one). **The plot in the second pass is not in the picture**, and the order shows why: `combat7-chaffheadorder.txt` stops at the chaff drawer and then at the entity drawer `0x21C44`: in the creation pass the next entity drawer call is in the following pass (the entity pass came **first**, the draw scan `0x2AD18` answered 0 on the empty pool), and in the passes `0x111` to `0x115` both calls fall in the **same** pass with the chaff drawer first (the scan answered 1: the active chaff slot passes the distance test, 12.5); so from the second pass the aircraft's body is drawn after the chaff pixel and covers it, and the plane memory at the end of that pass shows colour 8 at (157, 83) as well. **The burst** (from the third pass: the twelve lines of kind 0, pen 13) is in none of the chase, look-back and head-on views inside the view pyramid at its draws. The **fixed tower camera** (SHIFT and KP-ENTER, `combat7-chaffdrawfixed.txt`, 16 stops) keeps the slot in view throughout: there both plain pixels are in the picture, at (160, 90) and (159, 90), changed against the previous frame (the aircraft is far from the camera and covers nothing), and from the third pass the drawer makes its twelve line calls, all in pen 13, as 11.4 states (`chaffdraw_check.py`). Their end points lie within one to three pixels of each other, around (159 to 173, 137 to 141), because the slot is thousands of du from the tower; **the original's own point and line routines, run unstubbed in the rig on the logged state of each of the 14 burst stops, write no pixel for them** (the pixel passes write exactly one), and the frames of all 16 stops hold no pen-13 pixel there. So the burst is drawn and **plots nothing at that distance**; a view in which the slot is close and in front of the camera for passes 3 to 60 does not exist among the shipped views (the chaff is released behind the aircraft, the chase and rear cameras are behind it, the head-on camera loses it at the third pass), so the burst's lines are not seen in any picture of the game and the rule stands on the rig (`weapon_check.py chaff`: 12, 9 and 7 line calls at life `0x3A`, `0x30` and `0x10`, all pen 13) and on the code. **Rule for the port**: the plain pixel (pen 13, the creation pass and the next one) is drawn at the projected point when it is inside the view and the draw order of 12.5 puts the chaff drawer after the entity pass (the creation pass; later passes when the scan answers 0); the burst as 11.4 says.

### 7.8 What the gun cannot do

A tracer is tested against the entity entries of queue C (8.2) and, read only, against the landmark volumes of the lists (8.2, "Landmarks"; a building stops it): an aircraft that is not in the queue (outside the tile window that the list builder generates, note 04a section 6) cannot be hit; records that have W0 bit `0x0040` clear are skipped; tracers do not hit the owner. Tracers do hit friendly and not-counted aircraft (the hit effect does not look at W0 bit 3): shooting a friendly counts like shooting a hostile.

## 8. Guidance and hits

Section 7 is the firing side. This section is what happens after: the life and the speed of a missile (8.1, the gate), the gun's hit test (8.2, with the hull lists), the destination, steering and turn rate of a missile (8.3), the contact, the hits and the log counters of the missiles (8.4), the three cases of Review Focus 1 (8.5), the AI's launch decision and the 30-step wait (8.6), the gun pipper (8.7) and the port list (8.8). The countermeasures' effect on a missile is 9.4 and 9.5, the damage a hit does is section 10, the explosion is section 11. "Pass" is the world-update pass of section 7; "du" is a draw unit (256 position units). Rig counts are from `tools/m68k-rig/tests/lists_model/weapon_check.py` unless another script is named; the emulator captures are in 8.9.

### 8.1 The missile gate (`0x27DC2`, the class nibble 0 branch of `0x278F6`)

The gate runs at the head of the flight step of a record (`0x2A2A2`); for a record whose class high nibble is 0 (`+0x62` 0 the Sidewinder, 1 the AMRAAM) it is this routine and none of the AI gate of section 4. A missile is a record like any other (a copy of its owner's first `0xA4` bytes, 7.6); the player's three missile records are records 1 to 3, the hostile ones 5, 9, 11, 13. Per pass, in this order [V: `weapon_check.py gate`, a model of the routine against the original on 6,000 random missile records (life -3 to 210, speed words over the whole 16-bit range, W0, W2 and B20 combinations, target bytes, designations, decoy slots, pause and freeze), whole state hunk compared, 0 differences; the first run found one difference, the speed addition wrapping at 16 bits, which no reachable state shows]:

| step | rule | address |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | the pause `V:4A866` returns at once; nothing changes and the flight step is skipped | `0x278F6` |
| 2 | W0 bit `0x0040` (valid) clear: return at once (a stored record) | `0x27DC2` |
| 3 | **life**: unless the pause or the freeze `V:4A840` is set, `+0x4C` is decreased by 1. At 0 or below the missile **expires**: the whole W0 word becomes 0, the display request `V:4A910` = `0x0C`, and the gate returns 0 (the flight step is skipped). Nothing is counted | `0x27DD8`, `0x27DB0` |
| 4 | **coast**: when W0 bit `0x0400` is set, or the life is below `0x32` (50): the life below `0x0C` (12) sets W0 bit `0x0400` first (latched for the rest of the life: the bit is the burnt-out mark; it is the dying bit of section 5, which the gate's own expiry pre-empts). The destination height `+0x34` is cleared to 0 and the speed word `+0x6C` moves by `0x240` per pass towards **`0x3000`** (down when above, up when below, never past); `+0x6E` takes the same value; return 1. No guidance runs: the destination words stay as the last guidance left them, and the height is 0 | `0x27DE2`-`0x27E34` |
| 5 | **boost** (life `0x32` or more): `+0x6C` gains `0x1E0` (16-bit word addition), at most `0x4200`; `+0x6E` takes the same value | `0x27E3A`-`0x27E50` |
| 6 | **guidance**: W0 bit `0x0002` (the spawn flag, set by the launch) clear: the destination update of 8.3 (`0x2826A`); set: W0 bit 0 (engaged) is cleared and, when W0 bit `0x0008` (a *player weapon*, copied from the player's `0x11C8`) is clear, the same update; when it is set the missile **retargets live** (8.3) | `0x27E54`-`0x27ECC` |

The life is 150 for the Sidewinder and 200 for the AMRAAM at the launch (7.6) and the launch pass already decrements it, so the first pass of the missile's life reads 149 or 199. The timeline of a missile that is never destroyed, in passes after the launch pass: **boost** for 100 passes (Sidewinder) or 150 (AMRAAM) while the life is 50 or more, the speed rising from the owner's speed word by `0x1E0` per pass to `0x4200` (so a player at `0x2263` reaches the cap in 17 passes); then **coast** for 38 passes at a speed falling by `0x240` per pass to `0x3000`; the last 11 passes with W0 `0x0400`; **freed in pass 150 or 200** counting the launch pass as 1 (the life reaching 0). A missile travels about 4 position units per pass per unit of the speed word (the capture, 8.9): `0x4200` is about 264 du per pass, `0x3000` about 192 du; an aircraft at the speed word `0x1200` is about 72 du per pass.

The gate keeps one rule that looks like a bug and is the original's: **the coast clears the destination height**, so a missile whose motor has burnt out steers towards the ground below its last destination point. Together with the live retarget of 8.3 a Sidewinder fired at long range usually dives in its last 50 passes.

**The freeze byte `V:4A840`** (steps 3 above and the step skip of 3.1 and 4.1) [R: the complete list of the image's references: the three stores, the three reads, no register-based access found; V: the byte reads 0 in the flight-start peeks of the captures of note 08 5.10, `F:4A83C` (12 bytes) at the flight start of the training part 2 reads `01 00 00 01 00 01 ...`]. It is one of the twelve switch bytes `V:4A83C` to `V:4A847` that the common mission-start fill (`0x1D38A`) sets to 1; three stores clear it: the cockpit start of the flow state at `0x12D3E`, the same start's other entry at `0x12D78` and the exit state at `0x1251A`. It therefore holds **1 between the mission start (briefing, orders, the approach stages of note 08 6.x) and the start of the flight**, and the three readers act only in that interval: the gate of 8.1 does not count a missile's life down, the flight step of a non-player record is skipped (`0x2A2AC` ORs it into the skip test with the pause) and the seat's step (`0x27BFA`, section 6.4) holds as well. In every flight pass it is 0, so **the port needs no such state in flight; a stage that holds the non-player records (the briefing) is the front end's hold, not this byte's rule**. The earlier "meaning not named" is closed: it is the front end's freeze of the records before the flight.

### 8.2 The gun's hit test (`0x2ADDE`)

Called once per pass for every active tracer slot (7.4 step 1), with the slot number; it returns 1 for a hit, after which the slot becomes stopped (flags `|= 0x12`, `&= ~0x08`, life 2). It does nothing (returns 0, and the slot does not move) when `V:4A875` or the pause is set. It writes the step vector `+0x0C..` shifted right by 8 (the three words) to `V:4B230`, clears `V:4AA70` (the record under test, 0) and walks **queue C** (the entity and object entries of the display lists, note 04a section 6) backwards from the last entry `V:4AA7E`, entries of `0x18` bytes, for at most `V:4A916` entries (the count of queue C) [V for the walk and the entity branch: `weapon_check.py entity`, 40,000 random queues of 1 to 7 entity entries (record flags, classes `0x00`, `0x11` to `0x15`, `0x20`, part ids, positions around the tracer, the owner skip, secondary-object entries mixed in) against a model of the original, 245 hits of which 72 kills, 7,176 skipped because the entry's model is a big hull (below), whole state hunk and the 15 records compared, 0 differences; 600 + 12,000 more in other seeds]. For each entry:

1. the flag byte (`+0x01` of the entry): bit 6 set (a secondary object): skipped; bit 4 clear (and bit 6 clear): a landmark volume, see "landmarks" below; bit 4 set: an entity;
2. an entity: at most **6** entity entries are examined per call (the seventh and later are ignored);
3. the entry's record index is the high byte of its first word (signed); the entry is skipped when it is the slot's owner (`+0x2E`, always 0, the player), when the record's cell words (`+6`, `+8`) differ from the slot's (`+0x30`, `+0x32`), or when the record's W0 bit `0x0040` is clear. **Nothing else about the record is read**: a friendly, a not-counted record, a crashed or dying record and an inactive one are all hit (7.8);
4. the **box**: with the model flag byte f (byte `+7` past the model's first block, the byte the placement read for `+0x7D`; note 07 2.3) the half-size is `0x800` if f bit 4 is set (a big hull: the carrier and the ships) and otherwise the word `V:4AA5E`, which the flight start (`0x1D246`) sets to **`0x140` (320 du)**. The record's fine position (`+0x0C` word, `+0x10` long, `+0x0E` word) is compared with the slot's position shifted right by 8 on each axis (the x and z differences as 16-bit words, y as a long); the entry is skipped unless all three absolute differences are **below** the half-size;
5. inside the box the tracer is moved by its **sub-step vector** (`+0x18..`, one eighth of the step) up to **8 times**, and after each move the hull test runs (`V:4AA70` = the record index): **`0x2B4BA` for an aircraft** (8.2a), `0x2B3F0` for a big hull. At the first sub-step that tests inside, the walk ends with a hit (the slot keeps the position of that sub-step). If none of the 8 hits, the position is restored (the step vector is subtracted once: the net of the 8 additions) and the walk goes on with the next entry;
6. when the walk ends without a hit, the motion of 7.4 runs (gravity, drag, the move by the whole step).

The effect of a hit on an entity whose model is **not** a big hull: the log counter `+0x3C` (cannon hits) gains 1 (`V:1D024` = the flight log record, note 08 10.9); the low nibble of the record's `+0x3C` plus 1 is computed; **when that is below 3 it is stored (the high nibble kept); when it is 3 or more nothing is stored**: W0 bit `0x0200` (crashed) is set and, when the class byte is not `0x15` and its high nibble is not 0, B20 bit 1 (out of control) is set, `V:54E72` (the camera-follow record) = the record's offset, W2 bit `0x1000` and the state byte `+0x05` = 1. **The third hit kills, and every later hit on the same record repeats the kill marks** (the nibble stays 2: W0 `0x0200`, B20 bit 1, `V:54E72`, W2 `0x1000` and state 1 are written again, and the log counter `+0x3C` still counts). A repeated hit on a dying record only sets the crashed bit again; the dying step ignores it while the count runs, so the count of 15 is not restarted and the crashed bit stays set until the expiry [V: `weapon_check.py repeat`, 3 cases of the step `0x2A2A2` with counters 14, 7 and 2, and the `entity` part's model, which stores the nibble only below 3; emulator `combat-hit-gunkill.txt`: after the kill the MiG's `+0x3C` still reads 2 and the second tracer hit is counted]. (The same three marks as the missile's hit, 10.3, but with one difference: this test compares the whole class byte with `0x15` and so exempts the cruise missile from the out-of-control mark, while the missile's contact code at `0x2B7B4` compares the masked nibble and exempts nothing: **a cruise missile shot by the gun is crashed only; hit by a missile it is crashed and out of control**. Both are the code's.) The record's `+0x3C` low nibble counts hits 1 and 2 for the life of the record (the placement clears it). A **big hull** hit (the carrier, ships) returns 1 and does **nothing else**: no counter, no mark, the tracer is merely stopped.

**Models whose flag byte has bit 4 (the box of `0x800` and `0x2B3F0`).** The flag byte is read from the model, not from the class: it is byte `+7` of the **block named by the first word of the model's detail list** (the leaf word masked to 14 bits; when that first word is a non-negative threshold the leaf is the word at `+4`), not the scale byte at `+6`. Bit 4 is set for the ships (templates 8, 9, 12: `0x11`), for template 10 (`0xFC`) and for **the Sidewinder's model (template 0: `0x18`)**, but **not** for the AMRAAM's (template 1: `0x48`) [R: read from the image; V: the rig `gunhit` part of `test_weapons_diff` with descriptor pointers, 800 cases, 0 differences after the port's fix]. A tracer within `0x800` du of a Sidewinder (the player's own or a hostile one) or of a record with template 10 therefore runs `0x2B3F0`, not the aircraft hull; an **AMRAAM** reaches the aircraft branch (box `0x140`, the hull test `0x2B4BA`), but **the hull test reads the record's part id first, and a drawn AMRAAM's id is `0xFF`** (every level of template 1, the part-id table of 8.2a): it returns no hit, so a tracer cannot crash an AMRAAM [V for the rule: `weapon_check.py entity` and `hull` include the negative id (no hit, nothing read); R for the id of the missile's blocks by the template scan, `template_parts.py`]. The only window is between the launch and the missile's first draw: the new record keeps the owner's `+0x7B` from the copy of its first `0xA4` bytes, and its vertex slots (from `+0xA4`) are whatever the record held before, so a tracer in that gap would be tested against the owner's id and stale slots [R, not exercised; the port copies `+0x7B` with the first `0xA4` bytes like the launch, and takes the missile's id `0xFF` from its first draw]. `0x2B3F0` (the record's class byte `0x20` selects the carrier lists `0x3E0F8`/`0x3E174`, any other class the lists `0x3EDCC`/`0x3EDEC`, both read from the record's rotated vertex slots like the player's deck and hull test of note 05 17.11): it compares the tracer's **absolute** height in du with the **record-relative** height of the reference vertex of the first list (the vertex named by the **word at +2 of the first structure** of the list, not the word at +0 that the player's deck test reads: slot 1 for `0x3EDCC`, slot 28 for `0x3EDEC`; the y word **shifted right by the unit shift**, compared as a word with the tracer's height) and only a tracer **lower** than that height (a missile on or near the ground) is tested against the first list's volumes. Otherwise the second list is used: its references (the word at +2 of its first structure, and of the next group `0x12` bytes on) are compared **unshifted** and sign-extended as a **long** against the tracer's long height (a reference at or above the tracer sends it to the volumes, a reference below it for the second group is a miss) [V: `tests/rig/test_ship_diff.c` `bighull`, which found both points in the port: 4 seeds x 4000 cases per part, 0 differences after the fix]. The volumes are (`0x3EDCC`: six planes through the slots 0, 1, 2, 7, 8, 9 and 14 to 19; `0x3EDEC`: four planes through the slots 20 to 27); a point is inside when its dot product with every plane normal is **negative** (a zero normal, as from a vertex slot that holds nothing, is outside). **For a missile in the air the answer is always 0**: the height compare fails (the tracer is thousands of du above the small relative vertex heights) and the slots 14 to 27 lie beyond the missile model's vertices. [V: `weapon_check.py bighull`, 1,500 tracer points per row within 60 du of a class 0 record: 0 hits at 3,000 to 30,000 du with 12 real slots and zeros or with all slots random, hits only near the ground (8 and 15 of 1,500), 143 for a class `0x20` record at the ground, 0 differences from this reading.] So a tracer is **not** stopped by a missile in flight; the port's fallback that stops tracers near the player's own missile is wrong and must return 0 for a record that is not on the ground.

**Landmarks** [V: `weapon_check.py landmark`: the original's `0x2ADDE` against a model written from the routine, on the five landmark volumes that the original's own list builder wrote for the logged air scene (copies of two of them placed at the ordinals 7 and 10), random tracers around each volume (a third of them far from the volumes, the rest near the volume's own vertices), random queue lengths up to 12 entries; 12,600 cases in seven seeds, about 2,500 hits; the return value and the slot's 64 bytes equal and the 15 records unchanged in every case, 0 differences]. An entry that is not an entity (flag byte bits 4 and 6 clear) is a landmark volume of the lists (note 04a, the slots `0x5283C`/`0x52868`; note 05 17.11 for the volume's record). The walk counts such entries with an ordinal that starts at 0 and **stops after the entry with ordinal 10: eleven ordinals, 0 to 10, one per slot of the table** (the earlier text said ten; the port's loop counted ten). A secondary object takes no ordinal. For the ordinal k: a table pointer that is negative skips the entry; otherwise the volume's record at `0x52868 + 256 k` is read: the cell bytes at +2 (x) and +3 (z), the box words at +4 (the height ceiling), +6 and +8 (the x limits), +10 and +12 (the z limits), then the chain of sub-volumes: the first element starts at +0x0E with the same five words, its link (a long that holds the absolute address of the next element, negative at the end) is at +0x18.

1. **Cells.** The tracer's cell words (`+0x30`, `+0x32`) must be within one cell of the record's cell bytes on each axis (the word difference, signed). The cell difference times 16,384 is added to the tracer's x and z in every test below. The **z offset also carries the high word of the table pointer shifted right by 2 in its low bits** (the original's word-only sign extension leaves the pointer's high half in the register before the shift; the same artefact as the player's volumes of note 05 17.11: 48 on the reference machine, where the program lives at `0xC3....`, and 1 on the rig image). The port must add the same value to its z offset (it is the value of note 05 17.11's artefact for the same slot).
2. **Top box.** The tracer's height (the position y shifted right by 8, a long) must be at most `0x7FFF` and, as a word, not above the record's ceiling; its x and z (the positions shifted right by 8, plus the offsets) must lie **strictly inside** the record's x and z words.
3. **Chain.** The elements are tried in turn with the same three comparisons (ceiling, x, z); an element that fails passes to the next by the link (a negative link ends the volume with no hit). An element that passes goes to the plane test.
4. **Plane test, up to 8 sub-steps.** In each sub-step the tracer advances by its sub-step vector (`+0x18..`), the point (x and z plus their offsets, y) is formed, and for each plane of the element (the count is the word at +0x0E of the model-data list that the table pointer names for this element; a plane is a vertex triple in the record, 6 bytes apart from the element's +0x0E, and a normal triple in the model data, 12 bytes apart from +0x16 of the list) the dot product of the normal with (point minus vertex) is formed from 16-bit words added in 32 bits: the point is **inside the volume when every dot product is negative**, and the first sub-step that is inside ends the call with a **hit** (the slot keeps the position of that sub-step; the caller then stops the tracer). When all 8 sub-steps are outside: a link that is positive moves the tracer back by the whole step vector and tries the next element **with box coordinates that no longer carry the cell offsets** (the original reloads x, y and z without them **for the box compares only**: the plane test of that element re-adds the cell offsets and the z artefact, which is non-zero even in the volume's own cell: 48 on the reference machine); a link that is not positive **ends the call with no hit and without the motion step of 7.4** (the tracer keeps the position of the 8 sub-steps, one whole step, and receives neither gravity nor drag nor the first-pass latch).

A hit changes no record and no counter, and the tracer stops (the same flag change as a hit on an entity). So **a tracer is stopped by a building**; the port must not let tracers through landmark volumes. [Code change for the port: `weapons.c` `hit_test` counts landmark ordinals to 10 (`lms >= 10` skips the eleventh) and must walk ordinals 0 to 10; its landmark hook must apply the cell offsets and the pointer artefact of step 1 and the miss exit of step 4.]

**Landmark and entity entries in one queue: the shared register, the limits and the compares** [V: `weapon_check.py mixed`, 1,500 cases of queues of 2 to 20 entries mixing landmark and entity entries (entity records in the tracer's cells with the part id `0xFF`, so a box that is entered never hits), the return value and the slot's 64 bytes equal, 0 differences; a mutation of the rule below (the ordinal kept across an entered box) fails 184 of the 1,500]. (1) **The landmark ordinal and the entity sub-step counter share one register**: the ordinal is its low word. An entity entry whose box is entered swaps the register's halves and counts the 8 sub-steps in the low word, which ends at -1 after the eight misses, so **the landmark ordinals restart at 0 after every entity entry whose box was entered without a hit** (the old ordinal stays in the high half and is never restored); an entity entry that fails an earlier test (cell, valid, box) leaves the register alone. A landmark chain element that passes its box swaps the halves too (the swap at `0x2B1E2`), and the plane test overwrites the register with its z difference (a long copy of the difference into the register at `0x2B334`), but **that value does not survive**: when all 8 sub-steps of an element miss and its link is positive, the whole step is undone by a multi-register load at `0x2B376` that reads the three longs of the step vector (at offset 0x0C of the slot) into the three working registers D4, D5, D6, so **D5 holds the step's y component (a long) from then on**, and every way of going on after a plane-test miss passes through that undo (a link that is not positive ends the call at `0x2B392` with no hit, before any further ordinal is used). **The next landmark ordinal is therefore the low word of the step's y plus one** (the long at `+0x10` of the slot, the middle word of the step vector at `+0x0C`; the position's y is at `+4`), not the ordinal plus one and **not the z difference** [R: `0x2B334`, `0x2B376`, the signed compare of the register's low word against 10 at `0x2AF4A`, which branches when it is greater or equal; V: the rig `gunhit` part of `test_weapons_diff` with landmark volumes in the original's memory layout, 3,000 cases (915 with landmarks, 640 stopped), 0 differences, where the first reading, the z difference, failed 1 of 800 and the reading "step y" failed none; mutation checks: dropping the leftover assignment fails 3 of 800]. Errata: the first reading of this paragraph (the z difference) was wrong, and `weapon_check.py mixed` does not catch it (its queues never enter the path: `'entered': 0`).

**An ordinal outside 0 to 10 (the leftover below 0)** [R from the code at `0x2AF4A`-`0x2AFFC`; V for the memory contents: one emulator capture of the state hunks in free flight, `note0708-statedump.txt`, evaluated for all 32,778 possible leftovers]. The compare is a signed word compare against 10: a leftover (the low word of step y) of **10 or more ends the walk** (no read), a leftover **from -32,768 to 9** lets the walk go on with the ordinal k = leftover + 1, from -32,767 to 10, and a **negative leftover is common** (the low word of a long is negative about half the time: the step's y includes the gravity share and its low 16 bits are arbitrary). The original then reads, with **16-bit wrapped arithmetic**, the pointer long at `0x5283C + (int16)(4 k)` (so from `0x4A83C` to `0x5A83B`: the state hunks 71 and 72 before and after the table, which the loader placed contiguously in the captured run, and beyond `0x53818` the following hunks and the RAM after them) and, when that long is not negative, the volume record at `0x52868 + (int16)(256 k) + 2`. What the reads yield in the capture: of the 29,340 leftovers whose pointer address lies inside the loaded hunks, **2,254 read a negative long (a skip), 27,086 read a non-negative one**, mostly 0 (the zero-filled variables) or code and data words, so **the pointer test does not skip them**; the entry then passes through the cell test on the wrapped record bytes (the cell bytes within one of the tracer's cell on each axis), and **318 of the 27,086 pass it, all of them leftovers whose k is congruent to 0, 1 or 2 modulo 256** (the wrapped record address `256 k` then lies on the real records 0, 1, 2, the three that the captured scene had near the player's cell; the capture used the player's cell words for the tracer); every other ordinal fails the cell test on the bytes it finds and **skips the entry like a negative pointer, the counter going on** (the register keeps k). The exceptions are: (a) **exact aliases of real entries**: k = j - 16,384 and k = j - 32,768 (j = 0 to 10; the second only from j = 1) make both `4 k` and `256 k` equal `4 j` and `256 j` modulo 65,536, so the original reads **the real pointer and the real record of entry j** and the volume is tested in full (a possible hit); (b) the 318 cases with k congruent to 0 to 2 modulo 256 other than those aliases, which test the real record's box (the tracer is inside it only near that landmark) with a **model pointer read from unrelated state memory** (often 0, so the plane count and normals come from the low memory of the machine): a junk plane test, a hit or a miss by the memory's contents; (c) the leftovers whose pointer address lies beyond the loaded hunks, whose reads belong to whatever the allocator put there. **Port rule (C): an ordinal outside 0 to 10 acts as the real entry j when it equals j - 16,384 or j - 32,768 (the register's low word kept as a signed 16-bit value, the walk goes on from it), and otherwise skips the entry and keeps counting.**

**Cases (b) and (c), run with the whole machine state (milestone 7)** [V: the emulator script `combat7-statedump.txt` (`airborne.vasnap`, then a `peek` of the seven state hunks at their flat addresses, of the whole slow RAM `0xC00000` to `0xC7FFFF` and of the chip RAM below `0x10000` and above the image), converted by `state_dump.py` into `extracted/captures/state-flight.bin`; `landmark_state_check.py` loads it into the rig (the state hunks at their flat addresses, the RAM at the machine's real addresses: the 12 bytes of loader header between hunks make the machine's layout differ from the flat one, so the bytes of each case's wrapped reads are patched in from the real RAM) and runs the original's hit test on a single landmark entry with the ordinal register loaded with the leftover (a three-instruction trampoline in free rig memory replaces the register's initial load) for every leftover from -32,768 to 9; `test_weapons_diff.c` part `lmstate` runs the port beside it]. The state of that run: the table at real `0xC4A390`, the records at `0xC4A3BC`, the last hunk ending at `0xC50E78`. Of the 32,778 ordinals k = -32,767 to 10: **11 are the real ordinals** and **21 the exact aliases** (the port tests them as the real entry: 126 cases in the real volumes 0 to 8 of the dump, tracers inside their boxes, 74 stopped by a hit, **0 differences**); **2,492 read a negative pointer** (skip); **27,198 cannot be entered by any tracer** (the junk record's cell bytes are not within one of a cell of 0 to 127, or its top box is empty); 630 are not run (the wrapped read falls on a variable the test sets). The **2,426 that can be entered** (a tracer inside the junk record's cell and top box) split: **348 behave exactly as a skip** in the original (the junk model's chain misses at the first element: the port equals the original there, 348 cases, **0 differences**), and **2,078 do not**: 160 end in a hit; 1,918 miss but leave the tracer moved by the eight sub-steps without the gravity and drag of the motion step (a call that ends at a non-positive link, 7.4 and step 4 above), or run on past 400,000 instructions (1,036 of them: a loop through the junk record's chain that the 68000 would not leave; the game would stop). They read a **zero pointer** in 1,296 of the 2,078 (a zero-filled variable of the state hunks: the model is then the machine's low memory, whose first words are the exception vector table that Kickstart set up, `0x00FC....`, so the plane count and normals are ROM addresses), a pointer into RAM in 62, and another value (code or data words) in the rest; **1,042 of the 2,078 read one of the volume records 0 to 10 through the 256-byte wrap (k congruent to 0 to 10 modulo 256: the record is real, only the model pointer is junk) and 1,036 read unrelated memory.** The memory past the last hunk is **not zero**: **2,614 of the 2,700** pointer reads beyond it (the state of that run) found a non-zero long, left by earlier users of the RAM.

*Verdict.* **(b) and (c) are UNOBSERVABLE as a rule, and the port keeps its skip.** The outcome of an ordinal outside 0 to 10 is a function of memory that is not the game's data: the machine's exception vectors (Kickstart-dependent), the loader's placement of the hunks (the 12-byte headers, the order the allocator took) and the RAM leftovers beyond the program, all of which differ between boot paths and machines; a rule written for the captured layout would be a rule about that capture. The two quantities that matter in play are small: the leftover is the low word of the step vector's y, uniform over 65,536 values, so an ordinal that can be entered at all (a tracer inside a junk or real record's box in the one pass in which a failed chain element has loaded the register) is rare, and the 348 cases that equal a skip are the part of the 2,426 that the port reproduces. The data are in `landmark_state_check.py`'s census (the numbers above are its output); `test_weapons_diff.c` part `lmstate` pins the part the port must reproduce (real ordinals, aliases, the junk reads that equal a skip) and counts the rest without comparing it.
The "eleven ordinals" of this section therefore hold between such resets: a queue can be walked past ordinal 10 only through the resets, and the loop ends when the low word reaches 10 or more. (2) **The walk ends, it does not skip**, when the low word of the register is 10 or more, when the sixth entity entry has been examined, or when the entries (the count `V:4A916`) are used up. (3) The x and z box compares of the top box and of every chain element are **long compares** (the position shifted right by 8 plus the offset, as a long, against the sign-extended box word): no wrap to a word, so a position past the word range is outside. (4) **A plane count of 0 (or a negative one) tests one plane**: the first dot product is formed before the count is looked at, and the loop ends inside when the decremented count is no longer positive; the four volumes of the logged air scene have counts of 4 and 5, the other volumes' counts were not scanned. **The one shipped node with a count of 0** (milestone 7; extractor version 26): the footprint of the object record 34 (item id 18, not one of the table C items of note 02 section 9: the footprint is decoded from the model bytes at the odd address the block word names, and is no landmark of any cell list) has a single node with a count of 0, and the six words that follow the count are the plane the original's first dot product reads. `tables/lists_land.bin` carries that plane since extractor 26 (a node with a count of 0 stores one plane after its count byte; both extractors, `tools/extract/README.md`), and `lists.c` `lists_footprint` hands it to the slot. The real boxes of that footprint are degenerate (the outer box's z limits are reversed), so no tracer is ever inside it in play; the rig case widens the boxes around the tracer and keeps the table's plane and count: `test_weapons_diff.c` part `lmnode`, 400 tracers through the real table's node against the original in the memory layout of the volume records, 365 stopped by a hit, **0 differences**; the same run with zeros for the plane (the port before extractor 26) fails 365 of 400. The port's `lms` counter must be the shared register as stated in (1).

### 8.2a The hull lists `0x3DACC` (the hit volume of an aircraft)

The aircraft hull test `0x2B4BA` decides whether the tracer point is inside a **convex volume built from six planes**, whose planes are taken from the record's own rotated model vertices. The table `0x3DACC` has **three longs**, the lists for the part ids 0, 1 and 2 (the low nibble of `+0x7B`, note 04a section 8a: 0 the near body block, 1 the coarser block, 2 the coarsest; the F/A-18 uses the ids 0, 1, 2 and the MiG-class templates 3, 6 and 7 the ids 0, `0x21`, `0x32`, 04a), pointing to three lists in the model data of the image at `0x3DAFA`, `0x3DD28` and `0x3DD94`. [V: `weapon_check.py hull`, a model written from the lists read out of the image against the original's `0x2B4BA` on 3,000 random records (part ids 0, 1, 2, `0x10` to `0x12`, `0x80`; unit shifts 0 to 5; random vertex arrays, 836 inside), 0 differences; 600 + 6,000 in other seeds.]

**Layout of a list**: seven triples of words (42 bytes): **six face triples** and a terminator triple whose first word has bit 15 set (the other two words are not read). A face triple (a, b, c) holds three **byte offsets into the vertex array of the record**, the array that starts at record `+0xA4` with 6 bytes (three words x, y, z) per vertex slot; the offset is relative to `+0xA4`, so slot k is offset 6k.

* **form A** (bit 14 of a clear): the plane passes through vertex a and its normal is `V[c] - V[b]` (the 16-bit differences of the three components);
* **form B** (bit 14 of a set; the vertex is `a & 0x3FFF`): the plane passes through vertex a and its normal is the cross product of `V[b] - V[a]` and `V[c] - V[a]` with each component computed in 32 bits as `u*v' - u'*v` and shifted right by 4 (logical), the low word kept.

The decoded lists, as vertex slot numbers (form; plane point; b, c): list 0 (`0x3DAFA`): A 9; 3, 5 | B 0; 1, 2 | B 0; 9, 1 | B 0; 2, 9 | B 9; 7, 1 | B 9; 2, 16. List 1 (`0x3DD28`): A 8; 3, 5 | B 0; 1, 2 | B 0; 8, 1 | B 0; 2, 8 | B 8; 6, 1 | B 8; 2, 10. List 2 (`0x3DD94`): A 4; 1, 0 | B 0; 2, 3 | B 0; 4, 2 | B 0; 3, 4 | B 4; 1, 2 | B 4; 3, 1 (list 2's form A normal is `V[0] - V[1]`). The slots up to 16 are read, so the vertex array must hold 17 slots (`+0xA4` to `+0x10A`).

**The test**: for the part id p = `+0x7B` of the record under test (`V:4AA70`): **negative (`0xFF`): no hit, nothing is read** (the far level and the cockpit block); the list is `0x3DACC[p & 15]`; the vertex scale shift sh = (`+0x7D` & 15) - (p >> 4), at least 0 (a coarser block has its vertices halved per level: the part ids `0x21` and `0x32` of 04a read the lists 1 and 2 with shifts reduced by 2 and 3). The tracer point is T = (position x shifted right by 8 logically, y as a long shifted right by 8, z likewise; x and z are 16-bit words). For each face in order: Q = the plane point vertex (the three words) shifted right by sh (arithmetic), plus the record's fine position (`+0x0C` word, `+0x10` long, `+0x0E` word); d = T - Q per axis (the 16-bit differences); the face passes when `n . d` (a 32-bit sum of three 16-bit products) is **zero or positive**. The first face that fails ends the test (no hit); all six passing reaches the terminator and is a **hit**.

**The part ids of all sixteen templates** [V: `template_parts.py`, a static scan of the template list at `0x25ECC` and of each template's detail list in the grammar of note 02 2.1: for every level the block the leaf names, the first word of the block (its low byte is stored at `+0x7B`) and the block's flag byte; the result agrees with 04a 8a for the templates 3, 4, 6 and 7 that were read before]. The id of a level is the low byte of the block's first word:

| template (model) | ids of the levels, nearest first | low nibbles |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0 (AIM-9L), 1 (AIM-120) | `0xFF` at every level (blocks with flag byte `0x01`) | F |
| 2 and 13 (the cone objects: the rescue pod's two models) | `0x00` | 0 |
| 3, 6, 7 (the MiG class) | `0x00`, `0x21`, `0x21`, `0x32`, `0xFF` | 0, 1, 2, F |
| 4 (the F/A-18) | `0x00`, `0x00`, `0x01`, `0x02`, `0xFF` | 0, 1, 2, F |
| 5, 14, 15 | `0x00`, `0x00`, `0x00`, `0x00`, `0xFF` | 0, F |
| 8, 9, 12 (ships) | `0xFF` at every level (flag byte `0x11`) | F |
| 10 | `0xFF` at every level | F |
| 11 (the parachute) | `0x00` | 0 |

So **the low nibbles that occur are 0, 1, 2 and `0xF`**: the three-entry hit table `0x3DACC` is enough, no record can take the index past it (the earlier fear is closed), and `0xF` is only ever the id `0xFF`, which is negative and gives no hit. The ids with a high nibble (`0x21`, `0x32`) reduce the vertex shift as above. **Consequence for the missiles:** the AIM-9L and the AIM-120 draw only blocks whose id is `0xFF`, so after any completed draw their `+0x7B` is negative and **the aircraft hull test returns no hit for them: the gun cannot hit an AIM-120 either** (see 8.2, "A tracer against an AMRAAM"). The records of the ships (8, 9, 12) and template 10 can never be hit by the aircraft test for the same reason; the big-hull test applies to them.

**What the port needs.** Vertex slots 0 to 16 of every drawn aircraft record, i.e. the rotated model vertices that the display builder writes at `+0xA4` (note 05 `+0xA4` row: slot k = the model's vertex k rotated by the record's attitude, `(M . v_k) >> 8` with M the attitude matrix `>> 6`, in the model's own unit; the draw pass of note 03/04a writes them when the record's program runs). The hull test reads the values of the last pass in which the record was drawn; a record that is not in queue C is not tested at all (8.2). The three lists are 126 bytes of tables: **Task 4b exports them** (obligations in the report; the extractor must not copy model vertices into the document).

### 8.3 Guidance: the destination, the steering and the turn rate

**Where the destination is written.** The destination words of a missile are the cell words `+0x2C`, `+0x2E`, the fine words `+0x30`, `+0x32` and the height long `+0x34` (the layout of a position, 1.4); the steering of section 3 turns the vector to them into rate targets. Who writes them [V: gate model, 6,000 records; `cm_check.py` part C for the decoy branch, 500 missiles]:

| missile | writer | rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **the AI's missile** (W0 bit `0x0008` clear) | `0x2826A` (guidance, every pass of the boost phase) | sets W0 bit 0; the target byte `+0x38` low seven bits give the record (bit 7 is not tested); for the index 0 (the player) and a non-zero decoy reference (`V:4A944` for class 0, `V:4A945` for the other classes; 9.4) the destination is the decoy slot and the near test of 9.4 follows; otherwise the destination is the record's cell, fine and height words. If the missile's valid bit is clear, bit 7 of W0 is cleared (the on-ground bit: a dead branch) |
| **the player's missile** (W0 bit `0x0008` set, spawn flag set) | `0x27E7E` (the live retarget) | reads the **player's current designation `V:4AA78`** every pass: not `0xFFFF` and not the missile's own record offset: the destination is that record's cell, fine and height words and `+0x38` = (offset >> 9) | `0x80`; `0xFFFF` or its own offset: the speed target `0x4200` with the destination height 0 (see "no target") |
| coast phase | none | 8.1 step 4 |

**The live retarget in the emulator** [V, milestone 7: `combat7-retarget.txt` (generator `gen-combat7.py retarget`: the staged Sidewinder run of `followup-combat-kills-missile.txt` with the designation `V:4AA78` poked to `0xFFFF` in the samples 5 to 8 and back to the target in the samples 9 to 12, six video frames a sample)]: with the designation dropped the missile (record 1, class 0, W0 `0x10CA`, target byte `0x88`) keeps its destination cell and fine words and **clears the destination height at once** (3566 to 0 in the next sample) while its speed word climbs by `0x420` a pass towards `0x4200` (`0x1280`, `0x16A0`, `0x1AC0`, `0x1EE0`), and with the designation set again the destination words follow the target record from the next pass (fine words 1305, 147 to 1197, 348 and then by about 27 and 50 a pass, the height 3701 and up), the target byte unchanged. This is the W0 bit 1 case of the target handling that note 07 Part A listed as read only.

So the player's missile **follows the designation, not the target it was fired at**: re-designating (T, note 06) in flight retargets it the next pass, and a designation that is dropped (the drop `0x26F28` after the designated record is destroyed or out of control, section 4.7) leaves it with the last destination cell and height 0. The launch itself needs no designation (7.6) and a missile fired with `V:4AA78` = `0xFFFF` has the destination x `0xFFFF` of the launch: **the steering returns at once for a destination x below 0** (`0x30BE8`), so it flies straight on its launch heading [V: 8.5].

**No target.** The retarget with no designation takes the speed branch of the coast (`0x27E06`) with the target `0x4200`: after the boost's `+0x1E0` the speed word moves by `0x240` more per pass towards `0x4200`, i.e. `0x420` per pass in all (rig, owner speed `0x1096` after its step: `0x14B6` in the launch pass, `0x18D6`, ..., `0x4200` in the 12th pass), and the destination height is cleared to 0 every pass.

**The steering.** The state routine of section 3 runs for every missile after the gate (flight step, 3.1) in state 0. For the class nibble 0 it first decreases `+0x26` (set to 20 at the launch, 7.6) and **returns without steering while the value is 10 or more** (`0x30BFE`): the first ten passes of a missile's life fly straight, the first steered pass is the eleventh (where `+0x26` reads 9). The steering is the generic one of 3.5 and 3.6 for a non-aircraft class: the lateral axis gives a *rudder* target and the vertical axis a *pitch* target taken from the table `0x31316` (160 to 1,280 in eleven steps by the size of the angular error, negated by its sign), stored at `+0x52` and `+0x50`; the roll target `+0x54` comes from the roll command (3.6) and the levelling of the conversion (3.8). A class 0 record has no stick: the targets go straight to the response below.

**The turn rate.** `0x31D12` (the response for non-aircraft classes) converts the targets into the rates `+0x56`, `+0x58`, `+0x5A` [V: `cm_check.py` part D, 500 missiles, 11 divisor kinds, 0 differences, section 9.5; and the world-update ramp of the cm single runs]:

1. the divisor D: **10** when B20 bit 5 (the lost mark, 8.4) is set; else **5** for a missile of class other than 1 (in play the Sidewinder, class 0) whose target byte is the player's (index 0) while the ECM `V:4A96D` is on; else `+0x26` (limited to 15); **no division** when `+0x26` is 0 or below;
2. each target is divided by D (a signed 16-bit divide, truncating);
3. the low pass: new rate = ((old + q) / 2 + q) / 2 in 16-bit words with arithmetic shifts (q the quotient).

With the ramp the divisor is 9 at the first steered pass and falls by one per pass: **the turn authority grows from one ninth of the table limit to the full limit over nine passes** (the first rate for a command of -1280 is -107 = ((0 + q) / 2 + q) / 2 with q = -142). The full limit is the table's `1280` per pass in the rate units of note 05 section 6. An ECM-jammed hostile Sidewinder keeps the divisor 5 for ever (a constant fifth), a lost missile 10.

The **maximum turn** of a missile is therefore the table limit (1,280 per pass per axis at the full ramp). The staged runs of 8.5 show the consequence: with a target 30 passes of the player's velocity ahead the missile hits, with the same target 20 and 60 passes ahead it passes it (closest approaches 148 and 202 du), the ramp and the limited rate deciding.

### 8.4 The contact, the hits and the counters of the missiles

A missile is a mover of the contact scan (10.2); its box against an aircraft (or the player) is `0x10000` outer / `0x5000` inner (256 and 80 du), against the cruise missile (class `0x15` exactly) `0x5000` / `0x1000`, against a ship `0xA0000` with the deck tests. **The scan compares the other record's position with the mover's position *before* this step's move** (the previous position), so a missile moving 264 du per pass checks only where it was at the start of the pass: the hit needs that position within 80 du of the target (the other's own displacement halved is added back as the swept test). A missile therefore **can fly through a target** between two passes without a hit [R from the code; the staged runs of 8.5 give closest approaches of 67 du (a hit) and 148 and 202 du (misses)]. Nothing else, no proximity fuse, exists: the proximity rule below only marks the missile as having lost its target.

What a hit does is 10.3 (the other record's marks, the counters, the removal of the mover) and is verified in `damage_check.py` part C (1,800 pairs) and by 8.5: for the player's missile and a counted hostile the hostile gets W0 `0x0200` (replaced by the dying bit `0x0400` in its own next step, section 5), B20 bit 1, `V:54E72`, W2 `0x1000`, state 1; the log counter `+0x44` (Sidewinder hit) or `+0x40` (AMRAAM hit) gains 1 when the other is a counted record; the missile is removed in the same pass (valid bit clear, `V:4A910` = `0x0C`). **Correction to 5.1**: the missile's contact code compares the masked class nibble with `0x15` at `0x2B7B4` and so never exempts the cruise missile (the sentence of 5.1 "classes `0x15` and class nibble 0 get only the crashed bit" holds for the **gun** and for a missile as the other record, not for a cruise missile hit by a missile; 10.3 rule 1 already states it). [V: `weapon_check.py hits`, 3,000 pairs (movers 0, 1, 2, 9; classes `0x00`, `0x01`, `0x10` to `0x15`; W0, groups, displacements) of the scan `0x2B5E2` with the effect code, 247 hits, 0 differences, the class `0x15` other included.]

**The lost mark.** The result code of the flight step (`0x2AA28`) copies the proximity flag `V:4A957` of the scan into W2 bit 1 of the mover each pass. When the flag is clear and W2 bit 1 **was** set (the missile was inside another record's outer box last pass and is not now) and the record's class nibble is 0, the code sets **B20 bit 5**, the lost mark, and clears `V:4A96C` (the fire-prompt state). B20 bit 5 is never cleared; it selects the divisor 10 in 8.3 and nothing else. (The decoy near test of 9.4 sets it too.)

**Hostile missiles.** The AI's launch (`0x275F6`, 7.6) copies the owner (the target byte `+0x38` = the owner's, which for a hostile after the player is `0x80`, the player) and picks the kind from the owner's range class: high nibble 3 a class 1 (long-range, life 200) missile, any other a class 0 (short-range, life 150). The radar walk (12.3, 12.4) sets the **threat bit 4** for a class 0 missile whose target byte is the viewed aircraft and **bit 5** for any other class, only while the missile's W0 bit 3 is clear (a player's own missile never raises them). The missile flies 8.1 and 8.3 exactly like the player's except that its destination comes from `0x2826A` (the AI path, decoys 9.4) and it ignores the designation. It hits the player through the **player's** scan (the player is record 0 and the scan visits only records after the mover, so a hostile missile is the *other* record): 10.2 gives the box, 10.3 rule 4 the log counter **`+0x48`** (`0x2C082`: plus 1 when the mover is the player and the other is a class nibble 0 record that is counted), the other (the missile) gets W0 `0x0200` only (a class nibble 0 other is not marked out of control) and dies by its own gate (8.1 step 3) or the dying path of 5.4a; the player runs the damage handler of 10.4 (a contact at an indicated speed of `0x3C0` or less does nothing; otherwise the parity of the frame counter chooses the engine failure or only a message, 10.4). The decoy rules 9.4 and the ECM divisor 9.5 are the defences.

### 8.5 Review Focus 1: no target, a target that dies, a target out of reach

`weapon_check.py focus` launches a Sidewinder from the mode-3 air scene through the **world update** (`V:4A872` = 1; the placed MiG-29 in record 8 made inert, speed word `0x1200`, no velocity, put 30 passes of the player's velocity ahead; the other cases as stated) and follows record 1 pass by pass [V: one run per case, asserted]:

| case | what the original does | result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **no target designated** (`V:4AA78` = `0xFFFF`) | the launch gives the destination x `0xFFFF`; the steering returns at once, the missile flies straight on the owner's heading; the speed word follows 8.1 (step 5 plus the no-target ramp: `0x420` per pass to `0x4200` by the 12th pass), coasts to `0x3000` from 50 passes left and carries W0 `0x0400` for the last 11; the destination word stays `0xFFFF` for all 150 passes | the record is **freed in pass 150** (W0 = 0, `V:4A910` = `0x0C`); never longer |
| **the target dies in flight** (the hostile's record freed after pass 13) | the drop `0x26F28` sets `V:4AA78` = `0xFFFF` in the next pass; the live retarget finds no designation and the destination height becomes 0 for every later pass while the destination cell stays that of the last position; the missile dives, strikes the ground in pass 40 (W0 bit `0x8000`, the dying bit; over land the impact creates an effect object, section 11.2), the dying count of 15 follows | **freed in pass 55** (15 passes after the impact pass) |
| **a target out of reach** (1,500 passes of velocity ahead) | the missile steers to the designation for the full boost, coasts and expires | **freed in pass 150**, the life running out |
| **a hit** (30 passes ahead; the closest approach is 67 du) | in pass 35 the scan finds the pre-move position inside the inner box: the missile is removed (valid bit clear, `V:4A910` = `0x0C`), `+0x44` is 1 (the fired counter `+0x42` was 1), the hostile has W0 `0x0600` bits, B20 bit 1, state 1, `V:54E72` = its offset; the designation is dropped | the hostile's dying count is 15 passes: **`V:4A963` becomes 1 at the expiry** (the airborne wreck is counted and kept, 5.3) |

So the original never leaves a missile alive past 150 or 200 passes and never a record valid after its expiry. What *stays behind* are the secondary effect objects of a ground impact: they are never freed (11.2), at most 16 per flight. The AI's missile ends the same way (its life, its hit or its ground impact). The Sidewinder counters count the **fired** missiles whether or not a target exists (`+0x42` at the launch, 7.6) and the **hit** counter only a contact with a counted record (10.3 rule 2: a hit on a not-counted record adds nothing).

### 8.6 The AI's launch decision (`0x2701A`) and the 30-step wait

The decision is section 4.6 with one correction found by the check: when the owner's target byte `+0x38` has **bit 7 clear** (no target; `0xFF` has bit 7 set and names record 127) the tests of the target record are **skipped** and the decision goes on to the flags. The order is [V: `weapon_check.py decision`, 3,000 random owners (records 8, 10, 12; W0 including the hit, dying and crashed bits and the engaged bit; target bytes none, valid, invalid, engaged, out of control, the owner itself; flag bytes `+0x64`; range classes; the request `V:4A874` 0/1), result and the cleared request compared, 95 yes, 0 differences; `conv_check.py` part 2 earlier, 1,000 cases]:

1. W0 mask `0x8700` (hit, crashed, dying) set, or B20 bit 1 (out of control): **no**;
2. the request `V:4A874` set: **yes** and the request is cleared;
3. W0 bit 6 (valid) clear: no;
4. a target byte with bit 7 set: the target record (bits 0 to 6) must be valid, not out of control and have W0 bit 0 (engaged) clear, else no. (A byte with bit 7 clear skips this test);
5. the flags `+0x64` must have bits 5 and 6 both set, the owner's W0 bit 0 must be set, and the range class `+0x63` high nibble must be 2 or 3: then **yes**, the request cleared.

The yes only asks for the launch; the launch 7.6 applies the rate gate (the mask of `V:4A992` by progress, the ECM halving it) and the stock. An AI aircraft therefore fires on at most one pass in 8, 4 or 4 (progress 0 or 1, 2, 3 or more; in 4, 2, 2 with the ECM on) while the decision holds.

**The 30-step wait.** The Task 5 implementer's note "the AI waits 30 steps after it had its target in front" is the **input conversion of the attitude step** (`0x31AE0`, the instructions at `0x31C60` and `0x31C66`), not a fire routine: for a record of aircraft class whose flag byte shows the target ahead (bits 5 and 6 together, 3.8) and that is in state 0 or 1, the conversion sets the state byte to 6 and the counter `+0x4C` to **30** and levels the wings (note 07 3.8, item 4). The wait does not gate firing: the decision above is asked every pass, and its conditions (flags 5 and 6, the engaged bit) hold during the wait, so the aircraft fires while its controls are held; a state 6 counter then runs down and state 6 falls into state 1 with the counter 30 again (3.3). [V: `conv_check.py` part 1, 3,000 cases, 0 differences; the writer search of the image found the only stores of state 6 (`0x309F6`, the end routine) and of the counter 30 (`0x30B62`, `0x30B70` the scripted commands, `0x31C66` the conversion).]

### 8.7 The gun pipper (`0x3878E`)

The gun sight is the projection of an **aim point two calls old**. At each call (the common tail of the cockpit HUD pass `0x385F4`, section 12.7) the routine projects the point stored in `V:4A7DA` (the point computed two calls ago, because the shift below happens after the projection) through the viewing matrix `V:4AC90`, taking the difference to the position of the viewed record (`V:4A996`) shifted right by 8 and calling the projection `0x3377C`, and stores the screen point in `V:4A9F6` (x) and `V:4A9F8` (y).
