It then computes the new point: the viewed record's position plus (`0x600` x the second column and `0x4000` x the third column of its attitude matrix at `+0x92..`, the words `+0x94/+0x96`, `+0x9A/+0x9C`, `+0xA0/+0xA2`) shifted right by 6, i.e. **24 units above and 256 units ahead of the nose** (position longs of the 1/256 unit, so the point is 24 and 256 du; the displacement shifts), copies `V:4A7CE` to `V:4A7DA` and stores the new point in `V:4A7CE` [V: `hud_target_check.py` part D, 200 runs against the original's own projection, 0 differences, section 12.7]. The sight is a fixed line along the nose with a lag of two passes; it is **not** the tracer's ballistic aim (the tracer leaves with the direction (64, 1024) of 7.5, an elevation of 1/16, the sight's is 24/256, 3/32). The pipper pixel and the gun's fire prompt (12.7) use the stored point.

### 8.8 What Task 6 implements from sections 7 and 8

* The gate state machine of 8.1 for classes 0 and 1 with the launch life 150 or 200; the speed rules; the coast (`0x3000`, W0 `0x0400` below 12, the destination height 0); expiry freeing the record with `V:4A910` = `0x0C`; the `V:4A840`/pause conditions.
* The live retarget of the player's missile from the designation each pass, with the `0xFFFF`/own-offset no-target branch; the AI missile's destination `0x2826A` with the decoy branch.
* The steering with the 10-pass blind period (`+0x26` from 20), the table limit, the divisor ramp, the lost mark, the ECM divisor and the low pass of `0x31D12`.
* The gun hit test with the queue C walk (at most 6 entities, the owner skip, the cell and valid tests, the `0x140`/`0x800` box, 8 sub-steps), the hull test with the three lists of 8.2a and the vertex slots 0 to 16 of the drawn record, the three-hit kill with the class `0x15` rule of the gun, the big-hull and landmark stops without effect.
* The missile contact with the previous-position rule (10.2), the marks of 10.3 with the nibble test that exempts nothing for a missile, the proximity mark and the lost mark of 8.4.
* The AI launch decision (the target byte bit 7 skip) and the rate gate; the pipper of 8.7.
* Review Focus 1: the timeline of 8.5, exactly: freed in pass 150 or 200 after the launch, 15 passes after the impact pass of a ground impact, in the hit pass for a hit.

* Rig, `tools/m68k-rig/tests/lists_model/weapon_check.py` (inputs git-ignored: `extracted/interceptor.bin`, `librig.so`, the logged air scene; run under `ulimit -v 2500000` and `timeout`): `gun` 3,000 cases (the pool, the reservoir, the slot step, the initialiser), `launch` 3,000 (1,303 launched), `gate` 6,000, `hits` 3,000 (247 hits), `hull` 3,000 (836 inside) + 6,000, `entity` 40,000 (245 hits, 72 kills), `focus` (the four cases of 8.5, asserted), `decision` 3,000; all 0 differences. `cm_check.py` parts C and D (500 each) for the decoy branch and the rate divisor.
* Emulator (scripts in `tools/vamiga-ref/scripts/`, logs git-ignored in `extracted/captures/logs/`, generators `gen-combat-fire.py` and `gen-combat-hit.py`; rows in note 01a):
  * **The demo's AIM-120** (`combat-fire-demo.txt`, 70 samples of one video frame from the demo snapshot at its fire event): the request `V:4A872` = 1 at pass `0x0257`; the stock `0x24` becomes `0x23` (the AMRAAM, class 1, selector `0x3B`); record 1 reads W0 `0x10CA`, life 199 (200 less the launch pass), target byte `0x8A` (record 10) and destination cells (68, 70) equal to the target's cells; the speed word `0x2443`, `0x2623`, `0x2803`, `0x29E3`, `0x2BC3`, `0x2DA3` in the following passes, **`+0x1E0` per pass** (8.1 step 5), the life 199, 198, ... one less per pass, `+0x26` 19 falling by one per pass, the pitch and yaw rates `+0x56`, `+0x58` still 0 (the blind period; the roll target `+0x54` = -3005 and the roll rate `+0x5A` = -150, -188, -197, -200, -200, -211 already run: the levelling of the conversion, 3.8); the missile moves 36,000 to 42,000 position units per pass at those speeds.
  * **The demo's AIM-120 hits the MiG and the kill is counted** (`combat-hit-demo.txt`, 150 samples of 3 video frames, and `combat-hit-demolong.txt`, 215 samples with records 1 and 10 only, both from the demo snapshot at the fire event): launch in pass `0x0257` (log `+0x3E` = 1); the speed reaches `0x4200` in pass `0x0267` (life 183), `+0x26` falls below 0 (no division); the MiG (record 10, state byte 9, W0 `0x50C1`) is hit in pass **`0x027C`**, 37 passes after the launch (life 162): the missile's valid bit is clear (W0 `0x509A`); the MiG has W0 `0x54C1` (the dying bit; the crashed bit set by the hit is already converted in the MiG's own step of the same pass), **W2 `0x1001`** (bit `0x1000` and the outer-box bit), **B20 `0x42`**, state byte 1, **`V:54E72` = `0x1400`**, its counter `+0x4C` = 15, and the log counter **`+0x40` (AMRAAM hit) = 1** (`+0x44` stays 0); the designation `V:4AA78` is dropped. **Fifteen passes later, in pass `0x028B`, `V:4A963` becomes 1** (W0 `0x70C1`: the airborne wreck is counted and kept, 5.3 step 5): the first counted hostile destroyed by the original's own weapon in the emulator (note 07 open question A4). Every mark and counter of 10.3 and 8.4 is as the rig said.
  * **The gun kills** (`combat-hit-gunkill.txt`, the gun capture with the MiG's `+0x3C` poked to 2): the first tracer hit in pass `0x00ED` gives the third hit: W0 `0x52C1` (crashed), **W2 `0x1000`, B20 `0x42`, state 1, `V:54E72` = `0x1000`**, the log counter `+0x3C` = 1; the next pass a second tracer hits (`+0x3C` = 2: **a crashed record is still hit and counted**) and the MiG's W0 is `0x54C1` (dying); **`V:4A963` becomes 1 in pass `0x00FD`**, fifteen passes after the dying start; the MiG's own `+0x3C` stays `0x02` (the kill stores no nibble). 8.2 as stated.
  * **A Sidewinder that misses a staged MiG** (`combat-hit-missilehit.txt`, 200 samples of 3 frames; the MiG 120 passes of the player's velocity ahead, about 5,800 du, designated): the same flight as above (blind to pass `0x00F4`, then the ramp); the MiG keeps its AI (its distance to the player changes, its state byte reads 0 then 6), the missile passes it at 167 du at the sample resolution (pass `0x0110`), takes the lost mark and flies on to 6,694 du: no hit, `+0x44` = 0. Why the guidance missed (the MiG's own movement, the ramp) was not traced; the demo MiG above was hit.
  * **A Sidewinder that passes its target** (`combat-hit-missile.txt`, 150 samples of 3 video frames; `airborne.vasnap` with the placed MiG-29 of the mode-3 row poked into record 8 about 1,450 du ahead, inert, designated by `V:4AA78` = `0x1000`, the Sidewinder fired by SPACE at sample 3): launch in pass `0x00EA` (`V:1D024`'s `+0x42` = 1, life 149, speed `0x127B`); `+0x26` 19, 18, ... one per pass; **the rates stay 0 until `+0x26` reads 9 in pass `0x00F4`, the eleventh pass**; then the rudder target `+0x52` is 1280 (the table limit) and the rate `+0x58` is 106, 146, 173, 203, 242, 300, 394, 578 (the divisor 9, 8, ..., 2 and the low pass: ((0 + 142) / 2 + 142) / 2 = 106, then ((106 + 160) / 2 + 160) / 2 = 146: **8.3 reproduced exactly**); the closest approach is 228 du in pass `0x00F9` (W2 `0x2002`: the proximity bit set in passes `0x00F9` and `0x00FA`), in pass `0x00FB` W2 bit 1 is cleared and **B20 bit 5 is set** (`0x60`: the lost mark, 8.4) and the rate falls to 240, 156, 135, 129, **128 = 1280 / 10** (the divisor 10 for ever); the missile flies on to 9,355 du and is still alive at sample 149 (life 93). No hit: the original's missile that passes its target between 80 and 256 du is lost. `+0x44` stays 0.
  * **A gun kill from a fresh MiG, and the kill counted again** (`followup-combat-kills.txt`, generator `gen-followup-combat.py gun`: `airborne.vasnap`, a MiG placed by the mode-3 row into record 8 as a formation member 12 passes of the player's velocity ahead (the player's attitude and speed words, W0 `0x1140`, W2 `0x0100`, throttle byte 90 so that it keeps its place about 500 du ahead; hit counter 0; designated by `V:4AA78` = `0x1000`), the gun held from sample 3, 170 samples of 6 video frames): the tracers hit in pass 239: the log counter `+0x3C` and the MiG's `+0x3C` nibble read 1 and 2 in two consecutive samples; **the third hit, in pass 240, marks the MiG**: W0 `0x5241` (crashed), W2 `0x1100`, B20 `0x42`, state 1, `V:54E72` = `0x1000`, the log counter 3; in pass 241 the dying bit is set with the counter 15 (W0 `0x5441`; the crashed bit is converted) and **the designation is dropped** (`V:4AA78` = `0xFFFF`; the pass after the crash, 12.5); the counter holds 15 for three passes and then counts down 14, 13, ... to 0 in pass 258, where **`V:4A963` becomes 1** (W0 `0x5051`, then `0x5041`: the dying bit and the crashed bit gone; the MiG is an airborne wreck and is kept, 5.3 rule 5). The gun goes on hitting the kept wreck (the staged MiG does not fall: it is not AI valid): the next hit in pass 264 sets the crashed bit again (`0x0200`, the nibble stays 2, the log counter 6, 7, 8 for the further tracers) and **the dying count restarts at 15 and ends in a second count: `V:4A963` = 2 in pass 283, = 3 in pass 307** (24 to 25 passes per cycle: 15 of the count plus the passes until the next tracer arrives). So **a counted hostile that is shot down and whose wreck is still in the air is counted again for every later dying cycle that the gun starts**: the kill counter of a mission can exceed the number of hostile aircraft (a candidate for the F6 victory test of note 08, carried item C4: four counted kills where three counted fighters exist; not tested). The rig's `dying_check.py` part 3 shows the same by a collision result; the gun is the other way (no hit bit, the record kept for ever). [Rule for the port: the gun's hit effect on a record with the nibble at 2 sets the crashed bit again for every hit, whatever the dying state; the step converts it to dying when the dying bit is clear; the expiry counts again for an airborne counted aircraft; no `V:4A963` limit.]
  * **A staged Sidewinder kill** (`followup-combat-kills-missile.txt`, `gen-followup-combat.py missile`: the same formation MiG 40 passes of the player's velocity ahead (about 1,950 du), designated, the Sidewinder selected by poking `E+0x63` and fired by SPACE at sample 3, 200 samples of 6 frames): launch in pass 234 (the log counter `+0x42` = 1, the first weapon record W0 `0x10DA`, then `0x50CA`), **hit in pass 262, 28 passes after the launch** (the missile's valid bit gone, W0 `0x509A`; the log counter `+0x44` = 1), and in the same sample the MiG carries the marks of 8.4 and 10.3: W0 `0x5441` (the crashed bit already converted into the dying bit), W2 `0x1101`, B20 `0x42`, state 1, `V:54E72` = `0x1000`, the counter 15, and **the designation `V:4AA78` is dropped** (`0xFFFF`); the counter runs 15 to 0 and **`V:4A963` becomes 1 in pass 277**, 15 passes after the hit, the MiG kept as an airborne wreck (W0 `0x5041`). So the staged Sidewinder hits and kills when the target holds its place (the earlier staged Sidewinders missed because the player flew past an inert target at the speed of the approach); the target dead ahead and in step gives the guidance of 8.3 a straight approach, and no lost mark was set.
  * **The gun against a MiG** (`combat-hit-gun.txt`, 150 samples of 3 video frames; the same MiG about 600 du ahead, the gun selected by poking `E+0x63`, SPACE held from sample 3): `+0x3A` 3, 6, 9, 12, 15 (three per shot, five shots in the burst), the second burst starting 21 passes later (18, 21, ... 45); **two tracers hit** in pass `0x00ED`: the log counter `+0x3C` = 2 and the MiG's own `+0x3C` = `0x02`; the third tracer missed (the aircraft flew past the MiG at 188 du); no mark yet on the MiG (B20 bit 1 clear, state 0).

### 8.10 The record's vertex slots (the draw store, `0x21F68` onwards)

The hull tests of 8.2, 8.2a and 10.2 read the rotated vertex slots at record `+0xA4` (6 bytes each, up to 58). The slots are written by the entity drawer `0x21C44` when it draws a record; the rules of that store, found by running the original's drawer on one record against the port's [V: `tests/rig/test_slots_diff.c` (`make test-slots`): 3 seeds x 3,600 = 10,800 cases, 0 differences in the 58 slots, the part id `+0x7B` and the record after; the default run uses 2,700 cases; code read for each rule]:

1. **Gating.** The store runs only for a block whose flag bit 0 is set, and not for a **dying record whose counter `+0x4C` is 12 or less**: `0x21F68` leaves for `0x22772` before the vertex loop (a dying record with the counter above 12 still stores; at 14 it also sets the sound variables `V:4A921`, `V:4A922`, `V:4A870`).
2. **Which vertices.** Only the **block's own vertices**, the leading run of the level's vertex array, are stored. The synthesised slots of the extractor and the extra vertices of the items are **not** in the record.
3. **The record copy of the synthesis ops.** Ops 42, 43, 44 and 59 (with their base operand) and ops 56, 63, 64, 65 (base 0) run a copy of their formulas on the **record's rotated slots**, in program order, in 16-bit word arithmetic (the formulas of note 03's vertex-synthesis tables); the other synthesis ops (34, 35, 36, 38, 41, 45, 46, 47) have no copy in the record.
4. **Nothing is written past the record.** The results the ops 42 and 59 give for the slots 100 and 110 are written nowhere: the record after the draw is unchanged in `+0x00` to `+0xA3` and beyond the slot array.
5. **The trailing data slot** (entity blocks only: the record store belongs to the entity drawer `0x21C44` and its loop reads three words per vertex; the flat blocks, flag bit 1, two words per vertex, are terrain models drawn by the terrain routines, never stored into a record, and none is an entity model, so the rule has no flat case) [R]. The vertex loop is driven by the block header's two counts, b8 (byte `+8`, vertices transformed) and b9 (byte `+9`, those also projected): it stores one vertex before the loop, then b9 more, then b8 - b9 - 1 more while that is positive. **(The reviewer of the port's fourth round reads this as "the loop always writes one slot past the block's vertex count b8", and the rig agrees on 6,000 cases; the two readings coincide on every shipped 3D block, where b9 = b8, note 02 2.2. They differ only for a block with b8 > b9, none in the image, where the control flow gives b8 slots; the port may take b8 + 1 as the rule.)** A block with b8 = b9 therefore stores b8 + 1 slots: the (b8+1)-th is the rotated data triple that follows the vertex list (not a vertex, nothing reads it); a block with b8 > b9 stores exactly b8 slots; b8 < b9 abandons the draw (the error post `0x17`).** [R: the control flow of `0x21FE6`-`0x221E2`; consistent with the 4-vertex cockpit block that stores 5 slots, 04a 8a.]

## 9. Countermeasures

Flares, chaff and the ECM switch are three keys with a counter or a switch each. The flare and the chaff are not objects of their own: the key only sets a request byte, and the gun routine (7.3) turns the request into a slot of the tracer pool (7.2) with the flags `0x1000` (flare) or `0x2000` (chaff). The only consumers of those slots are the steering of hostile missiles aimed at the player (9.4) and the draw scan. The ECM switch changes the steering of the same missiles (9.5) and the firing rate of the AI (7.6). Times are passes of the world update (one per frame routine, section 7). The rig counts are from `tools/m68k-rig/tests/lists_model/cm_check.py` and the emulator captures are `combat-cm-keys.txt` (see Verified by).

### 9.1 The three keys

The key bodies are reached through the event bits of note 08 section 7.3 (word 1, bits 1, 2 and 3: `0x1EE0C` F, `0x1EE9E` C, `0x1EF1E` J); with the shift flag set the F body is the rescue-pod deployment of mode 6 and does not touch the flare store. They run in a pause as well (note 06, "Which handlers act in a pause": the counters fall in a pause). Each body first sets an event bit of `V:4AA53` (bit 1 flare, bit 2 chaff, bit 3 ECM: the key-click events of the display) and ends with the common tail that queues the event echo.

| key | store | rule on a press | post | request | tag |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| F flare | `V:4A905`, 16 after a rearm or a mission start | the byte is decreased by 1; if the result is above 0 (signed) the release happens: the flare cool-down `V:4A907` = 30, the request byte `V:4A943` = 2, post `0x4026`; if the result is 0 or below the store is set to 0, nothing is released, the request and the cool-down are not touched and `0x4027` is posted | `0x4026` flare released / `0x4027` no flares (message entries 38 and 39 of note 06) | 2 | V (rig, 500 key presses of the three keys, 171 flare and 167 chaff presses: counts 0 to 20, requests 0 to 2, cool-downs 0, 5, 30: 0 differences) |
| C chaff | `V:4A904`, 16 | the same with the chaff cool-down `V:4A906` = 30, the request `V:4A943` = 1, posts `0x4028` chaff dispensed / `0x4029` no chaff (entries 40 and 41) | | 1 | V (same runs) |
| J ECM | `V:4A96D` (0 or 1), 0 at the mission start (`0x1D906`) | the switch is toggled; `V:4A8F8` = 3 (redraws the ECM lamp, note 06); in the cockpit view (`V:4A83D` = 0) the click `0x37C4A` calls the sound routine `0x1A042` (nine parameters: `0x12C`, 4, 1, `0x12C`, 4, 1, 1, 2, 2; the two 4s are 2 when `V:4A88F` is non-zero) unless the cue cool-down `V:4A942` is above 0; no post [R for the sound] | none | | V (same runs, 162 toggles) |

Consequences of the rule: a full store of 16 gives **15 releases** (the 16th press finds the store at 1, decreases it to 0 and refuses); every later press posts the "none" message again (the post routine accepts repeats here: the key bodies do not test `V:4AB98`); a refused press does not set the cool-down; the request byte is **not** cleared by a refusal, so a press made while an earlier request is still waiting for a free slot replaces it (a flare request and then a chaff request before the gun routine runs leaves only the chaff). The stores and the request bytes are cleared or refilled only by the rearm routine (9.7). The counts are not shown anywhere on the instruments (no reader of `V:4A904`/`V:4A905` besides the key bodies and the rearm).

### 9.2 From the request to a slot

The gun routine `0x172DC` (7.3) is called once per pass. With `V:4A943` non-zero it takes the first free **even** slot of the pool (7.2) provided the burst reservoir (high nibble of `E+0x7D`) is non-zero and the gun rounds `E+0x60` are at least 1. The release costs one reservoir step and **3 in the log's cannon-rounds counter** (`+0x3A`, note 08 10.9) and sets the log-dirty flag `V:4A87D`, but no rounds, no readout redraw and no reload timer. Then `V:4A943` = 0 and the reference byte of the kind is set to the slot number plus 1: `V:4A944` for a flare, `V:4A945` for chaff. A request that finds no usable slot, no reservoir or no rounds stays pending and is retried every pass (the reservoir is refilled by the rule of 7.3 once the reload timer has run out; the timer is not set by a release, so a request that was refused only for want of a reservoir is served in the pass after the refill); the sound `0x1A0DC(0x1C, 0x30)` plays at the release unless `V:4A865` is set. The reference bytes name **one** flare and **one** chaff slot, the most recent of each kind: a second flare overwrites `V:4A944` and the first flare is no longer a target (it still falls and is drawn until its life ends). At most one flare and one chaff are targets at any time.

[V: `cm_check.py` part B, 500 random cases (227 releases) with random attitude words, velocity, reservoir, rounds, reload timer and 0 to 10 slots taken, flare and chaff: the slot flags, life, owner, `V:4A944`/`V:4A945`, `V:4A943`, the reservoir (including the refill to 5 when nothing could fire and the timer is 1 or less), the rounds, the log counter, the sound call count and the dirty flag, then position, step vector, sub-step vector, cells and latch after the creation pass, then 5 further passes: 0 differences.]

### 9.3 The life of a decoy

The initialiser (7.5) gives a flare the offset (0, -8, 0) and a chaff the offset (0, -8, -56) in the model frame, the launch direction (0, -64, 0), the speed parameters 13 and 5 and the player's velocity on top. The step (7.4) applies gravity and drag as 7.4 states. The rules the guidance depends on, verified by the rig against the original (part B: 227 flares and chaff created and followed for 6 passes; part E: 500 flares and chaff followed to their end, 122 of which reached the ground):

| rule | flare | chaff | tag |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| life at creation (passes in which the slot is active) | 30: active in the creation call and the 29 that follow; in call 30 the active bit is cleared and `V:4A944` cleared | 60, `V:4A945` cleared in call 60 | V (rig and emulator `combat-cm-keys`: flag word `0x1401`/`0x2401`, life 30/60) |
| vertical step vector per pass | minus 256 (gravity), no drag in y | gravity cancelled; all three components lose a quarter per pass (`v - v/4`, arithmetic shift): the chaff stops falling after about 20 passes and hangs | V |
| horizontal step vector | loses a 64th per pass | loses a quarter per pass | V |
| first pass after creation | the velocity is updated but the position does not change (the latch `+0x24`) | same | V |
| hits the ground (y below 0 after a move, including the creation pass for a low aircraft) | flags `(f & ~0x18) \| 2` (word `0x1403`), life 2, the position backed up to y = 0 along the step (x and z moved by the step component times the penetration over the vertical step, 16-bit signed divisions, a quotient that overflows 16 bits is replaced by the low word of the dividend), no further moves; the active bit is cleared in the second pass after the impact pass (the slot stays a valid target meanwhile) | same (flag word `0x2403`) | V (rig part E, 500 cases of which 122 reached the ground, 0 differences) |
| cell crossing | the cell word `+0x30`/`+0x32` is incremented or decremented (wrapping 127 to 0 and 0 to 127) and 0x400000 added or taken off | same | V |

The slot also takes part in the hit test of the tracer pool (8.2): the test has no condition on the slot kind, so a flare or chaff inside the model volume of a queued aircraft or of a landmark would score like a gun hit (the log's cannon-hit counter, the record's hit counter). The decoys leave 8 position units under the aircraft with the aircraft's velocity, so a hit is possible only on an aircraft flying right under the player [V: `weapon_check.py decoy`, the entity test of 8.2 run on slots staged with the flag words `0x1401` (flare) and `0x2401` (chaff), 1,500 cases each against the model written from the note without any condition on the slot kind, whole state hunk and the 15 records compared: 12 hits each (the log counter `+0x3C` and the record's `+0x3C` nibble counted like a gun hit, a kill after three), 0 differences]. So **a flare or a chaff slot inside the volume of a queued aircraft counts as a gun hit** and the port's hit test must not look at the slot kind (it does not: `weapons.c` `hit_test` tests every active pool slot the same way; only the motion step of 7.4 reads the kind).

### 9.4 What a decoy does to a missile

The only code that reads `V:4A944` and `V:4A945` is the destination update of the missile steering `0x2826A`. It runs for a missile record in the guidance phase (life `+0x4C` of 50 or more, section 8) every pass in which the gate reaches it. Rules [V: `cm_check.py` part C, 500 random missiles (214 near and 60 far decoy cases, 136 aimed at the player without a decoy, 90 at a record): class 0 and 1, targets 0 (the player), a record, with and without decoy slots, W2 bit 0 and the lost bit random; 0 differences]:

1. The target byte `+0x38` has bit 7 set (a record index in its bits 0 to 6; the byte `0xFF` is treated as record 127 and never occurs here). With the **index 0 (the player)** the missile asks for a decoy; with any other index its destination is that record's cell, fine and height words (the plain case) and no decoy is involved.
2. The missile of **class 0** (the Sidewinder, the short-range IR missile) looks at `V:4A944` (the flare); every other class (class 1, the AMRAAM, the cruise missile cannot get here) at `V:4A945` (the chaff). If the byte is 0, no decoy exists for the missile and its destination is the player (same copy as in the plain case, with the record 0).
3. With a decoy slot s = byte - 1 the destination words are replaced by the slot's: the destination cells (`+0x2C`, `+0x2E`) = the slot's cell words, the destination fine x and z (`+0x30`, `+0x32`) = the slot's x and z shifted right by 8 (16-bit words), the destination height (`+0x34`, long) = the slot's y shifted right by 8. The missile then steers at the decoy with the ordinary steering (section 8, note 07 section 4) exactly as it would at the aircraft.
4. The **near test**: with a(x), a(y), a(z) the absolute coordinates of the slot (cell times 2^22 plus the in-cell position for x and z) and the missile's position longs, if the three differences are each at most `0x4800` (18,432 position units, 72 draw units) in magnitude, W2 bit 0 (`+0x03` bit 0) of the missile is set; otherwise, if W2 bit 0 is already set, B20 bit 5 (`0x20`) is set. W2 bit 0 is cleared for all records at the start of every pass and set during the pass by the collision scan when a record overlaps the missile's outer box (a mover earlier in the order than the missile, 10.2), so in practice: **a missile that is inside another aircraft's outer box (usually the player's) and not near the decoy loses its lock**. B20 bit 5 is the "lost" mark of a missile (it is also set by the tail of the missile's own step when the missile leaves the proximity box of another record without a hit, section 8): from then on the rate divisor is 10 instead of the ramp (9.5), and the mark is never cleared.
5. When the slot expires (`V:4A944`/`V:4A945` back to 0), the next pass the destination is the player again; the missile keeps its position, speed and the lost mark.

Which missiles take this path: only those whose steering reaches `0x2826A`, that is, **the missiles of the AI** (W0 bit `0x0008` clear: counted hostiles). The player's missiles carry the bit (they copy the player's W0 `0x11C8`), the spawn flag W0 bit 1 stays set for them for ever, and the branch `0x27E54` re-reads the designated target `V:4AA78` every pass instead (section 8): they ignore decoys. [V, rig: a staged AIM-9 at a hostile, `V:4AA78` changed to the carrier in flight: the destination words follow the new designation the next pass; `V:4AA78` = -1 keeps the last destination.] So flares and chaff protect the player from hostile missiles only, and from a hostile aircraft's guns not at all (the AI has none).

**How much a decoy helps** (single scenarios, `cm_scenario.py`; the player in the airborne state at 4,236 speed, a hostile missile copied from the player's record, 4,200 speed word `0x4200`, placed 45 or 60 passes of the player's own velocity behind, aimed at the player, no ECM; the "hit" is the player's W0 bit `0x8000` from the contact test of 10.2): the missile's per-pass closing is about 76 draw units near the end and the player is hit whenever the missile passes within 80 draw units (the inner box `0x5000`), so a decoy only helps when it pulls the missile more than 80 draw units off the player's track. With the missile 45 passes behind (a hit at pass 18 without a decoy): a **flare** against the Sidewinder released at pass 0 or 5 prevents the hit (closest approach 130 and 91 draw units), released at pass 10 or later does not (closest 40, 5, 7); a **chaff** against the AMRAAM does not prevent it at any release time (closest 64, 43, 18, 5, 7). With the missile 60 passes behind (a hit at pass 23): the flare prevents the hit at release passes 10 (closest 165), the chaff at 0, 5 and 10 (379, 273, 97), the flare released at pass 0 or 5 delays the hit to pass 54. A decoy released after the missile is within about 10 passes never helps. The decoy falls under gravity and the flare drifts back relative to the aircraft's track, which presumably pulls the missile (the cause is not isolated); the chaff, with a quarter of its velocity lost per pass, stops almost at once and hangs where it was released, so the missile turns back to it after passing. **Emulator** (`combat-cm-missile.txt`, `combat-cm-missile-flare.txt`, generator `gen-combat-cm.py missile` and `missileflare`, 150 samples of one video frame from `airborne.vasnap`): a hostile Sidewinder is poked into record 9 as a copy of the player's record (class 0, target byte `0x80`, life 150, speed `0x4200`, group 3, placement tag `0xFF`, no destination) 45 passes of the player's velocity behind the player. Without a flare: the destination words follow the player (cell 65/66, fine words changing by about 27 and 50 per pass), the message line shows `0x8010` (the IR-missile alert) from sample 12, the missile reaches the player at frame counter 249 to 250 (distance (-1, 1, -2) draw units at sample 111, W2 bit 0 set), and the contact pass leaves the player's W0 `0x91C8`, B20 `0x02`, the post `0x9014` (the frame counter 250 is even), `V:4A984` bit 8 and the jolt byte 1; the missile's W0 turns to `0x54C3` (dying) and its B20 stays `0x40` (the radar bit). With the flare released at sample 1 (`0x4026`, `V:4A944` = 1 at sample 6): the missile gets the lost mark (B20 `0x60`) at sample 107 when it comes within the player's outer box and is not near the flare, passes the player at (-29, -127, 50) draw units, the closest sample, and the player is **not hit** (W0 stays `0x11C8`, no damage, no jolt). The rig's prediction for the same geometry (release at pass 0 or 5: no hit, closest 130 and 91 draw units) is the same outcome. [V, emulator, one run each]

### 9.5 The ECM switch

`V:4A96D` has three readers [V, rig]:

| reader | rule | tag |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the response routine for non-aircraft classes (`0x31D12`, the part of `0x31AE0` that turns the commanded rates `+0x50`, `+0x52`, `+0x54` into the rates `+0x56`, `+0x58`, `+0x5A`) | the commanded rate is divided (signed, truncating) by a divisor D before the low-pass: D = 10 if B20 bit 5 (lost) is set; else 5 if the target byte has bit 7 set with index 0 (the player), the ECM is on and the class is **not** 1; else the missile's turn ramp `+0x26` limited to 15 (when `+0x26` is 0 or below there is no division). The low pass: new rate = ((old + q) / 2 + q) / 2 in 16-bit words with arithmetic shifts, q the quotient. So the ECM makes a hostile **Sidewinder** (class 0) aimed at the player turn with a constant fifth of the commanded rate, instead of the ramp of 8.3 (a ninth at the first steered pass, which is the eleventh pass of the missile's life, falling to the full rate: the divisor is `+0x26`, 9 at the first steered pass and 1 less each pass, and from the pass in which it is 0 or below there is no division): **a missile with the ECM on never reaches the full turn rate**; the AMRAAM (class 1) is not affected, and the ECM does nothing for a missile aimed at anything but the player | V (500 cases, 11 divisor kinds, 0 differences; the 16-bit sums of `old + q` wrap as the original's; the ramp: a launched hostile missile in the world update, `+0x26` 18 at the first pass, steering from the pass in which it reads 9, the first rate -107 for a commanded -1280 = ((0 + q)/2 + q)/2 with q = -142) |
| the AI launch gate `0x275F6` (7.6) | the rate mask is halved, so the AI fires **more often** with the ECM on (one pass in 4, 2, 2 instead of 8, 4, 4 for the progress levels 0 or 1, 2, 3 or more) | R, section 7 |
| the HUD lamp (`0x35728`) | the ECM lamp is lit while `V:4A96D` is non-zero | note 06 |

The ECM switch is cleared at each mission start (`0x1D906`) and by the crash restart (`0x1D538` calls `0x1D8B8`, which also clears it; see 12.3), and survives a pause.

### 9.6 Sound

The release plays `0x1A0DC` with the parameters (0x1C, 0x30) in the 3D views (the same call as a gun tracer without the stall-lamp variant): sample index and pitch are set by section 13's table (note 10). The ECM toggle plays `0x1A042` through `0x37C4A` in the cockpit view only. The two posts of each key are the message line (note 06); the key itself has no other sound.

### 9.7 Refill

The rearm (`0x1F11E` with the message `0x4005`, reached from the landing, or its tail `0x1F14A` at every mission start) sets `V:4A904` and `V:4A905` to 16, clears `V:4A943`, `V:4A944`, `V:4A945`, the pending decoy references, but not the pool slots (a flare in flight is still drawn, but no longer a target). The cool-downs `V:4A906`/`V:4A907` are not touched. The counters run down to 0 and are never refilled in flight. **The dead key body at `0x1ECA4`** (note 08 11 lists it with the rearm call `0x1ECAE`, which is a separate body) is one instruction followed by the common key exit: it clears bit 6 (`0x0040`, the valid bit) of the **word at A0 + D4**, and neither register is set by the body. Its siblings in the key table load A0 = `0x4B23C` and D4 = the viewed record's offset `V:4A996` before they use the pair (`0x1E3CC`), which would make it "remove the viewed record"; nothing branches or jumps to `0x1ECA4` or `0x1ECAE` (no long or relative reference in the image), so the entry is **not identifiable and the body is never run**: the port models neither [R].

### 9.8 What the port needs from this section

The decoy slots are slots of the tracer pool with the exact motion of 9.3; the missile steering must carry the two decoy references and the near test of 9.4 and must run for AI missiles only; the divisor of 9.5; the posts of 9.1 and the log cost of 9.2. The cool-down bytes are consumed by the damage handler (10.3): a release within 29 passes makes the player immune to the engine-failure outcome of the next contact.

## 10. Damage

There is no hit-point model. A record is either hit or not: contact between two records either destroys the other record outright, or, for the player, runs a small damage routine whose only lasting outcome is one bit, "out of control". This section gives the contact test (10.2), what a contact does to the other record and to the counters (10.3), the player's damage handler (10.4), what the player loses (10.5) and the kinds of hit that bypass the contact test (10.6). The rig counts are from `tools/m68k-rig/tests/lists_model/damage_check.py`; the emulator captures are `combat-hit-player.txt` and `combat-hit-player-flare.txt` (see Verified by). The gun's hits (the tracer test, three hits kill) are section 8.2; the explosion that a destroyed record leaves is section 11.

### 10.1 The pieces

| piece | where | role |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the contact scan | `0x2B5E2`, called for a mover by its flight step (`0x2A2A2`) | tests the mover against the records after it; returns `0x40` (aircraft contact) or 0; sets `V:4A957` and W2 bit 0 of every record whose outer box overlaps |
| the tail of the scan | `0x2BFFA` | the counters and the friendly-fire mark (10.3) |
| the mark on the other record | `0x2B798` | the crashed bit and the out-of-control bit (10.3) |
| the result handler | `0x2A758` to `0x2A9C0` | for the player, the damage rules (10.4); for the others, section 5 |
| the world update | `0x26AF8` | decreases the two cool-down bytes `V:4A907` and `V:4A906` once per pass (unless paused) before the player is stepped |

### 10.2 The contact test

The scan runs for a mover that is not a ship (class high nibble `0x20`: nothing is tested) and visits **the records after the mover in index order up to record 15** (a pair is tested once, from the lower index: a record before the mover is never the "other"). It first clears `V:4A957` and bits 6 and 7 of B4 of the mover (the deck points). A record is skipped unless all of these hold:

1. the other's W0 bit `0x0040` (valid) is set and its W0 bits `0x0600` (crashed, dying) are clear;
2. its group byte `+0x5E` differs from the mover's (the player's own missiles and tracers have the player's group 0; a missile copies its owner's group);
3. W0 bit `0x0008` is set on the mover or on the other (the player and the player's missiles always have it: a contact between two counted hostiles is never tested; a hostile and a friendly or an airliner is);
4. the other's class high nibble is not `0x30` (the ejection seat and the parachute are never hit).

The half-size pair (outer, inner) in position units depends on the pair of classes:

| other | mover | outer | inner | tag |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| class `0x15` (cruise missile), exactly | any | `0x5000` | `0x1000` | V |
| class nibble `0x20` (ship, carrier), **in any record** | an aircraft or a missile | `0xA0000` on all three axes, **sets the proximity flag `V:4A957` and the ship's W2 bit 0 at once**, then the deck and hull tests of note 05 17.11 (below the table) | | V: `weapon_check.py ships`, R for the code |
| any other | the mover's nibble is 0 (a missile) or differs from the other's nibble | `0x10000` | `0x5000` | V |
| any other | the same nibble (aircraft against aircraft) and neither record has W2 bit `0x0100` (scripted) | `0x7000` | `0x1000` | V |
| any other | the same nibble and W2 bit `0x0100` on either | `0x6000` | `0x0800` | V |

(The test for `0x15` compares the full class byte of the other; a cruise missile as the **mover** has the nibble 1 and uses the same-nibble row against an aircraft. The Sidewinder and the AMRAAM have the nibble 0, so a missile against an aircraft or the player uses `0x10000` / `0x5000` (256 and 80 draw units of 256 position units), and two aircraft `0x7000` / `0x1000` (112 and 16 draw units).)

**Ships.** The deck and hull tests (`0x2B7D2`-`0x2BB70`, result `0x20`) run for **every ship record and every mover**, not only the carrier in record 14 and not only the viewed record: the test point is the mover's previous position inside the cell (and, only when the mover is the viewed record `V:4A996`, plus its model vertex slots 0 and 3, which makes two passes of the test); the lists are those of the **ship's** class byte (`0x20`: `0x3E0F8`/`0x3E174`, any other class with the nibble `0x20`: `0x3EDCC`/`0x3EDEC`). A hit returns `0x20` to the caller and none of the marks of 10.3 [V: `weapon_check.py ships`: a copy of record 14 in record 14 and in record 6 with random rotated vertices, the player and a missile record as movers, 1,500 points each, 217 to 263 hits per row, the proximity flag set in all 1,500, 0 differences from this reading]. **Vertices and passes for a mover that is not the viewed record (this reconciles note 05 17.11 and this section).** The test point is the mover's previous position (cell part, shifted right by 8). Only when the mover **is** the viewed record (`V:4A996` equals the mover's offset `V:4AA6E`: the player in play, or a seat or missile while the camera views it) is a vertex term added: pass 0 adds the **mover's own** vertex slot 0 (`+0xA4`) shifted right by its unit shift `+0x7D` & 15, and a second pass (pass count 1, a local counter of the scan routine's frame, started at 0 for every ship hit) adds slot 3 (`+0xB6`). For a mover that is not the viewed record there is **no vertex term and no second pass**: one test with the bare previous position, whatever its `+0xA4` slots hold. The vertices are therefore never the player's model rotated by another record's matrix, and the "pass count" is not a field of any record. [V: `weapon_check.py shipverts`: a missile mover and a ship with random vertices, 600 points: with the missile not viewed the result is independent of its own slots (0 differences in 600), with it viewed the slots change the result in 65 of 600 points.]

The test, with d the three differences between the other's current position and the mover's **previous** position (the mover's position minus this step's displacement, passed in by the caller):

1. if any |d| exceeds the outer half-size, no contact;
2. otherwise a **proximity** is recorded: `V:4A957` = 1 and W2 bit 0 of the other (this is what the "near another aircraft" warning `0xD00A` and the decoy rule of 9.4 read; a proximity is not a hit);
3. if all |d| are at most the inner half-size: **hit**;
4. else the test is repeated with the other's position moved back by **half of its own displacement** (`+0x3E`, `+0x42`, `+0x46` shifted right by 1): a hit if all three are within the inner half-size, else no hit (the swept test catches fast crossings).

Differences of exactly the half-size count as inside. [V: `damage_check.py` part A, 2,400 random pairs, 171 of them hits (movers 0, 1, 2, 5, 9, 12; others up to record 15; classes `0x10`, `0x11`, `0x14`, `0x00`, `0x01`, `0x15`, `0x1F`, others `0x30`, `0x31`, `0x40`; flag words with and without the valid, crashed and dying bits; groups equal and different; W2 `0x0100` on either; differences at, one under and one over each half-size; displacement of the other random): the contact result and the proximity flag against the model, 0 differences; part D: 600 cases of an overlapping pair with the other before the mover and one ship mover: never tested.]

### 10.3 What a hit does to the other record and to the counters

When the test says hit (inner or swept), the following happens in this order [V: `damage_check.py` part C, 1,800 random pairs, 957 of them hits, of mover (the player, the three weapon records, an aircraft) and other (classes `0x10`, `0x12`, `0x13`, `0x15`, `0x16`, `0x00`, `0x01`; active or not; counted or not; groups 0 and 1 for the mover): 0 differences in the return value, the other's W0, B20, W2, state byte, the camera-follow word, the mover's B20 and the three log counters]:

1. the **other record**, if its W0 bit `0x1000` (active in the simulation) is set: W0 bit `0x0200` (crashed) is set. If its class high nibble is not 0 (an aircraft, the cruise missile `0x15` included: the class test compares the masked nibble with `0x15`, so the cruise missile gets no exemption), B20 bit 1 (out of control) is set, `V:54E72` (the record the camera follows) is set to the other's offset, W2 bit `0x1000` (the high byte's bit 4: the skid-and-follow flag) is set, and the state byte `+0x05` is set to 1. A missile as the other (class nibble 0) gets only the crashed bit. An other whose active bit is clear (a ship, a stored record) is not marked at all;
2. the **log counters** for the player's weapons: when the mover's W0 bit `0x0008` is set and its group is 0 (the player's missile): class 0 (Sidewinder) adds 1 to `+0x44`, class 1 (AMRAAM) adds 1 to `+0x40`, in both cases only if the other's W0 bit `0x0008` is **clear** (a counted hostile; hits on not-counted records are not counted);
3. the **friendly-fire mark**: if the mover has the bit `0x0008` and the other has it too, B20 bit 7 is set on both records (read by the mission script, which chooses the end code `0xFD` instead of `0xFE` when it finds the mark: note 08 part C). Otherwise B20 bit 7 is cleared on both;
4. when the mark is not made and the mover is the player (index 0) and the other's class nibble is 0 (a missile), the counter `+0x48` (hits from missiles) gains 1. (The player's own W0 has the bit `0x0008`, so this is the case "the other is counted": a hostile missile. A missile of a friendly would set the mark instead.);
5. the scan returns `0x40` to the caller.

The caller then runs the result handler. For a mover that is not the player the handler removes the mover at once (valid bit cleared, display request `V:4A910` = `0x0C`; section 5.1): this is how **a missile that hits is consumed**, and how an AI aircraft that touches another is consumed. A kill by contact is therefore: the other record is crashed (out of control), and it enters the dying path of section 5 at its own next step: 15 passes of falling, the kill counted at the expiry (`V:4A963`, section 5.3) if it is a counted aircraft that is still in the air.

| mover | other | mover afterwards | other afterwards | counter |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| the player's missile (group 0, W0 bit 3) | a counted hostile aircraft | removed (valid bit cleared) | crashed, out of control, camera follow, state 1 | `+0x44` (class 0) or `+0x40` (class 1) |
| the player's missile | a not-counted record (friendly, airliner) | removed | the same marks | none; B20 bit 7 on both |
| the player's missile | a ship | removed | not marked (active bit clear) | none |
| the player | a hostile missile (counted) | the damage handler (10.4) | crashed (it is removed when its dying count ends, section 5) | `+0x48` |
| the player | a hostile aircraft | the damage handler | crashed and out of control; it dies by section 5 | none |
| the player | a not-counted record (friendly, airliner) | the damage handler | the same marks | none; B20 bit 7 on both |
| an AI aircraft | another aircraft (after it in the order) | removed | crashed, out of control | none |

### 10.4 The player's damage handler

The handler is the part of the result code that runs for the player (index 0), without an ejection nibble (`E+0x7C` low nibble 0: with a nibble the branch at `0x2A7F0` goes to `0x2A8F2` for **any** result: the dying path of note 05 17.12 for the ground, hull and landmark results, **but for the result `0x40` (a contact with another aircraft) the marks of 13.1 and the removal of the player's record**, see 13.1) and with the result `0x40` [V: `damage_check.py` part B, 600 random staged passes of the world update `0x26AF8` with a second aircraft record on the player (frame counter 0 to 7, cool-downs 0, 1, 30, immunity, collisions on and off, speeds `0x100` to `0x2000` including `0x3C0` and `0x3C1`, an ejection nibble in a few): the posted code `V:4AB98`, B20 bit 1, `V:4A984` bit 8, the jolt byte, the hit bit; 0 differences; outcomes `0x9014` 59, `0xD00B` 68, `0xD00C` 77 and no post 279 (speed gate, immunity, collisions off or a cool-down) of the 483 passes without a nibble]:

1. `V:4A844` = 0 (collisions off): nothing happens (the handler ends: no hit bit either). Collisions are on in play; the game's value is 1;
2. W0 bit `0x8000` (hit this step) is set;
3. if the indicated speed `E+0x6E` after this step's speed step is at most `0x3C0` (80 knots): **nothing more**: a contact below 80 knots does no damage and posts nothing (the proximity warning of note 05 17.13 may have fired);
4. if the immunity byte `V:4A952` is non-zero (set for the crash hold of note 05 17.17): nothing more;
5. now the low bits of the frame counter `V:4A993` (the low byte of `V:4A992`) choose the outcome:
   * bit 0 **clear**: if `V:4A907` or `V:4A906` is above 0 (both are decreased once per pass before the step, so a flare or chaff released up to 29 passes earlier keeps them above 0) **nothing happens**; otherwise B20 bit 1 (**out of control**) is set, `V:4A984` bit 8 (the damage bit, 10.5) is set, the message `0x9014` is posted (the engine-failure alert of note 06) and the jolt byte `V:4A878` = 1;
   * bit 0 **set**: the message `0xD00B` is posted when bit 1 is clear and `0xD00C` when bit 1 is set (the hydraulic and the structure alerts of note 06), and `V:4A878` = 1. No state changes, whatever the cool-downs;
6. the posts go through the acceptance routine `0x29BC8` (note 05 17.13): the message line shows the alert for its duration (note 06), a repeat of the same code is accepted.

So the "first hit" of the text of note 05 is a misnomer: the choice has nothing to do with the number of earlier hits. It is the parity of the frame counter at the moment of contact: about half of the contacts at speed produce the engine failure (when no decoy was released in the last 29 passes), the others produce a message and a jolt only. The other record normally leaves the scan after the pass of the contact (its crashed bit takes it out: rule 1 of 10.2); a contact with an other whose active bit is clear repeats every pass and each pass rolls the parity again [R].

Emulator captures (see Verified by): a staged aircraft hitting the player on an odd frame counter (`0xD00B`, no state change), on a pinned even counter (`0x9014`, out of control, the damage bit, the jolt), the same with a flare released before it (cool-down: no outcome), and a real hostile Sidewinder hitting the player (`0x9014`) or missing it after a flare (section 9.4).

### 10.5 What a damaged player loses

The only persistent effect of any hit is **B20 bit 1, out of control** (and the damage bit `V:4A984` bit 8, which the only reader, the collisions-off hand-over post `0x4021` of note 05 17.17 (`0x13D0A`), tests, and which no code clears: the word has 42 absolute references in the image, all listed in the scan of this round: they test or change the bits `0x0001` (the message latch), `0x0004`, `0x0040` (the flight simulation switch), `0x0080` (the alert flag) and `0x0400` (the jolt) and bit 11 (`0x0800`, tested as a byte at `0x1EB8C`), the mask `0x0081` of the message line (`0x36DBC`), and the single store of bit 8 is the damage handler's at `0x2A880` (it ORs `0x0100` into the word); **the collisions-off post at `0x13D0A` is the only reader of bit 8** [R, complete over absolute references; the image also loads the addresses of state blocks into registers (`0x4A802`, `0x4A8B2`, `0x4A978` and others); accesses through those bases to this word were not found but are not exhaustively excluded]). Nothing clears B20 bit 1 except the start of a flight (the flight start, `0x1D616`): neither landing nor time repairs it. Out of control means, from note 05 [R, rig in note 05]:

* the speed step is not run (section 1 e of note 05): the speed `S`, `W` stay where they were, whatever the throttle, the afterburner or the fuel;
* the stall nose-drop rule (note 05 section 5) applies at every step: the pitch rate is zeroed and the nose drops toward the stored pitch plus the term `0x50`;
* the pitch part of the input conversion takes its out-of-control branch (`0x31F40`): the pitch rate is cleared and the pitch is driven by the nose-drop term only; **the roll and rudder keys still act** [V: the player's record stepped by the original's world update 0x26AF8 for 12 passes with and without B20 bit 1, keys roll right, roll left, rudder right and rudder left held: the roll rate `+0x5A` and the yaw rate `+0x58` after 12 passes are equal in the two runs (roll -571 and +572, yaw +128 and -127), and the conversion routine `0x1DFAA` itself gives the same three command bytes for all 256 key bytes with the bit set and clear; only the stick key differs (the pitch rate stays 0 and the nose drifts) and the indicated speed stays at 4,241 where it grows without the bit]; the roll and yaw words are not changed by the bit;
* the post chain of note 05 17.13 considers only the eject clause: `0xD02A` and the cue `V:4AC0C` bit `0x40` when the descent reaches the ground within 64 steps;
* the entity drawer chooses another colour word (`V:4AA0C`) for a record with B20 bit 1 (section 11, note 04a section 8a) [R].

The aircraft then falls to a ground contact (result `0x10` or `0x20`: the crash path of note 05 17.12) unless the pilot ejects (the nibble cycle of section 6). There is no partial damage: no loss of thrust distinct from the loss of control, no damaged weapons, radar, gear or hook. The messages `0xD00B` and `0xD00C` have no state behind them.

### 10.6 Hits that do not use the contact test

| hit | rule | where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the gun on an aircraft | the tracer's own test (8.2): three hits, the low nibble of `+0x3C` (stored only up to 2), the third and every later hit mark crashed and out of control with the camera follow, W2 bit `0x1000` and state 1 as 10.3 rule 1; the log counter `+0x3C` counts every hit | section 8 |
| ground, hull and landmark contact | results `0x10`, `0x20` of the player's collision phase, note 05 17.11 and 17.12; for others section 5.1 | note 05 |
| an AI record on the ground | section 5.3 | section 5 |

The AI aircraft have no weapon but the missile (7.6) and the player's weapons have no area effect: every hit kills one record.

### 10.7 What the port needs from this section

Contact test with the table of 10.2 for all the classes, the swept test and the proximity flag; the marks and counters of 10.3 in the same order; the handler of 10.4 with the parity of the frame counter taken from the port's pass counter (the original's `V:4A992` counts passes; the port's step counter is the same variable by construction in the golden runs); the cool-down bytes set by the decoy keys; the single persistent state, the out-of-control bit; the jolt kind 1.

## 11. Explosions and effects

A destroyed or crashing record is drawn with two different effects. (a) While the record's dying bit (`W0` bit `0x0400`) is set, the entity draw adds a **burst** of twelve small triangles (or nine discs for the cruise missile) that fly outwards for the 15 dying steps (11.4). (b) A weapon record that hits the ground or a landmark also creates a persistent **effect object** (a secondary object, section 1.5) that draws a fire column for 128 passes and then nothing for ever (11.2 and 11.3). Aircraft never create effect objects. The draw handlers of aircraft and of the pod and parachute cones are in 11.5. Rig checks: `effects_check.py` (parts 1 to 4), `effects_stream_check.py`, `effect_create_check.py` (with the back-up check), `burst_check.py`.

### 11.1 Where

| item | address | what |
| --- | --- | --- |
| effect object creator | `0x2AA8E` | called with A1 = a pointer to 32 bytes (the head of a record) and D1 = the model id or 0; the only caller is `0x2A996` (below) |
| call site and position back-up | `0x2A976` to `0x2A996`, back-up routine `0x2AA5E` | in the result handler of the step (`0x2A758` onwards, note 05 17.12), only for a class with high nibble 0 |
| life words | `V:4D240 + 0x20 * k` (the word `+0x04` of object k) | decremented once per pass for all 16 objects by the world update (`0x26B02` to `0x26B42`), not while the pause byte `V:4A866` is set |
| clearing | `0x1D228` | the flight start (`0x1D1BE`) zeroes all 16 objects (and the 16 records) |
| descriptors of the three models | `0x26160`, `0x26174`, `0x26188` | descriptor index `0x11`, `0x12`, `0x13` = `0x2600C + 20 * id`: the template records 33, 34 and 35 of the table at `0x25ECC` (the draw routine `0x21D0C`, models at `0x400EC`, `0x400F4`, `0x40102`); the queue entry of an object gets its descriptor from the id word `+0x02` (`0x20C60`) |
| model programs | `0x40140` (the starburst, shared by 33, 34 and 35), `0x40118` (the scar, 34 and 35), `0x4036A` (the far point) | ops 70, 71, 62, 3, 13 and 58 of note 03 |
| op 70 (timer skip), op 71 (disc list), op 62 (disc) | `0x23588`, `0x2576E`, `0x25750` (the disc routine `0x257B2`) | note 03 lists 70 and 62; **op 71 draws a list of discs** (note 03 had it as unused; 11.3) |
| aircraft draw handler | `0x26944` | descriptors of models 3 to 10, 12, 14, 15 (note 02 section 1) |
| cone handlers | `0x2699E` (models 2 and 13), `0x26A3E` (model 11) | |
| dying burst | call site `0x2273C` to `0x227CA` (the tail of the entity draw), routine `0x31766`, layer routine `0x31850`, centre distance `0x31A88` | 11.4 |
| burst tables | pointer table `0x315A2` (8 longs), shapes `0x315C2` to `0x31651` (8 x 18 bytes), fragment offsets `0x31652`, `0x31679`, `0x316A0` (12 signed-byte triples each), `0x316C7` (the discs), pen tables `0x316E5`, `0x316F1`, `0x316FD`, `0x31709`, `0x31715` (12 bytes each), shape cycle `0x31722` | exported by the extractor since extractor 18 (shapes, offsets and discs earlier; the pen tables and the shape cycle with extractor 18) |

### 11.2 Creation of an effect object

**Who.** Only the result handler of a record step creates one, and only for a record whose class has the high nibble 0 (the weapon records: the AIM-9L, class 0, and the AIM-120, class 1; section 8). The result must be a ground result `0x10` or a hull or landmark result `0x20`. A record that is an aircraft, a ship, the cruise missile or the player never creates one: they die by the dying path of section 5 (and the burst of 11.4) [R: the handler's class tests; V for the missile case below].

| result | position | model id | condition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `0x20` (a landmark volume; the carrier hull is tested only for the record the camera views, `V:4A996`, so for a weapon record only while the weapon camera views it, section 6) | the record head as it is | `0x11` (model 33: the starburst without a scar) | always |
| `0x10` over land | the record's position backed up along this step's displacement to the ground plane (`0x2AA5E`: x and z move by the displacement times (depth below ground) / (vertical displacement), y becomes 0; the cell and fine words of the copied head stay at the pre-back-up values) | by the slot: `0x13` (model 35) for an even slot, `0x12` (model 34) for an odd one | the sea test (note 05 section 10, record byte `+0x04` bit 1) says land |
| `0x10` over the sea | none | none | the test says sea |

**The back-up** (`0x2AA5E`) in numbers: with the displacement of the step (dx, dy, dz), d = dy shifted right by 8 (nothing happens when it is 0) and the depth h = -(y shifted right by 8) as a word, each of x and z moves by (((dx shifted left by 4) divided by d) times h) shifted right by 4, the divide and the multiply being 16-bit word operations (an overflowing quotient leaves the dividend, whose low word is then used), and y becomes 0 [V: `effect_create_check.py` `backup_check`, 800 random states, 524 with a move, 0 differences].

**The id rule corrects section 1.5.** With D1 = 0 the creator takes `0x12` or `0x13` from bit 0 of **its own scan counter** (it starts at 15 and counts down while it looks for a free slot), not from a flag of the caller: a free slot k gets `0x13` when k is even and `0x12` when k is odd [V: `effects_check.py` part 1, 300 random tables and 16-slot-full cases, 0 differences, and the model's `slot` parity in the test; `effect_create_check.py`: the first object of a flight goes to slot 0 and gets `0x13`, the second to slot 1 and gets `0x12`]. The caller's D0 is not an input.

**Allocation.** The first object whose valid bit (`W0` bit `0x0040`) is clear is taken; the 32 bytes at A1 are copied over it; then `W0` becomes `0x0040`, the life word `+0x04` becomes `0x7F` and the id word `+0x02` becomes the id; the display request `V:4A910` becomes `0xFF`. **With all 16 objects valid nothing happens** (no overwrite, no request; the life words still count down) [V: `effects_check.py` part 1 and `effect_create_check.py`: a full table of 16 valid objects with the world pass run, only the life words changed]. **An object is never freed**: no routine of the image clears an object's valid bit, so after 16 impacts in one flight no further effect can appear until the flight is restarted (`0x1D228` clears the table) [R: every reference to `0x4D23C` and `0x4D240` in the image was read: the creator, the clear, the decrement, the pending marking of note 04a section 6 and the readers of the draw]. The object is dead for the eye after 128 passes (below), but it keeps its slot.

**The record that made it.** In the same step the weapon record gets `W0` bit `0x8000` (hit), the active bit `0x1000` cleared, the dying bit `0x0400` and the counter `+0x4C` = 15 (section 5; rig: `W0` `0x10CA` becomes `0x84CA`), so the burst of 11.4 runs for 15 steps and the record is freed at the expiry [V: `effect_create_check.py`, 144 cells of the land/sea map, 73 land (object created, id `0x13`, life `0x7F`, `V:4A910` = `0xFF`) and 71 sea (none), 0 differences].

**Sources in play.** A missile that flies into the ground or a landmark is therefore the only source of effect objects: a missile that hits an aircraft is removed at once by the result `0x40` (section 5.1) without an object, and an expired missile is freed without one (section 8).

### 11.3 What an effect object draws (models 33, 34, 35)

The three models share one program (the starburst) and differ only in the near-level extras and the block: model 33 (id `0x11`) is the starburst alone; models 34 and 35 (ids `0x12`, `0x13`, mirror images in x of each other) add a **ground scar** drawn first: a pentagon of five of the twelve ring vertices filled with the word `0xFC00` (op 3) and a line list between ring vertices in pen 3 (op 13) [R: the extractor's decode of the two items]. The block has 18 vertices: twelve on the ground ring (y = 0, radius about 70 to 170 model units) and six on a column above its centre (heights rising with shrinking steps, in model units; the six words are model data and are not listed: FNV-1a-32 `e743242a` of the six 16-bit little-endian y words of the column's vertex records, 16 bytes apart, from offset 364 of `models/model_033.bin` and offset 418 of `model_034.bin` and `model_035.bin`), slots 12 to 17. Level thresholds (model units against the object's distance, note 03): model 33 draws up to 176 and nothing beyond; models 34 and 35 draw scar and starburst up to 176, **one point sprite (pen 3) up to 896** and nothing beyond.

**The starburst program** is read at its first word (`0x40140`) as follows [V: `effects_stream_check.py`: the original's handlers walk the program for every life word t from -4 to 0x7F and all 16 object indices, 2,112 walks, 11,328 discs, 0 differences against the table below]:

1. **op 70** reads the word at `+0x04` of the **secondary object** `V:4AA6C` of the table `0x4D23C` (stride `0x20`: `0x23588` computes `0x4D23C + (V:4AA6C << 5)`), the life word t. It does not read the entity table: were it run by an entity's program it would read the secondary object of the same index, not the record's `+0x04`/`+0x05` bytes and not the counter `+0x4C`; no entity program contains it. It skips stream bytes: with t < 0 it skips nothing and the next item ends the program without drawing, so **a negative life word draws nothing**. Otherwise it skips 2 bytes (the empty item) plus `0x86` bytes times the **variant** k = (t & 7) shifted right by 1 (0 to 3: four copies of the same picture that differ in their pens, a two-pass flicker); in addition, for t below `0x0C` it skips `0x26` bytes plus 8 bytes times ((`0x0B` - t) shifted right by 1) and for t above `0x74` it skips `0x56` bytes plus 8 bytes times ((t - `0x74`) shifted right by 1).
2. Each variant is one **op 71 item** (a list of six discs, the last marked by bit 15 of its size word; the item ends the program; **a disc that fails its projection does not end the list**: the loop of `0x2576E` ends only on the size word's bit 15, every disc is handed to the disc routine and the results are ORed, so the remaining discs are still tried; the pen word `V:4AA0C` after the list is the last disc's pen) [R] followed by two runs of six **op 62 items** (one disc each, the sixth of each run ending the program): an ascending run over the column vertices 12 to 17 and a descending run 17 to 12. A disc is (vertex slot, pen, size): the centre is the block vertex shifted by the frame shift; the radius in pixels is the size divided by the distance estimate of the centre (note 03, op 62), at most `0x7F`.
3. The sizes are `0x1D00`, `0x1C00`, `0x1900`, `0x1600`, `0x1300`, `0x1000` for the vertices 12 to 17 in every run and every variant. The pens (8 or 10) are, per variant, for the six discs of the list and of the ascending run (vertex 12 first): variant 0: 8 8 10 10 8 8; variant 1: 8 10 10 8 8 10; variant 2: 10 10 8 8 10 10; variant 3: 10 8 8 10 10 8; the descending run (vertex 17 first) has the pens 8 8 10 10 8 8, 10 8 8 10 10 8, 10 10 8 8 10 10 and 8 10 10 8 8 10 for the variants 0 to 3.

So the picture over the object's life, with t the life word at the draw (0x7F at creation, one less per pass; the first draw after the creation sees `0x7F`):

| t | passes | discs drawn | note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `0x75` to `0x7F` | 11 | the descending run from disc m = (t - `0x74`) >> 1 (m = 0 to 5): 6 - m discs, the base disc (vertex 12) always among them | the column grows upward; t = `0x7E` and `0x7F` draw the base disc only |
| `0x0C` to `0x74` | 105 | the six discs of the list, vertices 12 to 17, variant (t & 7) >> 1 | steady fire, flickering pens |
| `0x00` to `0x0B` | 12 | the ascending run from disc m = (`0x0B` - t) >> 1 (m = 0 to 5): 6 - m discs, the top disc (vertex 17) always among them | the column burns down from the base; t = 0 and 1 draw the top disc only |
| below 0 | for ever | nothing (the scar of models 34 and 35 stays) | |
| `0x80` and above | unreachable at creation | **no special case**: the skip formula goes on (item 1) and lands at an arbitrary byte of the stream or beyond it, so the handlers decode whatever they find | the 16-bit life word wraps from -32768 to 32767 after 32,896 passes (about 68 minutes at 8 passes per second). Rig `effects_stream_check.py wrap` (the original's handlers, life words `0x80` to `0xFF`, 128 values): **14 draw discs (1 to 6), 110 draw nothing, 4 run on past 40 items into RAM** (`0x8C`, `0x9C` and two more: the walk leaves the stream); larger words (`0x180`, `0x1FF`, `0x300`, `0x1000`, `0x7FFF`) draw nothing. There is no formula worth porting: after 68 minutes of flight the original's effect objects flicker or garble. The port draws nothing for t >= `0x80` (a declared deviation, reachable only in flights of over an hour) [V for the table, C for the deviation] |

### 11.4 The dying burst (entity tail `0x2273C`, routine `0x31766`)

**When.** At the end of the drawing of an **entity** item (queue item flag bit 4 of `V:4A913`; not a secondary object, whose tail returns first) the drawer checks the record: dying bit set and the counter `+0x4C` between 0 and 15 give the **age** = 15 - counter (0 on the first step after the start, 15 on the expiry step). The centre is the record's fine position (`+0x0C`, `+0x0E`) plus the cell difference to the camera cell times 2^14 on x and z and the record's `+0x10` as y (the units of the drawer before the LOD shift). The **kind** is 3 for class `0x15` (the cruise missile), else 2 when the record's byte `+0x04` bit 1 is set (water: set by the sea test at a ground impact, so a first burst in the air is never kind 2), else 1. The LOD shift s is `V:4AB70`. Nothing is drawn when |y + `V:4AB30`| exceeds `0x8000`. The same tail then calls the trail drawer (section 13.2). The tail belongs to the end of the plain drawer's walk (its entry `0x2273C` is reached from the tests at `0x22612` and `0x226FC` after the program items and the face tree have been walked); the other exits of the drawer (the stream ended by a `0xFFFF` word, `0x22692`; a program abandoned by a negative handler result) return before it, so an entity whose draw is abandoned shows neither burst nor trail in that frame [R]. The tail runs for every dying entity in the queue: aircraft after a kill (section 5), the weapon record of 11.2, a wreck that has hit the ground, the player in an external view [R for the callers; V for the routine].

**What the drawer does for a dying record** [R: `0x21F10`-`0x21F68`, `0x22772`; the slot store of 8.10 has the same cut]. The entity drawer's vertex stage tests the dying bit: with the counter `+0x4C` **at 14** it writes the explosion's distance words for the sound (`V:4A921`, `V:4A922` from the record's distance, `V:4A870` = 4 less their shifted value; section 13.4); with the counter **12 or less** it jumps to `0x22772` before the vertex loop: **no model is drawn and no slot is stored, only the burst (above) and the trail (13.2) are**. A dying record with a counter of 13 to 15 is still drawn as the wreck, so the wreck is visible for the first 3 passes of its 15 and then only the burst remains.

**What drew the wreck (the damage picture of the hostile models, settled).** There is no damage picture: **the wreck is the intact model**. The programs of the aircraft models contain selectors that read the record (note 02 2.2, State-dependent items): the gear and flap bytes, the low nibble of `+0x7C` (the ejection variants: canopy), bit 3 of W2 (the afterburner flame), the frame counter's low nibble, the status byte `V:4A8FF` and the record index; no selector reads W0, the hit counts `+0x3C`, the counter `+0x4C` or B20 [R: the selector table of note 02 2.2 for the models 3, 4, 6, 7 (ops 42-44, 49-54, 59, 66, 77), 8, 9, 12 (68, 69), 10, 14, 15 and 102, 103 (72, 73)]. A shot-down MiG therefore shows its ordinary picture for 3 passes (burning flame if W2 bit 3 was set, gear as it was), the dying-bit cut of the vertex stage then removes the model for the burst and nothing replaces it. The dark trail of 13.2 and the other colour word of an out-of-control record (`V:4AA0C`) are the only marks.

**The result word of the tail-only draw.** The drawer's result is the word at `-$7C(a6)`, cleared at `0x2260A` when the program walk starts and ORed by the walk. The cut at counter 12 (`0x21F68`) jumps to `0x22772` before that clear, and the tail returns `-$7C(a6)` at `0x227D6`: **the burst's own results (`0x31766`) and the trail's are discarded, and the returned word is whatever the drawer's stack frame held at that place** (the accumulated result of the **previous drawer call of any kind at that depth**: the entity routine `0x21C44`, the terrain routine `0x21C34`, a secondary object and the player's near body all enter the drawer at `0x21D0C` with the same frame, and any of them that passed `0x2260A` (or the terrain path's own clear at `0x22E52`) left its word there; the cockpit block, whose stream is the single `0xFFFF`, passes the clear and leaves 0; nothing else the walker calls between two draws uses that depth). **It is not observable**: an entity's or a secondary object's result is read by nothing (04a 8b), and the tail-only path is taken by entity records only, so the stale word never reaches a reader. The walker treats it as any drawer result (04a 8b); a port that wants the original's exact behaviour needs the previous entity draw's result, otherwise 0 (a declared approximation) [R].

**The fragments** [V: `burst_check.py`: 800 random calls of kinds 1, 2 and 3 with camera-space points in view (2,339 fragments, 2 seeds), the polygon filler and the disc routine logged: positions in the window, pens and order equal the model below, 0 differences]. A layer draws n = 12 fragments (9 for kind 3's first layer), j from n - 1 down to 0. Fragment j of age a:

1. the offset triple (dx, dy, dz) = signed bytes j of the layer's offset table times a (word product, wrapped), each arithmetically shifted right by s;
2. the centre (cx, cy, cz) = the burst centre shifted right by s (with the camera's offset words `V:4AB2A`, `V:4AB2E` added to x and z and `V:4AB30` to y first) as words; the fragment centre is that plus the offset;
3. the shape index = word number (j + a) of the cycle table `0x31722` (it counts 0, 4, 8 ... 28 and repeats: the eight shapes turn over as the burst ages); the shape is a triangle of three word triples at the pointer `0x315A2 + index`; each vertex is the fragment centre plus the shape triple, shifted right by s **a second time** (the centre is therefore shifted twice; the original's LOD rule, kept);
4. each vertex is rotated by the camera rows of `V:4AC90` (nine words, Q8: x' = row 0 . v >> 8, y' = row 1 . v >> 8, z' = row 2 . v >> 8); the fragment is dropped (not drawn, no clipping) when any vertex has z' <= 0 or |x'| > z' or |y'| > z' (words);
5. the screen point is x = `0x13F` - clamp(160 + 160 x' / z') and y = `0xB3` - clamp(90 + 90 y' / z') (the clamps 0 to `0x13F` and 0 to `0xB4`; divisions truncate toward zero); the triangle goes to the polygon filler with the pen from the layer's pen table (byte j).

| kind | layers (age, offset table, pens, centre) | notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 (air or land) | one: age a, `0x31652`, pen 9 | twelve triangles |
| 2 (over water) | one: age a, `0x31652`, pen 2 | the same triangles in pen 2 |
| 0 (chaff, from `0x3140C` only) | one: age a, `0x31652` (the table of kind 1), the pen forced to **13** (`0x3194E`: a kind byte of 0 loads 13 instead of the layer's pen byte) | the same twelve fragments and shapes as kind 1, but each is drawn as the **line from its first to its second vertex** with the line routine `0x3455E` (`0x31A78`), not as a filled triangle: twelve line segments, pen 13 [V: `weapon_check.py chaff`: 12, 9 and 7 line calls at life `0x3A`, `0x30` and `0x10` (fragments outside the view are dropped), all with pen 13; the shapes and offsets by code R] |
| 3 (cruise missile) | three: (a) nine discs, offsets `0x316C7`, pens 13 and 10 alternating; (b) age a - 1 (not below 0), `0x316A0` (3 times the base offsets), pen 9, twelve triangles; (c) age a - 2 (not below 0), `0x31679` (2 times), pen 9, centre moved by (+`0x14`, +`0x0A`, +`0x14`) after the shift | the disc layer has no shapes: its disc is at the fragment centre with the radius (`0x2800` x a / 6 + `0x2800`) / distance (a word division, at most `0x7F`; for a >= 14 the sum passes `0x7FFF`, is read as negative and the radius is a negative word, which the disc routine `0x33CAC` ignores: it returns at once for a radius below 1 [R]) |

Draw order inside a layer: j = 11 first. The whole burst is drawn after the entity's model and before its trail.

### 11.5 The draw handlers of aircraft and cones

**Aircraft handler `0x26944`** (the descriptor routine of models 3 to 10, 12, 14, 15; it ends in the model drawer `0x21C44`) [V: `effects_check.py` part 4, 1,500 random records, 0 differences]. For the record being drawn (`V:4AA6E`):

| record state | action |
| --- | --- |
| valid (`W0` bit `0x40`) and crashed (`0x0200`) | `+0x4C` = 15; `W0` bit `0x0200` cleared and `0x0400` (dying) set; then the lock drop: when the designated target `V:4AA78` equals this record's offset the notice `0x4016` is posted, `V:4AA78` becomes `0xFFFF` and the bits `0x4200` of `V:4AC08` are cleared; then the model is drawn |
| valid, not crashed | the model is drawn |
| not valid, `+0x7A` = 5 | nothing is drawn, nothing changes |
| not valid, `+0x7A` other than 5, `+0x4C` negative | the valid bit is **set again** (`0x0040`) and nothing is drawn: a record that was freed with a negative counter and is still in a stale queue is revived when it is drawn once more [R as a possibility; reproduced by the rig on a constructed state; no capture; open question 2] |

So the crashed bit set by a hit (gun, missile, collision) is converted to the dying state either by the step tail (`0x2A99A`, section 5.1) or, when the record is drawn first, by this handler; both produce the same state.

**The revival is unreachable in play** [R; the capture of "a snapshot at the draw" cannot be made: the state needs a record that is invalid, has a negative counter and is still in queue C]. A record that is freed with a negative counter is a seat, a parachute or a pod in its first 20 passes (the counter runs from -20 up); the places that free such a record (the dying branch of the seat's step, 6.4, the pod and parachute landings, the dying expiry of 5.3) each set the display request `V:4A910` to `0x0C` in the same pass, and the request makes the next list build regenerate queue C before the draw of that frame, so the freed record is not in the queue when the handler runs. In the capture `followup-combat-seat-shot.txt` (a seat marked crashed in flight) the record was freed and not drawn again (below). The port keeps the rule inert: its draw handler never sees an invalid record.

**Cone handlers** (the rescue pod, models 2 and 13, `0x2699E`; model 11, `0x26A3E`; the phase is the record's counter `+0x4C`) choose the start of the detail list into `V:4AAEE` and `V:4AAF2` and then continue into the model drawer [V: `effects_check.py` part 3, phases -3 to 8]: 

| phase p = `+0x4C` | models 2 and 13 | model 11 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| p >= 5 | list `0x41638` (the model's own) | `0x418DE` (the model's own), and `+0x7C` bit 0 set |
| p = 3, 4 | `0x41658` | `0x418FE`, `+0x7C` bit 0 set |
| p = 1, 2 | `0x41666` | `0x4190C`, `+0x7C` bit 0 set |
| p = 0 | `0x41678` | `0x418DA` when the low nibble of `+0x7C` is not 0, else `0x4191A` |
| p < 0 | `0x41674` | `0x4191A` |

The five lists are sub-lists inside the model data (the pod opening and the parachute canopy deploying: five frames of one animation, the largest list for p >= 5). Who counts the phase down and sets `+0x7C` is section 6 (the ejection and the pod).

**The parachute's slot case and the trailing data slot, against the original's own handler** [V, milestone 7: `tests/rig/test_slots_diff.c` no longer writes the list into the rig itself; for the models 2, 13 and 11 it runs the original's handlers `0x2699E` and `0x26A3E` (they choose the list, set `V:4AAEE`/`V:4AAF2` and jump into the model drawer), with the record's counter, `+0x7C` and the 58 prefilled slots random: 6,000 cases of the default run (300 per model), 1,044,000 slots, **0 differences**; `SLOTS_DIFF_MODELS=11 SLOTS_DIFF_N=3000` gives 3,000 parachute cases, 536 of them at phase 0 (247 with a non-zero low nibble of `+0x7C`: the list `0x418DA`; 289 with a zero nibble: `0x4191A`, the slot case), 0 differences]. The handler of the parachute also **sets bit 0 of `+0x7C` from the phase 1 up** (the record's gear byte, which the next pass's phase 0 then finds non-zero); the port does it in the draw handler (`game.c` `draw_handler_one`), not in the list code, and the test applies the same rule to the port's byte before comparing it with the rig's. The trailing data slot of 8.10 rule 5 is checked in the same test for every model (the original's loop stores one rotated data triple after the vertices of a block with b8 = b9: asserted on the rig's own writes in each case). The emulator shows the same for the player's cockpit block (4 vertices, 5 slots stored, 04a 8a).

### 11.6 What the port must do

1. **Effect objects** (Task 6): a table of 16 with the record head, the id and the life word; creation exactly as 11.2 (weapon records only, results `0x10` over land and `0x20`, the back-up along the displacement, slot parity ids, none when full, never freed, cleared at flight start); the life word decrements once per non-paused step (`FLIGHT_STEP_HZ` = 8 here).
2. **Draw** (Task 8): the object is a queue C entry drawn through its descriptor (ids `0x11`, `0x12`, `0x13` = models 33 to 35); the model interpreter needs **op 70** (the timer skip of 11.3, reading the object's life word) and **op 71** (the disc list); the extractor decodes both as selectors without variants today (note 03), so the program of models 33 to 35 must be exported with the skip arithmetic of 11.3 or evaluated by the core's program runner.
3. **The burst** (Task 8): for every drawn entity with the dying bit and a counter 0 to 15, as 11.4, after the model and before the trail; tables from the extractor.
4. **The handler 0x26944** (Task 5/6): the crashed bit becomes dying with the counter 15 and drops the lock on the drawn record. The two places that do it give different posts (12.5): the step converts the bit for a missile kill (no `0x4016`), the draw handler converts it for a gun kill of a drawn designated target (posts `0x4016`). The port must keep both: the conversion in the step, and in the draw pass of the same frame the handler's conversion, drop and post for a record still carrying the crashed bit.

## 12. The radar list, threat bits, target designation and the HUD target data

This section is the radar list and its scope, the threat bits, target designation, the lock and the fire prompt, and the target data of the message line. It is the rule set behind the picture facts of note 06 ("Radar scope", "Target symbols", "Warning line"); the shapes and pixel positions stay there. Rig checks: `radar_check.py` (parts 1 to 4, with 2b) and `hud_target_check.py` (parts A to G).

### 12.1 Where

| item | address | what |
| --- | --- | --- |
| list pointer reset | `0x29D28` | clears `V:4B23A` and points the list writer `V:4AA82` at `0x53368`; called once per frame by the frame routine before the world builder (`0x111A6`) |
