| list builder | `0x1FEE6` (gate), `0x1FF24` (body), `0x29D3A` (one entry) | the tail of the world builder (`0x1FBCC`), reached when the queue walks are done |
| scope draw | `0x35CF2` | called by the frame routine (`0x112D8`); its schedule picks the own-ship plus (`0x35DDE`), the cone lines (`0x35D66`) and always ends in the walk |
| scope walk | `0x35E5E` to `0x362D6` | erase of last pass's blips, the walk over the list (detection, threat bits, blips), the cue dispatch |
| designation | `0x362D8` | the T key request |
| range of the designated target | `0x2835A` | a **pre-step of the world update**: `0x26BFC`, called just **before** the player's step `0x2A2A2` (`0x26C00`), once per pass, not after the input conversion; the head of the update runs, in this order, the life words and the region tick, the clear of W2 bit 0, the two cool-downs, the stale-lock drop `0x26F28` (`0x26BC6`), the player's own counter decrement and W0 `&= ~2`, the strain cue `0x270A0`, the instructor `0x27B1E`, the range, then the player's step (4.8); the routine's second half `0x28422` is shared with the AI range routine `0x283E0` (section 4.2) |
| lock state and marker | `0x38884`, slew `0x38D8C`, lock clear `0x38860` | per frame in the cockpit; note 06 "Target symbols" |
| lock circle, fire prompt, range dot, range number | `0x385F4` (calls `0x3905C`, `0x36930`, `0x392AE`, `0x367DA`) | note 06 gives the shapes |
| gun pipper history | `0x3878E`, called from the common tail `0x38780` of `0x385F4` | `V:4A7CE`, `V:4A7DA`, `V:4A9F6`, `V:4A9F8` |
| message-line target data | `0x36DB2` | every frame in the cockpit; the page word is changed by the Y key (`0x1DF62`) and by designation |
| keys | T `0x1DED0`, Y `0x1DF62`, R `0x1DEF4` | note 08 section 11 |

**Where the pieces sit in the frame** [R: the frame routine `0x11124`, flight path in order]: key poll, accounting and state routine, message generator `0x13C94`; view toggle, **world update** (`0x1F54E` -> `0x26AF8`, the steps, the stale-lock drop at its tail), display lists `0x1F770`, jolt `0x29A5E`, the list pointer reset `0x29D28`, the world builder; then the **gun** (before or after the entity pass `0x1FBCC`, 7.3), whose tail runs the **list builder** `0x1FEE6`/`0x1FF24`; then the **alert arbiter `0x13D4C`** (`0x1127C`), the sound routine `0x14AA0` with the cue player; then the panel and HUD routines, among them the **scope draw and walk** (`0x35CF2` at `0x112D8`: the walk, the threat bits `V:4A925` and the cue bits), the lock and target symbols, the message-line data (`0x36DB2`), and last the step counter. So: **the walk follows the gun and the world update in the same pass**, a gun kill (crashed bit set in the gun call) is seen by the same pass's walk, and a missile kill (set in the world update) too; **the arbiter runs before the walk**, so the threat bits and cues the walk raises reach the arbiter and the cue player in the **next** pass (one pass late), as does the lock state of `0x38884`. **The `0x4016` post and the lock-bit clear of the draw handler** (12.5) are made in the entity pass, which precedes the arbiter in the same pass: the arbiter sees the posted code word in the **same** pass, and the cleared lock bits `0x4200` of `V:4AC08` are seen by `0x38884` later in that pass; the designation drop of the world update is earlier still.

**The scope draw `0x35CF2` and how often it walks** [V: `weapon_check.py scope`: `0x35CF2` run with the walk entry counted, 0 differences]. After its gate `0x35BB6` it dispatches on the **scope redraw counter `V:4A8EF`** (not `V:4A8F0`, which the walk itself tests as its enable and which only the builder `0x1FEE6` clears): **`V:4A8EF` = 0**: by the step counter, n & 14 = 0 (n = 0 or 1 of every 16) the cone lines `0x35D66`, n & 14 = 8 (n = 8 or 9) the own-ship plus `0x35DDE`, every other n the walk alone; each of the three ends in **one** walk `0x35E5E`. **`V:4A8EF` = 1**: the dispatch calls the cone-line routine `0x35D66` and then the own-ship plus routine `0x35DDE`, each of which ends in the walk: the cone lines, a walk, the plus, a second walk. **`V:4A8EF` of 2 or more**: the same, after both erase-list heads `0x537C8` and `0x537F0` have been set to `0xFFFF`. So **on a redraw pass the walk runs twice** (this holds on **every** pass with `V:4A8EF` of 1 or more, i.e. the three passes after each writer listed below; the erase of the previous blips, the new blips and the erase list are written twice on the same page: the picture is the same, `V:4A93B` rises by 2, and the threat bits `V:4A925` are cleared and rebuilt twice; the second walk raises no cue because the first already stored `V:4A926`). The page flip belongs to the display flip, not to the walk. Rig: counts (1, 1), (1, 1), (1, 1) for `V:4A8EF` = 0 at n = 0, 5, 8; (2, 2) for `V:4A8EF` = 1, 2, 3 (walks, `V:4A93B` added). **Complete list of the writers of `V:4A8EF`** [R: every reference to its address and every call of the helper `0x1EFF4`]. The helper stores 3 in **fourteen bytes** at once, each the redraw counter of one HUD drawer, which draws its part again while the counter is positive and counts it down itself:

| byte | drawer (consumer) | what it gates |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `V:4A8EE` | `0x35238` (decrements at `0x35240`) | the instrument panel picture (55 rows from row 145) and, through the block test, whether the panel block runs at all in an external view |
| `V:4A8EF` | the frame routine's scope dispatch `0x35CF2` (decrement `0x113E8`) | the scope's double walk (12.2) |
| `V:4A8F1`, `V:4A8F2` | `0x36C96`, `0x36D24` | the latitude and the longitude digits |
| `V:4A8F3`, `V:4A8F5` | `0x36528`, `0x36590` | the radar range digits and the magnification digit |
| `V:4A8F4` | the message-line driver `0x36DB2` | the redraw of the line with its old text (12.8) |
| `V:4A8F6` | `0x35630` | the afterburner line, BRAKE, ECM and EJECT lamps |
| `V:4A8F7`, `V:4A8F8` | `0x35698`, `0x356F4` (lamp groups of the lamp routine) | their lamps |
| `V:4A8F9` | none (stored, never read) | nothing |
| `V:4A8FB`, `V:4A8FC` | `0x354D4`, `0x3736C` | the weapon station markers and the weapon text (the readout redraw of 7.1 and 7.6 sets the same two bytes to 3) |
| `V:4A8FD` | `0x35808` | the GEAR lamp and symbol |

The helper ends by clearing the window offsets `V:4A990` and `V:4A9D0` **unless the jolt byte `V:4A878` is non-zero** (`0x1F04A`-`0x1F058`). All but `V:4A8F9` matter to a port that keeps the picture in a bitmap that is not cleared every pass. The helper is called by: (1) the frame routine at `0x114A0`: **every 32nd pass** (n & `0x1F` = `0x10`, tested after the n & `0x1F` = 8 case, which does something else and leaves) **while the camera-mode byte `V:4A966` is 0**; the call stands in the common tail of the flight path after the panel and external-view blocks, with **no test of `V:4A994`** (the reading of the earlier port report that it sits in a tracking-camera branch is not borne out by the code); (2) the **resume from the pause screen** (`0x130FE`, in `0x13056`-`0x13114`, note 08 N7), after the window offset words are rewritten; **the whole restore is skipped when `V:4A994` is 0** (`0x130A2`: the common case of a pause while flying the player), so this writer acts only after a pause while another aircraft (the seat, a tracked record) is the flown one; (3) the window-row change of the routine at `0x14338`-`0x14390` (`0x14388`: `V:4AA3C` set to `0xA7`), which needs **camera mode 6** (`V:4A966` = 6) with the camera height `V:4ACFA` below `0x24000`: no flight pass has that mode (the approach cameras are the front end's, 6.1), so this writer is unreachable in flight, but **it acts in the approach** (the pass-93 write of a free flight, captured: `combat7-pass93site.txt`); (4) the head and window set-up helper `0x1E7B2` (it loads the window offsets from the table `0x1E800` by the head code `V:4A85F` and calls `0x1EFF4`), whose callers are the view toggle cases of the view routine (`0x142BC`, `0x14322`, `0x143EA`), the window-row key body `0x1DE8A` (`0x1DECC`) and the view switch for the ejection seat and tracking camera (`0x1EC94`, in the body that starts at `0x1EC60` for a viewed record of class `0x30`). The **head-view key tail** (`0x1E720`-`0x1E7B0`) does **not** reach the helper in two cases: for a viewed record of class `0x30` it leaves at once, and for the head codes 3 to 9 and 12 and above (the codes 10 and 11, like those below 3, always take the table path to the helper), when the window row it would set (`0xB3` for the codes 5 to 7, `0xA7` for the others) already equals `V:4AA3C`, it only resets `V:4AA3E` = `0x32` and `V:4AA40` = `0x320` and leaves ("equal-row" case); otherwise it calls the helper; (5) the end of the jolt routine (`0x29B0A`, after it clears `V:4A9D0` and stores `V:4AA3C` = `0x90`); and (6) the **viewed-record-lost routine `0x14392`** (its call `0x143EA` of `0x1E7B2`): it runs at `0x11434`, in the common tail of the flight path **after the panel block**, not in the world update; when `V:4A994` names a record whose valid bit is clear it resets `V:4A994` and `V:4A996` to 0 and, in the cockpit view, the head code and the window offsets, and calls the helper.

**Where the counters count down.** The frame routine's flight path runs the **panel block** (`0x112CC` onwards: the panel, the scope draw, the HUD and the lamps) when the view is the cockpit (`V:4A83D` = 0), or in an external view only while `V:4A8EE` is non-zero (and, for a viewed aircraft of class `0x30`, only while `V:4A8EE` is non-zero too); otherwise the external-view block `0x113F8` runs. **The panel counter `V:4A8EE`** has the writers: the helper, the head-key body at `0x1E6FE` (**every** change of the head code, including the exits through the seat class and the equal-row case, since it stores before those tests), the seat view switch `0x1EC54`, and the flow states `0x120E0` (in the **dispatch state `0x1201E`**, 08 6.1 item 5, long before the flight: it is not a flight-start writer), `0x1250C` and `0x12730` (flow states before the flight) and `0x12BA0` (the location-chosen state `0x12B74`, which first clears `V:4A86D` and `V:4A865`; it stores 3); the seat view switch (`0x1EC14`) is preceded, at `0x1EC3C`, by the zoom reset `0x1F060` for **both** views (`V:4AAFA` = `0x80`, `V:4A895` bit 7, `V:4A8F5` = 3); its **external** branch stores `V:4A8EE` = 3 at `0x1EC54`; its **cockpit** branch (`0x1EC42` to `0x1EC60`) saves the record's heading `+0x68` in `V:4A9E2` and then, for a viewed record of class `0x30` (the seat), sets the window offsets `V:4AA3E` = `0x32`, `V:4AA40` = `0x320`, clears the head code and the look-at byte, calls the helper `0x1E7B2` (all fourteen bytes = 3) and stores `V:4AA3C` = `0xA7`, while any other class goes to `0x1E632` (head code 0 and the common body `0x1E6F8`, which stores `V:4A8EE` = 3 at `0x1E6FE`, repeats the zoom reset, sets `V:4A9EE` = -1 and `V:4A910` = `0xFF`); it is read at `0x1129A` and `0x112C2` and decremented by the panel draw (`0x35240`). **In an external view the panel block therefore runs for the 3 passes after every switch into the external view (while `V:4A8EE` is non-zero)**: in them `V:4A8EF` counts down, the scope walks (twice) and the HUD driver's external return `0x37D70` clears the fire-prompt state `V:4A96C`. **What `V:4A8EE` holds on the first passes of a free flight**: the gate-1 passes between the stores above and the flight (the 30 blanks of passes with the gate open in `0x12C6E`, after the location-chosen store) run the panel block in the external view and count it down, so at the first flight pass it is **0** and the panel block does **not** run in the chase view on the first three passes (no countdown of `V:4A8EF`, no double walk); it becomes 3 again only through a writer above (the 32nd-pass writer first acts at n = 16, a view or head key, the seat). For the mission modes the first pass is in the cockpit view, where the block runs whatever `V:4A8EE` holds [R; a capture of the first free-flight passes would confirm]. **`V:4A8EE` and `V:4A8EF` at the first flight pass, per mode** [V: `combat-panel-first-demo.txt`, `-train1.txt`, `-train2.txt`, `-qual.txt` (note 01a), the first pass being the first with `V:4A984` bit `0x40` and the step counter 0]: **demo** (menu item 1 after the boot; the view is the cockpit): `V:4A8EE` 0, `V:4A8EF` `0xFF`; **training part 1** (the external chase view): both **0**, after the gate-open passes of the briefing's end (the panel block ran in them and counted both down: `V:4A8EE` 3, 2, 0 and `V:4A8EF` 3, 2, 1, 0), so the panel block does **not** run on the first flight passes and the periodic writer first acts at n = `0x10` (the reads at n = `0x11` and `0x31` show 3, then 2, 1, 0); **training part 2** (cockpit): `V:4A8EE` 0, `V:4A8EF` `0xFF`; **the qualification** (cockpit): `V:4A8EE` 0, `V:4A8EF` `0xFF`. In the cockpit views the panel block runs whatever `V:4A8EE` holds. Before the flights the passes that run it are those with the gate 1 and a cockpit view or `V:4A8EE` above 0: in the demo the passes after the dispatch store (3, 2, 1, 0) and after the camera-mode change into the flight view (`V:4A8EE` = 3 at the switch), in training part 1 the four gate-open passes before the flight, in the qualification three. Free flight [V: `followup-combat-first-pass.txt` in `followup-combat-batch-b.txt`: `aircraft-select.vasnap`, key 1, one sample per 2 video frames for 350 samples]: **`V:4A8EE` = 0 and `V:4A8EF` = `0xFF`** from the first sample (the step counter reads 30 and the gate `V:4A84D` is 1, the view `V:4A83D` = 1, the chase view) **until pass 93**, where both become 3 and then 2, 1, 0 in the passes 94, 95 and 96: so the panel block does not run in the chase view on the first passes of a free flight, `V:4A8EF` holds its boot value `0xFF` (negative: the signed compare of 12.2 takes the single-walk path), and **the first writer is at pass 93 of this counter, not a pass with the 32nd-pass rule's n mod 32 = 16** (80 or 112; the counter of this capture was not reset at the flight start, so its offset against the rule's n is not known): **the writer that sets both to 3 at pass 93 is the window-row change at `0x14388`, writer (3) below, in the approach camera** [V, milestone 7: `combat7-pass93trace.txt` and `combat7-pass93site.txt` (generator `gen-combat7.py pass93trace`, `pass93site`): the same run as the capture above (`aircraft-select.vasnap`, key 1) is stepped to `V:4A992` = 92 (568 video frames after the key) and stopped by `runto`: at pass 93 the machine reaches the call site `0x14388` and then the helper `0x1EFF4` in that pass, with the **camera mode `V:4A966` = 6** (the approach camera of the start, the flow state `0x12D58`) and the camera height `V:4ACFA` = `0x00023D2E`, below `0x24000`; the window row `V:4AA3C` changes from `0xB3` to `0xA7` there, and after the helper `V:4A8EE` = `V:4A8EF` = 3 together with the redraw bytes `V:4A8F1` to `V:4A8F8`]. So the writer 3 of this section is **not unreachable**: it acts in the approach that precedes the flight (the camera mode 6 belongs to the front end, 6.1, so no pass of the flight proper has it), when the descending approach camera crosses the height `0x24000`. The pass-93 write is therefore a property of the approach, whose passes the port counts (`approach_passes`) without running the mover; its effects (three passes with the panel block and the scope's double walk, then 0) are inside the approach, in an external view whose panel draw shows nothing (below), and the flight starts with the values of the table. **UNOBSERVABLE in flight**: nothing the player sees or the core reads after the first flight pass depends on it. The port's initial state is as the table says (0 and `0xFF`). **`V:4A8EF` on the first pass of a flight**: no writer sets it in the flight-start tail (the flag fill `0x1D35A` does not cover it, and `0x120E0` sets only `V:4A8EE`); it holds the value the previous screens left (0 after boot), and that first pass (n = 0) takes the cone-line branch with one walk. **The paused pass**: the pause screen has the gate `V:4A84D` = 0, so the frame routine skips `0x1114A`-`0x114BE` altogether: no decrement of `V:4A8EF`, no map call (`0x11440`), no periodic writer (`0x114A0`), no scope or HUD. The decrement of `V:4A8EF` (`0x113E8`, only while positive) is at the end of the **panel block**, so an external view with `V:4A8EE` = 0 **does not count `V:4A8EF` down** (it keeps whatever value it had), while in the cockpit it falls by one per pass. So the scope walks twice on the three passes after each of these events (3, 2, 1 as the redraw counter), not only on the first pass of a flight.

**What the screen shows in the panel-block passes of an external view** [V: emulator `combat-panel-block.txt`, `airborne.vasnap`, KP-ENTER from the cockpit at sample 2, 48 samples of 2 video frames with a frame dump each (note 01a)]. `V:4A83D` turns `0xFF` in the pass `n` = `0xE8` and `V:4A8EE` and `V:4A8EF` read **3 together** in that pass, then 2 in `0xE9`, 1 in `0xEA` and 0 from `0xEB` (the panel block ran in those three passes and counted both down in lockstep); the periodic writer is seen at `n` = `0xF0`, which sets both to 3 again for `0xF1` to `0xF3` (3, 2, ...), as 12.1 says. **The cockpit panel picture is never drawn over the external view**: the first external picture appears two passes' worth of video frames after the switch (the flip lags the pass), and in every frame from then on, including those of the passes with `V:4A8EE` non-zero and of the re-run at `n` = `0xF1`, the panel rows (232 to 250 of the 913 x 313 frame, 640 pixels wide) hold **0 non-black pixels** (against 2,214 in the cockpit frames), the window shows the chase view and the three-label text line (heading, speed, altitude) in rows 208 to 231, and nothing else changes between the passes (no scope or panel remnant: the colour at the window's lower edge only follows the scene). The panel draw `0x35238` is a blitter copy of 55 rows from row 145 (`d1` = `0x16A8` plus `V:4A9D0`) of the clean panel picture; in the external view it leaves the screen unchanged. **Why the panel draw changes nothing in an external view (mechanism, R + rig; the picture V).** The draw (`0x35238`) first decrements `V:4A8EE`, then asks the window-clip helper `0x35BB6` how many of the 55 panel rows are inside the window: the helper takes the window-extension word `V:4AA3E` (0 in the cockpit; **`0x32` in a full-width external view**, 12.1's table) and answers "none" (a negative result) when it is **0x14 (20) or more**, so the blitter copy of the panel picture is **skipped**. What the draw does instead is the second part, which runs whenever `V:4AA3E` is non-zero: four blitter fills of the **20 rows from the window's last row `V:4AA3C`** over the full width (the destination row is `V:4AA3C` plus one, times 40 bytes, into each plane: `0x1C20` is row 180, the value of the earlier reading for `V:4AA3C` = `0xB3`; in the chase view, where the window ends at `0xA7`, the fills start at row 168 of the plane (the plane pointers of the draw table `V:4A76E` sit one row above the first row the window clear touches) and cover rows 168 to 187; 20 rows of 20 words) with a constant source word of `0xFFFF`: the two planes at the **lower addresses** (the hardware's planes 1 and 2, the bits 0 and 1 of the colour index; the draw table lists the planes from the highest address down, so these are its planes 2 and 3) are **set** (`BLTCON0` `0x03FA`) and the other two cleared (`0x030A`): the pattern is the **colour index 3** (`0x111` in that view's palette, a near-black grey), not 12 as the earlier reading of the table order said [V in the rig: `0x35238` run with the blitter registers logged at each blit start, cockpit: four copies of the panel (BLTCON0 `0x09F0`, 55 rows of 20 words, destination rows from 145), external: no copy, four fills]. **Traced in the emulator** (milestone 7; `combat7-panelband.txt`, `blittrace` with the pc of each BLTSIZE write, one `runto` of the frame routine's entry per pass after KP-ENTER, and `combat7-panelmem.txt`, the band rows of both pages and the frame at each pass): the display pages alternate between two sets of planes (page A at `0x10368`, page B at `0x4B2D8` for the lowest plane, the planes `0x1F40` apart; the first clear of a pass starts one row below) and the draw of a pass opens with clears of the window planes; the **four fills are the last four blits of the pass** (written by the panel routine, in the passes in which the panel block runs: the three passes after the switch, 201 and 202 blits in the passes of the trace) and nothing later in the pass writes those rows; the memory of both pages then holds the colour 3 pattern in rows 168 to 187 (the two lower planes `0xFF` apart from the text strip's own bytes, the two upper planes 0) at the start of every following pass; **and the frames show colour 0 (black) in those rows in every pass** (the 3D window ends at row 166 of the picture; the rows below it hold black and the text strip at rows 186 and 187; 19 rows of 320 game pixels counted in each of the passes after the switch: 5,960 of 6,080 black, the rest the text). So the fill is neither overwritten before the flip nor drawn into a buffer other than the drawn page; it is simply **not shown** as colour 3: the picture holds black there where the memory holds colour 3, and the text strip (colour 7, `0x444`) in the same rows is shown, so the rows are displayed and the colour 3 of the display below the window is black; a palette change below the window (a copper split; the driver cannot read the copper list, and `colors` was not taken at the bottom of a frame) is the likely cause and was not traced further. The effect for a port is unchanged: the passes show nothing of the fill, so it needs no band. So **the three passes are invisible**: a port needs only their side effects (the counters, the scope walks' threat bits and `V:4A93B`, the HUD's external return `0x37D70` clearing `V:4A96C`), not a panel draw. The counters were not observed to change anything else on the screen.

**`V:4A9D0`** is **40 x `V:4A990`**: the byte offset of the HUD's vertical offset (40 bytes per row of a plane), kept as a long for the blitter-addressed drawers (the panel copy `0x35284`, the scope `0x35D0A`, the message line `0x3708A`). It is written together with `V:4A990` by the window-row key body (`0x1DEBE`: 40 x the new offset) and by the jolt routine, and cleared with it by the helper's tail and the jolt's end (`0x1F058`, `0x29AFC`). A port that places the HUD by rows needs only `V:4A990`; `V:4A9D0` is its byte form and carries no information of its own [R].

### 12.2 The radar list builder

**Gate** [V: `radar_check.py` part 1, 64 gating cases and 300 random lists, 0 differences]. The builder runs only in the cockpit view (`V:4A83D` = 0); in an external view the list and the flags stay as they are and `V:4A8ED` and `V:4A8F0` are cleared. With the page-redraw counter `V:4A8EF` above 1 (the compare in the scope dispatch `0x35CF2` is a signed byte compare against 1 [V, rig `weapon_check.py scope`: counter 0xFF gives one walk and one `V:4A93B` addition, like 0, 5 and 8, so the boot value 0xFF takes the single-walk path with no erase-list head reset]) it builds on every pass and sets the scope-walk flag `V:4A8F0`. Otherwise (counter 0 or 1) with n = the low bits of the frame counter `V:4A992`: it builds on passes with n mod 4 = 0; `V:4A8F0` is set (incremented) on passes with bit 1 of n clear, that is n mod 4 = 0 and 1. So the scope walk (blips, detection, threat bits) runs on two passes out of four, on the second of the pair without a rebuild (the stale list is walked again). The frame counter does not advance during a pause.

**Selection.** For each record index 0 to 15 except the viewed record (`V:4A996`; the player in a cockpit view): the record is taken when its valid bit (`W0` bit `0x0040`) is set. There is no distance, class or in-play test beyond that: missiles, ships, the carrier, pods and wrecks all enter the list [V].

**Entry** (16 bytes; `0x29D3A`) [V]. The difference vector d of the record's position longs minus the viewed record's; the shift k = 0, 2, 4, ... is the smallest even number for which the largest of |dx|, |dy|, |dz| shifted right by k is at most `0x7FFFFF`; each component is shifted right by k + 8 and truncated to a signed word. With the viewed record's rotation words (`V:4ACB4`, nine words, `0x0100` = 1.0): x = (m0 dx + m1 dy + m2 dz) shifted right by 8 and left by k; z = (m6 dx + m7 dy + m8 dz) shifted right by 8 and left by k (the products are word times word). The entry is x (long), 0 (long), z (long), the flag word `(record offset >> 1) | 0x10`, that is the record index in the high byte and the "plot me" bit `0x0010`, and an unused word. A list is ended by a 16-byte zero entry. The list value is therefore the relative position divided by 256 in position units (the shift only keeps the word precision), z positive is ahead (the scope plots it up) and x positive is plotted to the left of the own-ship mark (the scope shows minus x); y is not in the list.

### 12.3 The scope walk

**Order of one walk** (`0x35E5E`, entered only when `V:4A8F0` is non-zero) [V: part 2, 300 passes in the final run, 0 differences].

1. **Erase.** The pen is 0. The erase list of the current page (`V:4A724` selects page 0 at `0x537C8` or page 1 at `0x537F0`, `0x28` bytes each: ten entries of two words) is read for up to eleven entries: a first word of `0xFFFF` ends it; a non-negative first word is the x of a single pixel, a word with bit 15 set is the x (bits 0 to 14) of a two-pixel blip (x - 1 and x); the second word is the y. **The eleventh entry is read past the page**: from the first word of page 1 when the page is 0, from the word at `0x53818` when it is 1 (a full page has no room for its terminator). When the list holds ten entries the erase also acts on whatever that word says (a stale (x, y) pair is erased, or nothing when it is `0xFFFF`) [V: the model reads the eleven entries from the rig's memory].
2. `V:4A93B` (the radar frame counter) is incremented; the threat byte `V:4A925` is cleared.
3. **The walk** over the list in order until a zero entry. Per entry (flag bit 4 = plot me; the record is at index = high byte of the flag word):
   * flag bit 4 clear: nothing is done except clearing the drawn bit (flag bit 5) and going on.
   * The record is the viewed one, or its valid bit is clear, or its on-the-ground bit (`W2` bit `0x0080`): `W0` bit `0x4000` is cleared, flag bit 5 is cleared, next entry.
   * **Window.** nx = minus the list x shifted right by 13 (rounded half up), nz = minus the list z shifted right by 13 (rounded half up); the record is inside when -27 <= nx <= 27 and -16 <= nz <= 21. Outside: `W0` bit `0x4000` is cleared, flag bit 5 is cleared, next entry.
   * **Detection** (skipped in the modes 2 and `0x7D` (`V:4A95E`), where the detected bit is left as it is and the threat classification is skipped). When |x| or |z| of the list entry exceeds `0x10000` (more than 2^24 = 16,777,216 position units, 81,920 ft or 13.5 nautical miles in each of the two horizontal axes; the height is not tested) the detected bit (`B20` bit 6, byte `+0x20`) of the record is cleared and the classification below is skipped; otherwise the bit is set.
   * **Classification** (only for a detected record; class nibble `0x20` (ships) ends it here). **First the mission-1 counter** `V:4A927`: when a target is designated (`V:4AA78` not negative), the record's class nibble is `0x10` (**any aircraft**, not only the designated one) and the viewed record is the player (`V:4A996` = 0): the byte is 0 -> `0x1E`, a positive value is decremented, and when the decrement reaches 0 it is decremented once more to `0xFF` (negative: never touched again); it becomes negative at the 31st such contact (the F1 identification mission tests it, note 08 section 12.5). **Then the threat bits**, only when the record's out-of-control bit (`B20` bit 1) is clear, its `W0` bits `0x0600` (crashed, dying) are clear and its class nibble is not `0x30`: class nibble 0 (a missile): if `W0` bit 3 (set in the player's own weapons, copied from the player's flags at launch) is set nothing; otherwise, when the missile's target byte (`+0x38`, low seven bits) equals `V:4A994` (the aircraft being flown: 0) bit 4 of `V:4A925` is set for class 0 (the short-range missile) and bit 5 for any other class (class 1, long range). An aircraft (nibble 1): `W0` bit 3 set (not counted: friendly, airliner, pod) sets bit 1; otherwise class `0x15` (the cruise missile) sets bit 3; otherwise bit 2 (hostile). The viewed record's range is not used; every record inside the detection box counts.
   * The record's `W0` bit `0x4000` (radar bookkeeping, section 1.4) is set when the flag bit 4 is set; the two cases above clear it.
   * **Blip.** Both coordinates are shifted by the low nibble of the viewed record's `+0x63` (the scope range: 13, 11 or 9; keys R, note 08 section 11; rounded half up), negated, and tested against the same window (|nx| <= 27, -16 <= nz <= 21); the screen point is x = nx + 158 + the view offset `V:4AA40`, y = nz + 167. A point with x outside 0 to 319 is not drawn but the drawn bit is set. A blip needs flag bit 4 set. **The designated target's blip is drawn only on passes where bit 0 of `V:4A93B` is set** (it blinks at half the walk rate; the drawn bit is set in either case). The blip is two pixels wide when the record's height long (`+0x10`) is at least minus `V:4AB30` (the viewer's height in the same unit), else one pixel. The pen is 5 for the class nibbles `0x20` and `0x30`; else 8 when the detected bit is clear or the out-of-control bit is set; else 4 when `W0` bit 3 is set; else 2 for class nibble 0; else 1. At most **ten** blips are stored per page: the entry is written to the page's erase list (x, or x with bit 15 for two pixels; then y plus the HUD row offset `V:4A990`) and drawn; a later entry is neither stored nor drawn but its drawn bit is set.
   * Flag bit 5 (drawn) is set on every entry that passed the window and the bookkeeping.
4. The list's terminator `0xFFFF` is written after the last stored entry. After ten entries on page 0 this word is the first word of page 1's list; after ten entries on page 1 it is the word at `0x53818`, past both lists [V for page 0]. **The word at `0x53818` is read**: by the erase of the next walk on page 1 (step 1, the eleventh entry), and it is also **the first word of the display-list builder's footprint scratch** (the builder loads `0x53818` as the base of its working table at `0x21260` and reads a block from it at `0x213B4`). So when page 1 is full (ten blips) the walk writes its terminator into the builder's scratch, and the next erase of page 1 reads whatever the builder left there since: if the word is `0xFFFF` the erase ends, otherwise the word is the x of one more erase and the next word its y (an extra erase of one or two pixels in pen 0 at that point of the window, clipped by the pixel routine). The effect needs ten stored blips on page 1 and a list build in between; **it is not observable in play** (an erase of a black pixel in the scope window or in the rows below it) and the port does not model the word [R: every reference to `0x53818` and to the page bounds (`0x537F0`, `0x53818` in the blip store's page limit at `0x3619C`) was listed].

**The scope range of the picture, measured against the mile labels** [V: `followup-combat-scope.txt` and `followup-combat-range-key.txt` in the batch `followup-combat-batch-a.txt` (note 01a): a MiG placed to keep its place ahead of the player at known distances, the scope's rows read from the frame dumps]. One screen pixel is 2^n list units, n the nibble (a list unit is 256 position units, 1 du): **8,192 at the nibble 13 (10,240 ft, 1.94 statute miles), 2,048 at 11 (2,560 ft, 0.485 miles) and 512 at 9 (640 ft, 0.121 miles)**; the window is 27 pixels either side, 16 pixels ahead (up) and 21 behind (down) of the own-ship mark at row 167, column 158. Measured: a target 1.01, 2.99, 4.94 and 6.89 statute miles ahead (4,274, 12,610, 20,869 and 29,107 du, the nose along the velocity) at the nibble 13 is a two-pixel blip (the target is at or above the player's height) at rows **166, 165, 164 and 163** (the offsets 0.52, 1.54, 2.55 and 3.55 pixels rounded up); a target at 4.96 miles (20,941 du) is at row **157** at the nibble 11 (10.2 pixels up: 10) and at row **164** at 13 (2.56: 3), and **absent at the nibble 9** (41 pixels is outside the 16 ahead). **The labels** drawn in the window's lower left are the distance of the window's lower edge, 21 pixels at the scale, rounded down: **"40 MI" for the nibble 13 (21 pixels = 40.7 statute miles), "10 MI" for 11 (10.2 miles), "2 MI" for 9 (2.55 miles)** (the label code stores 40, 10 and 2 for the three nibbles); the miles are statute miles (5,280 ft), and the labels are not proportional to the scale (2.55 shown as 2). **The range key** (R): every press steps the low nibble of **the viewed record's** `+0x63` (the body adds `V:4A996`, the viewed record's offset, to the record table: record 0 in the cockpit, the seat's record in the seat view, the followed record of a tracking camera; the rig run poked `V:4A996` = 0, so it did not separate the two, the code does) one place round the cycle **9, 0xD, 0xB, 9** (9 to 0xD, 0xD to 0xB, 0xB to 9; **any other value goes to 0xB**: rig `weapon_check.py rangekey`, 80 cases of the key body, high nibble kept), sets `V:4AA52` bit 6 (the key click) and the redraw counter `V:4A8F3` = 3; in the emulator 39 presses from the start value 0xD gave exactly the sequence 0xB, 9, 0xD, 0xB, ... one place per press in every frame dump (the label sequence "40 MI", "10 MI", "2 MI" in order, the label picture identical pixel for pixel at each repeat), and **no other nibble value occurred**: the three values of the census are the only ones the key can produce from a start of 0xD. (The nibble read 3 frames after a press sometimes showed the previous value: the key is taken at the next pass boundary.) [Rule for the port: `radar.c` `radar_key_range` must send every nibble other than 9 and 0xB to 0xB (the port sent it to 0xD).]

**The own-ship mark and the cone lines** are drawn by `0x35DDE` and `0x35D66` on the schedule of note 06 (n mod 16 in {8, 9} for the plus, n mod 16 in {0, 1} for the lines, both when `V:4A8EF` is non-zero); pixels and pens are note 06's [V by `tools/hud-emu/verify_addendum.py`, not re-run here].

### 12.4 Threat bits and the cues

The walk leaves the new bits in `V:4A925`; at its end it compares them with the previous walk's `V:4A926` and raises one-shot cue bits in the long `V:4AC0C` (which the cue player `0x14B5A` plays one per pass, note 05 17.13) [V: part 2, cue dispatch compared on every pass, 45 passes with a cue in 300, and part 2b, 300 lists built for chosen bit sets against chosen previous sets, 181 distinct combinations, 0 differences]. Only the first matching row applies:

| new bits (`V:4A925`) | rule | cue raised | hold-off `V:4A93F` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| none | nothing; the code word is not touched (a test of `V:4AB98` against a value its low byte can never take is dead) | | |
| bit 4 (short-range missile at the player) | when bit 4 was not set at the previous walk | `0x0001` | |
| else bit 5 (long-range missile) | when bit 5 was not set before | `0x0001` | |
| else bit 3 (cruise missile) | when bit 3 was not set before. When it was set: with bit 2 set, bit 2 newly set raises the cue; with bit 2 clear the friendly row below applies | `0x0002` | `0x18` |
| else bit 2 (hostile aircraft) | when bit 2 was not set before; when it was set, the friendly row below applies | `0x0002` | `0x18` |
| else bit 1 (friendly aircraft) | when bit 1 was not set before and `V:4A95E` is not 5 (the mode where the two F-16s are placed) | `0x0800` | |
| after the row | `V:4A926` = the new bits (always) | | |

**The hostile Sidewinder, in the emulator** [V: `followup-combat-batch-a.txt`, part `threat` (`gen-followup-combat.py threat`, the `gen-combat-cm.py missile` staging with `V:4A925`, `V:4A926`, `V:4AC08`, `V:4AC0C` added per sample): a class-0 missile 45 passes of the player's velocity behind the player, aimed at it, from `airborne.vasnap`]: in pass 232 the missile's W0 gains `0x4000` (the scope walk has reached it) and **`V:4A925` and `V:4A926` both read `0x10` (bit 4)**; one pass later the post code is **`0x8010`** and `V:4A984` gains bit 7 (`0x0840` becomes `0x08C0`: an alert is shown): the alert of note 06 row 1 appears the pass after the threat bit. So the bit-4 rule is confirmed on the original: the short-range missile aimed at the player sets bit 4 (and no other bit), and the cue and the alert follow one pass later. **The long-range missile** (the same staging with the class byte `+0x62` = 1, `followup-combat-batch-f.txt` part `threat1`): `V:4A925` and `V:4A926` both read **`0x20` (bit 5)** from the pass in which the scope walk reaches it (W0 `0x50C3`), and the post code becomes **`0x8012`** five samples later: bit 5 for class 1 confirmed, with the alert one code above the short-range one.

The hold-off `V:4A93F` = `0x18` (24) delays the message-line target data (12.8) for 24 passes so the warning line shows first; designation sets it to `0xFF` (no delay). The bits feed the lamps (note 06 "threat lamps") and the alert arbiter (note 06 "Arbiter": rows 1, 2, 6, 7 and 9). Which cue sound each bit plays is the cue player's and is listed in section 13.

### 12.5 Target designation (the T key)

The T key (raw `0x14`, event bit `0x8000` of the first demo word) calls the key click `0x37C4A` (a sound request, section 13; skipped in an external view or while `V:4A942` is positive), sets `V:4AA52` bit 7 and the request byte `V:4A871` = 1. The request is served by `0x362D8` in the same radar walk that follows (the byte is cleared there) [V: `radar_check.py` part 3, 600 requests, 0 differences; 83 first designations, 142 steps to the next, 375 ended with no target (deselected), 225 with the old target not in the list].

* **Start.** With no target (`V:4AA78` negative) the search starts at the first list entry. With a target the list is searched for the entry whose record is the target (flag word high byte); the search starts at the entry after it. When the target is not in the list a notice id `0x30` is stored in `V:4AA56` (the routine it is handed to, `0x1F1D6`, is a bare return: no effect) and the search starts at the top.
* **Candidate.** An entry with bit 4 (plot me) **and bit 5 (drawn by the scope this pass)** both set, whose record's class nibble is neither 0 (missiles), `0x20` (ships) nor `0x30`, and whose out-of-control bit (`B20` bit 1) is clear. So a contact must be on the scope, inside the window, to be designated; the detected bit is not required (an undetected contact can be designated and shows "no signal" data, 12.8). Friendly aircraft and airliners are candidates.
* **Result.** The first candidate becomes the target: `V:4AA78` = its record offset (the index times `0x200`), the page word `V:4AA7C` = 1, `V:4A93E` = 1, the hold-off `V:4A93F` = `0xFF`, `V:4A920` = the entry's position in the list plus 1 (written and never read), bit 13 of `V:4A986` cleared. Reaching the zero entry without a candidate **deselects**: `V:4AA78` = `0xFFFF`, `V:4A920` = 0. The cycle is therefore: none, first candidate, ..., last candidate, none, first, ... in list (record index) order.
* **Drops.** The designation is also cleared by the flight start (`0x1D922`), by the stale-lock drop `0x26F28` at every pass (the target's valid bit clear, or its out-of-control bit set: `V:4AA78` and `V:4A9F2` = `0xFFFF`, `V:4A920` = 0; note 07 section 5), and by `0x284F8`, which the draw handler of an aircraft model (`0x26944`) calls when the record being drawn is the target and carries the crashed bit: the message entry 22 (`0x4016`, "target destroyed") is posted, `V:4AA78` = `0xFFFF`, and the lock bits `0x4200` of `V:4AC08` are cleared. **Who posts it depends on who marks the kill.** The frame order is: world update (the steps, then the stale-lock drop at its tail), display lists, the gun routine, the entity pass with the draw handlers [R: the call order of the frame routine `0x11124`: `0x1F54E` -> `0x1F5C6` -> `0x26AF8` at `0x11166`, the builder `0x1F770` at `0x11192`, `0x172DC` at `0x11228`/`0x11242`/`0x1126E`, the entity pass `0x1FBCC` at `0x11250`]. A **missile kill** is marked inside the world update by the missile's scan; the target's own step, later in the same pass, turns the crashed bit into the dying count, and the drop at the tail (the out-of-control bit is set) clears the designation: **no `0x4016` is posted** [V: emulator `combat-radar-demo2.txt`: the designation drops in pass `0x027C` with the message code `0x000E` and then 0 and a blank line; `combat-hit-demolong.txt`: `V:4AA78` clear at the hit; rig: `weapon_check.py order`]. A **gun kill** is marked in the gun call, after the world update. When the gun runs **before** the entity pass of the same frame (the draw scan returned non-zero: external views with an active slot, 7.3) the draw handler of the drawn target sees the crashed bit first: it posts `0x4016` and drops the designation, and the step of the next pass finds the dying bit already set [V: rig `weapon_check.py order`, the handler run on a gun-marked designated record; the post routine is the one used by the 14 post sites of note 06]. When the gun runs **after** the entity pass (the **cockpit view**: tracers are not considered by the scan) the next frame's world update converts the bit in the target's step and the drop clears the designation first: **no post**. So in the cockpit the message is not posted for any kill; in an external view only a gun kill of a drawn target posts it [R for the call order, V for each branch]. **The order rule, settled by the whole-pool captures of milestone 7** [R for the scan, V for both outcomes: `combat7-gunorder.txt` and `combat7-gunorder1.txt` (generator `gen-combat7.py gunorder`, `gunorder1`; logs `extracted/captures/logs/combat7-gunorder*.txt`, one video frame per sample, the whole pool of 20 slots, the camera longs, the player's and the target's records, the post code `V:4AB98`, the latch `V:4A984` and the designation `V:4AA78`), compared with the original's scan by `gunorder_check.py` (the routine run in the rig on each frame's logged pool, camera and records) and 2,000 random pools by `test_weapons_diff.c` part `gunorder` against the port's `weapons_scan`, 0 differences]. The frame routine calls the scan `0x2AD18` (when `V:4A865` is 0) and puts the gun **before** the entity pass only when it answers 1. The scan looks at **one slot only**: the active slot (bit 0 of the flag word's low byte) with the highest index, walking the 20 slots downward (the pool is 20 slots of 64 bytes but only the even ones are used, 7.2, so the slots 0 to 18 are the candidates); none active: 0. That slot answers 0 at once when it is not a chaff slot (flag `0x2000`) and the view is the cockpit (`V:4A83D` = 0); otherwise the distance test runs: the larger-axis distance (the routine `0x20884` on the per-axis absolute differences shifted right by 8) from the camera position `V:4ACF6/FA/FE` to the **slot's owner record** (the position longs at `+0x14`) and to the slot (its position with the cell words shifted left by 22 added to x and z), and the scan answers **1 when the owner's distance is not greater than the slot's** (a signed word compare of the two results), else 0. The distance routine works on the **low words** of its three inputs: a per-axis difference of two cells or more (a word of 0x8000 or more after the shift) makes its word compares go negative and its table lookup read the image at a wrapped offset; the port takes such a distance as an unsigned word (NOTE-GAP, `weapons.c` `scan_abs8`; only a stale camera of a cockpit view with a chaff slot, or a tracer two cells from its owner, reaches it; the rig test does not draw those cases). The camera longs are written by the external cameras only, so a cockpit view keeps the last external value (`game.c` `scan_cam`).

**What the captures show.** (i) *A kill by the third hit* (`combat7-gunorder.txt`, the chase view, the MiG about 150 du ahead with a hit count of 0, the trigger down at frame 0): the first tracer is made in frame 16 and hits in frame 17, the second in frame 22 and the third, made in slot 4, kills in frame 32. In the pass of the kill the pool already holds the stopped tracers of the two earlier hits (slots 0 and 2, flags `0x0413`), the scan answers 1 on the highest active slot (2) for the state of frame 28 to 30, the gun runs first and marks the kill, and **the same pass's draw handler sees the crashed bit**: in the sample of frame 32 the target's W0 is already `0x1441` (the dying bit set, crashed cleared), `V:4AB98` = `0x4016` ("target destroyed"), `V:4A984` bit 0 set and the designation `V:4AA78` = `0xFFFF`: **the message is posted and the designation dropped in the pass of the kill**. (ii) *A kill by the first tracer of an empty pool* (`combat7-gunorder1.txt`, the MiG staged with a hit count of 2 so that one hit kills; the same run as the earlier `gunext2` of `followup-combat-batch-g.txt`): the pool holds no active slot until the gun routine itself makes the tracer, the scan answers 0, the entity pass runs first, the gun marks the kill after it (frame 13, W0 `0x1241`: crashed), the designation drops two frames later in the next pass's world update and the dying conversion follows (`0x1441` in frame 15); **no `0x4016`** (`V:4AB98` stays 0 for all 19 frames). The milestone-5 reading ("in neither view was 0x4016 posted") was this second case; the slots of the earlier log that were read for the scan (0 to 9) were the **post-kill** state, and the rule was applied to a pool state that the scan never saw. **Port rule** (`game.c` `gun_precedes_entity_pass`, `weapons.c` `weapons_scan`): the map view puts the gun first; otherwise the original's scan decides; the draw handler's branch (post `0x4016`, drop the designation, start the dying count) acts in the pass in which it sees the crashed bit, whichever call marked it. In the cockpit view (no chaff slot) the scan is 0, so a cockpit gun kill posts nothing and is converted by the next world update, as before. [Deviation fixed in milestone 7: the port had never put the gun first in an external view and posted nothing for a gun kill there; the golden runs do not shoot in an external view, so none changed.]
* **Who else reads it.** The missile launch (section 8) takes the target from `V:4AA78`; the AI's reaction to a missile aimed at it reads `V:4AA78` and the launch flag `V:4A86F` (section 4.5); the scope blink (12.3); the object pipeline writes the target's projected screen point to `V:4A9EE`/`V:4A9F0` when it draws that entity (note 06).
* **The F1 counter.** (The crash restart `0x1D538` calls `0x1D8B8`, the same player-start routine, which clears `V:4A927`, `V:4A951`, `V:4A952`, `V:4A96B`, `V:4A969`, `V:4A941` and `V:4A96D` again [R]; note 08 12.5.) `V:4A927` of 12.3 is the success counter of the mode-3 identification mission (`0x285C0`, note 08 section 12.5): 31 contacts of aircraft in the window with a target designated end the mission with the code `0xFF`. The designation of any target (even a friendly) is enough [V for the counter in the rig; R for the script].

### 12.6 The range of the designated target (`E+0x4A`)

[V: `hud_target_check.py` part E, 400 updates of the original's `0x2835A`, 0 differences; 137 recomputed, 83 skipped, 38 beyond range, 99 in range]

* Only with a designated target (`V:4AA78` above 0: index 0 is the player and never a target). Each pass adds `0x10` to the high nibble of `E+0x39` (the call counter; the low nibble, the throttle ramp, is kept).
* **Schedule.** The range is recomputed on this pass when the old range `E+0x4A` is below `0x480`; otherwise only when the counter nibble exceeds 2 (old range below `0x900`) or 5 (further): every pass, every third pass, every sixth pass. On a recompute the counter nibble is cleared. A range word of `0x7FFF` (unknown) counts as far. The unit of the passes is the world update pass; note 08 section 8.1 gives the conversion to the port's step.
* **Value.** In fine units (256 position units, 1.25 ft; one cell is `0x4000`): dx and dz are the absolute differences of the two records' (cell x 16384 + fine x) and (cell z 16384 + fine z); dy is the absolute difference of the `+0x10` longs (height >> 8). If any of the three exceeds `0x7F00` the range is `0x7FFF` and **B4 bit 0 is not set**; otherwise the range is the approximate length `0x20884` (note 03 `fx_dist`) of the three, stored in `E+0x4A`, B4 bit 0 ("range computed this pass") is set, and the engagement byte `+0x7A` (zero for the player) is left alone. The player's routine does not write the range class `+0x63`.
* A cleared designation leaves `E+0x4A` and `E+0x39` as they were.
* The unit in the cockpit: the fire prompt needs a range of at most `0x900` (2,304 fine units, 2,880 ft); the lock limits are `0x1800` (7,680 ft) and `0x2700` (12,480 ft); the range dot's full scale is 36,000 (45,000 ft).

### 12.7 The lock, the fire prompt, the pipper and the range number

**The marker and the lock state** (`0x38884`) [V: part F, 600 runs, 0 differences; lock held in 34, ready in 97, fast-closing in 112, lock-lost cue in 109, periodic re-cue in 267]. The marker is the slewing mark `V:4A9FA/FC` of note 06; the target point `V:4A9F2/F4` is the point the **slew `0x38D8C`** has just copied from the scope walk's value `V:4A9EE` in the same pass. **The HUD driver `0x37D78` calls, in this order, the slew `0x38D8C`, the lock state `0x38884`, the line routine `0x36724`, the HUD lines and, last, the sight `0x385F4`**; the walk (`0x35CF2` at `0x112D8`) precedes the whole driver. The slew copies `V:4A9EE` to `V:4A9F2` (and sets `V:4A9EE` = -1) only when its loop **completes** (`0x38FE8`); it returns early, copying nothing, when the box point is at or below 0 (`0x38EFE`, `0x38F04`), and then the lock state and the sight use the **last copied point**. The fire prompt, the gun sight and the lock state read the **viewed record's** `+0x63` (the selector and range nibble of `V:4A996`'s record; `0x385F4` and `0x38884` add `V:4A996` to the record base), not record 0's. Missile weapons only (the selection nibble of `E+0x63` is `0x20` or `0x30`):

| condition (first match) | `V:4AC08` | `V:4A96C` | cue (`V:4AC0C`) | draws |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| no target, gun selected, or the marker invalid (x negative) | bits `0x4200` cleared; if any was set cue `0x08` | unchanged | `0x08` once | nothing |
| the marker outside x 97 to 221 or y 47 to 133 | as above | 0 | `0x08` once | marker (`0x3936E`) |
| the marker more than 5 pixels from the target point on x or y ("out of the box") | when the lock bit `0x4000` is set, or `0x0200` is clear: `0x4000` cleared, `0x0200` set, cue `0x100` (lock lost); with `0x0200` already set only on passes where `V:4A993` & `0x1F` = 9 | 0 | `0x100` | marker |
| in the box, selection other than `0x20`/`0x30` | `0x4000` set, `0x0200` cleared | unchanged | | nothing |
| in the box, range `E+0x4A` at or above the limit (`0x2700` for the long-range missile `0x30`, `0x1800` for `0x20`) | `0x4000` set, `0x0200` cleared; on passes with `V:4A993` & `0x1F` = 9 the lock-lost change above | 0 | `0x100` on those passes | marker |
| in the box and in range, closing delta `V:4ABFE` >= -675 (`0xFD5D`) | `0x4000` set | 1 | `0x04` when `V:4A96C` was not 1 or on passes with `V:4A993` & `0x1F` = 9 | marker and the in-range form of the prompt |
| in the box and in range, closing delta below -675 | `0x4000` set | 2 | `0x10` when `V:4A96C` was not 2 | marker and the prompt |

The prompt `0x36930` is drawn whenever `V:4A96C` is non-zero after the update. The sound each cue bit plays is the cue player's (section 13). "Lock" for the missile launch is the bit `0x4000`: **a launch consumes it** (cue `0x08`, `0x275C4`, section 8) and does not require it; a missile can be launched at any designated target (section 8 gives the guidance consequences). The closing delta `V:4ABFE` is the range number's argument (below): the change of the range word since the previous update, negative when closing; a target closing faster than 675 fine units per update gives state 2.

**The gun branch** (`0x385F4`, selection nibble `0x10`) [V: part C, 600 runs, 0 differences]. The fire prompt (the gun form, `0x36930`) is drawn when the target point x is positive, |`V:4A9F2` - `V:4A9F6`| and |`V:4A9F4` - `V:4A9F8`| are at most 8 and the range `E+0x4A` is at most `0x900` (signed compare). On the first pass of the condition with a target designated: cue `0x04` and `V:4A96C` = 1; while `V:4A96C` is 1 the prompt stays without a new cue. When the condition stops holding with `V:4A96C` non-zero: `V:4A96C` = 0 and cue `0x08`. The pipper pixel (`0x340A0`) is drawn at `V:4A9F6/F8` when its x is positive, **in pen 13**: `0x385F4` sets no pen before it, the pixel routine takes the pen word `V:4AA0C` as the HUD left it, and the HUD draw (`0x37D78`) runs `0x37E2C` (which stores 13) just before; the pipper exists only with the HUD on (`V:4A859` non-zero: with it off `0x37D78` returns before `0x385F4`) [V: the rig run of `0x37D78` with a logger at the pixel call from `0x386CE`: pen 13 for the gun with the HUD on in all four target/prompt combinations, no call with the HUD off]; **then**, gun or missile, the lock circle (`0x3905C`, 80 points, pen 10) at the same point plus the row offset, and, with a target, the range dot (`0x392AE`, pen 13) at index k = (`E+0x4A` x 76) / 36000 (unsigned word divide), and the range number. With a missile selected and a target, `V:4A9F6/F8` is set to (159, 91) while the target point x is not positive and otherwise left alone (the circle is centred on the stored point); with a missile selected and no target the routine draws nothing. Only at the zoom factor `V:4AAFA` = `0x80`.

**The sight reads the viewed record, not record 0**: besides `+0x63`, the range `+0x4A` (the compare with `0x900` at `0x38676`, the range dot `0x38722` and the number `0x38744`) and the record's B4, whose **bit 0 it clears** at `0x38756` (the range-update flag of 12.6), are those of `V:4A996`'s record. **In a script's count pass** (`V:4A866` non-zero with the gate 1) the whole of `0x385F4` runs, the prompt, the circle, the dot and the number included; only the range-delta update and the pipper history test `V:4A866` and are skipped, so the number uses the stored delta.

**The invalid marker** is set to x = -1 with y = **the marker's old x** (the registers hold x in D2 and y in D3; `0x38DD0`/`0x38EC4` store D4 = -1 at `V:4A9FA` and **D2** at `V:4A9FC`): after a whole pass of four iterations without the early return, the second iteration reloads x = -1 and so y becomes -1 as well; after an early return it keeps the old x as y. The initialising marker is (159, 91) (`0x38DDC`). **The slew `0x38D8C`** is a loop of four iterations (`V:4AA62` = 0, 4, 8, 12): each reloads the target point `V:4A9EE` and the marker `V:4A9FA/FC` and does at most one slew step, then builds the box point and draws one line of the box; the first call with the marker invalid or at x <= 0 takes one iteration to set it to (159, 91) and the others slew; `V:4A866` skips **only the marker update** (`0x38DA8` jumps to the box part `0x38ED0`); the box point is the target point plus the jitter (only when record 0's `+0x56` is non-zero: the pair of words at `0x38FFC` indexed by record 0's `+0x16` & `0x1C`), plus `V:4AA40` on x and `V:4A990` on y; when that point's x or y is at or below 0 the routine **returns at once** (so after one slew step, without the copy of the target point). It reads `V:4AC08` bit `0x4000` (in the box, `0x38DE8`) but **writes no bit of `V:4AC08`**; the in-box bits are the lock state's. [R]

**The range number** (`0x367DA`, only for a range of at most `0x7F00`) [V: part C]. The argument is the change of `E+0x4A` between the last two updates: when B4 bit 0 is set (and the game is not paused) B4 bit 0 is cleared, the argument is the new range minus `V:4ABFC`, which is stored in `V:4ABFE`, and the new range in `V:4ABFC`; otherwise (or paused) it is the stored `V:4ABFE`. So the number follows the update schedule of 12.6: with the schedule of one update per pass inside `0x480`, per three passes to `0x900`, per six beyond, it is the range change **per update, not per second**. It is printed as a sign and four digits [V: part G, 200 values, 0 differences]: **a space for a negative change (closing) and a minus sign for a non-negative one (opening or steady)**, then the magnitude modulo 10,000 as four decimal digits **with the leading zeros kept** (a steady range reads `-0000`); pen 13.

**Where the range number stands** [V: `combat-range-number.txt`, the demo's AIM-120 with the lock state `V:4A96C` = 1, pass 0x23A: the sign cell blank, the four digits read the closing delta -232 (0xFF18) as 0232; R for the rule]. The routine sets the text pen word `V:4AA0C` to 13 and hands the five-character string (the sign character, then the four digits, at `V:4A8B2`) to the 3 x 5 text drawer's fixed-position entry `0x3756A` with the text origin at the byte column 22 of scanline 123 (the screen offset 123 x 40 + 22) and a count of five characters. That drawer is the one of the message line and of the fire prompt: the first character's cell starts 12 pixels to the right of the origin's byte column (`0x3645C`: the cell table of the drawer starts at the shift of 12 pixels) and every further character is 4 pixels on; a space character is skipped, so the sign of a closing target leaves its cell empty. The pixels therefore are: **the sign at x 188, the digits at x 192, 196, 200 and 204, the top row of the 3 x 5 cells at scanline 123** (rows 123 to 127), in pen 13. The same rule puts the fire prompt (origin byte column 24, scanline 129) at x 204 and the message line (origin byte column 12, scanline 192) at x 108 as the notes above give them, and the capture confirms both axes (the outline top at row 37 fixes the capture's row offset, the digits come out at x 192 to 206). The head-turn offset `V:4AA3E` (in words, 0 in the straight cockpit view) and the jolt's row offset `V:4A9D0` are added by the drawer like for every other HUD text; both were 0 in the capture.

**The gun pipper** (`0x3878E`) [V: part D, 200 runs, 0 differences against the original's own projection `0x3377C`]. At every pass the cockpit draws (not paused, zoom `0x80`, through the common tail of `0x385F4`) the aircraft computes the point 256 units ahead of its nose and 24 units above (position + (24 x second column + 256 x third column of the attitude matrix at `+0x92..` ) >> 6 with the columns' x, y and z words at `+0x94/+0x96`, `+0x9A/+0x9C`, `+0xA0/+0xA2`), stores it in `V:4A7CE` and the point stored the call before in `V:4A7DA`; the pipper `V:4A9F6/F8` is the projection (through the viewing matrix `V:4AC90`, the difference of the older point from the aircraft's current position divided by 256) of **the point stored two calls earlier**. The gun sight therefore lags the nose by two passes; a stopped aircraft shows the pipper at the nose point. (The tracer pool and the bullets are section 7.)

### 12.8 The message-line target data and the Y pages

The driver `0x36DB2` [V: part A, 600 runs, 0 differences; pages 1, 2, 3: 44, 23, 19 builds, 70 undetected, 162 with no target]. With a target (`V:4AA78` not negative) and no latched message (`V:4A984` bits 0 and 7 both clear; the arbiter clears bit 7 for the hostile, cruise-missile and friendly rows, note 06 so those rows leave the line to the target data) the hold-off `V:4A93F` is decremented and the work proceeds only when it was negative before (it is then set to `0xFF`; the value `0x18` of 12.4 delays 24 passes, during which the no-target branch below shows the posted message; `0xFF` does not delay). Then:

* If the redraw counter `V:4A8F4` is positive the line is drawn again with the old text (counter - 1 on each draw while it lasts). Otherwise a new page is chosen: when `V:4A93E` is negative the page word `V:4AA7C` is increased by 1 (signed compare with 3: above 3 it becomes 1) and `V:4A93E` = 1; when `V:4A93E` is positive with bit 0 set (just after a designation) the page is kept; otherwise (zero, or positive with bit 0 clear) nothing is built or drawn. `V:4A93E` is a countdown that the clock routine (`0x29896`, once per **change of the system clock's seconds value** `V:4ABAA`, i.e. per second of the real (emulated) clock read by `0x18E54` from the timer device, **not per pass**: the block at `0x29868` runs only when `V:4ABAA` differs from the last value `V:4ABBA`; it also counts down `V:4A93D`, `V:4A949`, `V:4A942` and `V:4A93C`; the clock is not read while the pass loop is paused; the port counts game seconds by the one rule of note 08 section 8.1, not 8 passes) [R: the routine and its caller `0x1F54E`-side order; the 01a capture shows pages 2, 3 and 1 at the sampled steps] decrements while it is non-negative, 0 included: it starts at 1 after a rebuild and reads negative two ticks later, so **the pages rotate by themselves, 1, 2, 3, 1, ..., about every two to three seconds** (the redraw counter delays the advance until it has run out); after the Y key (`V:4A93E` = 5) the next automatic advance comes six ticks later. The byte starts at `0xFF` (negative), so the first designation's page 1 is built at once and the rotation begins two ticks later [R; the capture of 01a shows pages 2, 3 and 1 at the sampled steps]. The page word gets bit 15 (rebuild), `V:4A8F4` = 2.
* A rebuilt line (26 characters, colour bits of `V:4A91A`): blank, then the **name** at character 4 by the target's class (the strings are the names of the aircraft types; only the class decides): `0x12` and `0x13` the two hostile fighter types (colour bits `10`, the top two bits of `V:4A91A`), `0x14` Air Force One, `0x16` and `0x17` the two airliners (the three with colour bits `01`), `0x10` the F-16 (colour `01`), `0x15` the cruise missile (colour `10`), any other class no name (colour `10`); when the target's detected bit (`B20` bit 6) is clear the name is the "no signal" text and the colour bits are 0. **The pen of the colour bits** (the text routine `0x3706A` draws the glyphs into one plane with the set minterm and clears them in two others, the plane table `V:4A76E` being plane 3, 2, 1, 0 at the offsets 0, 4, 8, `0xC`): bits `10` and `11` (alerts) set plane 0 and clear planes 2 and 3 = **pen 1**; bits `01` (information) set plane 2 and clear planes 0 and 3 = **pen 4** (both as note 06 measured); bits `00` set plane 3 and clear planes 0 and 2 = **pen 8** (plane 1 untouched, the cell being black) [R, from the code and the plane order; the two measured pens fix the reading]. Then, at character 13, the **page**: 1 the label for altitude and five digits at characters 18 to 22: 5 x (`+0x18` >> 10) feet, leading zeros blanked; 2 the label for heading and three digits at 19 to 21: (`+0x68` >> 3) / 10, leading zeros kept; 3 the label for speed and four digits at 18 to 21: the absolute value of the speed word `+0x6E` divided by 12 (knots) and **0 when the target's hit bit `W0` `0x8000` is set**, leading zeros blanked. The labels are the strings at `0x37197`, `0x3719C` and `0x371A1`. The drawing is `0x3706A`. The strings are laid out in one run from `0x3717C` to `0x371DE`: the 26-character blank line that starts every build (`0x3717C`), the three labels (four characters and a terminator each), then eight names of six characters and a terminator, seven bytes apart from `0x371A6`: the classes `0x12`, `0x13`, `0x14`, `0x10`, `0x15` in that order, the undetected text (`0x371C9`), then `0x16` and `0x17`. The extractor writes the labels and the names as `tables/target_text.bin` (version 16); the frontend draws the name at character 4, the label at character 13 and the digits as above, each character 4 pixels apart from x 108, in the pen of the colour bits (pen 1 for bits `10` and `11`, pen 4 for bits `01`, pen 8 for bits `00`, see above) [R].
* **No target**: the line shows the posted message. With `V:4AA7C` = 0 the message text of the code word `V:4AB96` (28-byte records at `0x41FF0`, 26 characters from the second byte) is copied when the code word differs from `V:4AB9C` and `V:4A8F4` = 2, else redrawn while the counter is positive. With `V:4AA7C` positive (a target page was shown and the target is gone) the page word becomes `0xFFFF` and the blank message is drawn; with it negative the page word is cleared and the blank message is drawn.

**The Y key** (`0x1DF62`, raw `0x15`) [V: part B]: `V:4AA7C` + 1 (a signed compare with 3: above 3 it becomes 1, so with bit 15 still set from a previous press it can climb beyond 3 until consumed), bit 15 set, `V:4A93E` = 5, `V:4A8F4` = 2. It acts with or without a target; with no target the next pass of the driver turns the word into 0 (the pending bit makes it negative) and draws the blank message.

### 12.9 The entries 8, 20, 32, 34 and 53 of note 06 (the posters)

The 14 call sites of the post routine `0x29BC8`, the one call of `0x13D00` and the arbiter's nine codes were listed from the whole image [R, complete scan]. **Entry 20** is the first-hit engine failure `0x9014` posted at `0x2A87C`/`0x2A8A0` (section 10). **Entry 53** (`0x35`) is posted at `0x27480` by the training prompt poster when the manoeuvre index is 4 (the fourth record of the table at `0x2749A`) and the height `+0x18` is at most `0x100000` (about 5,120 ft): a "too low" prompt for that manoeuvre. **Entries 8, 32 and 34 are posted nowhere**: no immediate or table word of the image produces their codes (`0x..08`, `0x..20`, `0x..22`) at any of the sites. **`V:4A980` bit 15 and `V:4A98E` bit 5 are never set** [R: every reference to the two words was listed with the image scan of the instructions that name them, the long and byte neighbours (`0x4A97E`, `0x4A981`, `0x4A98C`, `0x4A98D`, `0x4A98F`) included, and every instruction that loads the address of a state block that contains them]: `V:4A980` has one reader (the arbiter, `0x13D7C`) and no store; `V:4A98E` has readers (the arbiter and the draw of the message line) and two stores that only clear bit 5 (the expiry of a message, `0x140DE` and `0x14194`); the only code that writes either word otherwise is the block clear of the words `0x4A978` to `0x4A9DF` at the flight start (`0x1D370`, zeros). Entry 6 (crash imminent) and the blank line of the arbiter's second row are therefore **dead code in the shipped game**, and with entries 8, 32 and 34 the messages the arbiter would show for them never appear. The port needs neither the bits nor the entries (a NOTE-GAP for them may be removed).

### 12.10 Obligations

* Task 6: `radar_step(g)` runs the builder every pass with n mod 4 = 0 and the walk on n mod 4 in {0, 1} where n is the original's pass counter, converted by the one rule of note 08 section 8.1; the erase lists with the page quirk need not be kept in the core (the frontend erases the previous blips), but the **ten-blip limit, the order of the list (record index), the designated blip's blink, the pens, the detected bit and the threat bits are the core's**. `radar_designate(g)` is the cycle of 12.5. The target block is `cp_designated` (`V:4AA78`), the page word, `cp_range` (`E+0x4A`), `cp_pipper` (the history of 12.7), the lock state `V:4A96C`/`V:4AC08` bits, the closing delta `V:4ABFE` and the cue bits as listed.
* Task 8: the range number is the change per update (12.7) with the sign rule; the prompt and cues follow 12.7; the message-line data follows 12.8.

## 13. The trail drawer, the jolt and the sound trigger points

This section is the smoke trail behind a followed record, the one-scanline jolt of the 3D window, every place where the image starts a sound (with the sound object it starts, so that note 10 and milestone 6 have the list) and the writers of the combat counters of the flight log. Rig checks: `trail_check.py`, `jolt_check.py`, `counters_check.py`, `gun_log_check.py`; captures: `combat-fx-jolt.txt`.

### 13.1 The trail ring and its writer (`0x2AC5A`)

**Variables.** `V:54E72` names the **followed record** (a record offset; `0x800`, the training lead, at every flight start `0x1D266`; set to the offset of the record that is hit or crashes by the writers listed below). `0x54E74` is a ring of six positions (three longs each, 12 bytes), `V:54E70` the count (0 to 6) and `V:54E71` the write index (0 to 5). The record byte `+0x3D` is the **trail length** (0 to 4).

**The writer** runs at the end of every record step (the tail `0x2A9BA` calls it, also for steps that end early) and does nothing unless the record is the followed one and the pause byte `V:4A866` is clear [V: `trail_check.py` part 1, 1,200 random states in four seeds, 0 differences]:

1. the record's position longs (`+0x14`, `+0x18`, `+0x1C`) are stored at the write index; while the count is below 5 they are stored in the **next** slot as well;
2. the index advances and wraps to 0 after 5; the count rises to 6 and stays;
3. when the speed word `+0x6E` is not zero **and** the trail bit is set (`W2` bit `0x1000`, the byte at `+0x02` bit 4), the length becomes count - 1 limited to 4;
4. otherwise the length counts down by 1 per step and, when it reaches 0 (or is 0), the ring is emptied (count and index 0).

**The non-player writer's condition (`0x2A954`)** [V: `weapon_check.py nonplayer`, R for the code]. It sits in the result handler for non-player records (`0x2A8E6`-`0x2A9C0`) and is reached only when **all** hold: the record's class byte is not `0x15` and its class nibble is not 0 (a missile jumps over it at `0x2A940`; the cruise missile, class `0x15`, at `0x2A946`; the seat classes are stepped by their own routine, section 6), **and the collision result is `0x40` or more**: the compare at `0x2A94E`/`0x2A952` sends results below `0x40` (the ground result `0x10`, the hull and landmark result `0x20`) straight to the dying start `0x2A99A`. Result `0x40` is the aircraft-with-aircraft contact. So `V:54E72` = the record's own offset, W2 bit `0x1000`, B20 bit 1 and the state byte 1 are written **only when a non-player aircraft touches another aircraft**, and the record is then removed at once (`0x2A9C0`). A ground impact, a hull or landmark impact and every missile impact leave `V:54E72` and W2 unchanged. [Rig: an AI aircraft (record 8) overlapping a friendly aircraft: W0 `0x1080` (removed), W2 `0x1000`, B20 `0x02`, state 1, `V:54E72` = `0x1000`; the same aircraft below the ground: W0 `0x84C0`, W2 `0x0000`, `V:54E72` unchanged. Those who pick the AI dive runs see no write there, as stated.] The other writers of the word: the gun's third hit (`0x2B2AC`, any hit on an aircraft record whatever its class group) and the missile contact code (`0x2B7BC`, 10.3), which mark the **other** record.

**The result handler by class, for the result `0x40` (aircraft contact)** [V: `weapon_check.py result40` and `nonplayer`; the branch addresses R]. The handler `0x2A8E6`-`0x2A9C0` for a non-player record: a result other than `0x40` first sets the hit bit (W0 `0x8000`); a dying record (`0x0400`) with its counter at 15 or below ends at once; **a seat or parachute (class nibble `0x30`) goes on only with the crashed bit `0x0200`** and otherwise the step ends without any change; B20 bit 1 is set, and for results other than `0x40` the active bit `0x1000` is cleared. Then, for the result `0x40`: **class nibble 0 (a missile)**: removed at once, with none of the marks (valid bit cleared, `V:4A910` = `0x0C`); **class byte `0x15` (the cruise missile)**: the dying start `0x2A99A` (dying bit, counter 15), **not removed**, and `V:54E72` and W2 untouched (the other record's mark by the contact code is what `V:54E72` then holds); **every other class** (the aircraft): the marks (`V:54E72` = own offset, W2 `0x1000`, B20 bit 1, state 1) and then removed at once (`0x2A9C0`: valid bit cleared, `V:4A910` = `0x0C`, return without the trail writer). Rig rows (record 8 touching a friendly aircraft): class 0 W0 `0x1080`; class `0x15` W0 `0x14C0` with the counter 15; class `0x12` W0 `0x1080`, W2 `0x1000`, `V:54E72` `0x1000`.

**The player with an ejection nibble and the result `0x40`** [V: `weapon_check.py result40`]. The player's handler jumps to `0x2A8F2` for any result when the nibble is non-zero; for `0x40` it reaches `0x2A954` and `0x2A9C0` with the player as the record: W0 `0x1188` (**the valid bit is cleared**), W2 bit `0x1000`, B20 bit 1, state 1, `V:54E72` = 0, the display request `V:4A910` = `0x0C`, and the contact code has marked the other aircraft as for any contact. The aircraft is therefore **removed from the display lists and from the contact scan** at that pass; **the ejection goes on regardless**: the nibble cycle runs from the world update, not from the record's step (rig: nibble 10 -> 9, 8, 7, then 1, 2, 3, and the **seat appears in record 1 in the sixth pass** after the contact, the viewed record V:4A996 = the seat, `0x200`). In the passes between the removal and the seat (viewed record 0, invalid) nothing of the aircraft is drawn; what the original's screen shows in those five passes was not captured (the window and sky only, R). With nibble 5 (holding at 6) the aircraft is removed the same way and no seat appears (the cycle is past it). Without a nibble the same contact runs the damage handler of 10.4 and the other aircraft is the one `V:54E72` names.

**Complete list of the accesses to `V:54E72`** [R: every literal reference to its address in the image, 7 in all]: written at the flight start (`0x1D266`, `0x0800`), by the player's skid or crash (`0x17024`), by the non-player hit or crash (`0x2A954`), by the gun's third hit (`0x2B2AC`) and by the missile contact code (`0x2B7BC`); read by the entity draw tail (`0x2580A`) and the trail writer (`0x2AC60`). No indirect writer exists.

**Who makes a trail.** Setting `W2` bit `0x1000` and the followed record together is done by: the player's touchdown on water or with the gear up at an indicated speed of `0x3C0` or more (`0x17022`, `0x17024`: the skid or crash trail of note 05, which also takes the camera); a **non-player aircraft's touch of another aircraft** (the collision result `0x40` only: `0x2A954`/`0x2A95E`; ground, hull and landmark impacts and missile impacts write nothing, as stated above); the third gun hit on a record (`0x2B2B4`, `0x2B2AC`); a record that another record's box test hit while it was active (`0x2B7C2`, `0x2B7BC`: the **target of a missile**, and any aircraft rammed); and, for the training lead only, the S key (`0x1ED8E`, note 08 8.6). The player's code clears the bit when the indicated speed falls below `0x3C0` on the ground (`0x171A4`) and at lift-off (`0x17226`), and the S key toggles it for the lead; for a non-player record nothing clears it, and a trail stops being drawn when another record becomes the followed one or when the speed word is 0 (the length then counts down). The training placement `0x2CFDA` (tail `0x2D09A`) empties the ring. **Only one record has a trail at a time**: a new hit takes `V:54E72` over and the old trail is not drawn any more.

### 13.2 The trail drawer (`0x25804`)

Called at the end of the entity draw tail (`0x227D0`), after the model and the burst of 11.4, and it draws only for the item whose record is `V:54E72` and only when the trail length is above 0 [V: `trail_check.py` part 2, 1,200 random states, 4,293 discs, 0 differences, with the distance estimate `0x20884` and the camera rotation `0x31566` taken from the original; states with a length larger than count - 1 are unreachable and read beyond the ring]. A **disc** (the same filled disc as op 62, `0x33788`) is drawn at each stored position, with three more between consecutive discs:

1. The walk draws the **oldest** entries of the ring, never the newest (the newest is the current position, where the model itself is): n = the length, reduced by 1 while the count is below 6, and n + 1 entries are visited. The direction follows the sign of (velocity . (record position - camera position)), each factor shifted right by 8 when the speed word is `0x100` or more and truncated to words before the product (three word-by-word products of 32 bits each, added **in 32 bits with wrap-around**: three signed word-by-word multiplies and two 32-bit additions, then the sign of the 32-bit result at `0x25892`; with factors up to 2^15 each the sum can wrap past 2^31 in extreme cases and the original takes the wrapped sign): **moving toward the camera** (negative) starts at the write index (slot 0 while the count is below 6) and steps up, wrapping to 0 after the last stored entry; **moving away** starts at (write index - count (- 1 while the count is below 6) + length) taken modulo the count and steps down, wrapping to the last stored entry. Both orders are painter order, the farthest entry first. A shorter length (the trail dying away) therefore keeps the oldest end and loses the newest.
2. Per entry: the camera-relative offset d = entry - camera position (`V:4AB34`, `V:4AB38`, `V:4AB3C`); the shift s = 0 when the largest |component| is at most `0x4000`, 4 up to `0x40000`, else 8; the absolute values and the signed values are shifted right by s separately (the distance estimate is taken of the shifted **absolute** values); the disc centre is the camera rotation of the shifted signed offsets (x', y', z'); the radius is `0x9C0` divided by (the distance estimate shifted right by 8 - s), at most `0x7F`, and `0x7F` when that divisor is not positive.
3. Pens by the ring index: even indices pen 10 (12 when the record is out of control, `+0x20` bit 1), odd indices pen 8 (3 when out of control): a wreck leaves a dark trail, an intact skidding aircraft a pale one.
4. Between a disc and the previous one (not for the first, and only when the length is above 1) three discs are drawn first at 1/4, 1/2 and 3/4 of the way from the previous to the current one in x', y', z' **and in radius**; the previous disc is brought to the current shift by shifting its coordinates left by (previous s - current s) as a word shift whose count is taken modulo 64 (a negative count, when the previous entry was nearer than the current, therefore **zeroes** the previous coordinates: the original's quirk, kept).
5. The last disc drawn (the length counted down to 0) has its radius reduced by 1/8. The drawer returns the OR of the disc routine's results.

So a followed record trails up to five chained discs plus the interpolated ones; the radius follows the distance only, there is no taper except the last disc.

### 13.3 The jolt (`0x29A5E`)

A kind byte `V:4A878` (0 none, 1 to 4) is stepped once per game pass by the draw path (a pass is about 6.4 video frames at the original's speed, 7.8 per second) [V: `jolt_check.py`, 8,570 calls in two runs, 1,903 offsets stored, 0 differences; captured on the reference emulator in `combat-fx-jolt.txt`: the index `V:4A947` reads 1, 2, 3, 4 in four consecutive passes after the kind byte is poked, with `V:4A990` = -1, +1, 0, 0, `V:4AA3C` = 143, 145, 144, 144 and `V:4A9D0` = -40, +40, 0, 0 for kind 1; the kinds 2, 3 and 4 give +1, -1 / -1, +1 / +1, -1 and the external-view poke (`V:4AA3E` = 50) clears the kind with no offset]:

| kind | offsets of the four passes (rows) | set by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | -1, +1, 0, 0 | a touchdown (`0x16F7E`), a hit on the player in the damage path (`0x2A8A8`, section 10) |
| 2 | +1, -1, 0, 0 | not set by any shipped code (the table exists; see the list of writers below) |
| 3 | -1, +1, 0, 0 | a landing after a stop (`0x170E8`), a hard touchdown (`0x1683E`) |
| 4 | +1, -1, 0, 0 | a lift-off (`0x171D4`), an event of the take-off roll (`0x1706E`) |
