(The writers are those of note 06 open question 1; the values stored at `0x16F7E` (1) and `0x1706E` (4) were read from the code [R].)

**The writers of `V:4A878`, complete** [R: every instruction of the image that names the byte, found by scanning for its address in the image; the stores are exactly six, and no other store, fill or block copy of the word is among the state-block clears]: the touchdown block at `0x16F7E` (kind 1), the hard touchdown at `0x1683E` (kind 3), the take-off roll event at `0x1706E` (kind 4), the landing after a stop at `0x170E8` (kind 3), the lift-off at `0x171D4` (kind 4) and the player's damage handler at `0x2A8A8` (kind 1, once per contact that reaches the damage path, section 10.3); the jolt routine clears it (`0x29A56`, `0x29AE8`) and reads it (`0x29A5E`), and the one other reader is the HUD helper's tail (`0x1F04A`), which clears the window offsets when the byte is 0 (so a running jolt keeps them). **Kind 2 is stored by nothing** (the two stores whose value comes from a register at `0x16F7E` and `0x1706E` load 1 and 4 immediately before): the table row exists and is unreachable. A hit by a weapon on the player (the damage handler) is the only combat writer; the cruise missile's, the gun's and the missile's contacts with the player all reach it through section 10. The port needs kinds 1, 3 and 4 and may omit kind 2.

Per call: with the kind 0 nothing happens. With |`V:4AA3E`| above 14 (the full-width external views set it to 50; the cockpit view with the panel has 0) the kind is cleared and nothing else changes: **the jolt is a cockpit-view effect only**. Otherwise: when the index has reached 4 the jolt ends: kind and index cleared, bit `0x0400` of `V:4A984` cleared, `V:4A9D0` = 0, `V:4AA3C` = `0x90` (`V:4A990` keeps the last offset, 0). Else on the first call (bit `0x0400` clear) the index is set to 0 and the bit set; the offset d is taken from the table by the index, the index steps, and `V:4A990` = d, `V:4AA3C` = `0x90` + d (the window row), `V:4A9D0` = 40 d (the long that shifts the HUD and panel row addresses by d bitplane rows). Every call that is not the cancelled one also asks for a redraw of the 14 request bytes (`0x1EFF4`, all set to 3), so that the panel and HUD are redrawn at the shifted row. Changing the kind while a jolt runs keeps the index (the poke of a kind 2 into a running kind 1 continued at index 2). Effect on the picture: the 3D window's bottom row moves by one scanline up and down, the HUD and panel rows follow [R for the pixels; V for the variables].

### 13.4 The sound trigger points

**Note 10 (`docs/re/10-sound.md`) now owns the sound: the objects, voices, periods and volumes of every trigger below are its section 8, the wrappers and the loader its sections 2 to 5; this section keeps the trigger points.**

**The sound objects.** The image plays sounds through 37 numbered sound objects (0 to `0x24`) (a table at `0x5591C`, four bytes per object; the player routine `0x19C7C` takes object, voice (channel 0 to 3) and volume). The loader (`0x199CA` to `0x19C58`, `0x19660` to `0x19830`) builds them from the files of the game disk: objects 0 and 1 from `textcpt` (the cockpit engine loop, a left and a right copy on voices 0 and 1), 2 and 3 from `textegn` or `textegn2` (the external engine pair, voices 0 and 1; corrected in note 10 1.3), 5 from `texttre` (the ground contact), 8 and 9 from `textwnd` (wind, voices 0 and 1), `0xB` from `textger` (the gear), `0xD` to `0x22` from `texti0a`, `texti0b`, `texti1` to `texti5` (instances share data), `0x23` and `0x24` from `text201`. Object 4 is a **synthesised tone** (one generator, parameters set by `0x1A042`, voice 3), object 6 a synthesised **noise** buffer of `0x800` bytes (voice 2), object `0xA` the noise buffer again at a lower pitch, object `0xC` an instance of the engine sample 2 at a constant pitch of 140 to 147 with a falling volume (corrected in note 10 3.5); object 7 is not used. A set bit in the longs `V:4AC10..V:4AC13` says that the objects of a sound exist (the loader sets them); a wrapper whose bit is clear does nothing (the noise wrapper `0x1A0DC` then posts a notice instead). All sound routines are skipped by the per-pass sound routine `0x14AA0` while the pause byte or the map/hide byte `V:4A865` is set.

| wrapper | enable bit | object (voice) | arguments | used for |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `0x1A0DC` (a, b) | `V:4AC12` bit 0 | 6 (2) | a = start amplitude, b = duration; the pitch is random | every thud, explosion, bump and the gun without the cannon sample (table below) |
| `0x1A2F0` (6, b) | `V:4AC13` bit 5 | `0xA` (2) | b = `0x16` in the cockpit view, `0x1E` in the others | the gun burst |
| `0x1A042` (nine words) | none | 4 (3) | the tone parameters (table below) | every cue, the HUD beeps, the seat |
| `0x1A18C` (v) | `V:4AC13` bit 2, and the record being stepped is the camera's | 5 (2) | volume v | ground contacts: `0x28` (descent more than `0x180` per step), `0x1E`, `0x0F` (`0x15C44`, `0x1703A`, `0x1704A`) |
| `0x1A1E6` (v) | `V:4AC13` bit 6, and the camera's record | `0xB` (2) | volume 5 or 10 | the gear (the key at `0x1E966` sets the countdown `V:4A93D`; corrected in note 10 5.1) |
| `0x1A258` (v) | `V:4AC12` bit 1 | `0xC` (2) | volume v (called from `0x152C6`) | the missile launch (below) |
| `0x1A24C` | | stops voice 2 | | end of the gear sound |
| `0x19CE6`, `0x19D7A` (v) | `V:4AC13` bit 7 / `V:4AC12` bit 2 | `0xD`, `0xE` / `0x23`, `0x24` (0, 1) | v = `0x32`, `0x0F`, `0x28` | the start-up music (objects `0xD`, `0xE`) and the `text201` pair, not phrases (corrected in note 10 7.1) |
| `0x19DB2`, `0x19E46`, `0x19EBE`, `0x19EFA`, `0x19F9A` | `V:4AC13` bits 0, 1, 4 | 0 and 1; 2 and 3; 8 and 9 | pitch, volume | the engine, wind and afterburner sound of the per-pass routine, selected by the notice byte `V:4A968` (`0xFE` normal, `0xFD`, `0xFC`, `0xFB` the launch, `0xFA` the overspeed message and the failed noise wrapper, not the bump; corrected in note 10 5.4) |

**Combat and damage events** (every one gated by `V:4A865` = 0 and the enable bit unless stated):

| event | trigger point | what is played |
| --- | --- | --- |
| gun, each shot | `0x172DC`, sound calls `0x1749A`, `0x174AC`, `0x174BE` | object `0xA` (voice 2) through `0x1A2F0(6, 0x16 or 0x1E)` when `V:4AC13` bit 5 is set, else object 6 (voice 2) through `0x1A0DC(0x1C, 0x30)` |
| flare or chaff released | `0x17426` | object 6 (voice 2), `0x1A0DC(0x1C, 0x30)`, only when the map-view byte `V:4A865` (which mutes the sounds) is 0 |
| missile launch | `0x27660`, `0x27668` (in `0x275C4`/`0x275F6`): when the launching record is the camera's record `V:4A996` the notice `V:4A968` = `0xFB` and `V:4A84F` = 8 [V: the demo's AIM-120 at the fire event, `combat-fire-demo.txt`: at the launch pass `V:4A968` = `0xFB`, `V:4A84F` = 8, `V:4AC0C` = 8, `V:4A87D` = 1, `V:4A8FB` = 3] | the per-pass routine's case `0xFB` calls `0x152C6` and then `0x1A258`: object `0xC` (the engine sample, voice 2) with a pitch from the notice routine [R]; **a launch by another record, or one outside the camera, makes no sound** |
| missile or gun hit, kill, effect object | none | no sound of its own. **Corrected in note 10 8.2: the explosion of a kill drawn in view makes the ejection-style bang** (`0x1A0DC(0x3C, 0x78)`, halved in the cockpit view) one or two passes after it is drawn, through `V:4A921`, `V:4A922` and `V:4A870` |
| aircraft collision, ground, hull or landmark contact of the player at an indicated speed of `0x3C0` or less | `0x2A834` | `0x1A0DC(0x1C, 0x30)`, once (the first contact, the hit bit `0x8000` not yet set) |
| crash hand-over (the player, cockpit view) | `0x12FA0`, `0x12FAE` | the engine sounds are stopped (`0x115F6`), then `0x1A0DC(0x3F, 0x78)` |
| ejection bang | **`0x14E0E` (outside view, full amplitude), `0x14E32` (cockpit view, amplitude halved)**: corrected in note 10 8.2, the order here was reversed | `0x1A0DC(V:4A922, V:4A921)` = (`0x3F`, `0x78`) when the countdown byte `V:4A870` (set to `0xFF` by the seat routine at the nibble 1 step) is stepped |
| ejection seat sound | `0x26FFE` (called at the nibble 9 step **in the cockpit view**, `V:4A83D` = 0, which includes the camera on the seat; corrected in note 10 8.3) | `0x1A042` with the words (`0xC8`, `0xE`, 8, `0xC8`, 0, 2, 1, 5, 1) |
| pod release (mode 6, the F key) | `0x1EE90` | `0x1A0DC(0x1C, 0x30)` |
| high turn rate | `0x270CE` | the HUD beep `0x37C32(8)` |
| gear down above `0x1200` | `0x2837E` | the HUD beep `0x37C32(4)` |

**Every other call site** (all callers of the wrappers in the image, classified by the routine that contains them [R]; the arguments are the constants in the code):

**The cue player (`0x14B5A`)** takes the bits of the long `V:4AC0C` one per pass (note 05 17.13 for the order and the loss of the others) and plays one tone on object 4 (voice 3) with nine parameters p1 to p9 (the arguments of `0x1A042` in the order a1 to a9: they are stored as p1 -> `0x55CB4`, p2 -> `0x55CBC`, p3 -> `0x55CC4`, p4 -> `0x55CCC`, p5 -> `0x55CD4`, p6 -> `0x55CDC`, p7 -> `0x55CAC`, p8 -> `0x55CA4`, p9 -> `0x55CEC`; note 10 owns their meaning). The cue bits, their parameters and who sets them [parameters and order V: `cue_check.py` runs the original's player with the tone routine logged, ten single bits and 300 random combinations, with the cool-down and the ejection nibble, 310 runs, 0 differences; the writers R]:

| `0x800` | `0x1F4`, `0x0A`, 5, `0x82`, 0, 1, 3, 1, 5 | `0x362B0`: threat bit 1 rises (not in mode 5) |
| `0x08` | stops voice 3 | `0x275EA` (a launch with the lock box set: the event bit is consumed), `0x386B6`, `0x38878` (the prompt or the lock ends), `0x14054` (the clear of an alert message with the sound byte) |
| `0x200` | not played | `0x170F0`: the roll-out (no branch tests it) |

The HUD beeps `0x37C32` and `0x37C4A` use the same generator with p1 = p4 = `0x12C`, p2 = p5 = 4 (2 in `0x37C4A` when the system is running), p3 = p6 = p7 = 1, p8 = p9 = n.

### 13.5 The writers of the flight-log counters

All offsets are in the 78-byte record at `V:1D024` (note 08 10.2); every counter also sets the dirty flag `V:4A87D` = 1.

| field | writer | rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `+0x3A` cannon rounds | `0x173EA` in the gun routine | plus 3 for every shot the routine takes, **including each flare or chaff release** (the dispenser takes a pool slot and the burst counter like a round, but the rounds word `E+0x60` is not reduced for it) [V: `gun_log_check.py` part 1, 2,623 calls, 449 shots or releases, 0 differences in the burst counter, the cool-down, the rounds, the log counter, the dispenser pointers and the flag bytes] |
| `+0x3C` cannon hits | `0x2B25A` in the tracer hit test (`0x2ADDE`) | plus 1 for a tracer that hits an entity whose model block flag `0x10` (the large objects: ships and the carrier) is clear; the same hit increments the target record's own hit counter (its byte `+0x3C`, low nibble, stored only below 3) and the third and later hits crash it (8.2) [V: `weapon_check.py entity`, `combat-hit-gunkill.txt`] |
| `+0x3E` AIM-120 fired | `0x276A6` in the launch | plus 1 when the launching record is the player (`0x4B23C`) and the selection nibble (`E+0x63`) is 3 [V: `gun_log_check.py` part 2, 42 launches and 18 refusals for lack of stock, 0 differences] |
| `+0x42` AIM-9L fired | `0x276C6` | plus 1 for a player launch with any other selection [V: same] |
| `+0x40` AIM-120 hits | `0x2C040` in the tail of the collision scan | plus 1 when the mover is a weapon record with `W0` bit `0x0008` (it copies the player's) and group 0, of class 1, and the other record's bit `0x0008` is clear [V: `counters_check.py`, 4,000 scans in two runs, 1,431 hits, 0 differences] |
| `+0x44` AIM-9L hits | `0x2C02A` | the same for class 0 [V: same] |
| `+0x46` crashes | `0x16FB0` (hard touchdown), `0x2A8C6` (the player's collision result `0x10` or `0x20`) | plus 1; both set `V:4A850` = 4 (note 05) |
| `+0x48` hits from missiles | `0x2C082` | plus 1 when the mover is the player and the other record's class has the high nibble 0 (a missile or any nibble-0 record) and the pair is not a "not counted" pair [V: `counters_check.py`] |

**There is no kill counter in the log.** The record has hit counters for the cannon and for each missile type (a hit is counted at the contact, not at the kill) and no other combat field; the number of destroyed hostiles is the run-time counter `V:4A963` (section 5.5), which the mission scripts read and which is never written to the file. The callsign overrun of note 08 10.5 overlays `+0x3E` to `+0x4D`, that is the AIM-120 and AIM-9L counters, the crash counter and the missile-hit counter, with callsign characters.

The tail of the collision scan also sets `B20` bit `0x80` on **both** records when both have `W0` bit `0x0008` (a weapon of the player hits a not-counted record: a friendly or an airliner) and clears it otherwise; the readers of that bit are in the mission scripts (note 08 part C) [V for the writes]. The scan's first part does to the **other** record what the hit does to the target of a missile: when its active bit `0x1000` is set it gets the crashed bit `0x0200`, and unless its class has the high nibble 0 it is also made out of control (`B20` bit 1), becomes the followed record with the trail bit (`V:54E72`, `W2` bit `0x1000`) and its state byte becomes 1 (the test for the cruise missile compares the masked class with `0x15` and never matches, so class `0x15` is treated like any aircraft) [V: `counters_check.py`].

### 13.6 What the port must do

1. **Trail** (Task 6 core, Task 8 draw): the ring, the followed record, the length rule and the writers of the trail bit as 13.1; the draw as 13.2 after the burst. The record's trail length is core state; the discs are drawn by the frontend from the ring (scene entry: the ring positions, the length, the out-of-control bit).
2. **Jolt** (Task 6): the kind byte with the four writers, the index and the four offsets; the scene carries the current row offset d (-1, 0, +1) and the cockpit-only rule (|`V:4AA3E`| <= 14); Task 8 shifts the window and the HUD by d scanlines.
3. **Sound** (milestone 6): the triggers of 13.4 as scene sound events with the object number, voice and arguments; the combat list is short: gun (object `0xA` or 6), release (6), launch of the camera's record (`0xC`), bumps, crash, ejection, and the tones.
4. **Counters** (Task 6/7): exactly the writers of 13.5, in particular that flare and chaff releases add 3 to the rounds fired, that only the player's launches count as fired and only player-owned weapons (copy of the player's `W0` bit 3 and group 0) count as hits, and that the cannon hit counter is separate from the target's own counter.

## Open questions

### Part A (sections 1 to 6)

1. (**Closed**, 2.4, measured) **The distribution of the mission-start selector `V:4ABB0`**: it is not uniform: the clock is read a fixed time after a vertical blank, so the selector is (20,000 x the frame number within the second + a path constant of about 1.2 ms for the demonstration and 1.6 ms for the missions) mod 65,536; 13 demonstration starts and 4 mission starts measured. The remaining uncertainty is the jitter of the read (a few microseconds) and the modes 5 and 7 (not measured).
2. (**Closed**, milestone 7) **Class `0x15` skip rule of the placer** (2.1 step 5: captured, `combat7-placer15.txt`, kept and placed cases; `tests/test_entities.c`) and the **W0 bit 1 case** of the target handling (`0x27E70`, the `V:4AA78` branch: the player's missile's live retarget, 8.3: captured, `combat7-retarget.txt`).
3. (**Closed**, milestone 7, 4.4) **The lost-target loop**: captured (`combat7-losttarget.txt`): a counted record with an invalid target and `+0x5D` = 3 (no entry for its slot in the sub-list or in region 3's list) stops the machine's pass counter at once; with a matching region entry or `+0x5D` = 0 it does not. No shipped record reaches it; the port ends the search (a safe choice, nothing to compare after the original's hang).
4. (**Closed**, 5.3, 8.9) **A counted hostile's kill in the emulator**: the demo's AIM-120 kill (`combat-hit-demolong.txt`), a staged Sidewinder kill (`followup-combat-kills-missile.txt`) and a gun kill from a fresh MiG (`followup-combat-kills.txt`) all end in `V:4A963` fifteen passes after the dying start; the gun capture also shows the kept wreck counted again at each later dying cycle (`V:4A963` = 1, 2, 3).
5. (**Closed**, milestone 7) **The mode 6 pod and the class `0x32` step**: the emulator's F4 run with the parachute moved onto the pod (`mission-m6-ok.log`, `gen-mission-pod.py`) shows the class `0x32` record stepped by the seat routine of 6.3a: it descends by 769 position units a pass (velocity y -769, `+0x4C` counting up from 21), like the class `0x31` parachute; what the rescue operation does with it (`0x1EE4E`) is the mission script's part (note 08 12.10), whose end-to-end witness is the F4 golden.
6. (**Closed**, 6.5) **The release of the hold by a destroyed seat**: staged by poking the dying bit into the flying seat (`followup-combat-batch-e.txt`): the seat is freed, the player's W2 gets the release bit and the nibble is cleared two passes later; a seat marked crashed only is not converted and keeps flying.
7. (**Closed**, 6.5) **Parachute flight**: captured (`followup-combat-parachute.txt`): class `0x31`, counter from -18, no view switch, a descent of 769 position units per pass, 600 passes without a landing.
8. (**Closed**, 6.4, 6.5) **The crash hand-over with an ejection nibble in the emulator**: captured (`followup-combat-crash-after-eject.txt`): the crash clears the nibble and the hand-over runs as for any crash (end code 4, the hold, the allowance 5 to 4, the restart); the seat is freed at the restart.

9. **UNREACHABLE (R, milestone 7): a target index of 16 or more and a target record beyond the 16** (the low seven bits of `+0x38` at 16 or more, read by the AI's threatened test 4.5, the launch decision 4.6 and the missile guidance 8.3): the placement writes `0xFF`, `0x80` or `0x80 + r` with r at most 14 (2.1), the range rule writes `0x80`, the live retarget writes the record index of the designation (0 to 15) or leaves the byte, and the exit of a region (2.5) writes `0x80`, `0xFF` or `0x80 + r` from the same entries; these are the writers this note found, no other store was looked for beyond them [R]; so the original's reads of the secondary objects and the variable area behind the 16 records never happen. The port treats such an index as no target (`ai.c`, `weapons.c`, NOTE-GAP) and no test pins that choice.

### Part B (sections 7 to 13)

Status after the rounds of the port's review (the commits that settled the closed ones are named by their section; "R" = read from the code and not run, "V" = verified). Closed items are kept with the section that settles them.

#### Sections 7-8

1. (**Closed**, 8.2, `weapon_check.py landmark`, 12,600 cases, 0 differences) **The landmark branch of the tracer test**: eleven ordinals (0 to 10, not ten), the cell and box tests, the chain, the 8-sub-step plane test, the pointer artefact in the z offset, the no-motion exit.
2. (**Closed**, 8.2 and 8.2a, `weapon_check.py bighull`, `tests/rig/test_ship_diff.c` `bighull` 4 x 4,000 cases) **The big-hull test `0x2B3F0`**: the flag byte, the two reference rules, the missile models (Sidewinder yes, AMRAAM no).
3. (**Closed**, 8.2a, `template_parts.py`) **Part ids of the other templates**: the low nibbles are 0, 1, 2 and 0xF only (all sixteen templates scanned); the missiles and the ships are always 0xFF.
4. (**Closed for the mechanism**, 8.3, 8.9) **The misses of the staged rig runs of 8.5** (148 and 202 du): the emulator run shows the cause (ten blind passes, the ramp, the lost mark); the rig runs are presumed the same and were not traced pass by pass.
5. (**Closed**, 8.9) **Missile kills in the emulator**: the demo's AIM-120 kill, a staged Sidewinder kill (`followup-combat-kills-missile.txt`) and a gun kill from a fresh MiG (`followup-combat-kills.txt`).
6. (**Closed**, 8.1) **`V:4A840`** (8.1 step 3): the freeze byte of the front end's stages, 1 from the common mission-start fill to the flight-start states, 0 in every flight pass (below).
7. (**Closed**, 8.2, 8.2a) **A tracer against an AMRAAM**: the aircraft hull test returns no hit for a drawn missile (its part id is 0xFF at every level); the gun cannot hit an AMRAAM.
8. (**Closed**, 8.6, `test_weapons_diff` `launchdec` 2,000 cases) **The launches of the records 9, 11 and 13**; (**closed**, 7.6) the AI rate gate; (**closed**, 7.6, R) **`V:4A873` and `V:4A874` have no writer** in the image.
9. (**Closed**, 8.1 and 8.5) **The missile in the launch pass**: the boost runs in it.

#### Sections 9-10

1. (**Closed**, 9.3, `weapon_check.py decoy`) **Can a flare or a chaff slot hit an aircraft?** Yes: the hit test has no condition on the slot kind (12 hits each in 1,500 cases per kind, the log counter `+0x3C` counts).
2. (**Closed**, 13.4) **Sample indices of the release sound and the ECM click.**
3. (**Closed**, 9.4, `combat-cm-missile.txt`, `-flare.txt`) **A hostile missile against a flare in the emulator.**
4. (**Closed**, 10.5, rig) **The roll and yaw part of "out of control"**: the roll and rudder keys still act (equal roll and yaw rates with and without B20 bit 1); only the pitch and the speed are lost.
5. (**Closed**, 10.5) **Other readers of `V:4A984` bit 8**: none; the collisions-off post at `0x13D0A` is the only reader and the damage handler at `0x2A880` the only writer (all 42 absolute references listed).
6. **The 29-pass protection by a released decoy**: not a question; the rule is a consequence of the cool-down bytes (9.1, 10.4) and is recorded so the port does not drop it.
7. (**Closed**, 13.1 and the table of result handling, `weapon_check.py nonplayer`, `result40`; 6.5) **Which result reaches `0x2A954`** and what the player with an ejection nibble does for the result `0x40`; the screen of the five passes between the key and the seat is captured (`followup-combat-eject-frames.txt`, 6.5): the unchanged cockpit with the flashing EJECT lamp, then the seat's picture with the old panel for one picture.
8. (**Closed**, 9.5) **The ECM divisor class**: the rule reads "class other than 1"; only classes 0 and 1 launch, so the two statements agree.

#### Sections 11 and 13

1. (**Closed**, 11.3) **The life word of an effect object wraps**: captured (`followup-combat-batch-d.txt`, part `wrap2`): at 100 du an object with the life word 0x7F or 0x40 draws the starburst (a large disc, 15,000 and 25,000 pixels) and with **0x7FFF, 0x80, 0x8C, 0x9C, 0xA0 or 0x100 draws nothing**, the scar included: the port's rule (nothing from 0x80 on) is the original's observed picture, no longer a deviation.
2. (**Closed**, 11.5, unobservable) **The revival in the aircraft draw handler**: needs an invalid record with a negative counter in the display queue; every freeing site requests a rebuild first, so the state does not arise (a seat freed in the emulator was not drawn again).
3. (**Closed**, 11.4) **What drew the wreck before the burst**: the intact model; no model program reads W0, the hit counts, the counter or B20 (selectors read the gear and ejection nibbles, W2 bit 3, the frame counter and the record index).
4. (**Closed**, 8.2, `weapon_check.py entity`, `combat-hit-gun.txt`) **The hit counter `+0x3C`.** **Open**: the pitch and volume parameters of the sounds and the cue tones belong to note 10.
5. **Which wrapper the launch of a non-camera record uses** (13.4): **open, R** (none; a capture of the voice registers at a launch would confirm that only the camera's launches are audible).
6. (**Closed**, 13.3) **The jolt writers** other than the hit: touchdown (kind 1), hard touchdown and landing after a stop (3), take-off roll and lift-off (4); kind 2 is stored by nothing.
7. (**Closed**, milestone 7; the burst **unobservable** in play) **The pixels of the flare star and the chaff** (7.7, 11.4): the flare star is closed (7.7: three one-pixel lines in pen 9, 50 pixels, exactly the rig's line calls for the logged slot and camera; `flare_chaff_replay.py`); the chaff's plain pixel is **seen** at the draw-time camera (7.7: head-on and fixed tower views, pen 13, one row above the routine's y in the picture; hidden by the aircraft from the second pass in the head-on view because the gun runs first; chase and look-back views: outside the view); the burst's twelve lines are drawn in pen 13 but plot nothing at the distance of the only view that keeps them in front of the camera (the original's own routines, rig), so no burst pixel is ever shown: UNOBSERVABLE, the rule stands on the rig and the code.
8. (**Closed**, 11.4: unobservable) **The result word of the tail-only draw** is stale stack data that nothing reads; the port may return 0.

#### Sections 12

1. (**Closed**, 12.3, measured) **The units of the scope window against the picture's mile labels**: 2^n list units per pixel (n = the nibble); the labels 40, 10 and 2 statute miles are the 21-row distance of the window's lower edge, rounded down.
2. (**Closed**, 12.3, R) **The word `0x53818`** past the second erase page: the first word of the display-list builder's scratch; read as the eleventh erase entry of a full page; not observable in play.
3. (**Closed**, 12.5, `combat-radar-demo2.txt`, `weapon_check.py order`, milestone 7: `combat7-gunorder.txt`, `combat7-gunorder1.txt`, `gunorder_check.py`, `test_weapons_diff.c` part `gunorder`) **The order of the draw-handler call `0x284F8` against the dying path**, and the gun's place in the frame: the scan `0x2AD18` decides on the highest active slot; a gun kill of a designated target posts `0x4016` in the pass of the kill when the pool held an active slot at the scan (captured), and one pass later with no post when it did not (captured).
4. (**Closed by reference**, milestone 7) **The game-time length of a "pass"** (12.2, 12.3, 12.6): it is defined by note 08 section 8.1's conversion (the port's one rule); the re-timed capture `combat-radar-demo2.txt` samples one pass per sample. Nothing in this note adds a rule of its own.
5. (**Closed**, 12.9, R) **`V:4A980` bit 15 and `V:4A98E` bit 5**: never set (only the flight start's block clear and the message expiry's bit clear write them): entries 6, 8, 32 and 34 are dead code.
6. (**Closed**, 12.4, `followup-combat-batch-a.txt` part `threat`, `-batch-f.txt` part `threat1`) **The hostile-missile warning** (threat bits 4 and 5): a class-0 missile aimed at the player gives `V:4A925` = `0x10` and the alert `0x8010`; a class-1 missile gives `0x20` and `0x8012`.
7. (**Closed**, 12.1) **`V:4A8EE` and `V:4A8EF` at the first pass of a free flight**: captured, 0 and 0xFF; the external-view panel draw is skipped by the window clip and fills a band that is not shown (12.1); the first non-zero write at pass 93 of that capture is the approach camera's window-row change `0x14388` (12.1, milestone 7; unobservable in flight) and the panel band's fills are written but not shown (12.1, milestone 7).
8. (**UNREACHABLE**, R, milestone 7) **The entry the dead key body `0x1ECA4` clears** (9.7): nothing in the image jumps to the body and it does not set the registers it uses, so it cannot be run in the machine by any key or state, and no run-time poke of data can reach code that no path enters (the driver has no command that sets the program counter); what it would clear is a property of its registers, which a caller would have to supply. It is the basis of the W key, the one PORT FEATURE of the port, and its behaviour is therefore a port choice, not a rule of the game.
9. (**Closed**, 12.7) **The HUD driver order, the slew, the sight's record, the pens** (pipper 13; message line 1, 4, 8), **the writers of `V:4A8EF` and `V:4A8EE`**, `V:4A9D0` = 40 x `V:4A990`.
10. **Part A's remaining items that touch this note** (not mine): the cruise missile's dive "x speed drift" of the port's comparison (4.12), and the launch-order conflict settled by the Part A author.

#### Sections 12

- **Radar list, scope walk, designation** (`tools/m68k-rig/tests/lists_model/radar_check.py`, needs `extracted/interceptor.bin`, `librig.so`; the rig loads the original's routines): part 1 the gate and the list builder, 300 random lists (record flags, positions to 2^31, view records 0 to 15, random rotation words) and 64 gating cases (view, redraw counter, frame counter), 0 differences; part 2 the scope walk `0x35E5E`, 300 passes per seed in two seeds, random lists with 0 to 14 entries, dense passes, records with random flags, classes, detected bits, modes 3, 5, 2 and `0x7D`, both pages, the eleven-entry erase read, view offsets, the designated blink, the pens, the ten-blip limit (4 passes at the limit), the threat bits and the cue dispatch against `V:4A926` (45 passes with a cue), the mission-1 counter, `W0` and `B20` bookkeeping, the terminator; the blips are compared as the calls of the pixel routines with their pens; 0 differences; part 2b the cue dispatch alone, 300 runs over chosen bit sets and previous sets (181 distinct combinations), 0 differences; part 4 the builder, the walk and a T request end to end on the placed mission-3 hostile at 100 random distances and bearings (76 in the window and designated, 65 detected, 0 differences); part 3 the designation `0x362D8`, 600 requests (83 first designations, 142 steps, 375 deselections at the end of the list, 225 with a target not in the list), 0 differences.
- **HUD target data** (`hud_target_check.py`): part A the message-line target data `0x36DB2`, 600 runs per seed (the three pages, the names by class, the undetected case, the hold-off, the redraw counter, the no-target branch and the posted-message copy), 0 differences; part B the Y key body, 60 runs over 9 page words; part C the fire prompt, pipper pixel, circle, range dot and range number logic of `0x385F4`, 600 runs; part D the gun pipper history `0x3878E`, 200 runs against the original's projection routine; part E the player's range update `0x2835A`, 400 runs; part F the lock state `0x38884`, 600 runs; part G the range number text `0x367DA`, 200 values; all 0 differences.
- **Emulator** (`tools/vamiga-ref/scripts/combat-radar-demo.txt`, generator `gen-combat-radar.py`): see `docs/re/01a-reference-captures.md`.
